# Tab Junkie — Product Backlog

**Version:** 1.0
**Date:** 2026-04-15
**Author:** Product Management
**Status:** Active
**Changelog:** v1.0 — Initial backlog creation · Populated from PRD v1.0 (2026-04-15) · B-001 split into B-001a/b/c/d after R0 spike (2026-04-15). Downstream references to `B-001` mean at minimum `B-001a` (the CRUD foundation).

---

## Backlog Legend

**Priority**
- **P0**: Critical — blocks launch or core value
- **P1**: High — essential for competitive product
- **P2**: Medium — important but not blocking
- **P3**: Nice-to-have — improves experience

**Effort**
- **XS**: < 2 hours
- **S**: 2-8 hours (half day to one day)
- **M**: 1-3 days
- **L**: 3-5 days (one week)
- **XL**: 5-10 days (one to two weeks)

**Status**
- **done**: Completed and verified
- **in-progress**: Currently being worked on
- **backlog**: Not started

---

## Phase A — Foundation

| ID | User Story | Priority | Effort | Status | Sprint | Dependencies | Acceptance Criteria |
|----|-----------|----------|--------|--------|--------|--------------|---------------------|
| B-001a | As a developer, I want a partitioned `chrome.storage.local` schema with Item/Group/Preferences CRUD and ULID identifiers, so that all downstream features have a stable, writable data layer. | P0 | M | done | 1 | — ⚠️ **New storage schema** → Full tier; supersedes B-001 (split per R0 spike 2026-04-15) | **AC1** All six partitions are initialized under their exact keys (`tj:meta`, `tj:items`, `tj:groups`, `tj:prefs`, `tj:drift`, `tj:floatingGroups`) on first SW startup; a subsequent `chrome.storage.local.get` on each key returns a defined value (not `undefined`).<br>**AC2** `listItems()` and `listGroups()` return `[]` (empty array, not `undefined` or `null`) when storage contains no items or no groups respectively; a freshly initialized extension with no user data must satisfy this invariant without additional handling by callers.<br>**AC3** Every ID generated by a create operation is a valid ULID string (26 characters, Crockford Base32, monotonically sortable within the same millisecond); IDs are never re-used after delete; IDs are never derived from or contain any substring of the item's URL or title.<br>**AC4** `createGroup()` or `updateGroup()` with `parentId` set to a group that already has a non-null `parentId` is rejected synchronously with a typed error (`ERR_DEPTH_EXCEEDED`); setting `parentId` equal to the group's own ID or to any of its descendants is rejected with `ERR_CIRCULAR_REF`; no partial write reaches storage on rejection.<br>**AC5** Any call to a CRUD write function (create, update, delete) made directly from a UI context (sidepanel, newtab, popup) is rejected with `ERR_DIRECT_WRITE`; the only code path that calls `chrome.storage.local.set` is the service worker message handler; this is enforced structurally (write functions not exported to non-SW modules).<br>**AC6** `writeTransaction(ops)` executes all logical steps and commits to storage via a single `chrome.storage.local.set`; if the SW is terminated after the call resolves, a subsequent read reflects either the full committed state or the pre-transaction state — never a partial intermediate state; `writeTransaction` is the only mechanism used internally for any multi-key write.<br>**AC7** Deleting a group that contains items moves all those items to `groupId: null` (Ungrouped) in the same `writeTransaction` — no item is deleted as a side effect of group deletion; the resulting storage state contains zero items referencing the deleted group ID.<br>**AC8** Reading from storage when the stored value is structurally invalid (missing required fields, wrong type) returns a typed error (`ERR_CORRUPT_DATA`) rather than silently returning a malformed object or throwing an unhandled exception; the corrupt partition is isolated and does not prevent reads from other partitions.<br>**AC9** Single-item read (`getItem(id)`) and single-item write (`updateItem(id, patch)`) complete in under 20 ms P95 measured against a collection of 1,000 items in a test environment using the chrome-mock; this is asserted in the automated test suite.<br>**AC10** `writeTransaction` is re-entrant-safe: if two `writeTransaction` calls are initiated concurrently (e.g., two rapid UI messages arrive before the first resolves), they are serialized — the second call waits for the first to commit before executing; the final storage state reflects both operations applied sequentially with no lost update. |
| B-001b | As a developer, I want a schema version field plus a migration runner gated by a service-worker `ready` barrier, so that schema evolution is safe and downgrades fail closed into read-only safe-mode. | P0 | M | done | 2 | B-001a ⚠️ **Migration logic** → Full tier | **AC1 — Schema version gate (normal path):** On SW startup, `tj:meta.schemaVersion` is read before `readyPromise` resolves; if `schemaVersion === KNOWN_VERSION` (currently `1`), migrations are skipped and `readyPromise` resolves; any `chrome.runtime.onMessage` handler that `await`s `readyPromise` before processing confirms this gate is live by verifying message dispatch does not begin until the promise settles.<br>**AC2 — Fresh-install path:** When storage is completely empty (no `tj:meta` key), the migration runner initializePartitions seeds schemaVersion: 1; absence is handled by init, not by the migration runner; `initializePartitions()` runs; `tj:meta.schemaVersion` is written to `KNOWN_VERSION`; `readyPromise` resolves without error; no migration step is executed.<br>**AC3 — Corrupted or missing `schemaVersion` field:** When `tj:meta` exists but `schemaVersion` is absent, `null`, `NaN`, or a non-integer, the runner rejects `readyPromise` with `ERR_CORRUPT_DATA`; all write messages respond `ERR_NOT_READY`; read messages (`MSG_LIST_ITEMS`, `MSG_LIST_GROUPS`, `MSG_GET_PREFERENCES`) still resolve using the last-known partition data; the condition is observable in the automated test suite via a chrome-mock seeded with a corrupt meta partition.<br>**AC4 — Sequential multi-step migration (v1 → v2 → v3):** Given a migration registry with steps for v1→v2 and v2→v3, and storage seeded with `schemaVersion: 1`, the runner applies both steps in order within a single `writeTransaction`; the final `tj:meta.schemaVersion` equals `3`; if the v2→v3 step throws, `tj:meta` is left at `schemaVersion: 1` (no partial version bump); `readyPromise` rejects with `ERR_TX_CONFLICT`; the test asserts the version did not advance past the failed step.<br>**AC5 — Downgrade / read-only safe-mode:** When `stored.schemaVersion > KNOWN_VERSION`, `readyPromise` resolves (not rejects); all write messages (`MSG_CREATE_ITEM`, `MSG_UPDATE_ITEM`, `MSG_DELETE_ITEM`, `MSG_CREATE_GROUP`, `MSG_UPDATE_GROUP`, `MSG_DELETE_GROUP`, `MSG_SET_PREFERENCES`) return `{ ok: false, error: { code: 'ERR_SAFE_MODE' } }`; all read messages (`MSG_LIST_ITEMS`, `MSG_LIST_GROUPS`, `MSG_GET_ITEM`, `MSG_GET_GROUP`, `MSG_GET_PREFERENCES`) return `{ ok: true, data: … }` with current partition data; the safe-mode state is detectable via a new `MSG_GET_STATUS` message (or equivalent query) that returns `{ safeMode: true, schemaVersion: N, knownVersion: M }`; a test seeds `schemaVersion: 999` and asserts this behaviour pass/fail per message type.<br>**AC6 — Quota warning UX (80% threshold):** After every successful `writeTransaction`, the runner reads `lastQuotaSample.bytesInUse` via `_peekQuotaSample()`; if `bytesInUse / chrome.storage.local.QUOTA_BYTES >= 0.80`, a non-blocking quota-warning notification is surfaced to the UI (mechanism: a new message push or a flag readable via `MSG_GET_STATUS`); the warning does NOT block or delay the write that triggered it; it does NOT show on reads; it dismisses when the next sample falls below 80%; the automated test asserts the threshold trigger using a chrome-mock whose `getBytesInUse` returns a value at exactly 80% of `QUOTA_BYTES`.<br>**AC7 — Legacy `junkie_*` key migration:** On first SW startup where `junkie_*` keys are detected alongside `tj:*` keys, the runner copies any recoverable data into the `tj:*` schema (or discards if the shape is unrecognisable), then removes all `junkie_*` keys via `chrome.storage.local.remove()`; the removal executes within `readyPromise` before the gate opens; after the gate opens, a `chrome.storage.local.get()` on any `junkie_*` key returns `undefined`; if the removal itself fails, `readyPromise` still resolves (best-effort cleanup — orphaned legacy keys are not a blocking condition); the automated test seeds both key namespaces and asserts the `junkie_*` keys are absent post-startup.<br>**AC8 — CRUD-before-ready is rejected:** Any `chrome.runtime.onMessage` arriving while `readyPromise` is still pending (not yet resolved or rejected) is held until the promise settles, then processed normally if resolved or returned as `ERR_NOT_READY` if rejected; the message channel is never dropped; the test drives this by delaying `readyPromise` resolution and asserting the held message returns the correct outcome once the gate opens.<br>**AC9 — Migration runner performance:** A migration run over the maximum realistic dataset (1,000 items, 100 groups, preferences) completes within 500 ms in the chrome-mock test environment; this is asserted as a timed automated test.<br>**AC10 — Rollback plan documented:** `docs/SOLUTION_DESIGN.md` contains an updated §12 Rollback Plan entry describing: (a) how to revert B-001b code, (b) what happens to on-disk `tj:meta.schemaVersion` after a code rollback, and (c) how safe-mode protects users on downgrade; this entry is verifiable by the [solution-architect] at R6 close. |
| B-001c | As a developer, I want an in-memory `LiveTabIndex` plus a `storage.session` `TabClaims` disambiguation table, so that saved items correctly resolve to a specific live tab (including when multiple items share a URL). | P0 | M | done | 2 | B-001a ⚠️ **New message contract for live-state reads** → Full tier | **AC1 — Cold-start rebuild:** On SW cold start, `chrome.tabs.query({})` is called once; the result is used to populate `LiveTabIndex` (SW-memory `Map<tabId, {url, active, audible}>`) before any live-state read is served; the rebuild completes in under 100 ms for 50 open tabs in the chrome-mock test environment; this is asserted as a timed automated test.<br>**AC2 — TabClaims population on cold start:** After `LiveTabIndex` is built, `chrome.storage.session` is read for any surviving `TabClaims`; each claim is validated against `LiveTabIndex` (tabId still present, URL still matches); invalid claims are discarded; remaining unclaimed items with a URL present in `LiveTabIndex` are claimed in ascending `sortOrder` (first-unclaimed-wins); the final `TabClaims` state is written back to `storage.session` atomically; all of this completes before `readyPromise` resolves.<br>**AC3 — Multi-item URL disambiguation:** Given three saved items sharing the same URL and two open tabs at that URL, exactly two distinct items each receive a unique `tabId` claim and the third item's `live` state is `false`; no two `TabClaims` entries share the same `tabId`; this invariant is asserted in an automated test seeded with duplicate-URL items.<br>**AC4 — Tab-event in-memory updates (zero storage.local writes):** `chrome.tabs.onUpdated`, `chrome.tabs.onActivated`, and `chrome.tabs.onRemoved` each update `LiveTabIndex` in SW memory only; none of these handlers call `chrome.storage.local.set` or `writeTransaction`; the automated test spies on `chrome.storage.local.set` and asserts it is never invoked in response to any tab event.<br>**AC5 — Tab closed — claim cleanup:** When `chrome.tabs.onRemoved` fires for a tab that holds a `TabClaim`, the claim is removed from `storage.session` and the corresponding item's `live` state becomes `false`; the item is NOT deleted from `tj:items`; the update is reflected in the next `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` response.<br>**AC6 — Tab URL change — claim follows URL:** When `chrome.tabs.onUpdated` fires with a new URL for a claimed tab, the `LiveTabIndex` entry is updated; if the new URL no longer matches the claimed item's saved URL, the claim is released and reassigned (or dropped); if the new URL matches a different saved item, that item may receive a new claim in the next re-evaluation cycle; no `storage.local` write occurs.<br>**AC7 — Browser window closed — all claims for that window cleared:** When `chrome.windows.onRemoved` fires, all `TabClaims` entries whose `tabId` belonged to that window are removed from `storage.session`; the corresponding items' `live` states become `false`; this is asserted in an automated test that removes a window containing two claimed tabs.<br>**AC8 — storage.session wiped (browser restart) — re-claim on startup:** `storage.session` is cleared on browser restart by Chrome; on the next SW cold start, `TabClaims` is absent; the cold-start rebuild path in AC2 runs from scratch and re-establishes claims from live tabs; no stale claims from the previous session are carried forward.<br>**AC9 — Read-time live-state merge via enriched MSG_LIST_ITEMS:** The `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` response includes a new `liveStates` map alongside the `Item[]` array: `{ items: Item[], liveStates: Record<itemId, { live: boolean, active: boolean, audible: boolean }> }`; the map is built at read time from `LiveTabIndex` + `TabClaims`; items with no claim have `{ live: false, active: false, audible: false }`; no live-state field is stored on the `Item` object in `tj:items`; the shape change is documented in `shared/messages.js` and in `docs/SOLUTION_DESIGN.md`.<br>**AC10 — Claim resolution performance:** Building `TabClaims` from a 500-item collection against a 50-tab `LiveTabIndex` completes in under 50 ms in the chrome-mock test environment; this is asserted as a timed automated test. |
| B-001d | As a user, I want drifted items and floating tab groups to survive browser restarts with exact window+index re-association, so that my workspace is restored faithfully after closing the browser. | P0 | L | done | 3 | B-001a, B-001b, B-001c ⚠️ **Drift + floating state in storage** → Full tier | **AC1 — Drift record written on URL divergence:** When `chrome.tabs.onUpdated` fires and the new URL, after passing through `normalizeForMatch`, differs from the item's saved URL, a drift record `{ itemId, driftedToUrl: <raw new URL>, detectedAt: Date.now() }` is written to `tj:drift[itemId]` via `writeTransaction` within 200 ms of the event; a `chrome.storage.local.get('tj:drift')` immediately after confirms the entry exists with correct fields; the test asserts using chrome-mock seeded with a claimed item and fires a synthetic `onUpdated` event.<br>**AC2 — Drift record cleared on navigation back to saved URL:** When a drifted tab navigates to a URL that — after `normalizeForMatch` — matches the item's saved URL, the `tj:drift[itemId]` entry is removed via `writeTransaction` within 200 ms; `chrome.storage.local.get('tj:drift')` immediately after confirms the key is absent; the test asserts against a chrome-mock pre-seeded with an existing drift record.<br>**AC3 — Fragment-only change does NOT create a drift record:** "Fragment-only" is defined as: the two URLs are identical after stripping the fragment component (`#…`) and applying `normalizeForMatch` to both; specifically, stripping is done by setting `url.hash = ''` on a `URL` object before normalization, not by string split. Given a saved URL `https://example.com/page` and a tab that navigates to `https://example.com/page#section`, no entry is written to `tj:drift`; `chrome.storage.local.get('tj:drift')` returns `{}`; the test asserts against two distinct URLs that differ only in fragment.<br>**AC4 — Normalized-match clears drift even when raw URLs differ:** Given a saved URL `https://example.com/page/` (trailing slash) and a drifted tab that navigates to `https://example.com/page` (no trailing slash), `normalizeForMatch` resolves both to the same canonical form; the drift record is cleared as per AC2; the test uses two URLs that are unequal as raw strings but equal after normalization.<br>**AC5 — Drift records survive browser restart:** Given `tj:drift` contains one or more records in `chrome.storage.local`, simulating a service-worker restart (clearing all SW in-memory state and re-running the cold-start init path) and then reading `chrome.storage.local.get('tj:drift')` returns the same records unchanged; no B-001d code wipes `tj:drift` on startup; the test drives the full cold-start path via the chrome-mock.<br>**AC6 — Unclaimed tab drift is a no-op:** If `chrome.tabs.onUpdated` fires for a tabId that holds no `TabClaim` (i.e., it is not associated with any saved item), no entry is written to `tj:drift` and no `writeTransaction` is called; the test spies on `writeTransaction` and asserts it is not invoked when an unclaimed tab changes URL.<br>**AC7 — `tj:floatingGroups` record shape and persistence:** When a tab is designated as floating (not yet defined by this item's build, but the partition must accept the shape), each entry in `tj:floatingGroups` must conform to `{ groupId: string, windowId: number, tabIndex: number, url: string, savedAt: number }`; writing a well-formed entry via `writeTransaction` and reading it back returns the same object with no field mutation; `windowId` and `tabIndex` are finite integers ≥ 0; the test uses the chrome-mock to write and read back a sample record.<br>**AC8 — Cold-start re-association: window+index match succeeds:** On SW cold start, for each record in `tj:floatingGroups`, the system attempts to find a live tab in `LiveTabIndex` whose `windowId` and `index` both match the stored `windowId` + `tabIndex`; if found, that tab is claimed for the group before any floating-group URL fallback is attempted; the test seeds `LiveTabIndex` with a tab at the exact stored position and asserts the claim is established via the window+index path (not the URL path).<br>**AC9 — Cold-start re-association: URL fallback when window is gone:** If no live tab in `LiveTabIndex` matches the stored `windowId`+`tabIndex` (e.g., the window was closed), the system falls back to matching by `normalizeForMatch(storedUrl)` against `normalizeForMatch(tab.url)` for unclaimed tabs; the first matching unclaimed tab (ascending tabId) is claimed for the group; if no URL match exists either, the floating-group record is left in `tj:floatingGroups` with no claim assigned (not deleted); the test uses two separate chrome-mock scenarios: one with a surviving window-match tab (must pick window path) and one with no window-match but a URL-match tab.<br>**AC10 — Re-association completes before `readyPromise` resolves:** The floating-group re-association logic runs as part of the cold-start sequence gated by `readyPromise`; a `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` message dispatched after `readyPromise` resolves sees the final post-re-association `liveStates`; no race is observable; the test asserts that `buildLiveStates` called after `readyPromise` resolves reflects the re-associated claims.<br>**AC11 — Multiple floating tabs in the same group all re-associate independently:** Given a `tj:floatingGroups` containing two records sharing the same `groupId` but different `windowId`/`tabIndex` pairs, both tabs attempt re-association independently; a successful position-match for one does not prevent the other from also matching; the test seeds two records and two live tabs and asserts both claims are established.<br>**AC12 — `storage.session` wipe does not lose floating-group hints:** Because `tj:floatingGroups` is stored in `storage.local` (not `storage.session`), a browser restart that wipes `storage.session` does not affect floating-group re-association; after simulated session wipe and cold start, `tj:floatingGroups` records are still present and re-association runs normally; the test asserts `chrome.storage.local.get('tj:floatingGroups')` is non-empty after the cold-start path runs with `storage.session` cleared.<br>**AC13 — Performance: drift write + floating re-association within budget:** Drift record write (single item update) completes in under 20 ms P95 in the chrome-mock test environment (consistent with AC9 of B-001a); full floating-group re-association over 50 `tj:floatingGroups` records against a 50-tab `LiveTabIndex` completes in under 100 ms in the chrome-mock; both are asserted as timed automated tests. |
| B-002 | As a user, I want URLs I enter to be normalized and validated (protocol defaulting, trailing-slash stripping, fragment preservation), so that bookmarks match correctly during live-tab detection and I see a clear error for invalid input. | P1 | S | done | 3 | B-001a | **AC1 — Protocol defaulting:** Given a bare input such as `example.com` or `example.com/path` (no scheme present), `normalizeUrl()` prepends `https://` and returns a fully-qualified URL; the PASS condition is `normalizeUrl('example.com') === 'https://example.com'`; the FAIL condition is any result that is not a valid URL or does not begin with `https://`.<br>**AC2 — Scheme allowlist (storage boundary):** `assertValidUrl()` in `background/storage/items.js` accepts exactly `http:`, `https:`, and `file:` schemes; `ftp:` and `mailto:` (previously accepted) now throw `ERR_VALIDATION`; `javascript:`, `data:`, `chrome:`, and any other scheme also throw `ERR_VALIDATION`; the PASS condition is that calling `createItem` or `updateItem` with each of those schemes produces the correct accept/reject outcome as listed; the FAIL condition is any deviation from the allowlist.<br>**AC3 — Empty string and non-string input rejected:** Passing an empty string `''`, `null`, `undefined`, or a non-string value to `normalizeUrl()` throws a synchronous `TypeError` or returns an empty string (implementer chooses); `assertValidUrl('')` throws `ERR_VALIDATION`; the PASS condition is that no falsy or non-string URL silently passes through to storage.<br>**AC4 — Trailing-slash stripping for matching:** `normalizeUrl('https://example.com/')` returns `'https://example.com'`; `normalizeUrl('https://example.com/path/')` returns `'https://example.com/path'`; trailing slashes are NOT stripped when a query string is present (`https://example.com/?q=1` is returned unchanged); the PASS condition is that the stored URL and the live-tab URL normalize to the same string after stripping; the FAIL condition is any mismatch that would prevent a valid claim match.<br>**AC5 — Fragment preservation in stored URL, strip for matching:** `normalizeUrl('https://example.com/page#section', { forStorage: true })` returns `'https://example.com/page#section'` (fragment kept); `normalizeUrl('https://example.com/page#section', { forMatch: true })` returns `'https://example.com/page'` (fragment stripped); the PASS condition is that both call-sites (storage write and tab-claim match) receive the correct form; the FAIL condition is a stored URL with its fragment silently discarded.<br>**AC6 — Hostname lowercasing:** `normalizeUrl('HTTPS://Example.COM/Path')` normalizes hostname to `example.com`; path case is preserved; the PASS condition is `normalizeUrl('HTTPS://Example.COM/Path') === 'https://example.com/Path'`.<br>**AC7 — Shared `normalizeUrl()` consumed by both call-sites:** A single `normalizeUrl()` function is exported from `shared/url.js`; `background/storage/items.js` imports and calls it before calling `assertValidUrl`; `background/tabs/tab-claims.js` imports and calls it in place of the local `normalizeForMatch`; the PASS condition is that both files import from `shared/url.js` and the local `normalizeForMatch` function in `tab-claims.js` is removed; the FAIL condition is any remaining ad-hoc URL manipulation in either file.<br>**AC8 — Consistent application across create, edit, and import flows:** Calling `createItem`, `updateItem`, and the future bulk-create API (B-005) with an un-normalized URL (e.g., `EXAMPLE.COM`) results in the normalized form being stored; the PASS condition is that a `getItem` immediately after `createItem({ url: 'example.com' })` returns `url: 'https://example.com'`; the FAIL condition is the raw un-normalized string being stored.<br>**Design note — `file:` vs prior behavior:** B-001a's `assertValidUrl` previously rejected `file:` (only allowed `http/https/ftp/mailto`). B-002 changes the allowlist to `http`, `https`, `file` only; `ftp` and `mailto` are removed. The `[frontend-engineer]` must update `ALLOWED_URL_SCHEMES` in `items.js` as part of this item.<br>**Design note — IDN / punycode:** International domain names entered as unicode (e.g., `münchen.de`) are passed through the `URL` constructor which performs punycode encoding automatically; no additional IDN handling is required; this behavior is confirmed by the AC6 test.<br>**Design note — ports:** URLs with explicit ports (`https://example.com:8080/path`) are valid and pass through `normalizeUrl()` unchanged; port normalization (stripping default port 443 for https) is out of scope for B-002. |
| B-003 | As a user, I want to create, edit, and delete saved bookmarks via a dialog that captures title, URL, and target group, so that I can build and maintain my persistent bookmark collection. | P0 | L | done | 7 | B-001, B-006 | **AC1 — Dialog Open (Create path):** Clicking the `button.empty-state-cta` element when the empty state is visible opens the create dialog. PASS: A modal element with `role="dialog"`, `aria-modal="true"`, and a visible title of "Add Bookmark" (or equivalent) appears in the DOM; `#empty-state` remains visible behind it. FAIL: No modal appears, or the CTA click has no effect.<br>**AC2 — Dialog Open (Create path, secondary trigger):** A dedicated "Add Bookmark" control in the sidepanel header or toolbar (distinct from the empty-state CTA) also opens the create dialog when clicked. PASS: The same `role="dialog"` modal opens regardless of whether the list is empty or populated. FAIL: The control does not appear, or only works in the empty state.<br>**AC3 — Create Dialog — Field presence:** When the create dialog is open, it contains exactly three form fields: a text input for Title (`id="dialog-title"` or equivalent, type text), a text input for URL (`id="dialog-url"` or equivalent, type text), and a `<select>` element for Group (`id="dialog-group"` or equivalent). PASS: All three controls are present and editable. FAIL: Any field is absent or read-only.<br>**AC4 — Group Picker — Population and default:** When the create dialog opens, the Group `<select>` is populated by a `MSG_LIST_GROUPS` response. PASS: The first `<option>` in the select has value `""` or `"__ungrouped__"` and visible text "Ungrouped"; all existing groups appear as subsequent options in sort order; the select defaults to "Ungrouped" when no contextual group is pre-selected. FAIL: "Ungrouped" is absent, is not the first option, or the list is empty when groups exist.<br>**AC5 — Create Dialog — Submit sends MSG_CREATE_ITEM:** Filling Title with a non-empty string of ≤ 512 characters, URL with a valid absolute URL (e.g. `https://example.com`), selecting a group, and clicking the Submit button (or pressing Enter while the form is valid) dispatches a `chrome.runtime.sendMessage` call with `type === "tj/createItem"` and a payload containing `title`, `url`, and `groupId` (or `null` for Ungrouped). PASS: The response has `ok: true`. FAIL: No message is dispatched, payload is malformed, or response is `ok: false`.<br>**AC6 — Create Dialog — Success closes dialog and re-renders:** After `MSG_CREATE_ITEM` returns `ok: true`, the dialog element is removed from (or hidden in) the DOM, and the item list re-renders to include the newly created item. PASS: The new item's title appears in the `#item-list` within 500 ms of dialog close, triggered by the `MSG_STATE_CHANGED` broadcast with `scope === "items"`. FAIL: Dialog stays open, or the new item does not appear without a manual panel reload.<br>**AC7 — Edit Dialog — Pre-population:** Activating an "Edit" affordance on an existing item row opens the edit dialog with Title, URL, and Group pre-populated from the item's stored values. PASS: The Title input value equals the item's stored title; the URL input value equals the item's stored URL; the Group select's selected option matches the item's stored `groupId` (or "Ungrouped" if `groupId` is null). FAIL: Any field is blank, shows a different item's data, or the select defaults to the wrong option.<br>**AC8 — Edit Dialog — Submit sends MSG_UPDATE_ITEM:** Modifying any pre-populated field and clicking Submit dispatches `chrome.runtime.sendMessage` with `type === "tj/updateItem"` and a payload containing the item's `id` plus the updated `title`, `url`, and `groupId`. PASS: Response has `ok: true`; the item list re-renders with the updated values. FAIL: `MSG_CREATE_ITEM` is sent instead, payload is missing `id`, or re-render does not reflect the edit.<br>**AC9 — Delete — Live tab path (no confirmation):** When "Delete" is activated on an item whose tab is currently open (live-tab indicator visible), the extension dispatches `MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM` with the item's `id` — no confirmation dialog is shown. PASS: The saved item disappears from the bookmarks list; the same URL continues to appear as an ungrouped floating tab. FAIL: A confirmation dialog appears for the live-tab path, or the item persists as a saved bookmark after demotion.<br>**AC10 — Delete — No live tab path (confirmation required):** When "Delete" is activated on an item with no matching open tab, a confirmation dialog appears with the item's title and "Delete" + "Cancel" buttons. PASS: Clicking "Delete" dispatches `MSG_DELETE_ITEM` with the item's `id`; the item disappears from the list. Clicking "Cancel" closes the confirmation without dispatching any message. FAIL: No confirmation dialog appears, or `MSG_DELETE_ITEM` is dispatched without confirmation.<br>**AC11 — Validation — Empty title:** Submitting the create or edit dialog with an empty Title field does not dispatch any message. PASS: An inline error message appears adjacent to the Title input (e.g. "Title is required") associated with the field via `aria-describedby` or equivalent. FAIL: The form submits, the error appears detached from the field, or no error appears.<br>**AC12 — Validation — Invalid URL:** Submitting with a URL that is not a valid absolute URL does not dispatch any message. PASS: An inline error appears adjacent to the URL input client-side; if a well-formed URL is rejected server-side with `ok: false` and `error.code === "ERR_VALIDATION"`, the same inline error field is populated with the server's `error.message`. FAIL: An invalid URL is accepted and persisted, or the error appears only in the console.<br>**AC13 — Validation — Title length limit:** Submitting a title longer than 512 characters is rejected client-side before any message is dispatched. PASS: An inline error appears on the Title field stating the length constraint (e.g. "Title must be 512 characters or fewer"). FAIL: A title of 513+ characters is accepted and persisted.<br>**AC14 — Keyboard behavior:** When a dialog is open: Escape closes without dispatching any message and returns focus to the triggering element; Enter while the form is valid submits it; Tab cycles focus through all focusable elements inside the dialog only; Shift+Tab reverses the cycle — focus never escapes the dialog boundary. PASS: All three keyboard interactions behave as described. FAIL: Escape leaves dialog open, Tab escapes the dialog, or Enter triggers an unintended action.<br>**AC15 — Edit — Item no longer exists (ERR_NOT_FOUND):** If the edit dialog submits for an item no longer in storage, the response has `ok: false` and `error.code === "ERR_NOT_FOUND"`. PASS: The dialog displays an inline or dialog-level error (e.g. "This bookmark no longer exists") and offers a Dismiss action; no unhandled promise rejection surfaces. FAIL: The extension crashes, a blank item is created, or the error is silently swallowed. |

PASS: codebase search for `chrome://favicon` returns zero matches. FAIL: any such URL present.<br>**AC9 — Favicon img Accessibility:** Every favicon `<img>` has `alt=""` (empty string, decorative). PASS: DOM inspection shows exactly one or the other per row. FAIL: both visible in any row under any condition. |

| B-006 | As a user, I want to create, edit, and delete groups with a name and a color chosen from the 9-color semantic palette, so that I can organise bookmarks into meaningful collections. | P0 | M | done | 4 | B-001 | **AC1 — Palette enforcement (create):** Submitting `createGroup` with a color value not in `['blue','purple','teal','red','orange','pink','indigo','yellow','slate']` throws `ERR_VALIDATION`; the dialog keeps the form open and shows an inline error. Exact-case match only — `'Blue'` is rejected.<br>**AC2 — Palette enforcement (edit):** Submitting `updateGroup` with an out-of-palette color value throws `ERR_VALIDATION`; existing group is unchanged in storage.<br>**AC3 — Name validation:** Empty string, whitespace-only string, or name exceeding 256 characters each throw `ERR_VALIDATION` on create and edit; the dialog surfaces a distinct inline error per case. Name of exactly 256 characters is accepted.<br>**AC4 — Duplicate-name warning:** Creating or editing a group to a name already used by another group at the same nesting level (same `parentId`) shows a non-blocking warning toast before saving proceeds. Groups at different nesting levels with the same name do not trigger the warning. The save completes regardless (soft block only).<br>**AC5 — Delete cascade — items:** Calling `deleteGroup(id)` moves every item with `groupId === id` to `groupId: null` (Ungrouped) in the same atomic `writeTransaction`. Verified by `listItems` after delete.<br>**AC6 — Delete cascade — child groups:** Calling `deleteGroup(id)` on a group that has child groups (groups with `parentId === id`) moves those children to `parentId: null` (top-level) atomically. Verified by `listGroups` after delete.<br>**AC7 — Delete empty group:** Calling `deleteGroup` on a group with no items and no children completes without error and removes only that group record. Idempotent: calling `deleteGroup` with an unknown id is a silent no-op (`ERR_NOT_FOUND` is NOT thrown).<br>**AC8 — Color persistence and render:** After creating or editing a group, `getGroup(id).color` equals the submitted palette token. The group header chip renders the correct semantic color class matching that token — verified visually in UAT.<br>**AC9 — Edit pre-population:** The edit dialog opens with `name` and `color` fields pre-populated from the stored group record. Submitting without changes leaves the group record unmodified (same `updatedAt` is acceptable if no fields changed, or may update — either is a PASS). |

Disabled groups: in edit mode the group being edited is hidden from the list (self-nest prevention at the UI layer, complementing the storage `ERR_CIRCULAR_REF` gate). PASS = select is present, populated correctly, and defaults to Top-level on create; FAIL = field absent, missing options, or pre-selection wrong.<br>**AC2 — Parent field in edit-group dialog.** When the dialog opens in edit mode (`group` param not null), the parent select pre-selects the group's current `parentId` (or the Top-level option if `parentId === null`). Groups that already have children (would become depth-2 parents if this group were nested under them) and the group being edited itself are excluded from the list. PASS = select reflects current parent + excludes self + excludes groups-with-children; FAIL = wrong pre-selection or self/children-group visible.<br>**AC3 — Create with parent dispatches MSG_CREATE_GROUP with parentId set.** Submitting the create dialog with a selected parent dispatches `MSG_CREATE_GROUP` with `parentId: "<selected-id>"` (not null). PASS = message carries the parentId AND new row renders as a child of the parent section; FAIL = parentId null in message OR renders at top level.<br>**AC4 — Edit parent → MSG_UPDATE_GROUP patch includes parentId.** Changing the parent select and submitting edit mode dispatches `MSG_UPDATE_GROUP { id, patch: { parentId: "<new-id-or-null>" } }`. On success the list re-renders with the group in its new position (moved between top-level and child of `<new-id>`). PASS = message patch includes `parentId` and re-render reflects the move; FAIL = parentId missing from patch OR DOM unchanged.<br>**AC5 — Depth-exceeded rejection surfaces a friendly inline error.** When the backend rejects with `ERR_DEPTH_EXCEEDED` (e.g., attempting to nest a group that already has children), the dialog stays open and `#group-error-dialog` displays "Can't nest this group — groups can only be one level deep." Mapping happens in a new `_translateGroupError(code)` helper. PASS = friendly text shown AND dialog stays open AND submit button re-enables; FAIL = raw code shown OR dialog closes on error.<br>**AC6 — Circular-reference rejection surfaces a friendly inline error.** When the backend rejects with `ERR_CIRCULAR_REF`, `#group-error-dialog` displays "Can't nest a group under itself or one of its own sub-groups." PASS = friendly text shown; FAIL = raw code shown.<br>**AC7 — Sub-group indented rendering.** `.group-section--child` (already applied by the `buildGroupSection` render path for `parentId != null` groups) gains a CSS rule that indents the entire child section by `calc(var(--group-indent) * 1)` relative to its parent container. `--group-indent` is a new CSS custom property (token) defaulting to `16px`. Works in both light and dark themes. PASS = child groups visually indented under their parent, DevTools confirms the left margin/padding; FAIL = identical indentation to parent.<br>**AC8 — Parent picker excludes groups-with-children (depth-2 prevention).** In both create and edit modes, the parent select MUST NOT list any group whose id appears as `parentId` on any other group (i.e., a group that already has at least one child). Defensive UI layer — the storage `ERR_DEPTH_EXCEEDED` gate is the authority, but the UI pre-filters so users don't hit the error. Verified by Object.keys of the pre-computed children map. PASS = the option list excludes every such group; FAIL = a group-with-children appears in the list.<br>**AC9 — Parent picker is keyboard-accessible.** The `<select>` is reachable via Tab (natural form focus order), label is associated via `<label for="group-field-parent">`, Escape on the dialog closes it without dispatching (Escape handling already shared with the form). PASS = tab lands on select, label announces correctly, Escape exits; FAIL = tab skips, label missing, Escape broken.<br>**AC10 — Delete-parent cascade is already atomic in storage; UI re-renders children as top-level.** When a parent group is deleted via context menu, the backend (already shipped in `groups.js:deleteGroup`) reparents child groups to `parentId: null`. The sidepanel re-render MUST surface those children at top level after the broadcast. PASS = post-delete, formerly-nested children render as top-level sections; FAIL = children disappear OR render nested under a phantom parent.<br>**AC11 — Empty-state enumeration (C-9 compliance).** (a) Zero top-level groups in create mode → parent select shows only "Top-level (no parent)". (b) Zero top-level groups other than the one being edited → same. (c) Attempt to create a group with a parentId that has since been deleted by another sidepanel → backend rejects with ERR_NOT_FOUND; UI shows "Selected parent group no longer exists. Refreshing…" (AC5-style friendly translation). PASS = all three states render correctly without console errors; FAIL = empty state throws or shows broken UI.<br>**AC12 — Permission-minimization check (C-6 compliance).** Zero new `manifest.json` permissions. All work is sidepanel UI + existing storage calls. PASS = `git diff manifest.json` empty; FAIL = any permission added.<br>**AC13 — Test coverage.** New test file `tests/b007-sub-group-nesting.test.js` covering: create-with-parentId → list shows indented render; edit-parent → patch dispatched; ERR_DEPTH_EXCEEDED translated; ERR_CIRCULAR_REF translated; delete-parent promotes children; parent picker excludes self + children-holding groups. Total suite ≥ 955/955 + ≥ 6 new tests (961+). PASS = new tests pass + full suite green; FAIL = any regression.<br>**AC14 — Out of scope (explicit).** (a) Drag-to-nest (B-031 territory). (b) Collapse/expand cascading (B-040 preference territory). (c) Keyboard reorder of groups (deferred by B-008 AC12). (d) Storage schema changes — already shipped in B-001a. (e) Depth > 1 — permanently disallowed by storage gate. PASS = none of the above touched; FAIL = any out-of-scope shift.<br>**AC15 — Destructive-action confirmation (new Sprint 20 DoR item 7).** Re-parenting via the edit dialog does NOT require a separate confirmation dialog — it's a reversible group-move, not a destructive action. Delete-parent cascade remains governed by the existing B-027 delete-group confirmation (unchanged). PASS = no new confirmation dialogs; FAIL = unnecessary confirmation added. |
| B-008 | As a user, I want to reorder groups and collapse or expand individual groups, with my collapse state persisted across browser restarts, so that I can keep the panel uncluttered and return to my preferred layout. | P1 | M | done | 8 | B-001, B-006 | **AC1 — Drag Handle Presence:** Every real group header (all except `__ungrouped__`) renders a distinct `.group-drag-handle` element; the `__ungrouped__` header has no drag handle in the DOM. PASS: all real group headers contain `.group-drag-handle`; `__ungrouped__` does not. FAIL: any real group missing handle, or `__ungrouped__` renders one.<br>**AC2 — Drag Initiation & Visual Feedback:** Pressing and holding the drag handle initiates drag with visual feedback (drag ghost + insertion indicator between valid drop positions); dragging from the header area outside the handle does not initiate drag. PASS: handle drag shows ghost and insertion indicator; header-area drag does not. FAIL: no visual feedback, or drag fires from entire header.<br>**AC3 — Drop Completes — Immediate DOM Reorder:** Releasing drag over a valid drop target reorders group sections in the DOM immediately, without a storage round-trip delay. `__ungrouped__` always stays at the bottom. PASS: instant DOM reorder on drop; `__ungrouped__` remains last. FAIL: groups revert on reload, or MSG_UPDATE_GROUP not called.<br>**AC5 — Collapse Toggle via Header (Not Drag Handle):** Clicking/Enter on the header area (excluding drag handle) toggles collapse. Clicking the drag handle does NOT toggle collapse. PASS: header click collapses/expands; drag handle click does not. FAIL: drag handle click triggers collapse, or header click during drag triggers toggle.<br>**AC6 — Collapse State Persisted to Storage and Survives Restart:** Toggling collapse sends `MSG_UPDATE_GROUP { id, patch: { collapsed: bool } }` written to `chrome.storage.local`. After a full browser restart, collapsed groups are restored from the `collapsed` field on Group records from `MSG_LIST_GROUPS` — not from `sessionStorage`. PASS: collapsed groups after full restart still collapsed. FAIL: state lost after restart or sourced only from sessionStorage.<br>**AC7 — Collapsed Group Shows Item Count Badge:** The `.group-header-count` badge always shows the direct item count of a collapsed group — not zeroed, not aggregating child groups. PASS: collapsing a group with N items shows N in badge. FAIL: count disappears, shows 0, or aggregates child items.<br>**AC8 — Expanding Collapsed Group Shows Items Without Re-fetch:** Expanding a collapsed group makes `.group-items` visible immediately by removing `hidden` — no `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` call, no panel re-render. FAIL: expansion triggers MSG_LIST_ITEMS or items take a perceptible moment to appear.<br>**AC9 — Drag Cancel Restores Original Order:** Pressing Escape mid-drag, or releasing outside a valid drop target, reverts DOM to original group order with no `MSG_UPDATE_GROUP` dispatched. PASS: after cancel, order unchanged in DOM and storage. FAIL: cancel partially commits a reorder.<br>**AC10 — `__ungrouped__` Cannot Be Dragged:** `__ungrouped__` has no drag handle, no `draggable` attribute, and does not respond to drag events. PASS: no drag ghost and no `dragstart` fires on `__ungrouped__` header. FAIL: `__ungrouped__` can be repositioned.<br>**AC11 — Single Real Group — Handle Visible, Drag Is No-Op:** With one real group, its drag handle is rendered but drag produces no DOM change, no `MSG_UPDATE_GROUP`, and no JS error. PASS: one-group drag completes silently. FAIL: exception thrown or MSG_UPDATE_GROUP called.<br>**AC12 — Keyboard Accessibility Disclosure:** Since native drag-and-drop is not keyboard-operable, the drag handle carries `aria-label="Reorder group"` and `title` text disclosing the limitation. The deferral of a keyboard reorder alternative is logged in SPRINT.md as a known accessibility gap. PASS: drag handle has accessible label; gap is documented. FAIL: handle is an unlabeled interactive element with no keyboard operability and no disclosure. |
| B-009 | As a user, I want a collapsed group to automatically expand when I hover over it while dragging an item, so that I can drop an item into a collapsed group without first manually expanding it. | P2 | S | done | 23 | B-008 ✅, B-030 (in S22) | **Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — expansion is a visual/state change, not destructive; the underlying `collapsed` field is already reversible via normal click-to-collapse.<br>**AC1 — Hover-hold timing.** While a drag is in progress (`dragstart` has fired, `drop` has not), hovering the pointer over a **collapsed** group header for `DRAG_EXPAND_HOLD_MS = 600` ms triggers that group to expand (its `.group-items` container becomes visible). PASS = dwelling on a collapsed header for ≥ 600 ms expands it during drag; FAIL = earlier or later trigger, or no expansion.<br>**AC2 — No expansion on fast cursor pass.** Cursor passes over the collapsed header for < 600 ms (e.g., crossing to a target below) → no expansion. PASS = cursor crossing without dwell does not expand; FAIL = expansion fires too eagerly.<br>**AC3 — Expansion only while dragging.** Hovering a collapsed group header for 600 ms WITHOUT an active drag does nothing (pre-existing click-to-toggle behaviour is untouched). PASS = idle hover is inert; FAIL = hover-expand fires when not dragging.<br>**AC4 — Expansion persists past the drag.** After the drag ends (drop succeeds, or Escape cancels), the group stays expanded. The expansion is written to the group's `collapsed` state via `MSG_UPDATE_GROUP { id, patch: { collapsed: false } }` (matching B-008 AC6 persistence). PASS = post-drag the group is expanded; reload of sidepanel confirms the new `collapsed: false` state; FAIL = reverts after drag or not persisted.<br>**AC5 — Cancellation on dragleave.** If the pointer leaves the collapsed header BEFORE the 600 ms hold completes, the timer is cancelled. PASS = dragleave before 600 ms resets the timer and the group stays collapsed; FAIL = expansion fires despite leaving.<br>**AC6 — Already-expanded group no-op.** Hover-hold on an already-expanded group header is a no-op (no unnecessary `MSG_UPDATE_GROUP` dispatch). PASS = spy on the message bus confirms zero MSG_UPDATE_GROUP during hover-hold on expanded groups; FAIL = redundant message.<br>**AC7 — Empty-state enumeration (C-9).** (a) Collapsed sub-group — same hover-hold behaviour applies. (b) Collapsed `__ungrouped__` — Ungrouped never collapses, so N/A. (c) Drag from within a collapsed group (impossible — you can't drag an invisible item), so N/A. (d) Drag to a group that expands then collapses back by user click mid-drag — user click wins; timer re-starts on next hover.<br>**AC8 — Out of scope.** (a) No new storage schema. (b) No new message types (reuses existing `MSG_UPDATE_GROUP`). (c) No keyboard alternative (native DnD is mouse-only; inherits B-008 AC12's deferral disclosure).<br>**Smoke UAT plan**: `docs/UAT_B-009.md` — 6 cases. |
| B-010 | As a user, I want every saved bookmark to reflect in real time whether its URL is open in a browser tab and, if so, whether it is the currently active (focused) tab, so that I can see at a glance which items are live without switching windows. | P0 | L | done | 8 | B-001, B-002 ⚠️ **Requires `tabs` and `windows` manifest permissions; new message contract for tab events** → Full tier | **AC1 — Live indicator on tab open:** When a tab opens at a URL matching a saved item (after normalization), the item row gains `data-live="true"` and a green left border within 500 ms. PASS: within 500 ms of tab loading, row has `data-live="true"` and green border. FAIL: indicator absent, delayed beyond 500 ms, or wrong row.<br>**AC2 — Live indicator clears on tab close:** When a live tab closes, the item row loses `data-live="true"` and the border within 500 ms. PASS: within 500 ms of tab removal, `data-live` absent and no green border. FAIL: indicator persists.<br>**AC3 — Active highlight on tab focus:** When a live tab is the currently focused tab in its window, the item row gains `data-active="true"` with blue background (`--active-bg`) and left border (`--active-border`). PASS: row has `data-active="true"` and blue styling while tab is focused. FAIL: no active styling, or wrong item highlighted.<br>**AC4 — Active highlight clears on focus switch:** When focus moves to a different tab, the previously active item row loses `data-active="true"` within 500 ms; the newly focused tab's item (if live) gains it within 500 ms. PASS: exactly one row has `data-active="true"` at any time when one claimed tab is focused. FAIL: two rows active simultaneously, or old row retains highlight.<br>**AC5 — Multi-window: live state spans windows:** With two browser windows open and saved items matching tabs in each, all matching items show the live indicator regardless of which window they belong to. PASS: all matching items across both windows show `data-live="true"`. FAIL: only items in the sidepanel's window are reflected.<br>**AC6 — Multi-window: active exclusive across windows:** Exactly one tab is the active foreground tab in the entire browser (focused window's active tab). Only that tab's claimed item shows `data-active="true"`; items claimed by active tabs in non-focused windows show only the live indicator. PASS: switching window focus transfers active highlight within 500 ms. FAIL: both windows' active-tab items show `data-active="true"` simultaneously.<br>**AC7 — 500 ms latency budget:** Every transition (tab opened, closed, focused, navigated) results in the sidepanel updating the affected row's live/active attributes within 500 ms of the browser firing the tab event. PASS: measured from tab event to DOM update never exceeds 500 ms. FAIL: any transition consistently lands outside 500 ms.<br>**AC8 — No full re-render on liveState broadcast:** On `MSG_STATE_CHANGED { scope: 'liveState' }`, only `data-live`, `data-active`, and `data-audible` attributes on existing `[data-item-id]` rows are patched — no rebuild of `#item-list`. PASS: group structure and scroll position unchanged after live-state update. FAIL: `#item-list` rebuilt or scroll position resets.<br>**AC9 — Items with no matching tab show no indicators:** Saved items with no matching open tab have neither `data-live` nor `data-active` and render with default styling. PASS: no phantom indicators on unmatched items. FAIL: phantom live or active indicators appear.<br>**AC10 — Duplicate-URL disambiguation: first-unclaimed-wins:** With two saved items sharing a URL and one tab open there, only the item with lower `sortOrder` receives the live claim and live indicator. PASS: item A (lower sortOrder) shows `data-live="true"`, item B does not. FAIL: both show live, or the wrong item is claimed.<br>**AC11 — Live claim transfers on tab navigation:** When a claimed tab navigates to a URL matching item B, item A loses its live indicator and item B gains it within 500 ms. PASS: after navigation, item A has no `data-live`, item B has `data-live="true"`. FAIL: A retains the indicator, B never receives it.<br>**AC12 — Correct state on sidepanel open:** Opening or re-opening the sidepanel shows correct live and active indicators on first paint from the initial `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` response, without requiring a subsequent broadcast. PASS: opening cold with saved-item tabs open immediately renders correct indicators. FAIL: indicators only appear after the first broadcast. |
| B-011 | As a user, I want bookmarks whose associated tab has navigated away from the saved URL to be visually flagged as "drifted" — and for that drift state to survive browser restarts — so that I know when a tab no longer corresponds to the URL I saved. | P0 | L | done | 9 | B-001, B-010 ⚠️ **Drift state persisted to storage schema; new message type for drift events** → Full tier | A bookmark is marked drifted when its live tab's current URL differs from the saved URL (after normalization).<br>Drifted items render with a visually distinct style (e.g., warning color or icon) separate from the live and active states.<br>Drift state is written to storage and survives extension reload and browser restart.<br>Navigating the drifted tab back to the original URL clears the drift state in real time.<br>Drift is not triggered by fragment-only changes when the base URL is identical. |
| B-012 | As a user, I want items whose live tab is playing audio to show an audible indicator, so that I can identify the source of audio without hunting through browser tabs. | P1 | XS | done | 9 | B-010 | Items whose tab has `audible: true` display an audio icon indicator.<br>The indicator appears and disappears in real time as audio starts and stops.<br>The indicator is visually distinct from the live and active indicators.<br>Indicator is accessible (not conveyed by color alone). |
| B-013 | As a user, I want new tabs opened from a grouped bookmark to automatically inherit that bookmark's group (via opener-chain lookup), so that related tabs are organised without manual intervention. | P1 | M | done | 10 | B-001, B-010 ⚠️ **New message contract for opener-chain association** → Full tier | Opening a tab from a grouped item via the Tab Junkie UI causes the new tab to appear in the same group.<br>Opener-chain walking is bounded (max 3 hops) to prevent runaway lookups.<br>If no ancestor within the hop limit is grouped, the new tab appears in Open Tabs (ungrouped).<br>Externally opened tabs whose opener is tracked also inherit the group. |
| B-014 | As a user, I want items in other browser windows to show a human-readable window-number badge and, when more than one window is open, to be able to filter the view to a single window, so that multi-window workflows are navigable without confusion. | P1 | M | done | 14 | B-001, B-010, B-055 ⚠️ **Absorbs B-034 (window filter row). Ordinal mapping is in-memory only — no storage schema change. New MSG_WINDOW_MAP message contract required.** → Full tier | **AC1 (session-ordinal mapping):** The service worker maintains an in-memory `Map<rawWindowId, ordinal>` (`windowOrdinalMap`) that assigns stable, human-readable ordinals (1, 2, 3, …) to `chrome.windows` integer IDs. Ordinals are assigned in first-seen order: a window's ordinal is `max(existing ordinals) + 1` at the time it first appears in `chrome.windows.onCreated` or during cold-start enumeration via `chrome.windows.getAll`. This map is never written to storage.<br>**AC2 (cold-start enumeration):** On service-worker cold start, `chrome.windows.getAll({ populate: false })` is called and all currently-open windows are assigned ordinals in ascending `id` order (lowest raw id = W1). This ordering is deterministic on a single machine across short session lifetimes.<br>**AC3 (ordinal stability on window close):** When a window closes (`chrome.windows.onRemoved`), its ordinal is released from `windowOrdinalMap`. Existing ordinals for open windows are NOT renumbered. Gaps are preserved (e.g., if W2 closes, remaining windows remain W1 and W3 — W3 is never renamed to W2).<br>**AC4 (window badge — cross-window items):** A window badge (e.g., "W2") is rendered on saved-item rows AND open-tab rows ONLY when the item's live tab is in a different window than the sidepanel's own window (`panelWindowId`). Items in the same window as the sidepanel show no badge. When all live tabs are in the same window, zero badges render.<br>**AC5 (panel window identity):** The sidepanel knows its own `panelWindowId` via `chrome.windows.getCurrent()` called at panel open time and stored locally. The panel re-fetches this value on every `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` response. `panelWindowId` is used exclusively for badge suppression (AC4) and is never persisted.<br>**AC6 (badge rendering — Open Tabs section):** The existing `_createWindowBadge` function in `sidepanel.js` currently renders the raw integer windowId (e.g., "W7384"). It must be updated to render the ordinal (e.g., "W2") using the window ordinal map received from the service worker. The `aria-label` must also use the ordinal ("Window 2", not the raw id).<br>**AC7 (badge rendering — saved-item rows):** Saved-item rows that have a live claim in a non-current window must also render a window badge using the same ordinal. Rows with no live claim, or with a claim in the current window, render no badge.<br>**AC8 (window filter row — visibility):** A window filter row is rendered in the sidepanel header if and only if 2 or more browser windows are currently open (as reported by the window ordinal map from the service worker). It is hidden when only 1 window is open.<br>**AC9 (window filter row — contents):** The filter row contains an "All" chip (selected by default) and one chip per open window ("W1", "W2", etc.), in ordinal order. Chips use `role="tab"` and `aria-selected` to indicate the active selection. The row itself uses `role="tablist"` with `aria-label="Filter by window"`.<br>**AC10 (window filter row — keyboard navigation):** Arrow keys move focus between chips within the filter row. Enter or Space activates the focused chip. Tab exits the filter row. This follows the ARIA `tablist` keyboard interaction pattern.<br>**AC11 (window filter — behavior):** Selecting a window chip hides all item rows whose live tab is NOT in the selected window. Saved items with no live tab are hidden when any specific window is selected (they are window-agnostic but not visible in the filtered view). Selecting "All" restores the full view.<br>**AC12 (filter lifecycle — auto-reset on window close):** If the currently selected filter chip's window closes (`chrome.windows.onRemoved`), the filter automatically resets to "All" before the filter row updates. The filter row itself disappears if only one window remains after the close.<br>**AC13 (real-time badge updates — tab moved between windows):** When a tab is dragged to a different window (`chrome.tabs.onDetached` / `chrome.tabs.onAttached`) or a tab's `windowId` changes via `chrome.tabs.onUpdated`, the service worker broadcasts `SCOPE.LIVE_STATE` and `SCOPE.WINDOW_MAP`. The sidepanel updates only the badge on the affected row(s) without a full re-render.<br>**AC14 (window ordinal map broadcast):** The service worker includes the serialized ordinal map (as a plain `{ [ordinal]: rawWindowId }` object, plus the `panelWindowId` the panel already knows) in the `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` response and re-broadcasts `SCOPE.WINDOW_MAP` on every `chrome.windows.onCreated` and `chrome.windows.onRemoved`. The sidepanel caches the latest map locally and uses it for all badge and filter rendering.<br>**AC15 (no storage writes):** The window ordinal map is purely in-memory and session-scoped. No window metadata is ever written to `storage.local` or `storage.session`.<br>**AC16 (cross-panel consistency):** If a standalone-window surface (B-035) is open alongside the sidepanel, both surfaces receive the same `SCOPE.WINDOW_MAP` broadcast and render consistent ordinals. No special handling required beyond broadcasting.<br>**AC17 (performance — no full re-render on window events):** `chrome.windows.onCreated` and `chrome.windows.onRemoved` must not trigger a full sidepanel re-render. The sidepanel must update only (a) the filter row and (b) badges on affected rows. The `SCOPE.WINDOW_MAP` broadcast scope is introduced for this purpose so the sidepanel can distinguish a window-map-only update from a full state change.<br>**AC18 (out of scope):** Cross-device synchronization of window ordinals; named/labeled windows; window-pane reordering of ordinals; windows with no Tab Junkie presence receiving ordinals; multi-profile handling. |

| B-016 | As a user, I want to save (promote) any currently open tab to a persistent bookmark with a chosen group, so that I can preserve important tabs without opening a separate dialog workflow. | P0 | S | done | 4 | B-001, B-003, B-006 | **[R1 — data layer only; UI deferred to sidepanel items]**<br>**AC1 (message contract):** A `MSG_PROMOTE_TAB` (`tj/promoteTab`) message handler exists in the service worker. Payload: `{ tabId: number, groupId?: string \| null }`. Response: `{ ok: true, data: Item }` on success or `{ ok: false, error: { code, message } }` on failure.<br>**AC2 (happy path):** Given a valid tabId for a live tab, the handler reads the tab's current title and URL via `chrome.tabs.get`, calls `createItem({ title, url, groupId })`, then calls `claimTabForItem(newItem.id, tabId)`. The returned item is fully persisted and survives a browser restart.<br>**AC3 (tab-to-item claim):** After a successful promote the item's live state is immediately `{ live: true }` — no reconcile cycle required.<br>**AC4 (URL normalization):** B-002 normalization is applied at `createItem` time; the handler passes the raw tab URL and does not pre-normalize.<br>**AC5 (duplicate URL guard):** If an item with the same normalized URL already exists, the handler returns `{ ok: false, error: { code: 'ERR_DUPLICATE_URL', message: '...' } }` without creating a new item or modifying claims.<br>**AC6 (non-promotable scheme):** If the tab URL has a non-http(s) scheme (e.g., `chrome://`, `about:`, `file://`), the handler returns `{ ok: false, error: { code: 'ERR_VALIDATION', message: '...' } }` without calling `createItem`.<br>**AC7 (tab gone):** If `chrome.tabs.get(tabId)` rejects (tab closed between request and handler execution), the handler returns `{ ok: false, error: { code: 'ERR_NOT_FOUND', message: '...' } }`.<br>**AC8 (invalid groupId):** If a non-null `groupId` is provided and no matching group exists, `createItem` throws `ERR_NOT_FOUND`; the handler surfaces this error without partial state (no item created, no claim written).<br>**AC9 (idempotent on failure):** Any failure path leaves storage and claims in the state they were before the message was received — no orphaned items, no stale claims. |
| B-017 | As a user, I want to remove the saved aspect of a bookmark while keeping its tab open, so that I can declutter my saved list without accidentally closing live tabs. | P0 | S | done | 4 | B-001, B-003 | **[R1 — data layer only; UI deferred to sidepanel items]**<br>**AC1 (message contract):** A `MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM` (`tj/demoteItem`) message handler exists in the service worker. Payload: `{ itemId: string }`. Response: `{ ok: true, data: null }` on success or `{ ok: false, error: { code, message } }` on failure.<br>**AC2 (happy path — live tab):** Given an `itemId` for an item whose tab is currently claimed, the handler calls `deleteItem(itemId)`, then `clearDrift(itemId)`, then `releaseClaimByTab(tabId)`. If the item had a non-null `groupId`, `saveFloatingGroups` is called with one entry `{ groupId, windowId, tabIndex, url, savedAt }` sourced from the LiveTabIndex before the claim is released. The live tab is not closed. The saved item no longer appears in storage after the operation.<br>**AC3 (happy path — no live tab):** If the item exists but has no current claim (no open tab), the handler calls `deleteItem(itemId)` and `clearDrift(itemId)` only. No floating-group entry is written. Confirmation UI is deferred; the data layer performs the delete unconditionally.<br>**AC4 (group preservation):** When a floating-group entry is written, `groupId` in the entry is the item's direct `groupId` (which may be a sub-group id). The entry is written atomically via `saveFloatingGroups`. On next cold start, `reassociateFloatingGroups` re-links the tab to the same groupId it had when saved.<br>**AC5 (drift cleanup):** `clearDrift(itemId)` is always called, even if no drift record exists (idempotent no-op per drift.js). This prevents stale drift entries for deleted items.<br>**AC6 (non-existent item):** If `deleteItem` is called for an unknown `itemId`, it is a silent no-op (idempotent per B-001a ruling #3). The handler returns `{ ok: true, data: null }` — no error surfaced.<br>**AC7 (claim release ordering):** `releaseClaimByTab` must be called AFTER `deleteItem` completes so that any in-flight `buildLiveStates` call during the transaction still sees the claim. On completion, the item is absent from storage and no claim entry references it.<br>**AC8 (roundtrip clean — demote then re-promote):** After a successful demote, a subsequent `MSG_PROMOTE_TAB` for the same tabId creates a fresh item with a new `id`. No orphaned claims, drift, or floating-group records from the previous item survive the roundtrip.<br>**AC9 (idempotent on failure):** Any failure (e.g., `StorageError` from `writeTransaction`) leaves storage, claims, and floating-group records in the state they were before the message was received. |

| B-019 | As a user, I want clicking any item in the panel to switch focus to its open tab (or open a new tab if none exists), preferring the exact tab association over URL-only matching, so that I navigate to the right tab every time. | P0 | S | done | 5 | B-001, B-010 | **[R1 — data layer only; UI binding deferred]**<br>**AC1 (message contract):** A `MSG_NAVIGATE_TO_ITEM` (`tj/navigateToItem`) handler exists in the service worker. Payload: `{ itemId: string }`. Response: `{ ok: true, data: { tabId: number, opened: boolean } }` on success or `{ ok: false, error: { code, message } }` on failure. `opened: true` means a new tab was created; `false` means an existing tab was activated.<br>**AC2 (live tab — happy path):** If `claimsMirror[itemId]` resolves to a `tabId` present in `getLiveTabIndex()`, the handler calls `chrome.tabs.update(tabId, { active: true })` followed by `chrome.windows.update(entry.windowId, { focused: true })`. Returns `opened: false`.<br>**AC3 (stale claim — tab already closed):** If `claimsMirror[itemId]` holds a `tabId` absent from `getLiveTabIndex()`, the handler calls `releaseClaimByTab(tabId)` to clean up the stale claim, then falls through to the open-new-tab path (AC5).<br>**AC4 (no URL guard):** If the item has no URL or the URL is empty/falsy, return `{ ok: false, error: { code: 'ERR_VALIDATION', message: 'Item has no URL' } }` — no tab operation is attempted.<br>**AC5 (open new tab):** If the item has no current claim (or stale claim released per AC3), the handler calls `chrome.tabs.create({ url: item.url })`, then `claimTabForItem(itemId, newTab.id)`. | B-020 | As a user, I want to close any open tab directly from the Tab Junkie UI — individually or in bulk — so that I can manage my tab count without switching to the browser's native tab bar. | P0 | S | done | 5 | B-010, B-019 | **[R1 — data layer only; UI binding deferred to B-026/B-027/B-028]**<br>**AC1 (message contract):** A `MSG_CLOSE_TABS` (`tj/closeTabs`) message handler exists in the service worker. Call `chrome.tabs.remove` only for `present`. Return `{ closed: present, notFound: absent }`. The handler treats this identically to any other close — no special-casing required.<br>**AC9 (closing last tab in a group):** Does not delete the group or any saved items. Live state for those items transitions to `{ live: false }` via the normal `onRemoved` → claim-release pipeline.<br>**AC10 (idempotent on Chrome error):** If `chrome.tabs.remove` rejects (e.g., tab vanished between the index check and the call), the rejection is caught; affected tabIds are moved to `notFound` and the response is still `{ ok: true, data: { … } }`. Claims for those tabIds are not touched — `onRemoved` will not fire so no stale claim is introduced. |
