PASS = all cases + zero regressions; FAIL = any gap or regression.<br>**Out of scope (explicit):** (a) Group-jump popup (B-023 separate); (b) Standalone window (B-035 separate); (c) Custom shortcut UI inside popup (via browser chrome); (d) Search history (≠ recency); (e) Type-ahead autocomplete; (f) Ctrl+Arrow group-jump (B-023 reserve only); (g) Drag-drop within popup; (h) Bulk selection within popup.<br>**R2 decision points (mandatory):** D-1 popup lifecycle (`default_popup` vs `chrome.action.openPopup()` — Edge compat check); D-2 shortcut path (`_execute_action` → Alt+J already registered — confirm fires popup not side-panel); D-3 recency storage — (a) new `tj:recency` partition (persistent, recommended cap 50), (b) `chrome.storage.session`, (c) in-memory; "access" defined as any Enter-triggered navigation from popup; D-4 keyboard model — focus trap + arrow-key interception + don't consume Left/Right (cursor nav); D-5 result ranking — B-052 index order + exact-title > prefix > substring > recency boost tie-break; D-6 dedupe — two rows (one per section) for saved+live same URL.<br>**Smoke UAT plan**: `docs/UAT_B-022.md` — 12 cases covering shortcut open scenarios, keyboard nav end-to-end, Enter three-path activation, Escape zero-mutation, empty-query recency, XSS probe, perf probe, accessibility, dedupe. |
| B-023 | As a user, I want a group-jump popup (accessible from a global shortcut or toggled within the quick search popup) that lets me fuzzy-search group names, drill into a group, and filter its contents, so that I can navigate to a specific group section quickly. | P1 | L | done | 27 | B-006 ✅, B-022 ✅ ⚠️ **Full tier — new popup extension surface or shared popup context with B-022 — R2 decision. Manifest `group-jump` command already at Alt+K (v1.18.0+). MUST apply C-11 popup-lifecycle message ordering from day one.** | **Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — popup performs navigation only (drills into group views, renders group contents, optionally focuses tabs). No groups, items, or preferences are created, modified, or deleted within this popup surface. Mutations remain owned by sidepanel + item CRUD paths.<br>**AC1 — Shortcut open.** Manifest `commands.group-jump` registers `Alt+K` (already present v1.18.0+). Pressing Alt+K opens the group-jump popup surface (R2 decides the surface per D-1). Remappable via `edge://extensions/shortcuts`. PASS = popup opens on Alt+K from an http/https tab; FAIL = no popup or wrong path.<br>**AC2 — Mode-toggle from B-022 quick-search popup.** A designated gesture (R2 decides per D-6 — candidate `Tab` or `Ctrl+G`) inside the B-022 popup switches to group-search mode. Focus stays on query input after toggle; no blank frame. PASS = toggle lands in group-search with input focused; FAIL = focus lost, popup closes, or no toggle.<br>**AC3 — Popup first paint < 200 ms.** From Alt+K keypress to popup visible with input focused < 200 ms P95 across 5 opens on ≥ 50-group collection. Body-width anchor pattern (`html, body { width: Xpx; min-width: Xpx }`) mandatory per B-022 D-UAT-1 — R2 enumerates width. PASS = P95 < 200 ms; FAIL = any individual ≥ 200 ms.<br>**AC4 — Initial focus on query input.** Programmatic `.focus()` at `DOMContentLoaded`. Placeholder: "Search groups…". PASS = cursor active immediately; FAIL = focus elsewhere.<br>**AC5 — Empty-query default: full group list.** Empty query shows the full flat list of all groups (top-level + sub-groups). Sub-groups show parent breadcrumb prefix (e.g., "Work › Projects"). Single list, no section headers. PASS = all groups appear; FAIL = empty state or recency mode shown.<br>**AC6 — Fuzzy group-name filter.** Case-insensitive substring match against group name. Sub-groups match their full display label. Query lowercased once per keypress. PASS = `work` narrows to groups with "work" in name; FAIL = matching group absent or non-matching present.<br>**AC7 — Group row content.** Each row: (a) group name 1-line truncated, (b) parent breadcrumb prefix for sub-groups, (c) `(N bookmarks · M open)` count badge. All text via `textContent`, never `innerHTML`. PASS = row visually correct; FAIL = field missing or innerHTML with group string (XSS).<br>**AC8 — Match highlighting via `shared/highlight.js`.** Matches wrapped in `<mark>` via `document.createElement('mark')` + text-node splitting. Reuses promoted helper — NOT a parallel implementation. PASS = DevTools shows `<mark>` nodes; `<script>` in group name stays literal; FAIL = innerHTML used or XSS probe UAT-13 executes.<br>**AC9 — Arrow-key navigation.** ArrowDown/Up moves selection + wraps. Initial ArrowDown from input selects first row (doesn't move caret). `aria-activedescendant` updated. PASS = 10 ArrowDowns cycle + wrap; FAIL = stalls/skips/no-wrap.<br>**AC10 — Enter drills into a group (or navigates to sidepanel per D-3).** Enter on a selected group row transitions to drill-in view OR sends `MSG_NAVIGATE_TO_GROUP` to sidepanel (R2 resolves D-3). PASS = drill-in renders correct contents OR sidepanel scrolls to group; FAIL = nothing happens or wrong group.<br>**AC11 — Back action + breadcrumb.** Drill-in view shows a Back button + breadcrumb ("All Groups › Work"). Back (or ArrowLeft at top of list per D-4) returns to group list. "All Groups" segment clickable as back shortcut. PASS = Back returns to group list; breadcrumb correct; FAIL = popup closes or wrong level.<br>**AC12 — Escape behaviour.** Escape closes popup at any navigation level (group list OR drill-in). Does NOT navigate back one level — always dismisses. R2 may override via D-4. Zero storage writes, zero side-effects. PASS = popup closes on Escape; FAIL = navigates to parent instead.<br>**AC13 — Drill-in filter.** Within drill-in, query input filters the group's bookmarks + sub-groups (not full collection). PASS = typing narrows visible rows; FAIL = filter applies globally or inoperative.<br>**AC14 — C-11 popup-lifecycle message ordering.** Any SW-side write (jump-count, group-recency, navigate-message) MUST be dispatched via fire-and-forget `chrome.runtime.sendMessage` BEFORE any focus-shifting API call. Post-shift `await sendMessage` is forbidden (B-022 D-UAT-3). If no SW writes triggered, AC is vacuously satisfied + marked SKIP in UAT. PASS = any write persists across popup close; FAIL = write dropped due to popup teardown.<br>**AC15 — Filter latency < 50 ms P95.** 50-group collection; `performance.now()` across 20 keystrokes P95 < 50 ms. Reuses existing data from single round-trip (no second message). PASS = P95 < 50 ms; FAIL = any P95 ≥ 50 ms.<br>**AC16 — CSS enumeration (R2 mandatory).** All positioned popup elements must have every CSS property enumerated: popup container, group row, selection highlight, breadcrumb strip, drill-in header, empty-state container, `<mark>`, favicon holder. Body-width anchor is mandatory. PASS = R2 output enumerates all categories; FAIL = missing category.<br>**AC17 — Accessibility ARIA model.** Popup: `role="dialog"` + `aria-modal="true"` + `aria-label="Jump to Group"`. Input: `role="combobox"` + `aria-expanded` + `aria-controls`. List: `role="listbox"`. Row: `role="option"` + `aria-selected`. Count via `aria-live="polite"`. Focus trap: Tab cycles input↔rows only. PASS = UAT-12 + axe-core clean; FAIL = any role/attr missing or focus escapes.<br>**AC18 — Visible focus indicator WCAG AA.** Selected row focus ring ≥ 3:1 contrast in light + dark themes. PASS = contrast checker ≥ 3:1; FAIL = < 3:1 or absent.<br>**AC19 — C-9 Empty-state enumeration.** (a) Zero groups → "No groups yet" + icon + CTA; (b) Zero matches → "No groups matching 'xyz'"; (c) Drill into empty group → "No items in this group"; Back still works. (d) Drill into sub-groups-only group → sub-group rows visible, no bookmark rows, no crash. (e) Back at top level → no-op or Back hidden. (f) SW cold-start mid-popup → no freeze; recovers ≤ 2s on next action. (g) Concurrent group delete mid-drill → no crash; stale view until next interaction. (h) Whitespace-only query → trimmed to empty → full group list.<br>**AC20 — Result cap.** If > 100 groups match, cap at 100 (R2 may adjust). Cap before render. PASS = 200-group collection + all-match query → exactly cap rows; FAIL = > cap or < cap when ≥ cap match.<br>**AC21 — Test coverage.** New `tests/b023-group-jump-popup.test.js`: (a) fuzzy filter over 50-group fixture + sub-group breadcrumb, (b) `buildHighlightedText` reuse + XSS, (c) arrow nav + wrap, (d) Enter → drill-in, (e) Back, (f) Escape zero writes, (g) empty-state 8 sub-states, (h) C-11 write-before-navigate, (i) cap 100, (j) whitespace query. Full suite (≥ 1119 + new) green. PASS = all + zero regressions; FAIL = any.<br>**Out of scope (explicit):** (a) Group CRUD in popup (B-006 owns); (b) Item CRUD (sidepanel); (c) Standalone window (B-035); (d) Custom shortcut UI; (e) Drag-drop; (f) Bulk selection; (g) Multi-level depth > 1.<br>**R2 decision points (mandatory):** D-1 popup architecture (shared toggled vs separate surface); D-2 shortcut dispatch path + Edge compat; D-3 drill-in model (in-popup vs send-to-sidepanel); D-4 breadcrumb + Escape semantics; D-5 open-tab count resolution (liveStates vs MSG_LIST_GROUPS enriched); D-6 mode-toggle key binding (if D-1 = shared); D-7 C-11 lifecycle audit — any write, any flow.<br>**Smoke UAT plan**: `docs/UAT_B-023.md` — 15 cases. |
| B-024 | As a user, I want to select multiple items (single click, Shift+Click range, Ctrl/Cmd+Click toggle, Ctrl/Cmd+A all, Escape to clear) and act on them via a bulk action bar, so that I can move, close, or remove many items at once. | P1 | M | done | 12 | B-001, B-010, B-019 | All four selection gestures produce the expected selection sets.<br>Ctrl/Cmd+A selects all currently visible (non-filtered-out) items.<br>Escape clears the selection without navigating or closing any item.<br>Bulk action bar appears with item count and actions: move to group, close tabs, clear selection, and context-appropriate destructive actions.<br>Selection IDs that no longer exist are silently pruned when the selection is used. |
| B-025 | As a user, I want to drag multiple selected items together as a single unit when repositioning them, so that bulk moves are as smooth as single-item moves. | P1 | M | done | 24 | B-024 ✅, B-030 ✅ ⚠️ **Full tier — extends `MSG_BULK_REORDER_ITEMS` or introduces `MSG_BULK_REORDER_MULTI` (R2 decision)** | **Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — multi-item reorder and cross-group move are non-destructive. Items retain all data; only `sortOrder` / `groupId` changes. Drag cancel (Escape or outside-release) restores pre-drag order with zero storage writes. No user data is at risk.<br>**AC1 — Multi-selection carried as one unit.** When a drag is initiated from any item that is part of the current B-024 multi-selection, ALL selected items are carried together as a single logical unit. The drag initiator plus every other selected item constitute the drag payload. PASS = releasing over a valid drop target moves all selected items; FAIL = only the initiator moves, or any selected item is silently dropped from the payload.<br>**AC2 — Drag initiator NOT in selection → solo-drag fallback.** If the drag is initiated from an item that is NOT part of the current B-024 selection, the selection is cleared on `dragstart` and only the initiating item is dragged (B-030 single-item path). PASS = dragging an unselected item clears selection, moves only that item, ghost shows item title with no count badge; FAIL = previously selected items are also moved or selection is not cleared.<br>**AC3 — Drag ghost count badge.** During a multi-item drag (N ≥ 2 items in payload), the drag ghost or a custom count overlay displays "N items" (e.g., "3 items"). For N = 1 (solo drag or AC2 fallback), no count badge is shown — ghost shows item title only (B-030 AC2). PASS = count badge visible with correct N for multi-drag; badge absent for solo drag; FAIL = badge missing, wrong count, or badge shown for N = 1.<br>**AC4 — Count ghost / indicator CSS enumeration (R2 mandatory).** If the count badge or multi-item indicator is a new positioned element, R2 MUST enumerate every CSS property in the design chapter: `position`, `top`, `left`, `transform`, `opacity`, `pointer-events`, `z-index` at minimum. A structural "position: absolute" note alone is a REJECT at R4 (S23 `top: 0` precedent). PASS = R2 output contains explicit values for all seven properties; FAIL = any property undocumented.<br>**AC5 — Drop inserts all items at drop position in stable relative order.** After a successful drop, all dragged items appear at the drop position in their original relative order (stable sort — the order they had among themselves before drag). Non-selected items in source and destination groups retain their relative order. PASS = stable-sort assertion green (e.g., selection A, C, E dropped at position X yields A→C→E at X); FAIL = any permutation of the dragged set, or non-selected items reordered.<br>**AC6 — Same-group reorder (multi-item).** Dropping the multi-item payload within the same source group reorders those items to the drop position (stable relative order), displacing non-selected items. PASS = post-drop same-group order is correct; reload confirms persistence; FAIL = same-group drop is silently ignored (B-052 hashItem render-path noop).<br>**AC7 — Cross-group multi-drop.** Dropping the payload onto a different group moves all dragged items into that group and places them at the drop position in stable relative order. Each moved item's `groupId` is updated to the destination group's id. PASS = items appear in destination group; source group loses those items; groupId updated for each; storage persists; FAIL = any item stays in source group or lands in wrong group.<br>**AC8 — Multi-drop onto `__ungrouped__`.** Dropping the payload onto the Ungrouped section sets each dragged item's `groupId` to `null`. PASS = all dragged items appear under Ungrouped; `groupId: null` in storage for each; FAIL = any item's groupId unchanged or set to the literal string `__ungrouped__`.<br>**AC9 — Multi-drop onto Open Tabs section — OUT OF SCOPE for B-025.** The Open Tabs section is not a valid drop target for multi-item drag. B-025's drop handler treats a drop on Open Tabs as a no-op (same as B-030 AC7). Multi-item drag-to-demote is deferred to a future item. PASS = drop on Open Tabs produces no reorder and no `MSG_BULK_REORDER_ITEMS` dispatch; FAIL = any write or partial demote occurs.<br>**AC10 — `sortOrder` normalisation post-drop.** After every committed multi-item drop, all affected items in all affected groups have their `sortOrder` values normalised to consecutive integers (0, 1, 2, ...). Normalisation runs inside the same write transaction as the drop commit. PASS = storage inspection post-drop shows consecutive integers in each affected group; no gaps, no duplicates, no floats; FAIL = gaps or duplicates remain.<br>**AC11 — Cancel via Escape or outside-release — zero storage writes.** Pressing Escape during multi-item drag OR releasing outside any valid drop target cancels the drag. All items return to pre-drag visual positions; NO `MSG_BULK_REORDER_ITEMS` dispatches; zero storage writes. PASS = Escape / outside-release restores pre-drag order; reload confirms no change; FAIL = any partial write or item displacement.<br>**AC12 — Render-path strategy (R2 mandatory decision — B-052).** B-030 `hashItem` does not include `sortOrder`, so same-group multi-item reorder may produce `deltaType: 'noop'` in `diffAndPatch`. R2 MUST document which strategy B-025 adopts: **(i)** add `sortOrder` to `hashItem` (structural fix, affects all items), OR **(ii)** dispatch explicit `renderAll` after the multi-drop commit (pattern B-030 post-fix used). R2 output names chosen path + blast radius + rollback implications. PASS = R2 documents the explicit choice; FAIL = R2 silent on render-path strategy.<br>**AC13 — C-9 empty-state enumeration.** (a) *Single-item selection drag* — delegates to B-030 single-item path (AC2 fallback). (b) *Zero-item selection drag* — impossible: drag originates from an item row, always at least self-selected. (c) *Mixed saved+live items in selection* — saved bookmarks move; Open Tabs live-only rows excluded from payload (no `groupId` to update). R2 defines whether a live-only initiator prevents the drag or whether saved items in selection are carried. (d) *Drop at exact current position (same-group, same rank)* — no-op, no storage write. (e) *Destination group empty (zero items)* — all dragged items land at sortOrder 0..N−1. (f) *Selection spans items in multiple source groups* — R2 MUST decide: (i) restrict multi-selection drag to single source group only (R1 recommendation for S24), OR (ii) allow cross-source payload. R2 documents the chosen constraint and how it is enforced.<br>**AC14 — Perf — 16 ms P95 dragover latency at N-item scale.** Extends B-030 AC10. Dragover rAF tick cost must not meaningfully scale with selection size for N ≤ 50. For N > 50 performance may degrade; acceptable and documented. PASS = continuous 10-second drag with 10 items selected shows no long tasks > 50 ms and sustains ~60 fps; FAIL = frame drop or compounding lag visible.<br>**AC15 — Broadcast-race guard (copy from B-030 AC24).** `_itemDragState` at dragstart captures `_cachedItemsGen`. At multi-drop time, if `_cachedItemsGen` has advanced, drop handler re-fetches fresh state via `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` before computing reorder spec. PASS = stale-generation multi-drop re-fetches; FAIL = drop proceeds with stale `_cachedItems`.<br>**AC16 — Message contract — R2 decision.** Either (i) extend `MSG_BULK_REORDER_ITEMS` with multi-item payload shape (array of itemIds + single destination spec), OR (ii) introduce `MSG_BULK_REORDER_MULTI` as a new message type. R2 documents the chosen contract shape with sender/receiver definition and updates `shared/messages.js`. PASS = R2 output includes typed message shape with field-level documentation; FAIL = message contract undocumented.<br>**AC17 — Test coverage.** New test file `tests/b025-multi-item-drag.test.js` covering: (a) multi-drag same-group stable-ordering assertion, (b) multi-drag cross-group (groupId updated for all items), (c) stable-ordering for non-contiguous selection (A, C, E → A→C→E at drop), (d) `sortOrder` normalisation for source and destination groups, (e) Escape cancel — zero storage writes, (f) broadcast-race guard (stale `_cachedItemsGen` triggers re-fetch), (g) AC2 solo-drag fallback. Full suite (≥ 1001 tests) must remain green. PASS = all 7 new cases green + zero regressions; FAIL = any gap or regression.<br>**Out of scope (explicit):** (a) Single-item drag (B-030 shipped Sprint 23 — B-025 delegates to that path); (b) Group drag / group nesting (B-031, same sprint); (c) Auto-scroll during drag (B-032, same sprint); (d) Multi-item drag-to-Open-Tabs demote (B-033 owns demote; multi-demote deferred post-S24); (e) Cross-source-group multi-drag payload (S24 restriction per AC13f); (f) Keyboard drag alternative (deferred per B-030 AC12 disclosure); (g) Touch/mobile (desktop-first).<br>**Smoke UAT plan**: `docs/UAT_B-025.md` — 9 cases (single-item fallback, 2-item same-group, 5-item cross-group, Escape cancel, stable-ordering non-contiguous, 10-second perf probe at N=10, sortOrder storage inspection, B-024 Shift+Click handoff, solo-drag fallback). |
| B-026 | As a user, I want a right-click context menu on individual bookmark items offering edit, delete, close tab, open in / save to group, and selection actions, so that I can perform common operations without opening dialogs manually. | P1 | S | done | 12 | B-003, B-006, B-020 | Right-clicking a single item opens its context menu.<br>All offered actions function correctly: edit opens the edit dialog, delete removes the bookmark (with confirmation), close tab closes the live tab.<br>Destructive actions are visually distinguished (red text or icon).<br>The menu is clamped to the viewport and never renders partially off-screen. |
| B-027 | As a user, I want a right-click context menu on group headers offering: open all bookmarks, close all open tabs, select-all / select-open / select-bookmarked, edit group, and delete group, so that I can manage a whole group from a single interaction. | P1 | S | done | 15 | B-006, B-020, B-024 | Right-clicking a group header opens the group context menu with all listed actions.<br>"Open all bookmarks" opens a new tab for each saved item in the group that is not already open.<br>"Close all open tabs" closes every live tab in the group with a single confirmation.<br>The three select actions produce the correct item selections and open the bulk action bar.<br>Delete group triggers the same confirmation and orphan-handling logic as the delete button. |
| B-028 | As a user, I want right-clicking when a multi-item selection is active to show a selection-aware context menu offering move, close, and remove operations for the whole selection, so that I can act on many items via right-click just as I can via the bulk bar. | P1 | S | done | 13 | B-024, B-026 | Right-clicking any selected item while multiple items are selected opens the selection context menu (not the single-item menu).<br>Move to group, close tabs, and remove bookmark actions operate on all selected items.<br>Destructive actions are visually distinguished.<br>Menu is clamped to the viewport. |
| B-029 | As a user, I want a group-picker modal for "move to group" operations that shows all groups with item counts, supports real-time name search, and is keyboard-navigable, so that I can quickly assign items to the right group regardless of how many groups I have. | P1 | M | done | 16 | B-006, B-024, B-027, B-028, B-055, B-059 | **AC1 — Unified Trigger Surface:** A single module-scope function `openGroupPickerDialog({ sourceGroupId, onSelect, triggerEl, mode })` opens the modal. All four existing move-to-group call sites are refactored to use it: (a) bulk action bar "Move to group" button (B-024 `_bulkMoveToGroup`), (b) group-header context menu — **add a new "Move items out of group" action to the B-027 menu as part of this item** (PO-confirmed; action was not shipped in Sprint 15's B-027 build), (c) selection context menu "Move to group" (B-028), (d) Open Tabs row context menu "Save to group" (B-059 — replaces the inline `<select>`). PASS: all four call sites invoke `openGroupPickerDialog` and no `<select>` element remains for group choice. FAIL: any caller retains a native `<select>` or duplicated picker code.<br>**AC2 — Modal Row Shape:** Each group row renders, left-to-right: (a) color chip (12×12 px swatch matching group color), (b) group name (`textContent` only), (c) saved-item count (e.g., "12 saved"), (d) open-tab count (e.g., "3 open"). `__ungrouped__` is listed first labeled "Ungrouped" with its counts. Sub-groups display their parent breadcrumb (`Parent / Child`). PASS: visual inspection shows all four fields per row; ungrouped is first. FAIL: any field missing, wrong order, or ungrouped not pinned to top.<br>**AC3 — Real-Time Filter:** A text input at the top of the modal filters rows via case-insensitive substring match on group name (and breadcrumb for sub-groups). Empty input shows all rows. Filter latency P95 < 50 ms on a 100-group collection (measured with `performance.now()`). PASS: typing narrows list within one frame; latency under target. FAIL: latency ≥ 50 ms, or match is case-sensitive / prefix-only.<br>**AC4 — Keyboard Navigation:** ArrowDown / ArrowUp move the highlighted row (wrap at ends); Enter confirms the highlighted row; Escape cancels; Tab cycles focus between the search input and the list (focus trap — Tab from last focusable element returns to first). Typing while the list is focused forwards the keystroke to the search input. PASS: all five key interactions behave as specified. FAIL: focus escapes the modal, Escape does not cancel, or arrows do not move highlight.<br>**AC5 — Source-Group Exclusion:** When the caller passes `sourceGroupId`, that group id is omitted from the rendered list. Moving items to their current group is therefore impossible via this picker. Save-to-group flow (B-059) passes no `sourceGroupId` because the open tab has no current group. PASS: calling with `sourceGroupId: "g123"` hides that row; calling without it shows all groups. FAIL: source group appears, or exclusion removes wrong row.<br>**AC6 — Single-Click Confirmation:** Clicking (or Enter on) a group row immediately calls `onSelect(groupId)` and closes the modal. No secondary confirm step inside the picker. PASS: one click moves items and dismisses picker. FAIL: additional "Confirm" button required, or picker remains open after selection.<br>**AC7 — B-059 Save-to-Group Handoff Contract:** When invoked from the Open Tabs "Save to group" call site (B-059 entry point), the picker resolves via `onSelect(groupId)` and closes. The caller then runs the B-059 soft-warn duplicate check (`_findDuplicateSavedItem`) — if a duplicate exists, the B-059 confirmation dialog opens AFTER the picker has fully closed. The picker itself performs no duplicate detection. PASS: selecting a group that already contains the URL closes the picker and then shows the B-059 confirm. FAIL: duplicate check runs inside the picker, or confirm dialog overlaps the picker.<br>**AC8 — Accessibility:** The modal root has `role="dialog"`, `aria-modal="true"`, `aria-labelledby` pointing at the heading id; the heading reads "Move to group" (or "Save to group" when `mode === "save"`). Each row is `role="option"` inside a `role="listbox"`. Visible focus indicator on the highlighted row meets WCAG AA contrast (≥ 3:1 against background). Focus trap cannot be escaped except via Escape / row click. PASS: axe-core / manual ARIA audit clean; keyboard-only user can operate picker end-to-end. FAIL: any ARIA attribute missing, focus escapes modal, or focus indicator below AA.<br>**AC9 — Empty-State (No Groups):** If the group list (after exclusion) is empty, the picker body shows the message "No groups yet — create a group first" and a "Create group" link/button. Clicking the link closes the picker and opens the existing B-006 group-create dialog. After the new group is created, the caller is responsible for re-invoking the picker if appropriate (picker does not auto-reopen). PASS: fresh profile shows empty-state message + working link. FAIL: blank body, link non-functional, or picker attempts to create group inline.<br>**AC10 — Performance (First Paint):** From trigger click to modal visible ≤ 100 ms (measured with `performance.now()` between click handler entry and `requestAnimationFrame` after render). Initial list renders from in-memory `_cachedGroups`; no message to the service worker during open or during filtering; zero storage writes during render or filter. PASS: latency < 100 ms; no MSG_* round-trip observed in devtools. FAIL: IPC issued on open, any storage write, or latency ≥ 100 ms.<br>**Out of scope (R2/R3 scope guard):** (1) Creating, editing, or deleting groups from inside the picker (except the empty-state link that dispatches to the existing B-006 dialog); (2) Multi-target selection (picker is single-select); (3) Drag-and-drop onto the picker (B-030 / B-033 own drag flows). |
| B-030 | As a user, I want to drag items within a group to reorder them and between groups to move them, with stable, normalised sort orders written to storage, so that my custom arrangement persists reliably. | P1 | L | done | 23 | B-001 ✅, B-008 ✅ ⚠️ **Full tier — item `sortOrder` storage already exists (B-001a); B-030 adds drag infrastructure + sortOrder rewrite path** | **Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — reorder and move are non-destructive (items keep all data; only sortOrder / groupId changes). Drag cancel (Escape) restores pre-drag order; no user data at risk.<br>**AC1 — Drag handle / draggable item rows.** Every `.item-row` in the sidepanel gains `draggable="true"`. An invisible or inline "grip" indicator on hover is acceptable but not required (native drag cursor is sufficient). `__ungrouped__` items and Open Tabs rows are also draggable (they can be reordered within Ungrouped and moved into saved groups per B-017). PASS = clicking-and-holding any saved-item row (including Ungrouped) initiates a drag; FAIL = any row mis-handles `dragstart`.<br>**AC2 — Drag ghost shows item title.** During drag, the browser's default drag ghost or a custom ghost shows the item title. PASS = ghost visible during drag with correct item label; FAIL = ghost empty, missing, or labelled wrong.<br>**AC3 — Insertion indicator.** While hovering over a valid drop target (another `.item-row` or empty space in a `.group-items` container), a horizontal insertion indicator (1-2 px line) renders at the potential drop position (above or below the hovered row). Indicator uses existing `#drop-indicator` element (already in sidepanel.html for B-008 group-reorder) or a dedicated item-drop indicator. PASS = indicator snaps to the correct position as the cursor moves; FAIL = indicator missing, jumps, or renders at wrong position.<br>**AC4 — Drop within same group → reorder.** Releasing the drag over another position in the same group reorders the dragged item to that position. All affected items' `sortOrder` values are recalculated and written via `MSG_UPDATE_ITEM` (or a new `MSG_BULK_UPDATE_ITEMS` batch — R2 decision). Storage uses a single `writeTransaction`. PASS = post-drop list reflects the new order; reload confirms persistence; FAIL = order reverts or drop is ignored.<br>**AC5 — Drop onto different group → move + reorder.** Releasing the drag over a different group's `.group-items` container (or an item inside it) moves the dragged item to that group AND places it at the drop position's rank. `groupId` is updated alongside `sortOrder`. PASS = item moves to new group at the drop position; FAIL = item stays in old group or lands at wrong rank.<br>**AC6 — Drop onto `__ungrouped__` → groupId → null.** Dropping an item onto the Ungrouped section sets its `groupId` to `null`. PASS = item appears under "Ungrouped" post-drop; storage reflects `groupId: null`; FAIL = groupId unchanged or `__ungrouped__` literal persisted.<br>**AC7 — Drop onto Open Tabs → no-op (B-033 territory).** Dropping a saved item onto the Open Tabs section is NOT a reorder — it's the B-033 drag-to-demote. B-030 treats the Open Tabs section as an invalid drop target for reorder purposes; B-033's drop handler (landing in the same sprint) owns the demote flow. PASS = B-030's drop handler no-ops for Open Tabs targets; B-033 handles it; FAIL = B-030 writes a sortOrder for an invalid target.<br>**AC8 — Drag cancel via Escape or release outside.** Pressing Escape during drag OR releasing the drag outside any valid drop target cancels the drag: the DOM reverts to the pre-drag state, NO `MSG_UPDATE_ITEM` dispatches. PASS = Escape / outside-release restores pre-drag order with zero storage writes; FAIL = partial write or order corruption.<br>**AC9 — `sortOrder` normalisation post-drop.** After every drop that commits, affected items' `sortOrder` values are normalised to consecutive integers (e.g., 0, 1, 2, ...) within the affected group(s). No gaps, no duplicates, no floating-point intermediate values. Normalisation runs inside the same `writeTransaction` as the drop commit. PASS = storage inspection post-drop shows consecutive integers; FAIL = gaps or duplicates persist.<br>**AC10 — Drag perf within 60 fps budget.** Pointer-follow latency during drag ≤ 16 ms P95 on a 500-item collection (measured via `requestAnimationFrame` timing spans around `dragover` handler). PASS = ≤ 16 ms P95; FAIL = jank visible or measured latency exceeds budget.<br>**AC11 — Post-drop storage write within 50 ms.** The `writeTransaction` commit completes in ≤ 50 ms P95 for a typical reorder (5-20 items touched via normalisation) on a 500-item collection. PASS = ≤ 50 ms P95; FAIL = write exceeds budget.<br>**AC12 — Accessibility disclosure.** Native HTML5 DnD is not keyboard-operable. Matching B-008 AC12, the `.item-row` elements disclose this limitation via an `aria-label` attribute suffix or `title` attribute: "Drag to reorder (keyboard reorder not yet available)". The drag deferral is documented in SPRINT.md as a known accessibility gap; a follow-up item MAY be filed if a keyboard-alternative reorder is prioritised later. PASS = disclosure present; gap documented; FAIL = silent keyboard inaccessibility with no hint.<br>**AC13 — C-9 empty-state enumeration (NEW C-9 active since Sprint 19)**: (a) Drop onto empty group (zero items) — item lands at sortOrder 0. (b) Drop at start of populated group — existing items shift right by 1. (c) Drop at end of populated group — item appends with sortOrder = max+1. (d) Drop between two items — item's sortOrder interpolates, all affected rows renumbered. (e) Drag from Open Tabs (live-only row) — if user promotes via drag to a saved group, promote path (B-017 equivalent) runs. (f) Source and destination are the same group AND the drop position is the item's current position — no-op, no storage write. (g) Drop on the group header (not inside the items container) — treat as drop at group-start or drop-beneath-header (R3 decision; document).<br>**AC14 — Out of scope (explicit).** (a) Multi-item drag (B-025 — S23). (b) Group drag-reorder (B-008 already shipped). (c) Group drag-nesting (B-031 — S23). (d) Auto-scroll during drag (B-032 — S23). (e) Touch / mobile support (desktop-first per CLAUDE.md). (f) Keyboard reorder alternative (deferred per AC12). (g) No new storage schema; no new message types beyond potentially a `MSG_BULK_UPDATE_ITEMS` batch variant pending R2.<br>**AC15 — Test coverage.** New test file `tests/b030-item-drag-reorder.test.js` covering: reorder within group (AC4), move between groups (AC5), drop onto ungrouped (AC6), sortOrder normalisation (AC9), cancel via Escape (AC8), no-op on same-position drop (AC13f), atomic storage write (single `writeTransaction`). Total suite ≥ 979 + 7 new B-030 tests. PASS = new tests pass + full suite green; FAIL = any regression.<br>**Smoke UAT plan**: `docs/UAT_B-030.md` — 9 cases (Sprint 23 re-authored at R1 with perf probes + B-008 regression check).<br>**AC16 — dragover handler MUST NOT call `getBoundingClientRect` outside a `requestAnimationFrame` callback.** R4 [code-reviewer] greps the sidepanel dragover path for `getBoundingClientRect(` occurrences; any hit outside a rAF closure is a REJECT. PASS = grep shows only rAF-scoped calls; FAIL = any synchronous rect read on dragover path.<br>**AC17 — dragover DOM mutations MUST be batched into a single `requestAnimationFrame` per frame.** Indicator position updates, class additions, and any other DOM writes triggered by drag events are coalesced via rAF. Dragover itself only records `pointerX / pointerY` primitives + requests a rAF tick (deduplicated). PASS = at most 1 rAF callback per frame during active drag; no DOM writes in dragover handler body; FAIL = either rAF ceiling breached or direct DOM write in dragover.<br>**AC18 — per-drag bounding-rect cache.** At `dragstart`, the sidepanel snapshots all `.item-row` client-rects into a `Map<itemId, DOMRect>` ("rect cache"). Cache is invalidated ONLY on `scroll` events registered on `itemListEl` (passive listener, attached at dragstart, detached at dragend). Subsequent rAF ticks consume the cache without calling `getBoundingClientRect` again unless invalidated. PASS = cache built once per drag + rebuilt on scroll + consumed by rAF tick; FAIL = cache missing or rebuilt per-tick.<br>**AC19 — R5 call-count assertion.** The fake-DOM drag simulation test in `tests/b030-item-drag-reorder.test.js` records the number of `getBoundingClientRect` calls during a simulated 3-second continuous drag on a 500-row cache. Budget: `≤ 500 (initial snapshot) + 500 × N_scroll_events` (typically 0 during a pointer-only drag) + occasional rebuilds capped at 5. PASS = call count ≤ 510 for a no-scroll drag; FAIL = any higher indicates uncoalesced reads.<br>**AC20 — Fake-DOM drag simulation test.** `tests/b030-item-drag-reorder.test.js` includes four fake-DOM drag-flow tests (follows `tests/b054-sidepanel.test.js` FakeNode precedent): (a) same-group drag-to-end, (b) same-group drag-to-start, (c) cross-group move, (d) drop-onto-Ungrouped. Each asserts the final `MSG_BULK_REORDER_ITEMS` dispatch spec is correct. PASS = 4 new tests green covering all four cases; FAIL = any gap.<br>**AC21 — Same-group reorder at three destinations.** In `tests/sort-order.test.js`, with a 5-item group [a, b, c, d, e], assert `computeItemReorder` produces correct updates for: (i) drag `a` to start (no-op), (ii) drag `a` to middle (destIndex=2), (iii) drag `a` to end (destIndex=4). Covers the S22 silent-failure mode where same-group reorder was the only broken path. PASS = 3 assertions green; FAIL = any assertion reveals wrong update spec.<br>**AC22 — Transform-positioned indicator (R0 D-A).** The indicator is `position: absolute` inside `itemListEl` (which gains `position: relative`). Moved via `style.transform = 'translateY(Npx)'` during drag. Hidden via `opacity: 0` rather than `hidden` attribute. PASS = DOM inspection confirms indicator is absolute-positioned + transformed during drag; not reparented mid-drag; FAIL = any `.before()` / `.appendChild()` on the indicator during drag.<br>**AC23 — Indicator has `pointer-events: none` (R0 risk mitigation).** Prevents `elementFromPoint` from hitting the indicator element itself during target detection. PASS = CSS rule in effect; FAIL = absent.<br>**AC24 — Broadcast-race guard.** `_itemDragState` at dragstart captures `_cachedItemsGen` (a monotonic counter bumped on every `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` response). At drop time, if `_cachedItemsGen` has advanced (broadcast landed mid-drag), the drop handler awaits a fresh `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` before computing the reorder spec. PASS = stale-state drops re-fetch; FAIL = drop proceeds with stale `_cachedItems`. |
| B-031 | As a user, I want to reorder groups by dragging their header and to nest a top-level group into another top-level group by dragging, so that I can organise my group hierarchy via drag-and-drop. | P1 | M | done | 24 | B-007 ✅, B-008 ✅, B-030 ✅ ⚠️ **Full tier — may extend `MSG_BULK_REORDER_ITEMS` with group discriminator or introduce `MSG_BULK_REORDER_GROUPS` (R2 decision)** | **Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — group reorder and nesting are non-destructive structural operations. Escape cancels with no storage write; drop-on-self is a no-op; items remain with their groups through any reorder or nesting. No bookmarks are deleted; no data is irreversibly overwritten.<br>**AC1 — Drag initiator: group drag handle (distinct from item drag).** Each group header exposes a dedicated group drag handle (R2 decides visual — `⠿` or `⋮⋮` icon). Dragging the group handle initiates group-drag mode; dragging an item row initiates B-030 item-drag mode. The two paths are mutually exclusive. PASS = grabbing the group handle activates group drag; grabbing an item row activates item drag; FAIL = either handle activates the wrong path, or both paths activate simultaneously.<br>**AC2 — Drag ghost: group title + item count.** A ghost element follows the pointer showing the group's display name and item count (e.g., "Work (5)"). Ghost uses the existing drag-ghost pattern from B-030 (rAF-positioned, `pointer-events: none`). PASS = ghost visible and tracks pointer within ±4 px; FAIL = ghost absent, stationary, or shows wrong label.<br>**AC3 — Drop-on-self: no-op.** Releasing the drag on the dragged group's own header (any zone) produces no reorder, no nesting, no storage write, no visual change. PASS = structure and storage unchanged after self-drop; FAIL = any mutation occurs.<br>**AC4 — REORDER mode: horizontal indicator line.** While pointer is in top 25% or bottom 25% of any non-self group header, REORDER mode activates: horizontal indicator line appears between the two groups flanking the insertion point (same CSS pattern as B-030 drop indicator). Target group header NOT highlighted. PASS = line appears in correct inter-group gap in outer 25% zones; FAIL = line absent, mispositioned, or appears in middle 50%.<br>**AC5 — NEST mode: target-group highlight.** While pointer is in middle 50% of a valid (non-rejected) group header, NEST mode activates: target header highlighted as nest destination (R2 specifies exact CSS — `outline` or `border`, `background-color`, `transition`, `border-radius`). No reorder line in this mode. Highlight visually distinct from rejection indicator (AC9). PASS = header highlight appears and no reorder line shown in middle 50%; FAIL = highlight absent, reorder line shown instead, or modes not visually distinct.<br>**AC6 — REORDER commit: `sortOrder` normalisation.** Valid REORDER drop renormalises `sortOrder` for all top-level groups to consecutive integers (same pattern as B-008 + B-030). Commit is a single atomic `writeTransaction` (R2 decides `MSG_BULK_REORDER_GROUPS` vs extension of `MSG_BULK_REORDER_ITEMS`). `_cachedGroups` updated; UI re-renders within 200 ms. Items remain under their respective groups — no item displacement. PASS = reorder persists post-reload; `sortOrder` has no gaps; items intact; FAIL = order reverts, gaps, or items displaced.<br>**AC7 — NEST commit: `parentGroupId` + `sortOrder` inside new parent.** Valid NEST drop (top-level dragged group, top-level target, neither has children, no circular path): dragged group's `parentGroupId` set to target id; `sortOrder` set to position within target's children; target's `sortOrder` among top-level unaffected. Single atomic `writeTransaction`. Sub-group appears indented under the parent immediately. PASS = nesting persists post-reload; sub-group items accessible via expand; collapse/expand works on parent; FAIL = `parentGroupId` not set, sub-group not visible, or items lost.<br>**AC8 — Reject: group with existing sub-groups cannot be nested (depth-2 prevention).** If the dragged group already contains at least one sub-group child, it cannot be dropped in NEST mode onto any target. Hovering in NEST zone over any group header: rejection indicator (AC9) fires; normal REORDER in outer 25% zones still works. Release in NEST zone: no nesting occurs. PASS = rejection feedback fires; no `parentGroupId` mutation; FAIL = nesting occurs, producing depth-2.<br>**AC9 — Reject: invalid nest targets — visual rejection indicator.** Invalid NEST targets trigger a rejection indicator (R2 specifies — e.g., red flash on header, `no-drop` cursor, or ❌ overlay — distinct from valid highlight AC5): (a) dragged group itself (AC3), (b) a group that already IS a sub-group (sub-group-onto-sub-group stacking), (c) a descendant of the dragged group (circular), (d) the Ungrouped pseudo-group (cannot have sub-groups). Release over a rejected target: no structural change, no storage write. PASS = all four rejection cases fire indicator and produce no storage mutation; FAIL = any case silently accepted, or indicator absent.<br>**AC10 — `filterGroupParentCandidates` reuse (mandatory R2 requirement — carry from S22 LOW through S24).** R2 MUST reuse the `filterGroupParentCandidates` helper (imported at `sidepanel/sidepanel.js` ~line 62, sourced from B-007 sub-group module) to compute the valid-nest candidate set during drag. The same filter driving B-007's move-group picker drives B-031 nest validation — no duplicate logic. R2 documents how it's called: likely once on `dragstart` to build a `Set<groupId>` of valid targets, then used in `dragover` for O(1) lookup. PASS = R4 [code-reviewer] confirms no duplicate filter logic; `filterGroupParentCandidates` is single source of truth; FAIL = B-031 introduces parallel filter without justified deviation in R2 output.<br>**AC11 — Escape / outside-release cancels drag.** Escape during active group drag immediately hides all indicators (reorder line, nest highlight, rejection indicator); reverts in-flight DOM transform; removes ghost; zero storage writes. Outside-release same result. PASS = structure and storage unchanged; no console errors; FAIL = indicator persists or any storage mutation.<br>**AC12 — Broadcast-race guard (B-030 AC24 pattern).** If `MSG_STATE_CHANGED` updates `_cachedGroups` during an in-flight group drag: on drop commit, handler re-fetches the current `_cachedGroupsGen` counter and aborts with a toast ("Order changed — try again") if the generation advanced since `dragstart`. No partial write against stale state. PASS = two-window race test produces either clean commit or clean abort; no phantom state post-reload; FAIL = partial write against stale state yielding corrupted hierarchy.<br>**AC13 — Performance: 16 ms P95 dragover latency.** Group `dragover` handler uses rAF coalescing (same pattern as B-030) — no synchronous `getBoundingClientRect` inside raw `dragover`; rects cached on `dragstart`, invalidated on scroll/resize. P95 dragover latency ≤ 16 ms over a 10-second continuous drag. No long tasks > 50 ms. PASS = 10-second perf probe shows no long tasks; frames sustain ~60 fps; FAIL = visible lag, long tasks, or compounding slowdown.<br>**AC14 — CSS enumeration requirement (mandatory for R2 + R4).** R2 MUST enumerate every CSS property for all positioned drag elements: (a) Group drag ghost — `position`, `top`, `left`, `transform`, `pointer-events`, `z-index`, `opacity`, `background-color`, `border-radius`, `padding`, `font-size`, `white-space`; (b) Reorder indicator line — `position`, explicit `top`/`left` anchor values (`top: 0` alone is a REJECT per S23 precedent), `height`, `width`, `background-color`, `z-index`, `pointer-events`, `transform`; (c) Nest highlight — `outline` or `border`, `background-color`, `transition`, `border-radius`; (d) Rejection indicator — distinct from highlight; `outline-color` or overlay, `transition`, duration. R4 [code-reviewer] verifies shipped CSS matches R2 enumeration. PASS = R4 confirms no positioned property omitted vs R2 enumeration; FAIL = any positioned property absent, OR R2 omits any category.<br>**AC15 — C-9 empty-state + edge-case enumeration.** (a) Drag into empty group container (zero items) — NEST/REORDER zones still apply by header position; ghost shows "(0)". (b) Drag onto collapsed group (NEST zone) — R2 decides: accept-and-expand post-drop vs reject-if-collapsed. Documented explicitly in R2. (c) Drag last top-level group — REORDER no-op (only one position); NEST zone still valid if other top-levels exist. (d) Drag onto Ungrouped — NEST rejected; REORDER between Ungrouped and adjacent valid. (e) Drag parent onto own child — circular reject. (f) Drag sub-group (already has `parentGroupId`) — REORDER within sibling sub-groups valid (R2 decides scope; may be deferred); NEST onto any group rejected.<br>**AC16 — Accessibility disclosure.** Group drag handles carry `title="Drag to reorder or nest (keyboard reorder not yet available)"`. PASS = tooltip visible on hover; FAIL = tooltip absent or wrong copy.<br>**AC17 — sortOrder normalisation.** After any group reorder or nesting commit, all affected sibling groups (at the same depth) have `sortOrder` renormalised to consecutive integers (R2 decides starting index consistently with B-008 + B-030 pattern). PASS = storage inspector shows consecutive `sortOrder`; post-reload order matches post-drop; FAIL = gaps or order differs post-reload.<br>**AC18 — Test coverage: `tests/b031-group-drag.test.js`.** Covering: reorder within top-level (ascending + descending), nest top-level into another, reject stacking (sub-group onto sub-group), reject circular (parent onto descendant), reject depth-2 (group with children in NEST mode), reject drop-on-self, `sortOrder` normalisation post-reorder, Escape cancel — DOM reverts + no storage write. Uses existing `chrome-mock.js`. Full suite (≥ 1001 tests) must remain green. PASS = new test file green; full suite green; FAIL = any failure.<br>**Out of scope (explicit):** (a) Item drag (B-030 shipped); (b) Multi-item drag selection (B-025 same sprint); (c) Auto-scroll during drag (B-032 same sprint); (d) Depth-2+ sub-group hierarchies (B-007 caps at depth-1; this item respects + enforces via drag); (e) Keyboard alternative for group reorder/nesting; (f) Touch/mobile (desktop-first).<br>**Smoke UAT plan**: `docs/UAT_B-031.md` — 11 test cases (reorder two top-level, nest into another, reject stacking, reject circular, reject self-drop, reject depth-2, reject onto Ungrouped, perf probe, persistence, broadcast-race, Escape cancel; merge-blocking gates defined). |
| B-032 | As a user, I want the scroll container to auto-scroll when I drag an item near the top or bottom edges, so that I can drop items anywhere in a long list without releasing the drag. | P2 | S | done | 24 | B-030 ✅ | **Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — B-032 is a scroll animation only. No state is mutated; no bookmarks or groups are created, modified, or deleted. The underlying drag reorder (B-030) retains its own confirmation-free drop flow per established precedent.<br>**AC1 — Activation zone.** Auto-scroll activates when the drag pointer enters a zone within 60 px of the top or bottom edge of `#item-list` (the scrollable sidepanel item container). Auto-scroll does NOT activate outside an active drag. PASS = pointer at 59 px from edge during drag → scroll begins; FAIL = no scroll, or scroll begins on plain mousemove.<br>**AC2 — Scroll direction.** Top-edge zone → `#item-list` scrolls upward; bottom-edge zone → scrolls downward. PASS = correct direction in each zone; FAIL = wrong direction or no directional response.<br>**AC3 — Speed proportional to proximity (linear ramp).** Scroll speed per rAF frame = `Math.round(MAX_SCROLL_SPEED × (1 - (distanceFromEdge / EDGE_ZONE_PX)))` where `EDGE_ZONE_PX = 60` and `MAX_SCROLL_SPEED = 20` (px/frame). PASS = visibly slower scroll at 55 px vs 5 px from edge; FAIL = binary on/off; speed does not vary with distance.<br>**AC4 — Max speed cap.** Scroll speed capped at 20 px per frame regardless of pointer position. PASS = even at extreme edge, list does not jump an entire viewport per frame; FAIL = frame-jumping at extreme proximity.<br>**AC5 — Deactivation.** Auto-scroll stops (next rAF tick) when: (a) `dragend`, (b) Escape, (c) `drop`, or (d) pointer leaves edge zone. PASS = scrolling stops in all four paths with no coasting; FAIL = residual scroll after any deactivation condition.<br>**AC6 — Boundary clamp (no over-scroll).** No attempt to scroll past `scrollTop = 0` or `scrollTop = scrollHeight − clientHeight`. PASS = holding at top edge with nothing above → no scroll attempt, no errors; FAIL = negative scrollTop or console error.<br>**AC7 — Rect cache compatibility.** Auto-scroll causes `scroll` events on `#item-list`. B-030's `_dragRectCache` passive scroll listener sets `_dragRectCache.invalid = true`; next `_scheduleDragTick` lazily rebuilds the cache (B-030 AC18). B-032 must not bypass or suppress this scroll event, and must not introduce a second competing scroll listener. PASS = after auto-scroll moves the list, drop indicator repositions correctly on next dragover; FAIL = indicator freezes or mis-positions.<br>**AC8 — Single rAF loop (no duplicate loop).** Auto-scroll rAF loop is integrated into or coordinated with `_scheduleDragTick` so only one rAF is active at a time. PASS = DevTools Performance shows one rAF callback per frame during drag; FAIL = doubled rAF callbacks or compounding frame budget.<br>**AC9 — Performance budget.** Auto-scroll does not increase `_scheduleDragTick` latency above B-030's 16 ms P95 budget. Measured over a 10-second continuous edge-drag — no long tasks (> 50 ms). PASS = no long tasks; frames sustain ~60 fps through 10-second probe; FAIL = long tasks or frame rate drops below ~50 fps sustained.<br>**AC10 — Implementation: scrollTop only (no CSS).** Auto-scroll implemented exclusively via `element.scrollTop += delta` inside a rAF callback. No new CSS classes, `position`, `transform`, or `transition` properties. PASS = code review confirms rAF + scrollTop only; no new CSS rules; FAIL = CSS animation or new positioned elements added.<br>**AC11 — No activation outside drag.** Plain pointer movement over edge zone (no active drag) does not trigger auto-scroll. State initialised at `dragstart` and fully cleaned up at `dragend`/`drop`/Escape. PASS = moving mouse to edge without a drag held → no scroll; FAIL = scroll on plain hover.<br>**AC12 — Test coverage.** New tests in `tests/b032-auto-scroll.test.js` (or additions to `tests/b030-item-drag-reorder.test.js`) covering: (a) pointer at top-edge zone → `scrollTop` decreases, (b) pointer at bottom-edge zone → `scrollTop` increases, (c) pointer exits zone → scroll stops, (d) `dragend` → scroll stops, (e) speed ramp assertion (5 px produces higher delta than 55 px). Full existing suite (1001 tests) must remain green. PASS = all new tests pass + zero regressions; FAIL = any test failure.<br>**Out of scope (explicit):** (a) Horizontal auto-scroll (sidepanel is vertical-only); (b) Touch / pointer events (desktop-first; `dragover`/`dragend` only); (c) Auto-scroll during group drag (B-031 may reuse the helper but B-032 does not own or gate that decision — B-032 targets item drag only via `_itemDragState`).<br>**Smoke UAT plan**: `docs/UAT_B-032.md` — 6 cases (top-edge activation, bottom-edge activation, pointer-leave deactivation, Escape/drop deactivation, speed ramp, 10-second perf probe). |
