| B-035 | As a user, I want Tab Junkie to be openable as a detachable standalone window that reuses any existing instance rather than creating a duplicate, so that I can run it outside the side panel without accumulating redundant windows. | P2 | M | done | 28 | B-001 ✅, B-010 ✅ ⚠️ **Full tier — standalone window lifecycle + popup-adjacent focus semantics. C-11 applies from day one.** | **Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — B-035 opens or focuses a window. No items, groups, bookmarks, or tabs are created/modified/deleted. Mutations inside the window flow through existing sidepanel primitives.<br>**AC1 — Shortcut registration.** `manifest.json` `commands.open-junkie-window` registered at Alt+Shift+J (v1.21.0+). SW listens for `chrome.commands.onCommand` with `"open-junkie-window"`. PASS = Alt+Shift+J triggers handler; FAIL = no handler or wrong command.<br>**AC2 — Open path: no existing window.** When no standalone window is open, SW calls `chrome.windows.create({url, type:"popup", ...})` with initial size/position per AC6. PASS = new window appears; FAIL = not created or wrong type.<br>**AC3 — Focus path: existing window detected.** Detection via `chrome.windows.getAll({windowTypes:["popup"]})` + URL match at each trigger (D-3 option c, cold-start safe). SW calls `chrome.windows.update(existingId, {focused:true})` and MUST NOT call `create`. PASS = existing receives focus, count stays 1; FAIL = duplicate created.<br>**AC4 — Window type.** MUST be `type: "popup"` (no tab bar, URL bar, bookmarks bar). PASS = popup chrome; FAIL = normal window.<br>**AC5 — Standalone content target.** R2 resolves D-2: recommended (a) load `sidepanel/sidepanel.html` directly (zero new HTML). If R2 rejects (a), new `standalone/standalone.html` with explicit justification. PASS = HTML loads + sidepanel modules init; FAIL = blank/404/module error.<br>**AC6 — Initial size + position.** ~1200×800 px (R2 confirms). Centered on active display, clamped to screen bounds. No persistence in v1. PASS = window fits on active monitor; FAIL = off-screen or wrong monitor.<br>**AC7 — User-resizable.** OS-default drag-resize enabled. Position persistence out of scope. PASS = resize works; FAIL = blocked by extension code.<br>**AC8 — Sidepanel content parity.** All sidepanel features (group expand/collapse, item activation, drag-reorder B-030, context menus, search) functional in standalone. PASS = feature parity; FAIL = any feature missing/broken.<br>**AC9 — CSS scope.** If D-2 option (a), no new CSS — sidepanel CSS applies as-is. R2 confirms. PASS = identical layout, no regressions; FAIL = broken layout or CSS leaks.<br>**AC10 — Real-time state sync.** Both surfaces open → any mutation in one reflects in other within existing `MSG_STATE_CHANGED` broadcast latency (<3s typical). No new message types. R2 verifies popup-window context preserves `chrome.runtime.onMessage` subscription. PASS = sidepanel edit appears in standalone; FAIL = stale data.<br>**AC11 — Window-close cleanup.** Standalone close (X button, OS, programmatic) clears any SW window-id tracking. Next Alt+Shift+J triggers open path (AC2). PASS = post-close Alt+Shift+J opens fresh; FAIL = SW attempts closed-window focus.<br>**AC12 — `chrome.windows` rejection handling.** All create/update calls wrapped in try/catch or `.catch()`. Rejection logged, no unhandled promise rejection. PASS = rejection handled gracefully; FAIL = uncaught exception.<br>**AC13 — SW cold-start safety.** Existing-window detection uses `chrome.windows.getAll` each trigger — NOT in-memory SW state. Post-SW-restart, Alt+Shift+J correctly focuses any open standalone. PASS = cold-start focus path works; FAIL = duplicate after cold-start.<br>**AC14 — SW cold-start mid-window-session.** Window's sidepanel.html re-hydrates from `chrome.storage.local` on next user interaction (same as sidepanel). R2 confirms recovery applies automatically to standalone context. PASS = interaction post-cold-start re-hydrates; FAIL = blank content.<br>**AC15 — B-063 context-menu forward.** `window.blur` close listener (sidepanel pattern per B-063 AC1-AC4) MUST fire in standalone. Automatic if attached in sidepanel.js. R2 verifies. PASS = right-click menu closes on Alt+Tab/blur; FAIL = stale menu persists.<br>**AC16 — Perf: first paint < 300 ms P95.** 5 consecutive opens via Alt+Shift+J measured. PASS = all 5 < 300ms; FAIL = any ≥ 300ms.<br>**AC17 — Keyboard navigation parity.** Tab/Shift+Tab cycles in logical order matching sidepanel. Enter activates. No focus traps beyond sidepanel baseline. PASS = full cycle without getting stuck; FAIL = trap or unreachable.<br>**AC18 — Accessibility parity.** ARIA roles inherited from sidepanel.html. Visible focus indicators. WCAG AA contrast. PASS = no critical axe-core violations; FAIL = ARIA or focus issues.<br>**AC19 — Concurrent surface independence.** Standalone + quick-search popup (Alt+J) OR group-jump popup (Alt+K) open simultaneously → operate independently. PASS = both work without interference; FAIL = one disturbs other.<br>**AC20 — Empty-state rendering.** Zero groups → sidepanel empty-state UI (icon + message + CTA). PASS = empty-state visible; FAIL = blank or error.<br>**C-9 empty-state enumeration**: (a) zero groups/items → sidepanel empty-state; (b) window already open → focus path (AC3); (c) secondary monitor — `getAll` + URL match works cross-monitor; (d) SW cold-start mid-create → race handled by re-trigger; (e) SW cold-start mid-session → re-hydrates on interaction (AC14); (f) drag events inside standalone → work via sidepanel.js; (g) `chrome.windows.*` rejection → caught + logged (AC12); (h) `windows` permission absent → R2 resolves D-7 before R3; [security-reviewer] confirms at R4.<br>**C-11 message-ordering audit (R2 mandatory)**: R2 enumerates every SW write that could precede `chrome.windows.create`/`update`. Candidates (D-6): (a) window-open recency (only if added); (b) B-038 display-mode preference (OUT OF SCOPE); (c) position persistence (OUT OF SCOPE per D-4/AC7). If vacuous, R2 confirms explicitly.<br>**R2 decision points**: D-1 window type (R1 recommends `popup`); D-2 URL target (R1 recommends load sidepanel.html); D-3 existing-instance detection (R1 recommends `getAll` + URL match); D-4 size 1200×800 centered; D-5 state-sync subscription verification; D-6 C-11 write enumeration; D-7 `windows` permission audit; D-8 B-063 context-menu listener survival.<br>**Out of scope**: (a) B-038 view-mode preference; (b) B-036 new tab page; (c) custom window chrome; (d) multiple simultaneous standalones; (e) position/size persistence.<br>**Test coverage (AC for R5)**: new `tests/b035-standalone-window.test.js` covering: SW `open-junkie-window` command listener, existing-window `getAll`+URL-match logic, open path `create`, focus path `update`, rejection handling, post-close re-detection. Uses `chrome-mock.js` — no ad-hoc stubs.<br>**Smoke UAT plan**: `docs/UAT_B-035.md` — 13 cases. |
| B-036 | As a Tab Junkie user, I want my new tab page to show a web search input and a live bookmark grid organised by groups — with the same open/active/drifted indicators as the side panel — so that every new tab opens directly to my bookmarks without having to open the side panel first. | P3 | L | done | 29 | B-001 ✅, B-010 ✅, B-021 ✅ ⚠️ **Full tier — new extension surface, new DOM, new CSS, live-state subscription, search integration. C-11 non-vacuous: click-to-navigate tears down the new tab page JS context.** | **Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — the new tab page is a read-only grid surface. No items, groups, or bookmarks are created, modified, or deleted. Click-to-navigate activates an existing tab or opens a URL — no destructive write. Confirmation is not required or applicable.<br>**AC1 — Manifest wiring unchanged.** `manifest.json` `chrome_url_overrides.newtab` MUST remain `"newtab/newtab.html"` exactly as already wired. R3 MUST NOT modify this entry. PASS = value is `"newtab/newtab.html"` with no other `chrome_url_overrides` keys added; FAIL = entry missing, changed, or new sibling key added.<br>**AC2 — Default-OFF gate.** On `DOMContentLoaded`, `newtab.js` reads the B-039 preference (`tj:prefs` key `newTabEnabled`, default `false`). If `false`, the page immediately redirects to the browser default (R2 resolves D-2a exact mechanism — `chrome.tabs.update({url:…})` or `window.location.replace`). No grid rendered; no blank or partial flash. PASS = new tab with toggle OFF shows browser default immediately; FAIL = grid renders or blank flash visible.<br>**AC3 — Enabled gate: grid renders.** If `newTabEnabled` is `true`, `newtab.js` renders the full layout (web search input + bookmark grid + quick-filter input). No redirect fires. PASS = grid visible; FAIL = redirect fires or blank when toggle ON.<br>**AC4 — Storage bootstrap sequence.** Enabled boot sends `MSG_GET_PREFERENCES` + `Promise.all([MSG_LIST_ITEMS, MSG_LIST_GROUPS])` before first render. Skeleton loader visible during fetch (not a spinner). On resolution, skeleton replaced by grid. PASS = skeleton visible for any fetch > 50 ms, replaced by grid on resolution; FAIL = spinner used, blank flash, or no skeleton.<br>**AC5 — Web search input.** A prominent search input at the top submits via `chrome.search.query({text, disposition:'NEW_TAB'})` (the `search` permission is already granted in `manifest.json`). R2 resolves D-2b (API call vs form-submit). Enter key or explicit submit button triggers the query. PASS = query fires, user lands on search results; FAIL = no navigation, wrong API, or `search` permission removed.<br>**AC6 — Bookmark grid: group sections.** Bookmarks rendered as collapsible group sections in the same visual order as the side panel. Each group header shows the group name. Items within a group sorted by `sortOrder`. PASS = groups in correct order with correct items; FAIL = wrong order, wrong group assignment, or groups absent.<br>**AC7 — Quick-filter input.** A filter input narrows the bookmark grid in real time using a pre-lowercased in-memory index (B-052 §34 pattern). Debounce: 200 ms (B-021 contract). Filter matches highlighted via `shared/highlight.js` `<mark>` fragments. R2 resolves D-4 (shared import vs duplicate). PASS = typing narrows grid within 200 ms debounce with highlights; FAIL = no narrowing, no highlight, or debounce absent.<br>**AC8 — Live-state indicators.** Items whose URL matches an open tab show the live (open) indicator. The currently active tab's item shows the active indicator. Items with a stored URL that drifts from the live tab URL show the drifted indicator. Same CSS tokens and icon patterns as the side panel — no new indicator variants. PASS = all three indicator states visible and correct; FAIL = any state absent or wrong icon.<br>**AC9 — Real-time state updates via MSG_STATE_CHANGED.** Page subscribes to `MSG_STATE_CHANGED` broadcasts (same `chrome.runtime.onMessage.addListener` pattern as sidepanel per §10.10). Tab open/close/navigation events cause the grid to update live without a page reload. PASS = opening a bookmarked URL in another tab causes the live indicator to appear within < 3 s; FAIL = stale indicators until reload.<br>**AC10 — Click-to-navigate.** Clicking a bookmark item dispatches `MSG_NAVIGATE_TO_ITEM` to the SW. The SW handles it identically to the sidepanel click path (activate existing tab or open new tab). C-11 applies (non-vacuous): if any SW write (e.g., recency) precedes a focus-shifting call, it MUST be fire-and-forgot via `chrome.runtime.sendMessage(...).catch(() => {})` BEFORE `chrome.tabs.update({active:true})` or equivalent — the new tab page JS context tears down the moment navigation commits. R2 D-5 enumerates all SW writes on this path. PASS = click activates or opens correct tab; FAIL = wrong tab, navigation fails, or C-11 ordering violated.<br>**AC11 — Keyboard navigation.** Focus lands on the web search input on `DOMContentLoaded`. Tab cycles: search input → filter input → first bookmark item → subsequent items in DOM order. Shift+Tab reverses. Enter on a bookmark item navigates (same as click). No focus traps. PASS = full Tab cycle without getting stuck; FAIL = trap or unreachable element.<br>**AC12 — Empty state: zero items.** If `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` returns empty, grid shows icon + "No bookmarks yet" + CTA linking to the side panel. No spinner, no blank. PASS = empty state with CTA visible; FAIL = blank or error.<br>**AC13 — Empty state: zero groups, items exist.** If `MSG_LIST_GROUPS` returns empty but items exist (ungrouped), items rendered under an implicit ungrouped section using the same ungrouped fallback as the side panel. R2 C-9 resolves exact treatment. PASS = ungrouped items visible; FAIL = items hidden or error.<br>**AC14 — Empty state: zero filter matches.** Non-empty filter query matching zero items shows filter-empty state "No matches for «query»" + "Clear filter" CTA. Group sections hide (not removed from DOM). PASS = filter-empty state visible with working Clear CTA; FAIL = blank, no message, or empty group containers remain visible.<br>**AC15 — Empty state: partial search input.** Typing 1–2 characters in the web search input (not yet submitted) causes no navigation and no SW call. Grid remains stable and unchanged. PASS = grid stable during partial input; FAIL = navigation fires or grid flickers.<br>**AC16 — Empty state: bootstrap error.** If `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` or `MSG_LIST_GROUPS` rejects, an error state shown ("Something went wrong — try reloading") with a Reload button. No blank, no silent failure. R2 resolves exact UX. PASS = error state with Reload visible; FAIL = blank or unhandled rejection.<br>**AC17 — Performance: first paint < 200 ms P95.** With a 500-item collection, `DOMContentLoaded` → first visible grid paint completes within 200 ms P95 (5 consecutive loads measured). PASS = all 5 < 200 ms; FAIL = any ≥ 200 ms.<br>**AC18 — Performance: filter latency < 50 ms P95.** With a 1 000-item collection, each post-debounce filter application (index query + re-render of matching rows) completes within 50 ms P95. PASS = [test-engineer] perf harness confirms P95 < 50 ms; FAIL = any breach.<br>**AC19 — Accessibility: ARIA roles.** Grid uses: `role="search"` on the search form, `role="searchbox"` on the filter input, `role="list"` on each group's item container, `role="listitem"` on each item row, `role="heading"` (appropriate level) on group headers. PASS = no critical axe-core violations; FAIL = missing or incorrect roles.<br>**AC20 — Accessibility: WCAG AA contrast + focus indicators.** All text meets WCAG AA contrast (≥ 4.5:1 normal, ≥ 3:1 large). All interactive elements have visible focus rings using `--color-focus` token. PASS = confirmed in both light and dark themes; FAIL = any contrast or focus regression.<br>**AC21 — Theme consistency.** `newtab.css` uses the same CSS custom property tokens (`--color-bg`, `--color-fg`, `--color-border`, `--color-accent`, etc.) as the sidepanel. Theme resolved via `newtab/theme-init.js` (reuse or symlink of `sidepanel/theme-init.js` — R2 D-1 resolves). No hard-coded colours in `newtab.css`. PASS = new tab and side panel visually consistent in both themes; FAIL = mismatched or un-themed elements.<br>**AC22 — No new manifest permissions.** `manifest.json` `permissions` MUST remain `["tabs", "tabGroups", "storage", "sidePanel", "search"]`. `search` already covers `chrome.search.query`. R3 MUST NOT add new entries. [security-reviewer] confirms at R4. PASS = permissions array unchanged; FAIL = any new permission added.<br>**AC23 — SW cold-start safety.** `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` + `MSG_LIST_GROUPS` hit `readyPromise`-gated SW handlers. Cold-start new tab shows skeleton until SW resolves. PASS = cold-start shows skeleton then grid; FAIL = permanent blank or error on cold start.<br>**C-9 empty-state enumeration (all paths explicit)**: (a) zero items → empty-state with CTA (AC12); (b) zero groups, items exist → ungrouped fallback (AC13); (c) filter matches zero items → filter-empty state + Clear CTA (AC14); (d) partial search input not submitted → grid stable (AC15); (e) `newTabEnabled` pref false → redirect to browser default (AC2); (f) SW cold-start → skeleton until SW ready (AC23); (g) `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` or `MSG_LIST_GROUPS` rejects → error state with Reload (AC16); (h) `MSG_NAVIGATE_TO_ITEM` rejects → toast or silent log (R2 resolves exact UX).<br>**C-11 message-ordering audit (R2 mandatory — non-vacuous)**: R2 D-5 MUST enumerate every SW write on the click-to-navigate path (e.g., recency tracking). For each write, confirm it is fire-and-forgot via `chrome.runtime.sendMessage(...).catch(() => {})` BEFORE any `chrome.tabs.update({active:true})` or navigation-committing call. Unlike B-035 (standalone window, vacuously satisfied), the new tab page IS torn down when navigation commits — any `await` after the focus-shift is dropped. This is a blocking R2 gate; R3 MUST NOT begin until R2 confirms ordering for every SW write on this path.<br>**R2 decision points**: D-1 render strategy — vanilla DOM in `newtab.js` vs importing sidepanel.js helpers (R1 recommends vanilla; importing the full 6 800-LOC sidepanel module into every new tab is a first-paint risk); D-2a disabled-pref redirect mechanism — `chrome.tabs.update` with browser-default URL vs `window.location.replace`; D-2b search submission — `chrome.search.query({text, disposition:'NEW_TAB'})` vs form-submit to search engine URL (R1 recommends API call; `search` permission already granted); D-3 grid layout — single-column list vs multi-column tiles (R1 defers to R2); D-4 fuzzy-filter source — import `shared/fuzzy.js` or inline duplicate (R1 recommends shared import, no duplication); D-5 C-11 SW-write enumeration on click-to-navigate path; D-6 storage-read pattern — `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` + `MSG_LIST_GROUPS` at boot, no polling; D-7 `MSG_STATE_CHANGED` subscription pattern.<br>**Out of scope (v1)**: (a) theme selection UI (B-037); (b) settings UI beyond B-039 toggle (B-089 owns settings panel); (c) bookmark creation or editing — read-only grid; (d) drag-reorder on the new tab page; (e) recently-visited or most-used ordering; (f) widgets, clock, or background image customisation; (g) Ctrl+T shortcut (browser-owned).<br>**Test coverage (AC for R5)**: new `tests/b036-newtab.test.js` — enabled/disabled pref boot branch, grid build from MSG_LIST_ITEMS + MSG_LIST_GROUPS, filter narrowing and `<mark>` highlight, click-to-navigate MSG_NAVIGATE_TO_ITEM dispatch (C-11 ordering verified), MSG_STATE_CHANGED subscription and live-state update, all C-9 empty states. Uses `chrome-mock.js` — no ad-hoc stubs.<br>**UAT plan scaffold**: `docs/UAT_B-036.md` (new file — [test-engineer] fills in R5; do NOT pre-fill). |
| B-037 | As a user, I want to select from at least twelve themes (a mix of dark and light variants inspired by popular IDE color schemes) and have the selection apply instantly across all open Tab Junkie surfaces, so that the extension fits my visual preferences. | P2 | M | done | 31 | B-001 ⚠️ **Theme preference persisted to storage; broadcast message to sync across instances** → Full tier | **Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — theme selection is fully reversible. Changing a theme writes a pref value only; no items, groups, bookmarks, or tabs are created, modified, or deleted. No confirmation dialog is required or applicable.<br><br>**AC1 — Theme catalog (13 themes).** The following themes are available by name and slug. 6 dark + 6 light + 1 auto-switch. Dark slugs: `dracula` (Dracula), `nord` (Nord), `one-dark` (One Dark), `monokai` (Monokai), `tomorrow-night` (Tomorrow Night), `atom-one-dark` (Atom One Dark). Light slugs: `github-light` (GitHub Light), `solarized-light` (Solarized Light), `tomorrow` (Tomorrow), `atom-one-light` (Atom One Light), `github-dark` (GitHub Dark — dark-base palette inspired by github.com dark theme), `solarized-dark` (Solarized Dark — dark-base palette; paired counterpart to Solarized Light). Auto: `system` (System Default — auto-switches with OS `prefers-color-scheme`; existing default behavior, preserved as-is). Total: 13 distinct slugs. The final slug list is a binding R2 decision point (D-1); R3 MUST NOT deviate from the R2-confirmed list. PASS = Settings page Theme section renders all 13 options; FAIL = missing option or extra unlisted option.<br><br>**AC2 — Theme picker UI.** The Settings page (`settings/settings.html`) Theme `<fieldset>` (section 4, currently showing `<p class="settings-section-placeholder">Theme selection coming in a future update.</p>`) is updated in-place: the placeholder `<p>` is removed and replaced with a `renderSelect` row (via `settings/settings-fields.js`) with `key: 'theme'`, `label: 'Theme'`, options mapping each AC1 slug to its display name, and `defaultValue: 'system'`. The `<fieldset class="settings-section"><legend class="settings-section-legend">Theme</legend>` wrapper is unchanged. PASS = Theme fieldset contains a labelled `<select id="settings-ctl-theme">` with 13 options; placeholder `<p>` is absent; `system` is selected by default on fresh install; FAIL = placeholder still present, select absent, wrong option count, or section structure changed.<br><br>**AC3 — Pref key + value set + storage migration (C-1).** `tj:prefs.theme` is extended from the current 3-value enum `'light' | 'dark' | 'system'` to the full 13-slug enum defined in AC1. Storage migration is mandatory: existing stored values `'light'` and `'dark'` written by any pre-B-037 install MUST be mapped on read to compatible theme slugs. Proposed migration: `'light'` → `'atom-one-light'`; `'dark'` → `'one-dark'`. R2 confirms the exact mapping (D-2) and whether a schema-version bump is required (D-5). Both `validatePrefsPatch` in `background/storage/preferences.js` and `isPreferences` in `background/storage/shapes.js` MUST be updated to accept all 13 slugs and reject any other string value. `DEFAULT_PREFERENCES.theme` remains `'system'`. PASS = reading a stored `'light'` returns the migration target slug; all 13 slugs pass `validatePrefsPatch`; an unknown slug throws `ERR_VALIDATION`; FAIL = migration absent, unknown slug accepted, or known slug rejected.<br><br>**AC4 — Live theme application.** Selecting a theme in the Settings page applies it immediately to the Settings page itself without a page reload. `document.documentElement.dataset.theme` is updated to the new slug. CSS `[data-theme="slug"]` selectors resolve the new palette immediately via CSS custom properties — color repaint only, no layout reflow. PASS = selecting a theme visually changes the Settings page within one animation frame (< 16 ms); no reload required; FAIL = theme does not change, or a reload is required.<br><br>**AC5 — Cross-surface broadcast.** When the user changes the theme, `MSG_SET_PREFERENCES` writes the new `theme` value; the SW then broadcasts `MSG_STATE_CHANGED` (scope: `prefs`) to all open surfaces via the existing infrastructure. Each receiving surface (sidepanel, newtab, standalone window, any additional Settings tab) re-applies `document.documentElement.dataset.theme` within 500 ms of the broadcast. PASS = sidepanel and Settings tab open simultaneously — changing theme in Settings causes sidepanel to repaint with new theme within 500 ms; FAIL = sidepanel stale after 500 ms or requires reload.<br><br>**AC6 — `theme-init.js` extended (FOUC guard).** All three copies (`sidepanel/theme-init.js`, `newtab/theme-init.js`, `settings/theme-init.js`) must handle all 13 slugs. The inline script reads the stored theme and sets `document.documentElement.dataset.theme` BEFORE the stylesheet loads. The existing fallback `|| 'light'` MUST change to `|| 'system'` to match `DEFAULT_PREFERENCES.theme`. For `theme === 'system'`, setting `data-theme="system"` is sufficient — the media-query CSS (AC8) resolves the actual palette. R2 decides the exact read pattern for Edge MV3 inline-script context: retain `sessionStorage` as a sync fast-path, or switch to a different approach (D-4 / C-8 check). If R2 selects CSS consolidation option A (AC7), this consolidation may include merging the three script copies into one shared file. PASS = loading any surface with a non-system stored theme shows the correct theme with zero FOUC; fallback produces `data-theme="system"` not `"light"`; FAIL = FOUC visible, wrong fallback, or script fails for any slug.<br><br>**AC7 — Theme-token CSS consolidation (R2 decides).** Current state: `[data-theme]` palette blocks are triplicated across `sidepanel/sidepanel.css`, `newtab/newtab.css`, and `settings/settings.css`. With 13 themes this would produce ~39 palette blocks across 3 files. R2 MUST choose between: (A) extract all `[data-theme="*"]` palette blocks to a new `shared/themes.css` imported by all three surfaces (preferred — single source of truth); or (B) add all 13 palette blocks to each per-surface CSS file (acceptable only with a documented blocking constraint against A). R3 follows R2's decision exactly; partial consolidation is not permitted. PASS = all 13 themes visually consistent across all three surfaces; if option A: no `[data-theme]` palette block in any per-surface CSS file; FAIL = any surface missing a theme, or palette blocks duplicated when option A was chosen.<br><br>**AC8 — System theme behavior preserved.** When `theme === 'system'`, the existing `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` and `@media (prefers-color-scheme: light)` blocks on `[data-theme="system"]` continue to auto-switch with the OS preference. The 12 named themes are explicit user choices and do NOT auto-switch — they display their fixed palette regardless of OS setting. PASS = with `theme = 'system'`, OS dark-mode toggle changes extension surfaces; with `theme = 'dracula'`, OS dark-mode toggle has no visual effect; FAIL = named theme auto-switches with OS, or system theme ignores OS.<br><br>**AC9 — Empty and error states (C-9 compliance).** (a) Fresh install (no stored theme): `DEFAULT_PREFERENCES.theme = 'system'` applies; surfaces auto-switch with OS; Settings picker shows "System Default". (b) Pref read failure: all surfaces fall back to `'system'`; theme picker shows "System Default". (c) Corrupt or unrecognised slug (e.g., a slug removed in a future downgrade): `isPreferences` rejects the partition as corrupt; `getPreferences()` returns `DEFAULT_PREFERENCES` via the existing safe-mode fallback; effective theme = `'system'`. (d) Legacy migration (pre-B-037 stored `'light'` or `'dark'`): mapped per AC3; no error state surfaced to user. (e) `theme-init.js` runs before prefs are available (cold load, first paint): fallback = `data-theme="system"`. PASS = all five paths produce a valid rendered surface with no console errors; FAIL = blank, console error, or wrong fallback for any state.<br><br>**AC10 — Performance.** (a) Theme picker change → all open Tab Junkie surfaces complete theme repaint within 500 ms of the select-change event. (b) Repaint MUST NOT trigger a layout reflow — only CSS custom-property updates on `<html>`; no DOM node additions or removals on theme change. (c) `theme-init.js` FOUC guard executes within the synchronous head-script window so no flash of wrong theme is visible. PASS = 500 ms broadcast + repaint budget met; DevTools Performance panel shows no layout reflow on theme change; no FOUC on surface load; FAIL = budget exceeded or reflow observed.<br><br>**AC11 — Accessibility.** (a) All 13 themes meet WCAG AA contrast (≥ 4.5:1 body text, ≥ 3:1 large text and non-text controls). R2 specifies palette values; [security-reviewer] and [qa-reviewer] verify at R4. (b) Focus ring is visible at ≥ 3:1 contrast against the surrounding surface color in all 13 themes. (c) `prefers-reduced-motion` does not interact with theme switching — change is instantaneous, no CSS color-transition added. (d) Theme `<select>` has a visible label association (`<label for="settings-ctl-theme">`). PASS = no WCAG AA contrast failures for body text or focus rings in any theme; select has valid label; FAIL = any theme fails AA body-text contrast, focus ring invisible, or label missing.<br><br>**AC12 — No new manifest permissions.** Zero. Theme selection operates entirely within the existing pref storage + broadcast infrastructure. `manifest.json` `permissions` array MUST remain unchanged. R2 confirms (C-6). PASS = `git diff manifest.json` returns empty (version bump excepted); FAIL = any new permission or other manifest entry added.<br><br>**AC13 — Test coverage (≥ 8 new tests).** New `tests/b037-themes.test.js` covers at minimum: (1) theme catalog — `renderSelect` options list matches all 13 AC1 slugs exactly; (2) pref save — `MSG_SET_PREFERENCES({theme: 'dracula'})` writes correct value and passes `validatePrefsPatch`; (3) pref load — `getPreferences()` returns stored theme slug; (4) broadcast sync — `MSG_STATE_CHANGED` (scope: `prefs`) causes `document.documentElement.dataset.theme` to update on the receiving surface; (5) storage migration — stored `'light'` value returns the AC3 migration-target slug; (6) storage migration — stored `'dark'` value returns the AC3 migration-target slug; (7) fallback on corrupt value — an invalid slug in storage causes `getPreferences()` to return `'system'`; (8) `validatePrefsPatch` — passing an unknown slug throws `ERR_VALIDATION`. Additional tests for FOUC fallback, `theme-init.js` sessionStorage path, and CSS consolidation (if option A, see AC14) are encouraged. PASS = all ≥ 8 new tests green + zero regressions in the full existing suite; FAIL = fewer than 8 new tests or any regression.<br><br>**AC14 — CSS consolidation tests (if R2 picks option A).** If R2 selects strategy A (`shared/themes.css`), the suite MUST include ≥ 2 additional tests: (1) `shared/themes.css` is imported or `<link>`ed by all three surface HTML files (`sidepanel/sidepanel.html`, `newtab/newtab.html`, `settings/settings.html`); (2) no `[data-theme=` palette block appears in any per-surface CSS file after extraction. PASS = both checks green; FAIL = any surface missing the import, or palette duplication found in per-surface files.<br><br>**R2 pre-conditions (forward to [solution-architect])**: D-1 final theme slug list (confirm or adjust AC1 list); D-2 storage migration mapping (`'light'` → ?, `'dark'` → ?); D-3 theme-token CSS consolidation strategy (option A: `shared/themes.css` vs option B: per-surface triplication — preferred is A); D-4 `theme-init.js` FOUC-guard pattern for Edge MV3 inline-script context (`sessionStorage` sync vs alternative — C-8 feasibility check); D-5 storage schema version bump decision (C-1: does extending the theme enum require a version bump, or is the OPTIONAL-field merge pattern from B-060/B-092 sufficient?); D-6 `renderSelect` ergonomics for 13 options (dropdown `<select>` is the default; R2 may propose a richer control type if warranted, noting `settings-fields.js` would need extension); D-7 C-12 manifest mutability audit (expected PASS — no manifest changes); D-8 stale-SW release-note flag (extending `validatePrefsPatch` enum is a validator change; per B-094 CLAUDE.md C-1 note, release notes MUST mention "extension reload required after update").<br><br>**Out of scope.** (a) Custom user-defined themes. (b) Theme preview swatches in the picker. (c) Per-surface independent theme selection — one global theme. (d) Animated theme transitions — instant swap only. (e) Theme export/import. (f) Any new manifest permissions. |

It contains one `<select>` rendered via B-089's `renderSelect` helper with `key: 'tj.prefs.viewMode'`, `label: 'Open Tab Junkie as'`, `options: [{value:'sidepanel', label:'Side Panel'}, {value:'standalone', label:'Standalone Window'}]`, `defaultValue: 'sidepanel'`. PASS = side panel opens on toolbar click; FAIL = standalone window opens or no action.<br>**AC6 — Behaviour: pref = `standalone`.** Toolbar icon click routes to `openOrFocusStandaloneWindow()` (the B-035 helper). If a standalone window is already open, the window is focused (B-035 D-3). If no window is open, a new one is created. PASS = standalone opens/focuses on toolbar click; FAIL = side panel opens, or no action.<br>**AC7 — Routing mechanism (R2 decision point D-1).** Two candidate patterns for R2 to resolve: (a) Clear `action.default_popup` dynamically via `chrome.action.setPopup({popup: ''})` on pref change and add a `chrome.action.onClicked` listener in the SW that reads the pref and dispatches; (b) Keep the popup but have the popup's JS read the pref and call `chrome.sidePanel.open` or `openOrFocusStandaloneWindow` before immediately closing itself. R2 MUST confirm whether `chrome.action.setPopup` is available in MV3 SW context, whether clearing the popup causes it to stop intercepting clicks (expected: yes), and whether the `chrome.action.onClicked` listener fires reliably in Edge (C-8 check). R2 verdict is normative for R3.<br>**AC8 — `_execute_action` not affected.** Alt+J always opens `popup/popup.html` (the quick-search popup). No pref reading. No re-routing. PASS = Alt+J opens quick-search popup with any pref value; FAIL = opens standalone or side panel.<br>**AC9 — Corrupt pref value.** If `tj.prefs.viewMode` contains an unrecognised value (e.g., `'panel'`, `null`, `undefined`, a number), the SW routing and the Settings dialog MUST both fall back to `'sidepanel'`. The Settings dialog corrects the stored value to `'sidepanel'` on next save. PASS = fallback to sidepanel routing; no uncaught error; FAIL = crash or standalone route on corrupt value.<br>**AC10 — Standalone already open + toolbar click (pref = `standalone`).** `openOrFocusStandaloneWindow()` called → existing window is focused per B-035 D-3. PASS = window count stays at 1, existing window focused; FAIL = new window opened.<br>**AC11 — Settings select reflects stored value.** If the user has set the pref to `standalone` and reopens the Settings dialog, the select shows "Standalone Window" (not the default "Side Panel"). PASS = timing gate met; FAIL = > 500 ms observed.<br>**AC14 — Perf: toolbar-click-to-window-open (pref = `standalone`).** From toolbar-icon click to standalone window becoming visible < 300 ms P95 (5 measurements). PASS = all 5 < 300 ms; FAIL = any ≥ 300 ms.<br>**AC15 — No new manifest permissions.** This feature MUST NOT add any entry to `manifest.json` `permissions` or `host_permissions`. PASS = `git diff manifest.json` permissions array unchanged; FAIL = any permission added.<br>**AC16 — No new storage partitions.** `tj.prefs.viewMode` is stored as a field inside the existing `tj:prefs` partition (managed by `MSG_SET_PREFERENCES`). PASS = all new tests green + zero regressions in full suite; FAIL = any regression or missing case.<br><br>**C-9 empty-state enumeration.** (a) Fresh install / no key: `tj.prefs.viewMode` absent → default `sidepanel` route. (b) Corrupt value: AC9 — fall back to `sidepanel`. (c) `standalone` pref + no open window: creates new window (B-035 AC2). (d) `standalone` pref + window already open: focuses existing (AC10). (e) `sidepanel` pref + standalone open: side panel still opens (AC5 coexistence).
