(4) Change pref back to "Side Panel" → toolbar click → verify side panel opens. (5) Alt+J → verify quick-search popup opens regardless of pref. (6) Set corrupt storage value → toolbar click → verify fallback to side panel, no error. (7) Reopen Settings dialog after setting `standalone` → verify select shows "Standalone Window". |
| B-039 | As a user, I want to toggle the new tab page override on or off in Settings, so that I have control over whether Tab Junkie replaces my new tab. | P3 | XS | dropped | 29 | B-036 (in-sprint), B-089 (in-sprint) | **DROPPED at S29 sprint close** — Manifest V3 constraint: `chrome_url_overrides.newtab` cannot be removed at runtime, so the OFF state could not deliver browser-default new tab behavior (only `about:blank` or a custom disabled-state page). Product-owner decision: ship newtab always-on; user uninstalls Tab Junkie to restore browser default. Code shipped in S29 reverted in same sprint. Pref key `newTabOverride` retained in `DEFAULT_PREFERENCES` for backward compat.<br><br>**Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — reversible toggle; no data is deleted or permanently altered in either state.<br><br>**AC1 — Control placement**: A toggle row labelled "Replace new tab page with Tab Junkie" appears in the Settings dialog under a "New tab page" section heading, rendered via B-089's `renderToggle` helper.<br>**AC2 — Default state**: The toggle defaults to OFF (`tj.prefs.newTabOverride = false`) on first install and on any cold-start where the key is absent from storage.<br>**AC3 — Persistence**: Toggling saves `tj.prefs.newTabOverride` (boolean) to `tj:prefs` via `MSG_SET_PREFERENCES`. The pref is durable across browser restarts.<br>**AC4 — Pref-check at newtab boot (ON path)**: When `newtab/newtab.js` boots and `tj.prefs.newTabOverride === true`, it proceeds to render the full B-036 bookmark grid.<br>**AC5 — Pref-check at newtab boot (OFF path / disabled fallback)**: When `newtab.js` boots and `tj.prefs.newTabOverride === false` (or key absent), it does NOT render the grid. Instead it renders a minimal disabled-state page: a Tab Junkie wordmark, a one-line message ("New tab override is off"), and a CTA button ("Enable in Settings") that opens the sidepanel settings dialog. No redirect to `about:blank` (blocked by Chrome); no attempt to approximate browser default search (out of scope).<br>**AC6 — Boot pref-check latency**: The pref read + branch decision in `newtab.js` completes in < 50 ms from page load (P95, cold storage read).<br>**AC7 — Pref broadcast**: Saving the toggle fires `MSG_STATE_CHANGED` with `scope: "prefs"` to all extension contexts. Any already-open new-tab pages receive the message but are NOT required to auto-re-render; they remain in their current render state until the user manually refreshes. This stale-until-refresh behavior MUST be documented in the disabled-state page copy or a tooltip.<br>**AC8 — Pref save + broadcast latency**: End-to-end time from toggle flip to `MSG_STATE_CHANGED` delivery < 500 ms.<br>**AC9 — Empty / first-install state (OFF)**: A user who has never touched the toggle opens a new tab and sees the disabled-state page (AC5). No console errors, no blank white flash.<br>**AC10 — Toggle ON then open new tab**: After enabling the toggle, the next new tab opened renders the full B-036 grid. No reload of existing tabs is required.<br>**AC11 — Toggle OFF mid-session**: After disabling the toggle, already-open new-tab pages are NOT forcibly re-rendered. Newly opened new tabs show the disabled-state page.<br>**AC12 — Keyboard accessibility**: The toggle is focusable and operable via Space/Enter. The "Enable in Settings" CTA on the disabled page is keyboard-reachable and activatable.<br>**AC13 — No manifest changes**: `chrome_url_overrides.newtab` remains declared at all times. The toggle controls render behavior only — it does NOT add or remove the manifest override.<br>**AC14 — Out of scope**: Web search input behavior (B-036 owns), any manifest permission additions, redirect to `about:blank` or browser-default search approximation.<br><br>**R2 Decision Points**: D-1 Disabled-state fallback UX (recommendation: option b — minimal disabled page with CTA; AC5 above); D-2 Mid-session new-tab auto-refresh (recommendation: stale-until-refresh; AC7 above); D-3 First-install default confirmed OFF (AC2 above).<br><br>**Test file**: ~~`tests/b039-newtab-toggle.test.js`~~ DELETED at S29 close (B-039 dropped).<br><br>**Smoke UAT** (HISTORICAL — superseded by drop): (1) Fresh install → open new tab → verify disabled page renders, no errors. (2) Enable toggle in Settings → open new tab → verify B-036 grid renders. (3) Disable toggle → open new tab → verify disabled page returns. (4) Toggle ON → toggle OFF → existing open new tab stays in previous render state (stale). (5) Keyboard: tab to toggle, press Space, confirm state changes. |
| B-040 | As a user, I want an optional setting that auto-collapses sub-groups when their parent group is collapsed, so that collapsing a parent also tidies up its children without requiring individual sub-group management. | P3 | XS | done | 29 | B-007 ✅, B-008 ✅, B-089 (in-sprint) | **Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — collapsing a group is a fully reversible UI action; no items, groups, or bookmarks are deleted or mutated. Sub-group collapse state is independently recoverable by expanding any sub-group header.<br>**AC1 — Settings control placement.** The Settings dialog (B-089, section "Groups") gains a toggle row rendered via `renderToggle({ key: 'autoCollapseSubgroups', label: 'Auto-collapse sub-groups when parent collapses', defaultValue: false })`. Toggle sits within the "Groups" section heading of `#settings-dialog`. PASS = toggle renders in correct section with correct label; FAIL = absent, wrong label, or wrong section.<br>**AC2 — Default OFF.** On fresh install and on first dialog open when the key is absent from `tj:prefs`, the toggle is in the OFF position (`false`). Existing B-008 per-sub-group collapse independence is the default behavior. PASS = toggle visually off + storage key absent or `false`; FAIL = toggle defaults on.<br>**AC3 — Preference persistence.** Toggling the control dispatches `MSG_SET_PREFERENCES` with patch `{ autoCollapseSubgroups: <boolean> }`. On success the control holds its new state; on error it reverts with an inline "Could not save. Try again." message (per B-089 AC4 pattern). PASS = `tj:prefs` reflects the new value within 500 ms of toggle; FAIL = write lost or UI desync.<br>**AC4 — Cascade behavior when ON (one-way collapse only).** When `autoCollapseSubgroups === true` and the user clicks the collapse affordance on a top-level group (`parentId === null` with at least one child group), the `toggleGroup` handler in `sidepanel/sidepanel.js` MUST additionally collapse every child group of that parent. Child collapse is one-way: expanding the parent does NOT auto-expand sub-groups. This keeps sub-group state predictable and avoids unwanted disclosure of previously-collapsed children. PASS = parent collapsed → all child group sections hidden + their headers show collapsed icon; expand parent → children remain collapsed; FAIL = children expand on parent expand OR children not collapsed on parent collapse.<br>**AC5 — Cascade write atomicity.** The cascade collapse MUST be written as a series of individual `MSG_UPDATE_GROUP { id: childId, patch: { collapsed: true } }` calls fired concurrently via `Promise.all` (no `MSG_BULK_UPDATE_GROUPS` exists in the message contract). All child-collapse writes and the parent-collapse write MUST resolve before `MSG_STATE_CHANGED` is considered complete. R2 confirms the exact ordering and error-handling strategy. PASS = all child `collapsed: true` persisted to storage atomically within the performance window; FAIL = partial write leaves some children un-collapsed in storage.<br>**AC6 — Standard broadcast.** After the cascade write resolves, the existing `MSG_STATE_CHANGED` broadcast (already triggered by each `MSG_UPDATE_GROUP` response) propagates to all open surfaces. No new message type is required. PASS = a second open sidepanel reflects all child groups collapsed without manual reload; FAIL = desync between surfaces.<br>**AC7 — Behavior when OFF.** When `autoCollapseSubgroups === false` (default), `toggleGroup` behavior is unchanged from the existing B-008 implementation. Child group collapse state is fully independent of the parent. PASS = collapsing parent with pref OFF leaves child group sections in their current state; FAIL = cascade fires when pref is OFF.<br>**AC8 — Empty-state enumeration (C-9 compliance).** (a) Parent group with zero child groups — cascade no-op; `Promise.all([])` resolves immediately, no writes dispatched. (b) Parent already fully collapsed when pref is toggled ON mid-session — no retroactive cascade; pref applies only on the NEXT explicit parent-collapse gesture. (c) All child sub-groups already individually collapsed — cascade dispatches `collapsed: true` patches; no-op writes are acceptable (storage value unchanged). (d) Pref OFF → ON mid-session while some sub-groups are expanded — no automatic cascade; state updates only on next user-initiated parent collapse. PASS = all four enumerated paths produce no console errors and correct UI; FAIL = crash, unexpected cascade, or retroactive state change.<br>**AC9 — Depth cap.** Cascade is capped at depth 1 (direct children only), consistent with B-007's depth-1 sub-group cap. No recursive walk of grandchildren is possible given the architecture. PASS = only immediate children collapse; FAIL = attempt to recurse causes error or uncaught exception.<br>**AC10 — Performance.** Cascade write + broadcast for a parent with up to 50 child groups completes within 500 ms. PASS = timed from parent-collapse click to last `MSG_STATE_CHANGED` delivery ≤ 500 ms; FAIL = exceeds threshold.<br>**AC11 — Manifest permissions.** Zero new `manifest.json` permissions. All work is sidepanel UI + existing `MSG_UPDATE_GROUP` + `MSG_SET_PREFERENCES` + `MSG_GET_PREFERENCES` contracts. PASS = `git diff manifest.json` empty (version bump excepted); FAIL = any permission added.<br>**AC12 — Out of scope (explicit).** (a) Keyboard shortcut for cascade-collapse. (b) Expansion cascade (expand parent auto-expands sub-groups) — intentionally excluded per one-way design. (c) Multi-level depth cascade (only depth-1 children per B-007 cap). (d) Retroactive cascade on toggle-ON (applies only to future gestures). (e) New storage schema, message types, or permissions.<br>**AC13 — Test coverage.** New `tests/b040-auto-collapse-subgroups.test.js` covering: (1) pref save — `MSG_SET_PREFERENCES` dispatched with correct payload; (2) cascade ON — parent collapse with pref ON fires `MSG_UPDATE_GROUP` for each child; (3) cascade OFF — parent collapse with pref OFF fires zero child `MSG_UPDATE_GROUP` calls; (4) zero-children parent — `Promise.all([])` no-ops cleanly; (5) one-way only — parent expand with pref ON does NOT fire child `MSG_UPDATE_GROUP`. Net new: ≥ 5 passing tests. PASS = all new tests green + zero regressions in existing suite; FAIL = regression or fewer than 5 net new tests.<br>**AC14 — Smoke UAT plan.** (a) Load unpacked extension. (b) Create a parent group with 2 sub-groups, leave sub-groups expanded. (c) Enable "Auto-collapse sub-groups when parent collapses" in Settings. (d) Collapse the parent → verify both sub-groups collapse. (e) Expand the parent → verify sub-groups remain collapsed. (f) Disable pref. (g) Expand both sub-groups. (h) Collapse the parent → verify sub-groups stay expanded. PASS = all 8 steps produce expected state; FAIL = any deviation. |
| B-041 | As a user, I want a "Sync Tab Order" action in Settings that arranges my browser's native tab groups and tab order to match Tab Junkie's grouping and ordering (with confirmation before executing), so that my browser tabs and my Tab Junkie organisation stay aligned on demand. | P2 | L | backlog | | B-006, B-014 ⚠️ **Requires `tabGroups` manifest permission; new message contract for sync action** → Full tier | Triggering "Sync Tab Order" shows a confirmation dialog describing the action before execution.<br>After confirmation, native Chrome tab groups are created (or updated) matching Tab Junkie group names and colors.<br>Sub-groups are flattened into their parent group during sync (browser tab groups do not support nesting).<br>Tab Junkie's semantic colors are mapped to the nearest Chrome tab-group color.<br>A success or failure notification is shown after the sync completes.<br>Sync operates only on the currently active window. |
| B-042 | As a user, I want to export my bookmarks to a standard Netscape HTML file with groups as nested folders, so that I can import my data into any major browser or bookmark manager. | P1 | M | done | 17 | B-001, B-006, B-043 (shared export infra: download-trigger helper + filename-date util) | **AC1 — Entry point:** An "Export to HTML" action is reachable from the sidepanel header overflow menu (kebab/more button adjacent to `#add-bookmark-btn`). The action is a native `<button>` or `role="menuitem"`, focusable via Tab, and activates on Enter/Space. **PASS:** keyboard-only user can open the menu and trigger export without mouse. **FAIL:** action is mouse-only, not focusable, or skipped by Tab order.<br>**AC2 — File format:** Output is a valid Netscape Bookmark File Format 1 document beginning with `<!DOCTYPE NETSCAPE-Bookmark-file-1>`, the standard comment block, `<TITLE>Bookmarks</TITLE>`, `<H1>Bookmarks Menu</H1>`, and a root `<DL><p>...</DL><p>` wrapper. **PASS:** Chrome's `chrome://bookmarks` Import re-ingests the file with no parse errors; Firefox Library > Import HTML accepts it. **FAIL:** any browser import rejects the file or silently drops nodes.<br>**AC3 — Group hierarchy:** Each top-level group renders as a top-level `<DT><H3>` folder inside the root `<DL>`; sub-groups render as nested `<H3>` folders inside their parent's `<DL>`; the virtual `__ungrouped__` partition renders as a top-level folder literally named `Ungrouped`; within every folder, items and child groups are emitted in ascending `sortOrder`. **PASS:** re-imported tree mirrors Tab Junkie's group tree 1:1 (name, nesting, order). **FAIL:** folders flattened, reordered, or Ungrouped omitted.<br>**AC4 — Item fields:** Each bookmark is `<DT><A HREF="{url}" ADD_DATE="{createdAt}" LAST_MODIFIED="{updatedAt}">{title}</A>` where `ADD_DATE` / `LAST_MODIFIED` are unix-epoch **seconds** (integer). If a cached favicon URL exists on the item, include `ICON="{faviconUrl}"`; if absent, omit the attribute entirely (do not emit empty `ICON=""`). **PASS:** re-import preserves title, URL, and creation date; items with no favicon omit `ICON`. **FAIL:** timestamps in milliseconds, missing attributes, or empty `ICON=""`.<br>**AC5 — Filename:** Default download filename is `tab-junkie-bookmarks-YYYY-MM-DD.html` (local date). The browser's native download dialog allows rename. **PASS:** filename on today's export matches the date pattern; user can rename via the save dialog. **FAIL:** hard-coded name, UTC/local mismatch, or no save dialog.<br>**AC6 — Trigger:** A single click / Enter on the Export action generates the file and initiates download. Use `chrome.downloads.download` **only if** the `downloads` permission is already justified; otherwise generate a `Blob` + `URL.createObjectURL` + synthetic `<a download>` click and revoke the object URL after the click. **PASS:** one activation produces exactly one download. **FAIL:** multiple dialogs, duplicate downloads, or leaked blob URLs.<br>**AC7 — Success feedback:** After the download is initiated, a non-blocking toast appears reading `Exported {N} bookmarks across {M} groups` (exact counts; exclude the Ungrouped folder from `M` only if it contained zero items). Toast auto-dismisses after ~4s and is dismissible by click/Escape. **PASS:** counts match the exported file; toast is announced to screen readers (`role="status"` or `aria-live="polite"`). **FAIL:** silent success, wrong counts, or toast not accessible.<br>**AC8 — Failure feedback:** If the builder throws, storage read fails, or the download API rejects, a dismissible error toast appears with a brief human reason (e.g., `Export failed: unable to read bookmarks`). Error details are `console.warn`-logged (no PII — titles/URLs are never logged). **PASS:** failure produces a visible error toast and no partial/empty file lands on disk. **FAIL:** silent failure, unhandled promise rejection, or an empty/truncated file written.<br>**AC9 — Performance:** On a seeded 1,000-item / 100-group collection, elapsed time from Export activation to `chrome.downloads.onCreated` (or anchor click for fallback) is **< 500ms** P95 on a dev-class machine. **PASS:** measured via a test-mode timer logged to the console; median of 5 runs ≤ 500ms. **FAIL:** exceeds 500ms on the seeded corpus.<br>**AC10 — XSS safety:** Every emitted title and URL is HTML-entity-encoded for text nodes (`&`, `<`, `>`) and attribute-encoded for attribute values (additionally `"`). A probe item with title `</A><script>alert(1)</script>` and URL `javascript:alert(1)` must round-trip through export → Chrome re-import as **literal text** in the title and a literal (non-executing) URL string; no script ever executes during export render or re-import. **PASS:** re-imported title reads exactly `</A><script>alert(1)</script>`; no alert dialog fires. **FAIL:** any injection executes, or output HTML parses the payload as real tags.<br>**AC11 — Privacy:** The exported file contains ONLY user-saved data (items, groups, titles, URLs, favicon URLs, timestamps, sortOrder). It MUST NOT contain: telemetry fields, session tokens, device/profile IDs, live-state flags (`live`, `active`, `drifted`, `audible`), tab IDs, window IDs, or claim records. **PASS:** a diff of exported content vs item/group storage shapes shows zero leakage of live-state or identity fields. **FAIL:** any live-state, tabId, windowId, or profile data appears in the file.<br>**AC12 — Manifest permission boundary:** If R2 determines `chrome.downloads.download` is required, the `downloads` permission must be added to `manifest.json` with a one-line justification comment; otherwise, the `<a download>` Blob fallback is used and `manifest.json` is unchanged. Either path is acceptable — the decision is recorded in SOLUTION_DESIGN at R2. **PASS:** manifest change (if any) is justified in R2 and flagged for [security-reviewer]. **FAIL:** new permission added without justification, or fallback path leaks blob URLs.<br>**AC13 — Out of scope:** (a) partial/selective export (whole collection only); (b) cloud upload or network transmission of the file; (c) encryption or password-protection of the export; (d) exporting live state, drift flags, claims, or tab/window IDs; (e) re-import via this item (HTML import is B-044); (f) JSON format (owned by B-043); (g) scheduled / automatic exports. |
| B-043 | As a user, I want to export a complete JSON backup of all my bookmarks, groups, colors, metadata, and timestamps (including a schema version field), so that I can restore or migrate my data without any loss. | P1 | M | done | 17 | B-001, B-006, B-042 | **AC1 — Format:** Output is UTF-8 encoded JSON with 2-space indentation and a trailing newline; `JSON.parse()` on the file content succeeds without error; MIME type on download is `application/json`.<br>**AC2 — Root shape:** The root object is exactly `{ schemaVersion: <int>, exportedAt: <ISO 8601 string>, items: Item[], groups: Group[], preferences?: { ... } }`. Excluded partitions: `tj:drift`, `tj:floatingGroups`, and any runtime/live state (open-tab claims, focus state, search state). `preferences` is present only when `tj:prefs` has been written by the user; otherwise the key is omitted.<br>**AC3 — schemaVersion:** `schemaVersion` is an integer equal to `KNOWN_VERSION` from `background/storage/migration.js` at export time (currently `1`). The value is read dynamically — hardcoding is a FAIL. Future B-045 import MUST validate `schemaVersion <= importer's KNOWN_VERSION` and reject files with an unrecognized version using a user-visible error.<br>**AC4 — Item shape:** Every exported item includes every persisted field as stored: `id` (ULID string), `title` (string), `url` (normalized string), `groupId` (string or `null`), `sortOrder` (finite number), `createdAt` (epoch ms int), `updatedAt` (epoch ms int), plus `lastAccessedAt` (epoch ms int) when present on the stored record. Runtime enrichments (drift status, live-tab claims, focus, match highlights) are excluded. Unknown / unexpected fields present on the stored record are passed through verbatim to preserve forward-compat for future schema additions.<br>**AC5 — Group shape:** Every exported group includes every persisted field as stored: `id`, `name`, `color`, `parentId` (string or `null`), `sortOrder`, `collapsed` (bool), `createdAt`, `updatedAt`. Transient fields such as the `warning: 'DUPLICATE_NAME'` return-value flag are excluded (they are never persisted and must not appear in the file).<br>**AC6 — Deterministic ordering:** `items` array is sorted by `(groupId ASC with null first, sortOrder ASC, id ASC)`; `groups` array is sorted by `(parentId ASC with null first, sortOrder ASC, id ASC)`. Two exports of identical storage state produce byte-identical files (modulo `exportedAt`). The PASS condition is a unit test that exports twice, strips `exportedAt`, and asserts byte equality.<br>**AC7 — Round-trip invariant:** When B-045 imports a file produced by B-043 on the same `schemaVersion`, the resulting `tj:items` and `tj:groups` partitions are bit-equivalent to the pre-export partitions: every `id`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`, `sortOrder`, `parentId`, `groupId`, and `collapsed` value is preserved exactly. No timestamp is re-stamped, no ID is regenerated. This AC is tested via a round-trip fixture in R5.<br>**AC8 — Filename:** Default download filename is `tab-junkie-backup-YYYY-MM-DD.json` where the date is the local-time date at export. Collisions are resolved by the browser's standard download-disambiguation (`(1)`, `(2)`, …) — we do not handle them ourselves.<br>**AC9 — Trigger:** Export is invoked via the same Settings/overflow-menu surface as B-042 HTML export, using the same confirmation-less one-click flow. Exact entry-point copy and placement are shared between B-042 and B-043 (e.g., submenu "Export → HTML backup / JSON backup").<br>**AC10 — Success / failure feedback:** On success a toast appears matching B-042's copy pattern (e.g., "Backup exported: <filename>"). On failure (serialization error, storage read error, download API error) a toast appears with the same failure-copy pattern as B-042 and the file is not created. No `console.log` / `console.error` in production builds per code-quality rules — errors route through the existing error surface.<br>**AC11 — Performance:** Export of a 1,000-item / 100-group collection completes in under 500 ms wall-clock measured from trigger to download-prompt, in an Edge/Chromium profile on reference hardware (asserted in R5 automated test using `performance.now()` on the chrome-mock fixture).<br>**AC12 — Privacy:** Zero telemetry. No network calls. No logging of item titles or URLs. Verified by [security-reviewer] in R4 — grep for `fetch`, `XMLHttpRequest`, `navigator.sendBeacon`, and any PII `console.*` in the diff must return zero hits.<br>**AC13 — No new permissions:** No addition to `manifest.json` permissions or `host_permissions`. Shares verdict with B-042 (both use `chrome.downloads` if already granted, or an anchor+object-URL fallback — decision deferred to R2 and MUST be resolved once for both items).<br>**Out of scope:** Encryption, cloud upload, per-group / filtered export, exporting `tj:drift` or `tj:floatingGroups` or live tab claims, importing (B-045 owns this). A "pretty-print off" mode is also out of scope — 2-space indent is the only supported format.<br>**Design note — authoritative schema:** The JSON shape defined here IS the future B-045 import contract. [solution-architect] must document it in `docs/SOLUTION_DESIGN.md` as a versioned spec so B-045 can be built against a frozen contract without re-negotiation. |
| B-044 | As a user, I want to import a standard Netscape HTML bookmarks file (from any major browser), with a preview of counts and a warning that import replaces all existing data, so that I can migrate my existing bookmarks into Tab Junkie. | P1 | M | done | 18 | B-003, B-005, B-006, B-042 (reciprocal format spec §32.6), B-058 (scheme allowlist), B-060 (duplicate policy default — skip) | **AC1 — Entry point:** An "Import from HTML" action lives in the same sidepanel header overflow menu as B-042's Export action (kebab adjacent to `#add-bookmark-btn`). Native `<button>` / `role="menuitem"`, focusable via Tab, activates on Enter/Space. PASS = keyboard-only user can trigger import without mouse. FAIL = mouse-only, missing, or not in the overflow menu.<br>**AC2 — File-picker filter:** Activating the action opens a programmatic `<input type="file">` click with `accept=".html,.htm,text/html"`. Any file whose name does NOT end in `.html` or `.htm` (case-insensitive) is rejected BEFORE parse with an inline toast "Select an .html or .htm file". PASS = `.json` / `.txt` / extension-less rejected pre-parse; `.HTML` and `.HTM` accepted. FAIL = non-HTML file reaches parser, or `.HTM` variants are rejected.<br>**AC3 — Parse: Netscape format recognition:** Parser accepts Netscape Bookmark File Format 1 identified by `<!DOCTYPE NETSCAPE-Bookmark-file-1>` (case-insensitive, leading whitespace tolerated) followed by at least one `<DL>` block. Walks nested `<DL>` / `<DT>` / `<H3>` (folders) / `<A>` (bookmarks) using `DOMParser` with `text/html` — NEVER `innerHTML` injection, NEVER a script-capable parser. PASS = exports from Chrome `chrome://bookmarks`, Firefox Library Export, Edge Favorites Export, and Safari (Bookmarks.html) all parse without error. FAIL = missing DOCTYPE produces a visible error toast "Not a valid Netscape bookmarks file" with zero state mutation.<br>**AC4 — Pre-commit count preview dialog:** After parse (BEFORE any storage write), a modal (`role="dialog"`, `aria-modal="true"`) appears: `"Import {N} bookmarks across {M} folders from {filename}. This will REPLACE all existing bookmarks and groups. Continue?"` where `N` = valid `<A>` entries and `M` = distinct folders (including `Ungrouped` IF any top-level items land there). REPLACE is visually emphasized (bold + warning color). Dialog also shows `"{K} malformed entries will be skipped"` when `K > 0` and `"{D} items have duplicate URLs — duplicates will be skipped"` per B-060. PASS = exact N/M/K/D match parsed file; REPLACE emphasized. FAIL = dialog absent, counts wrong, or storage written before confirmation.<br>**AC5 — Two-button confirm, Cancel default-focused:** Exactly two buttons: `Cancel` (default focus on open) and `Replace all` (destructive-styled, matches B-062 dark-theme contrast). Escape and backdrop click both invoke Cancel. Cancel writes nothing; only `Replace all` commits. PASS = Cancel has default focus, Escape cancels, backdrop click cancels, no storage mutation on any of those paths. FAIL = Replace is default-focused, Escape commits, or backdrop click commits.<br>**AC6 — Folder flattening to depth 1:** Tab Junkie group schema caps depth at 1 (see `background/storage/groups.js::assertDepthAndCycle`). Folders at depth ≥ 2 flatten by concatenating ancestor names with ` / ` (e.g., `Work / Projects / 2026` → depth-1 sub-group `Projects / 2026` whose parent is `Work`). Top-level folders → top-level groups (`parentId=null`); depth-1 folders → sub-groups (`parentId=<top-level id>`); no items are dropped during flattening. PASS = a 3-level path with 5 items yields a depth-1 group with all 5 items; total item count preserved. FAIL = items dropped, depth-2+ group created, or ` / ` separator missing.<br>**AC7 — `__ungrouped__` sentinel for top-level items:** Any `<A>` whose immediate parent is the root `<DL>` (not nested in any `<H3>`) imports with `groupId = null` — the `__ungrouped__` sentinel (`shared/group-picker-core.js:55`). No synthetic "Ungrouped" group record is created in `tj:groups`. PASS = top-level `<A>` entries land with `groupId=null` and render in the Ungrouped pinned section. FAIL = a real group named "Ungrouped" appears in `tj:groups`, or top-level items are assigned to an arbitrary group.<br>**AC8 — Deterministic group color:** Each imported group receives a color from `GROUP_COLORS` (`shared/constants.js`) via a stable hash of the group's final display name (djb2 on name, mod `GROUP_COLORS.length`). Two imports of the same file produce identical color assignments. Color always validates against `assertValidColor`. PASS = identical file imports yield byte-identical color assignments; a group named "Work" always gets the same palette entry across runs. FAIL = colors randomized, invalid palette entry used, or identical names receive different colors across runs.<br>**AC9 — Timestamp conversion (`ADD_DATE` / `LAST_MODIFIED`):** Per the Netscape spec (reciprocal to B-042 AC4), these attrs are unix-epoch **seconds**. Parser converts to epoch-ms by multiplying by 1000 before storage write. Missing, non-numeric, or out-of-range (< 0 or > year 2100 seconds) values fall back to `Date.now()` — NOT a malformed-entry skip. PASS = source `ADD_DATE="1700000000"` stores as `createdAt === 1700000000000`. FAIL = stored value in seconds (silent order-of-magnitude bug) or missing timestamp causes a skip.<br>**AC10 — Malformed-entry skip policy:** An entry is "malformed" (skipped) if ANY of: (a) `<A>` has no `HREF`, (b) `HREF` fails `normalizeUrl({ forStorage: true })` (e.g., `javascript:` / `data:` / post-B-058 disallowed scheme), (c) title is empty/whitespace AND URL-derived fallback fails, (d) title > `MAX_TITLE` after trim is silently truncated (NOT skipped — only a validation throw skips). Valid entries in the same file still import. Skip count `K` surfaces in AC4 preview and AC13 summary. PASS = 100 valid + 3 malformed imports 100 and reports `K=3`. FAIL = one malformed entry aborts the import, or malformed entries silently land in storage.<br>**AC11 — Duplicate-URL default = skip (B-060):** Because B-044 REPLACES all existing data, "duplicate" here means duplicates WITHIN the file (two `<A>` normalizing to the same URL). Per B-060 default-skip, only the FIRST occurrence (document order) imports; subsequent occurrences count toward `D` and are skipped. B-060's opt-in "Import duplicates anyway" checkbox is OUT OF SCOPE for B-044 — default-skip is the only supported mode; B-060 later wires the override. PASS = two entries for `https://example.com/` import one; summary reports `D=1`. FAIL = both imported, or an override checkbox ships under B-044.<br>**AC12 — Atomic REPLACE via `writeTransaction`:** Commit path (after Replace-all) is a SINGLE `writeTransaction` call that atomically overwrites `tj:items` and `tj:groups` in one op — per Sprint 17 D-3 lesson (partial writes leave split-brain state). Safe-mode is respected: if `isSafeMode()` is true, import aborts cleanly with inline error "Cannot import while in safe mode" and zero mutation. Any error inside the transaction (shape validation, depth cap, ULID collision, quota) rolls back; pre-import state is preserved byte-for-byte and a failure toast renders. PASS = forced mid-transaction throw leaves `tj:items` and `tj:groups` identical to pre-import; safe-mode aborts. FAIL = partial import lands on error, OR items written while groups are not.<br>**AC13 — Post-import summary toast:** Success toast reads `"Imported {IMPORTED} bookmarks into {GROUPS} groups. {K} skipped."` (omit skipped clause if K===0). Toast has `role="status"` + `aria-live="polite"` and auto-dismisses ~4s. On failure, a dismissible error toast with a brief reason (e.g., `"Import failed: invalid file format"`) appears; zero mutation persists. No item titles or URLs are logged (PII rule). PASS = counts match committed storage state; screen reader announces summary. FAIL = silent success, wrong counts, titles/URLs in console, or partial data persists after a failure toast.<br>**AC14 — Performance (1000 items / 50 folders):** On a seeded `.html` file with 1000 `<A>` entries across 50 folders (nested up to 3 levels, requiring flattening), elapsed time from Replace-all click to success toast is **< 2000ms P95** on a dev-class machine. Parse phase alone < 500ms. PASS = median of 5 runs ≤ 2000ms end-to-end; no long-tasks > 50ms during parse. FAIL = any run exceeds 2000ms, or the panel UI freezes during parse.<br>**AC15 — XSS safety — title / URL handling:** (a) Every imported title is read from the parsed DOM via `.textContent` on `<A>` — never serialized back as HTML. (b) Every imported URL flows through `normalizeUrl({ forStorage: true })`, inheriting B-003's validation and B-058's `ALLOWED_URL_SCHEMES` (rejects `javascript:`, `data:`). (c) Probe: title `</A><script>alert(1)</script>` and `HREF="javascript:alert(1)"` must (i) import title as literal text at render time, (ii) reject the URL per AC10, (iii) never execute script during parse or render. PASS = probe imports title as literal text; `javascript:` URL counted in `K` and never persisted. FAIL = any script executes, `javascript:` URL reaches storage, or title is interpreted as markup.<br>**AC16 — Zero network:** Entire import path (parse, preview, commit, post-import render) makes zero network requests. No `fetch`, `XMLHttpRequest`, `navigator.sendBeacon`, off-device image `src` for imported favicons, or `<script src>` injection. Favicons are NOT fetched during import — items render with letter-avatar fallback until live tab-match (B-010) supplies a real favicon. PASS = network trace during a 1000-item import shows zero outbound requests. FAIL = any network call originates from the import module.<br>**AC17 — No new manifest permissions:** `manifest.json` unchanged. File picker uses a programmatic `<input type="file">` click in the sidepanel context — no `downloads` / `fileSystem` permission. If R2 identifies a gap, the addition is called out and escalated to [security-reviewer] before build. PASS = `manifest.json` diff is empty. FAIL = any new permission added without R2 + [security-reviewer] sign-off.<br>**AC18 — Message contract:** New constant `MSG_IMPORT_COLLECTION = 'tj/importCollection'` added to `shared/messages.js`. Request payload: `{ format: 'html', content: string, options?: { skipDuplicates?: boolean } }` where `content` is raw file text (NOT a Blob — sidepanel reads the file to text, then dispatches). Response on success: `{ ok: true, data: { imported: number, skipped: number, duplicates: number, groups: number } }`; on failure: `{ ok: false, error: string, code: string }`. Dispatch is documented as the single extensible entry for future formats (B-045 JSON will share the constant with `format: 'json'`). PASS = `shared/messages.js` declares the constant + typedef; handler validates `format` and rejects unknown values. FAIL = ad-hoc message name or payload shape differs from spec.<br>**AC19 — Out of scope:** (a) **Partial / merge import** — B-044 is replace-only; merge is a separate future item. (b) **JSON import** — owned by B-045; `format` reserves `'json'` but B-044 ships HTML only. (c) **Pocket / Instapaper / browser-specific extension fields** (e.g., Firefox `FEED`, Chrome `PERSONAL_TOOLBAR_FOLDER`) — parser ignores unknown attributes gracefully without mapping them. (d) **"Import duplicates anyway" override** — B-060 wires the toggle; B-044 always default-skips. (e) **Favicon fetching** — out per AC16. (f) **Undo / rollback after commit** — user re-imports; no post-commit undo. (g) **Progress bar during parse** — AC14 target makes it unnecessary; if R2 discovers larger files exceed 500ms parse, a skeleton placeholder may be added at build. |
| B-045 | As a user, I want to import a Tab Junkie JSON backup, with validation and automatic repair of structural issues (orphaned sub-groups, circular references, duplicate IDs), so that a corrupt or partial backup is recovered rather than rejected entirely. | P1 | M | done | 18 | B-005, B-006, B-043, B-044 (shared `background/import/` infra + `MSG_IMPORT_COLLECTION { format }` envelope + count-preview dialog), B-060 (duplicate-URL default: skip), B-067 (§32.5 allow-list — must land before R3 per Sprint 18 Wave ordering) | **AC1 — File picker.** Importer is reachable from the same sidepanel overflow-menu surface as B-044 (submenu: "Import → JSON backup"). Activation opens a native `<input type="file" accept="application/json,.json">` dialog. The picker MUST reject any file whose extension is not `.json` (case-insensitive) and MUST NOT open a non-JSON file even if the user bypasses the accept filter on some platforms. PASS = only `.json` files can be picked; non-`.json` selection shows a dismissible toast ("Please select a .json file") and no parse is attempted. FAIL = non-`.json` file triggers parse.<br>**AC2 — Root shape validation (§32.5.1).** After `JSON.parse`, the importer validates the root object has: `schemaVersion` (integer ≥ 1), `exportedAt` (string), `items` (array), `groups` (array), optional `preferences` (object). If any required key is missing, of the wrong type, or the document is not an object (e.g., top-level array, string, null), the importer rejects with a user-visible toast ("Backup file is malformed and cannot be imported") and writes nothing to storage. PASS = every missing/mistyped key combination rejects cleanly with no partial write; FAIL = any shape-invalid file lands any mutation.<br>**AC3 — schemaVersion gate.** If `schemaVersion === KNOWN_VERSION` (from `background/storage/migration.js`): proceed. If `schemaVersion < KNOWN_VERSION`: run the same `MIGRATION_STEPS` chain used by B-001b against the imported snapshot (in-memory, not against live storage) before continuing. If `schemaVersion > KNOWN_VERSION`: reject with a user-visible toast reading exactly "Backup was created in a newer version. Please update Tab Junkie before importing." and write nothing. If `schemaVersion` is non-integer, < 1, or non-finite: reject as malformed (AC2). PASS = all three branches behave as specified on fixture inputs; FAIL = any lower-version file blocks without migration OR any higher-version file proceeds OR any write occurs on rejection.<br>**AC4 — Count preview + Replace confirmation (mirrors B-044).** After validation + repair pass, importer surfaces a confirmation dialog showing: "Import will replace all existing data with N items in M groups from this backup." (counts are post-repair, pre-write). Dialog has a cancel affordance and a primary "Replace and import" button matching B-044's confirm UX (primary button styling per B-062, focus-trap, Escape cancels, Enter confirms on primary). No import occurs without explicit confirmation. PASS = cancel writes nothing AND confirm writes exactly once; FAIL = any silent apply, or counts off by >0.<br>**AC5 — Auto-repair: orphaned sub-groups.** Any group whose `parentId` references an `id` not present in the imported `groups` array (post-dedup) is repaired to `parentId: null` (reparented to top level). Count of repairs recorded in the repair report. PASS = every orphaned child survives as a top-level group with correct id/name/items; FAIL = any orphan silently dropped or left with a dangling parentId.<br>**AC6 — Auto-repair: circular group refs.** If traversal of the `parentId` chain from any group loops back to itself (A→B→A, A→A, etc.), the cycle is broken by setting the junior-side edge's `parentId` to `null`. "Junior" is defined as the group with the greater `createdAt`; if `createdAt` ties, the greater `id` (lexicographic) loses. Every break is recorded in the repair report. PASS = no cycle remains after import AND no group is deleted as part of the break; FAIL = any cycle survives OR any group is dropped.<br>**AC7 — Auto-repair: duplicate IDs.** If two or more `items` share an `id`, or two or more `groups` share an `id`, the first occurrence (source-array order after sort) keeps the id and every subsequent occurrence is assigned a fresh ULID via `ulid()`. All cross-references (`item.groupId`, `group.parentId`) that pointed at the renamed id are rewritten to the new id **only if the renaming preserves the intended link** (i.e., the renamed record is the one the reference was adjacent to — R2 to specify disambiguation rule if ambiguous; default "links follow the last-written duplicate"). Each rename recorded in the repair report. PASS = after import, every item/group has a unique id AND no data is silently merged/lost; FAIL = any duplicate id survives OR any record is dropped to resolve a conflict.<br>**AC8 — Auto-repair: orphaned items.** Any item whose `groupId` references a `group.id` not present in the imported `groups` array (post-dedup and post-repair) has its `groupId` set to `null` (Ungrouped). Count recorded in the repair report. PASS = every orphan item survives as Ungrouped; FAIL = any orphan silently dropped.<br>**AC9 — Auto-repair: unknown fields on Item / Group (consumes B-067 allow-list).** Every item and group record is filtered through the B-067 allow-list before being written to storage — only fields enumerated in SOLUTION_DESIGN §32.5.2 (Item) and §32.5.3 (Group) survive the import. Unknown / future fields present in the backup file are silently dropped (not persisted, not counted as a repair). PASS = no allow-list-foreign key lands in `tj:items` / `tj:groups` post-import; FAIL = any unknown key (e.g., `live`, `tabId`, `favIconUrl`, future-dated field) is persisted.<br>**AC10 — Preferences apply (conditional).** If the imported file's top-level `preferences` key is present AND shape-valid per §32.5.4 (`theme` ∈ `{light,dark,system}`, `displayMode` ∈ `{sidepanel,window}`, `newTabOverride` boolean, `autoCollapseSubGroups` boolean), dispatch a single internal `setPreferences` call with the validated prefs as part of the same atomic import. If `preferences` is missing, null, or shape-invalid, the import proceeds and the current prefs remain unchanged — the post-import summary toast notes "preferences skipped (invalid shape)" (non-blocking). PASS = valid prefs apply atomically, invalid prefs are skipped with a warning, missing prefs are a no-op; FAIL = prefs clobber storage on invalid input OR valid prefs are silently skipped.<br>**AC11 — Atomic write-transaction replace.** The import "Replace" is performed via `writeTransaction` across `PARTITION_ITEMS`, `PARTITION_GROUPS`, and (when AC10 applies) `PARTITION_PREFS` in a single transaction — honouring Sprint 17 D-3 multi-partition atomicity. On any failure mid-write the transaction aborts and pre-import state is preserved byte-identical (no partial replace). PASS = on forced write failure, `listItems` / `listGroups` / `getPreferences` return pre-import values unchanged; FAIL = any partial state is observable after failure.<br>**AC12 — Duplicate-URL default: skip (B-060).** If two or more items in the imported file share a normalized `url` (per B-057 `normalizeUrl`), only the first (after §32.5.5 ordering) is written; subsequent duplicates are skipped and counted in the repair report. The "import duplicates anyway" toggle is **NOT in scope for B-045** — B-060 owns the UX toggle — but B-045 R3 MUST expose the underlying repair path so B-060 can flip the default without re-architecture. PASS = duplicate URLs in the file reduce to one item; FAIL = N duplicates write N items by default.<br>**AC13 — Performance: ≤ 1s on 1000 items / 100 groups.** Elapsed time from "Replace and import" confirmation to the success toast is ≤ 1000ms P95 on a seeded 1000-item / 100-group backup, measured via `performance.now()` on a dev-class machine (matches B-044 budget). Includes parse + validate + repair + writeTransaction. PASS = median of 5 runs ≤ 1000ms; FAIL = exceeds budget.<br>**AC14 — Zero network.** No `fetch`, `XMLHttpRequest`, `navigator.sendBeacon`, WebSocket, or any network API is invoked by the import path. Verified by [security-reviewer] grep in R4. PASS = zero hits on network APIs in the import diff; FAIL = any network call.<br>**AC15 — XSS safety on imported titles / URLs.** Every imported item's `title` and `url` is piped through the same B-003 validation paths as `createItem` (URL via `normalizeUrl` with scheme allow-list per B-058; title length-bounded). Titles are rendered via `textContent` at every consumer post-import (no re-render regression). A probe item with title `</div><script>alert(1)</script>` and URL `javascript:alert(1)` must: (a) have its URL rejected at import (item dropped into the repair report as "unsupported URL scheme"), (b) have its title survive verbatim as literal text in any sidepanel render. PASS = no script executes during import OR during subsequent render; FAIL = any injection surfaces as live DOM.<br>**AC16 — Post-import summary toast.** On success, a non-blocking toast (matching B-044 copy pattern + `role="status"` / `aria-live="polite"`) reads exactly: "Imported N items, M groups. K repairs." — where K is the total repair count from `buildRepairReport(decisions)` (sum of AC5+AC6+AC7+AC8+AC12 repair counts; AC9 unknown-field drops are NOT counted per AC9). If K === 0 the "K repairs" segment is omitted ("Imported N items, M groups."). Toast auto-dismisses after ~4s; is announced to screen readers; is dismissible by click/Escape. PASS = counts match written storage; FAIL = silent success OR wrong counts OR a11y regression.<br>**AC17 — Out of scope.** (a) Partial import / selective import (whole backup only). (b) Cloud restore or network-fetched backups. (c) Schema downgrade (file's `schemaVersion > KNOWN_VERSION` is rejected per AC3 — no downgrade path is offered). (d) Merge-mode import (replace-only in this item; merge is a future backlog candidate). (e) Encryption / password-protected backups. (f) HTML import (owned by B-044). (g) The B-060 "import duplicates anyway" UX toggle (B-060 owns the toggle; B-045 owns the default-skip path only per AC12). (h) Exporting (owned by B-042 / B-043). (i) Undo / rollback after successful import (the pre-import data is replaced; users must keep their own backup of the outgoing state — this is the same UX as B-044 "replace existing data" warning). |
| B-046 | As a user, I want global keyboard shortcuts to open the quick search popup and the standalone Tab Junkie window from any browser tab, so that I can access Tab Junkie without clicking the extension icon. | P1 | XS | done | 28 | B-022 ✅, B-035 (in-sprint) ⚠️ **Fast Track XS — R1 audit found all shortcuts already registered (v1.18.0+). Scope = verification + user-manual doc. AC-4 gated on B-035 SW dispatch handler (in-sprint).** | Two default global shortcuts are registered in `manifest.json`: one for the quick search popup and one for the standalone window.<br>Both shortcuts function from any active tab including chrome:// pages (within Chrome's shortcut constraints).<br>Shortcut assignments are visible and rebindable in `chrome://extensions/shortcuts`.<br>Activating the standalone shortcut when the window is already open focuses the existing window. |
| B-047 | As a user, I want keyboard shortcuts within the side panel for selecting all visible items and clearing the selection, so that I can use the panel efficiently without reaching for the mouse. | P1 | XS | done | 13 | B-024 | Ctrl/Cmd+A within the panel selects all currently visible items.<br>Escape within the panel clears the active selection.<br>Shortcuts do not fire when focus is inside a text input (e.g., the filter bar). |
| B-048 | As a user, I want all item visual states — live (open), active (focused tab), drifted, audible, and selected — to be visually distinct and accessible, so that I can read the state of any item at a glance. | P1 | M | done | 16 | B-010, B-011, B-012, B-024, B-062 (sequence B-048 R3 after B-062 R3 to avoid `--accent`/`--on-accent` token conflicts) | **AC1 — Non-color distinction:** Each of the five states (`live`, `active`, `drifted`, `audible`, `selected`) has a non-color cue in addition to color: border thickness/placement, icon, text weight, position, or pattern. Verified via a simulated grayscale screenshot of a list containing one row per state — each state remains identifiable with zero color information.<br>**AC2 — Coexistence of all five:** A single `.item-row` with `data-live="true" data-active="true" data-drifted="true" data-audible="true" data-selected="true"` renders all cues legibly; no cue fully occludes another. Visual stacking order documented in SOLUTION_DESIGN (background layer → border layer → icon row → selection checkbox → focus ring).<br>**AC3 — WCAG AA contrast per state × theme:** For both light and dark themes, every state's text (title, URL, badges) meets ≥4.5:1 against that state's effective background, and every non-text indicator (border, live rail, drifted/audible icons, checkbox) meets ≥3:1. Measurements published to `docs/a11y-audit-B-048.md` (sibling of B-062 audit).<br>**AC4 — Hover distinct from all states:** For each state X, a row in `state=X + :hover` is visibly different from the same row without hover, and no state's background or border obscures the hover affordance. Verified by side-by-side screenshots of X vs X+hover for all 5 states × 2 themes.<br>**AC5 — `:focus-visible` ring distinct on every state:** Keyboard tab-through a list containing one row in each state shows a clear `:focus-visible` ring with ≥3:1 contrast against the row's effective background in both themes. The ring is not clipped or overdrawn by any state's border/background.<br>**AC6 — Selected checkbox affordance (hover-reveal):** When a row IS selected, a persistent checkbox is visible on the row. When a row is NOT selected, the checkbox appears on `:hover` and on `:focus-visible` only (Gmail/Todoist pattern, PO-confirmed). The checkbox slot reserves layout space even when the checkbox is visually hidden so hover does not trigger a reflow. Keyboard activation (Space/Enter on the focused row, consistent with B-024) toggles selection. ARIA: `role="checkbox"` with `aria-checked="true\|false"` on either the row or an explicit checkbox element, matching the existing B-024 implementation; no duplicate/contradictory roles.<br>**AC7 — Screen-reader state labels (priority-first concat, lowercase):** Each state exposes a descriptive text label to assistive tech via a single `aria-label` on the `.item-row` (rebuilt by `_buildItemRowAriaLabel(item, live, drifted, selected)` per R2 §31.6): `"active tab"`, `"live tab"`, `"tab content has changed"`, `"playing audio"`, `"selected"`. Combined states concatenate in the deterministic order **`active → live → drifted → audible → selected`** (PO-confirmed: strongest identity first, user-action context last). Example row with all five flags reads `"active tab, live tab, tab content has changed, playing audio, selected"`. PO-confirmed all labels are LOWERCASE; any pre-existing sentence-case labels (e.g., `"Playing audio"`) are normalized to lowercase as part of this item. Icons switch to `aria-hidden="true"` to prevent duplicate announcements. Verified via the browser a11y tree.<br>**AC8 — No live-state latency regression:** `_patchItemRow` still settles the five `data-*` attribute writes within the existing B-010 AC7 ≤500 ms target; no new blocking storage reads, layout thrash, or style recalc regressions introduced in the patch path. Verified via the existing B-010 latency harness.<br>**AC9 — Zero full re-renders on state change:** A state-attribute change on one row mutates only that row's DOM; no full list re-render, no group-level re-render. Verified via a DevTools performance trace on a 500-item list with all 5 states toggled simultaneously — recalc-style scope is limited to the affected rows.<br>**AC10 — Out of scope:** (a) no change to the semantic meaning of any state — `live`/`active`/`drifted`/`audible` are defined by existing message contracts; this item is purely visual. (b) no new states introduced. (c) no change to focus-management architecture. (d) no change to any saved-item storage shape. (e) cross-browser/OS tab-color sync remains owned by B-041. |
| B-049 | As a user, I want appropriate empty-state messages (empty list, no filter results, empty group) and visible error feedback for failed operations, so that I always understand what is happening even when there is nothing to show. | P1 | S | done | 12 | B-003, B-006, B-021 | Empty bookmark list shows an icon, descriptive message, and a prominent "Add bookmark" CTA.<br>Empty filter results show a message and a clear-filter shortcut link.<br>Empty group shows a message indicating the group has no items.<br>Failed operations (import failure, sync error, invalid input) surface a dismissible error notification with a brief reason. |
| B-050 | As a developer, I want state broadcasts from the service worker to be sent to all open extension surfaces (side panels, popup, new tab page, standalone window) on every mutation, so that all views remain in sync without manual refresh. | P0 | M | done | 5 | B-001 ⚠️ **New message contract for broadcast events; `runtime.sendMessage` / `tabs.sendMessage` patterns** → Full tier | **AC1 — Mutation coverage:** Every write operation dispatched through `storage-handlers.js` (`MSG_CREATE_ITEM`, `MSG_UPDATE_ITEM`, `MSG_DELETE_ITEM`, `MSG_CREATE_GROUP`, `MSG_UPDATE_GROUP`, `MSG_DELETE_GROUP`, `MSG_SET_PREFERENCES`, `MSG_PROMOTE_TAB`, `MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM`) fires a broadcast **after** `sendResponse` completes and only when the operation returns `ok: true`. Failed operations never broadcast.<br>**AC2 — Tab-event coverage:** The four handlers in `tab-events.js` (`onUpdated` after claim reevaluation, `onActivated`, `onRemoved`, `windows.onRemoved`) fire a broadcast after their in-memory / session mutations complete. Broadcasts are suppressed until `isClaimsReady()` returns true to avoid spurious bursts during cold-start reconciliation.<br>**AC3 — Message contract:** A new constant `MSG_STATE_CHANGED = 'tj/stateChanged'` is added to `shared/messages.js`. The broadcast envelope is `{ type: 'tj/stateChanged', payload: { scope: 'items' \| 'groups' \| 'preferences' \| 'liveState', trigger: string } }` where `trigger` is the originating `MSG_*` constant (or `'tab/updated'` / `'tab/activated'` / `'tab/removed'` for tab events). The payload carries **no full state snapshot** — it is a delta-free invalidation signal; receivers must call `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` / `MSG_LIST_GROUPS` etc. to re-fetch.<br>**AC4 — Silent when no surfaces are open:** `chrome.runtime.sendMessage` throws / rejects with `Could not establish connection` when no receiver exists. The broadcaster catches and silently discards this error — it must never cause the originating mutation to fail or produce an unhandled rejection in the SW console.<br>**AC5 — Safe-mode behaviour:** Broadcasts are **not** sent during safe mode (writes are already blocked by the dispatcher, so no mutation AC1 broadcasts arise). Tab-event broadcasts (AC2) still fire in safe mode because LiveTabIndex/TabClaims state can still change while reads are allowed.<br>**AC6 — Ordering guarantee:** The broadcast is issued **after** `sendResponse({ ok: true, data })` has been called so the originating surface receives its mutation response before the invalidation signal arrives at any surface (including itself). Receivers must not assume the broadcast arrives before their own response.<br>**AC7 — Surfaces update within 500 ms:** All open instances re-fetch and re-render within 500 ms of receiving the broadcast under normal (non-throttled) conditions. |
| B-051 | As a developer, I want sort orders to be normalised after every operation that could leave gaps or duplicates, and selection sets to be pruned of non-existent IDs, so that data integrity is maintained without manual cleanup. | P1 | S | done | 13 | B-001, B-030 | Sort order normalisation runs after every create, delete, move, or import operation.<br>After normalisation, item sort order values are sequential with no gaps or duplicates within each group.<br>Selection sets are pruned of any IDs not present in the current item collection before bulk actions are applied.<br>Normalisation is idempotent: running it twice produces the same result as running it once. |
| B-052 | As a developer, I want the fuzzy search index to be cached and only rebuilt when the underlying item collection changes, and I want search results to return in under 50 ms at P95 on a 1,000-item collection, so that the search experience feels instant even for large libraries. | P1 | M | done | 19 | B-021, B-022 | Fuzzy search index is built once on load and cached in memory.<br>Index is invalidated and rebuilt only when items or groups are added, edited, deleted, or reordered.<br>Search returns results in < 50 ms P95 measured against a 1,000-item test collection.<br>Sidepanel first paint with a 500-item collection completes in < 200 ms after the panel is opened.<br>No full re-render occurs on single-item updates; DOM patches are targeted. |
| B-053 | As a developer, I want the circular dependency between `background/storage/partitions.js` and `background/storage/write-transaction.js` broken by extracting shape helpers (`defaultShape`, `assertShape`, partition key constants) into a third module, so that static analyzers stop firing false-positive unused-import warnings and the module graph stays acyclic. | P2 | S | done | 10 | B-001a |
| B-054 | As a user, I want the side panel to display all my saved bookmarks organized by group with live/active/audible/drifted indicators, group collapse/expand, click-to-navigate, and real-time updates via state broadcasts, so that I can see and manage my entire bookmark + tab collection from a persistent panel. | P0 | L | done | 6 | B-001a, B-006, B-050, B-019 | **AC1 — HTML shell:** `sidepanel/sidepanel.html` exists, declares `<!DOCTYPE html>`, links the theme-init script before any other JS (so no flash of unstyled content), and imports `sidepanel.js` as a module. PASS = file loads in Chrome with zero console errors; FAIL = any missing doctype, wrong script order, or console error on open.<br>**AC2 — Initial data fetch:** On `DOMContentLoaded` the panel sends `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` and `MSG_LIST_GROUPS` in parallel; the resolved data is used for first render. PASS = both messages are sent before any item DOM is written; FAIL = either message is absent or sent serially after the other resolves.<br>**AC3 — Group rendering:** Every `Group` returned by `MSG_LIST_GROUPS` produces a header element containing the group name, a color chip matching `GROUP_COLORS[group.color]`, and an item count badge. Items whose `groupId` matches the group's id are rendered as children; items with `groupId: null` appear in an "Ungrouped" section at the bottom. PASS = manual inspection of a collection with ≥2 named groups and ≥1 ungrouped item shows all three sections correctly; FAIL = any group header missing or item placed under wrong group.<br>**AC4 — Sub-group indentation:** Items belonging to a group whose `parentId` references another group are rendered indented (CSS `padding-left` ≥ 16 px more than top-level items) under their parent group header. PASS = visual indentation is present and measurable in DevTools; FAIL = sub-group items appear at the same indent level as top-level items.<br>**AC5 — Item display:** Each item row renders: title (truncated via CSS at 1 line), URL (truncated, second line), and a favicon. For live items the favicon is the tab's actual favicon (`chrome.tabs` favicon URL); for non-live items a letter-avatar derived from the item title's first character is shown. PASS = a live item row shows a real favicon and a non-live item shows a letter-avatar; FAIL = either is missing or reversed.<br>**AC6 — Live/active/audible/drifted indicators:** (a) A live item row has a `data-live="true"` attribute and a distinct visual treatment (e.g., left border or dot). (b) The active (focused) tab item has `data-active="true"` and an accent highlight distinguishable from the live indicator. (c) An audible item has `data-audible="true"` and a speaker icon. (d) A drifted item (present in `driftRecords`) has `data-drifted="true"` and a warning icon. PASS = each state is visually distinct and the data attribute is present; FAIL = any state is absent or two states are visually indistinguishable.<br>**AC7 — Group collapse/expand:** Clicking a group header toggles the visibility of its child items. Collapsed state is stored in local panel state (or `MSG_SET_PREFERENCES`) and survives a panel close/reopen within the same browser session. The header's toggle control has `aria-expanded="true|false"`. PASS = items hide on collapse, reappear on expand, state persists after panel is closed and reopened; FAIL = items remain visible after collapse or state is not persisted.<br>**AC8 — Click-to-navigate:** Clicking an item row (not the toggle or any future action button) sends `MSG_NAVIGATE_TO_ITEM` with the item's id. The side panel remains open after the message is sent. PASS = clicking an item switches the browser to that tab and the panel stays open; FAIL = panel closes or no message is sent.<br>**AC9 — Broadcast-driven re-render:** When `MSG_STATE_CHANGED` is received the panel re-fetches only the scope indicated (`items`, `groups`, `preferences`, or `liveState`) and updates the DOM without a full page reload. Re-render completes within 100 ms of receiving the broadcast under normal conditions. PASS = timed DOM mutation settles ≤ 100 ms after broadcast; FAIL = full reload occurs or update takes > 100 ms in a profiler trace with ≤ 500 items.<br>**AC10 — Empty state:** When `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` returns an empty array the panel renders a centred icon, a descriptive message ("No bookmarks yet"), and an "Add your first bookmark" CTA placeholder element (not yet wired to a dialog — that is B-003). No item rows or group headers are shown. PASS = empty collection shows only the empty-state block; FAIL = empty-state is absent or item/group DOM is also present.<br>**AC11 — Loading skeleton:** Between the panel opening and the first render, a skeleton loader (not a spinner) is shown in place of item rows. The skeleton is removed and replaced by real content once both `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` and `MSG_LIST_GROUPS` resolve. PASS = skeleton is visible for any artificially delayed response and disappears on data arrival; FAIL = a spinner is used or skeleton remains after data loads.<br>**AC12 — First-paint performance:** With a collection of exactly 500 items across ≥ 5 groups, the panel completes its first render (skeleton removed, all item rows present in DOM) in < 200 ms measured from `DOMContentLoaded`. PASS = Chrome DevTools Performance trace shows paint+DOM settled < 200 ms; FAIL = any trace exceeds 200 ms.<br>**AC13 — Theme support:** `MSG_GET_PREFERENCES` is called on open; the resolved `theme` value (`light`, `dark`, or `system`) is applied as a `data-theme` attribute on `<html>` (or equivalent CSS custom-property root). Changing the OS appearance while `theme=system` updates the panel without reload. PASS = panel renders correctly in all three theme modes; FAIL = theme attribute is absent or `system` does not respond to OS change.<br>**AC14 — Keyboard navigation:** Tab and Shift+Tab move focus through interactive elements (group headers, item rows) in DOM order. Enter on an item row triggers navigation (same as click). Enter on a group header toggles collapse. Focus is never trapped or lost after any of these interactions. PASS = full keyboard-only walkthrough reaches every item and navigates without mouse; FAIL = any element is unreachable by keyboard or focus disappears.<br>**AC15 — Focus indicators:** Every focusable element (group headers, item rows) has a visible focus ring meeting WCAG AA (3:1 contrast ratio minimum against adjacent background). PASS = focus ring is visible in both light and dark themes under browser default or custom CSS; FAIL = focus ring is absent or contrast < 3:1.<br>**AC16 — ARIA roles:** The item list container has `role="list"`; each item row has `role="listitem"`. Each group header toggle has `role="button"` and `aria-expanded`. Group header elements that wrap both the toggle and the child list use `aria-controls` pointing to the child list's id. PASS = axe-core or equivalent accessibility audit reports zero violations on the panel; FAIL = any listed role or attribute is missing.<br>**AC17 — Out-of-scope exclusions (explicit):** The following are NOT implemented in B-054 and their absence is not a failure: drag-and-drop reordering (B-030), context menus (B-026/B-027/B-028), search/filter input (B-021), multi-select (B-024), group reorder (B-008/B-031), create/edit/delete dialogs (B-003). PASS = none of the above UI elements are present; FAIL = any out-of-scope element is implemented (scope creep). |
| B-055 | As a power user who keeps many tabs open, I want a dedicated "Open Tabs" section in the side panel that surfaces all live browser tabs that are not yet saved or grouped, so that I can see, navigate to, and manage every open tab from a single persistent interface without losing track of ephemeral browsing sessions. | P1 | M | done | 13 | B-001a, B-010, B-050, B-054 ⚠️ **Extends `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` enriched-response contract and adds `openTabs` array; introduces new render section; unblocks B-033 and B-022's "Open Tabs" results panel** → Full tier | **AC1 — Definition of "live-only ungrouped" tab:** A tab qualifies for the Open Tabs section if and only if all three conditions hold: (a) its `tabId` is present in `getLiveTabIndex()`, (b) its `tabId` does NOT appear in `claimsMirror` (i.e., it is not claimed by any saved item), AND (c) its `tabId` does NOT appear as the claimed tab of any record in `tj:floatingGroups` (i.e., it is not a live floating-group member already rendering inside a group section). PASS = a tab opened externally with no saved counterpart and no group association appears in the section; a tab claimed by a saved item does NOT appear; a floating-group tab rendered inside its group does NOT appear. FAIL = any claimed or grouped tab leaks into the section, or any qualifying tab is absent.<br>**AC2 — Message contract (extend `MSG_LIST_ITEMS`):** The existing `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` (`tj/listItems`) enriched response is extended to include an additional `openTabs` array alongside the existing `items`, `liveStates`, and `driftRecords` fields. New shape: `{ items: Item[], liveStates: Record<itemId, {live, active, audible}>, driftRecords: Record<itemId, DriftRecord>, openTabs: OpenTab[] }` where `OpenTab = { tabId: number, windowId: number, title: string, url: string, favIconUrl: string|null, audible: boolean, active: boolean, tabIndex: number }`. This approach is chosen over a separate `MSG_LIST_OPEN_TABS` message to avoid an additional round-trip when the sidepanel loads: both saved items and open tabs are fetched in a single existing message. The `openTabs` array is populated by iterating `getLiveTabIndex()` and excluding any `tabId` present in `claimsMirror` or any resolved `tj:floatingGroups` claim. PASS = `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` response contains a non-null `openTabs` array (empty array `[]` when none qualify); FAIL = field is absent, undefined, or a separate message is required to retrieve open tabs.<br>**AC3 — `shared/messages.js` updated:** The `ListItemsResponse` typedef in `shared/messages.js` is updated to document the `openTabs` field and the `OpenTab` shape. The constant `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` itself is unchanged (same string `'tj/listItems'`). No new message constant is introduced. PASS = `shared/messages.js` typedef reflects the extended shape; the string constant is unmodified. FAIL = a new message type constant is introduced, or the typedef is not updated.<br>**AC4 — Rendering location:** A pinned "Open Tabs" section is rendered at the bottom of the sidepanel scroll area — below all named groups and below the "Ungrouped" saved-items section (if present). The section is always present in the DOM regardless of whether `openTabs` is empty or populated (it renders as an empty state, not as a missing element). The section header reads "Open Tabs" with a live item count badge (e.g., "Open Tabs · 3"). PASS = section header is visible in the DOM after initial render with either populated or empty `openTabs`; FAIL = section is absent when `openTabs` is empty, or section appears above any named group.<br>**AC5 — Row rendering:** Each open-tab row renders: (a) a favicon (`favIconUrl` if non-null, else a letter-avatar derived from the first character of `title`), (b) `title` truncated to one line via CSS, (c) `url` truncated on a second line, (d) a window badge showing the window number if more than one browser window is open, (e) a speaker icon if `audible: true`, (f) an active-tab highlight (distinct background or left-border accent) if `active: true`. Each row carries `data-live-only="true"` on its root element so CSS can style open-tab rows distinctly from saved-item rows. PASS = visual inspection of a row for an audible active tab in window 2 shows all five indicators simultaneously; FAIL = any indicator is absent or `data-live-only` attribute is missing.<br>**AC6 — Click-to-focus:** Clicking an open-tab row sends a `MSG_NAVIGATE_TO_ITEM`-equivalent message that calls `chrome.tabs.update(tabId, { active: true })` followed by `chrome.windows.update(windowId, { focused: true })` in the service worker. The sidepanel remains open after the click (no panel close). Because open tabs have no saved `itemId`, the navigation message must carry `{ tabId, windowId }` rather than `{ itemId }`. The implementation may reuse `MSG_NAVIGATE_TO_ITEM` with a `tabId`-only payload variant, or introduce a new `MSG_FOCUS_TAB` constant — the choice must be documented in `shared/messages.js`. PASS = clicking a row in a background window switches focus to that window and activates that tab; panel stays open. FAIL = panel closes, wrong tab is activated, or `chrome.windows.update` is not called.<br>**AC7 — Right-click context menu:** Right-clicking an open-tab row opens a context menu (reusing the B-026 menu infrastructure) with the following actions: (a) "Save to group…" — opens the group picker (B-029 when available, otherwise a simplified inline picker) and calls `MSG_PROMOTE_TAB` with the selected `groupId`; (b) "Close tab" — calls `MSG_CLOSE_TABS` with `{ tabIds: [tabId] }`. The action "Open in new window" is explicitly deferred to B-035 and must not appear in this implementation. Destructive actions (Close tab) are visually distinguished (red text). PASS = both actions function correctly; "Open in new window" is absent. FAIL = either required action is missing, "Open in new window" appears, or a non-destructive action is styled red.<br>**AC8 — Real-time updates via `MSG_STATE_CHANGED`:** The Open Tabs section re-renders its content when a `MSG_STATE_CHANGED` broadcast is received with `scope: 'liveState'` (covers tab opened, closed, navigated, or activated events) or `scope: 'items'` (covers a tab being promoted to a saved item and therefore leaving the section). The re-render path is targeted: only the Open Tabs section's DOM is patched; the saved-items groups sections are not re-rendered. PASS = opening a new external tab causes it to appear in the section within 500 ms; promoting a tab causes it to leave the section within 500 ms; no full panel reload occurs. FAIL = full panel re-render is triggered, or the section does not update on tab open/close.<br>**AC9 — Sort order within section:** Rows are sorted by `windowId` ascending, then by `tabIndex` ascending within each window. This matches the visual left-to-right order of tabs in the browser's native tab strip. Sort order is stable and deterministic; user-defined reordering is explicitly out of scope for this item. PASS = with tabs in windows 1 and 2, tabs from window 1 appear before tabs from window 2, each sub-sorted by tab position. FAIL = order is random, DOM-insertion-order, or changes non-deterministically between renders.<br>**AC10 — Empty state:** When `openTabs` is an empty array the section renders an empty-state block inside the section boundary: a small icon and the text "No untracked tabs — all open tabs are saved or grouped" (exact copy subject to UX review at build time). The empty-state visual style is consistent with B-049's established empty-state pattern. The section header and empty-state block remain visible (the section is never hidden when empty). PASS = empty-state message is visible when no qualifying tabs exist; FAIL = section is hidden, spinner shown, or empty-state text is absent.<br>**AC11 — Inline filter integration (B-021):** When the sidepanel's inline filter input (B-021) is active, open-tab rows are included in the filter pass. Matching is case-insensitive substring on `title` OR `url`. Rows that do not match are hidden. If no open-tab rows match the active filter, the entire "Open Tabs" section (header + empty-state) is hidden. When the filter is cleared, the section and all qualifying rows are restored. PASS = typing a query that matches one open-tab title shows only that row in the section; a non-matching query hides the entire section. FAIL = open-tab rows are unaffected by the filter, or section header remains visible when no rows match.<br>**AC12 — Multi-select integration (B-024):** Open-tab rows participate in B-024 multi-select gestures (single click, Shift+Click range, Ctrl/Cmd+Click toggle). When a selection that includes one or more open-tab rows is active, the bulk action bar shows only the actions that are valid for open-tab rows: "Close tabs" is always available; "Move to group" (i.e., promote to saved item in a group) is available if B-029 is shipped; "Remove bookmark" and "Demote" are NOT shown for open-tab rows (they are not saved items). A mixed selection containing both saved items and open-tab rows shows the intersection of valid actions (e.g., "Close tabs" if all selected rows are live). PASS = bulk action bar for an open-tab-only selection shows "Close tabs" and omits "Remove bookmark"; FAIL = "Remove bookmark" or "Demote" appears for an open-tab row selection.<br>**AC13 — No storage writes:** Rendering the Open Tabs section must not create, update, or delete any entry in `chrome.storage.local` or `chrome.storage.session`. The section is derived entirely from the in-memory `getLiveTabIndex()` snapshot at `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` read time. PASS = a spy on `chrome.storage.local.set` and `chrome.storage.session.set` confirms zero calls during a full panel render that includes 10 open-tab rows. FAIL = any storage write is observed during section render.<br>**AC14 — Safe-mode behavior:** The Open Tabs section renders normally in safe mode because it performs no storage writes. The "Save to group…" context menu action (which calls `MSG_PROMOTE_TAB`) is blocked by the existing safe-mode dispatcher and returns `ERR_SAFE_MODE`; the UI must surface this as a dismissible inline error (e.g., "Cannot save while in safe mode"). "Close tab" (`MSG_CLOSE_TABS`) is a tab operation, not a storage write, and must remain functional in safe mode. PASS = section is visible and populated in safe mode; "Close tab" works; "Save to group" fails gracefully with an error message. FAIL = section is hidden in safe mode, or "Close tab" is incorrectly blocked.<br>**AC15 — Accessibility:** The "Open Tabs" section container has `role="region"` and `aria-label="Open Tabs"`. The row list inside the section has `role="list"`; each row has `role="listitem"`. Rows are keyboard-navigable via Tab/Shift+Tab; Enter on a focused row triggers the click-to-focus action (AC6). The section header count badge is updated with `aria-live="polite"` so screen readers announce count changes when tabs open or close (implementation as a separate live-region element is acceptable). Focus is never trapped within the section. PASS = axe-core audit on the panel with a populated Open Tabs section reports zero violations; full keyboard-only walkthrough reaches every open-tab row. FAIL = any listed role or attribute is missing, or a row is unreachable by keyboard.<br>**AC16 — Performance:** With 50 open-tab rows, the Open Tabs section renders within the overall 200 ms first-paint budget (shared with saved-items rendering). Row additions and removals triggered by `MSG_STATE_CHANGED` are applied via targeted DOM patches (add/remove individual `<li>` elements); no operation causes a full section or full panel re-render. PASS = Chrome DevTools Performance trace with 50 open-tab rows shows section render completing within the 200 ms panel budget; a single tab-open event patches one row without re-rendering the full section. FAIL = full section re-render on every tab event, or 50-row render alone exceeds 200 ms.<br>**AC17 — Out-of-scope exclusions (explicit):** The following are NOT in scope for B-055 and their absence is not a failure: cross-window drag of open-tab rows (B-030 / PRD §4.10 out-of-scope), user-defined reordering of rows within the section (requires storage — deferred), bulk "Save all open tabs" action (scope creep — not in PRD), "Open in new window" context action (deferred to B-035), group picker modal full implementation (B-029 — use simplified inline picker if B-029 is not yet shipped). PASS = none of the above are implemented. FAIL = any out-of-scope element is implemented. |
