| B-062 | As a user with dark theme enabled, I want all primary action buttons (Save bookmark, Save group, Save-anyway confirm) to have sufficient text-on-background contrast, so that I can read button labels without straining or leaning on context. | P1 | S | done | 16 | B-003, B-006, B-059 — surfaced by B-059 QA review UAT-9 | **AC1 — Dark-theme `.dialog-btn--primary` text contrast.** Every call site of `.dialog-btn--primary` rendered in dark theme (`data-theme="dark"` on `<html>`) measures ≥ 4.5:1 foreground-to-background contrast ratio against its resolved button background, per WCAG 2.1 §1.4.3 (normal text). Call sites covered: (a) Save bookmark dialog primary button (B-003), (b) Save group dialog primary button (B-006), (c) any additional `.dialog-btn--primary` site discovered during R3 audit. PASS = every call site measured ≥ 4.5:1; FAIL = any single call site < 4.5:1.<br>**AC2 — Dark-theme `[data-variant="primary"]` text contrast.** Every call site of `[data-variant="primary"]` in dark theme (introduced by B-059 "Save anyway" confirm button) measures ≥ 4.5:1 foreground-to-background contrast. Applies to both the standalone selector and any compound such as `.dialog-btn--danger[data-variant="primary"]` if present. PASS = every call site measured ≥ 4.5:1; FAIL = any single call site < 4.5:1.<br>**AC3 — Light-theme regression guard.** For every affected selector (`.dialog-btn--primary`, `[data-variant="primary"]`), the measured light-theme (`data-theme="light"`) contrast ratio is ≥ its pre-fix baseline AND ≥ 4.5:1 in absolute terms. PASS = light-theme contrast unchanged or improved AND above floor for every selector; FAIL = any light-theme ratio decreased or dropped below 4.5:1.<br>**AC4 — Interactive-state contrast (hover / focus / active / disabled).** For every affected selector × theme combination, contrast is measured in each state: `:hover`, `:focus-visible`, `:active`, `[disabled]`. Thresholds: `:hover`, `:focus-visible`, `:active` ≥ 4.5:1 (normal-text rule); `[disabled]` ≥ 3:1 (WCAG §1.4.11 inactive-control exception — documented in audit file). PASS = every state at or above its threshold; FAIL = any state below threshold.<br>**AC5 — Focus-ring non-text contrast.** The `:focus-visible` focus ring (outline / box-shadow) on primary buttons has ≥ 3:1 contrast against the button background per WCAG 2.1 §1.4.11 (non-text contrast). Measured separately in light and dark themes. PASS = ≥ 3:1 in BOTH themes; FAIL = < 3:1 in either theme.<br>**AC6 — Method of proof: audit file.** A new file `docs/a11y-audit-B-062.md` is created. It contains a table with one row per (selector × theme × state) combination enumerated in AC1–AC5. Each row records: selector, theme, state, foreground hex, background hex, computed contrast ratio (to 2 decimal places), PASS/FAIL, tool used (e.g., WebAIM Contrast Checker or Chrome DevTools Accessibility pane). The file also records: pre-fix baseline ratios, chosen fix option (A / B / C) with rationale, and post-fix ratios. PASS = file exists at the path and contains a row for every required combination with numeric ratios; FAIL = file missing OR any required row absent OR any ratio not numerically documented.<br>**AC7 — Fix-option decision documented.** Exactly one of the three fix options is selected and documented in the audit file AND in the R6 close note: **Option A** — retune dark-theme `--accent` token globally; **Option B** — introduce `--on-accent` (and optionally `--on-accent-muted`) token pair that adapts per theme; **Option C** — dark-text-on-accent for dark theme only (deviates from light-theme visual). Rationale MUST include blast radius (which non-button usages of `--accent` are affected) and any regressions checked. PASS = decision + rationale + blast-radius check recorded; FAIL = absent.<br>**AC8 — No regression on non-primary button variants or other `--accent` surfaces.** `.dialog-btn--secondary`, `.dialog-btn--danger` (without `[data-variant="primary"]`), and any other dialog-btn variant render unchanged (or improved) in both themes. If Option A is chosen, every other on-screen use of `--accent` (links, borders, focus rings, selection highlights, live-row accent) is re-measured and recorded in the audit file; no such surface's contrast may drop below its pre-fix ratio. PASS = visual inspection + measured ratios show no regression on non-primary surfaces; FAIL = any non-primary surface regresses.<br>**AC9 — Call-site coverage (explicit).** The fix MUST cover all three dependency-introduced call sites: B-003 Save-bookmark dialog primary button, B-006 Save-group dialog primary button, B-059 Save-anyway confirm-dialog primary-variant button. R3 audit MUST grep the codebase for both `dialog-btn--primary` and `data-variant="primary"` and add every match to the audit file. PASS = audit file enumerates all three known sites plus every additional match; FAIL = any known site omitted.<br>**AC10 — Existing test suite green.** Full test suite (`npm test` or project equivalent) passes with zero new failures after the fix. No new automated tests are required for this Fast Track item — contrast verification is performed via the documented audit file, not via code. PASS = test suite green; FAIL = any new regression.<br>**AC11 — Out-of-scope exclusions (explicit).** The following are NOT in scope and their absence is not a failure: (a) contrast fixes for non-primary buttons already passing AA; (b) contrast fixes for item rows, group headers, filter chips, context menus, and other non-primary-button UI surfaces — separate sweep if needed; (c) changes to core palette tokens (`--bg-primary`, `--bg-secondary`, `--text-primary`, `--text-secondary`, `--border`) — only `--accent` and/or a new `--on-accent` token pair may be modified; (d) changes to the light-theme `--accent` value (light theme is passing and must remain so); (e) non-contrast accessibility concerns (screen-reader names, keyboard traps) — already covered by prior sprints. PASS = none of the above modified; FAIL = any out-of-scope element changed. |

| B-065 | As a developer, I want core helper logic that is currently reproduced verbatim inside test files (B-027 group-menu logic, B-029 group-picker rows/filter, B-048 aria-label builder) to be extracted into `shared/*` modules and imported by both production code and tests, so that test-copy drift stops silently producing false-green results when production helpers change. | P2 | S | in-progress | 17 | Sprint 16 retro action item — B-027, B-029, B-048 all carry this tech-debt | **AC1 — Extract `_buildItemRowAriaLabel`.** Move `_buildItemRowAriaLabel(item, live, drifted, selected)` verbatim from `sidepanel/sidepanel.js:1980-1993` into a new `shared/aria-label.js` as a named export `buildItemRowAriaLabel`. Signature, null-item contract (`!item → 'Untitled'`), and concat order (`title, active tab, live tab, tab content has changed, playing audio, selected`) preserved byte-for-byte. Sidepanel imports it; all 6 call sites (`sidepanel.js:1481, 1489, 2129, 2295, 2514, 2625`) keep working (local name may remain `_buildItemRowAriaLabel` as an alias or be renamed — R3 choice). The in-test copy at `tests/b048-visual-states.test.js:39-55` is deleted and replaced with `import { buildItemRowAriaLabel } from '../shared/aria-label.js'`; existing test bodies continue to reference the same local identifier via a one-line alias so body diffs are zero.<br>**AC2 — Extract `_buildGroupPickerRows` + `_applyGroupPickerFilter`.** Move both from `sidepanel/sidepanel.js:668-723` and `:837-860` into a new `shared/group-picker-core.js`. Exports: `buildGroupPickerRows({ groups, items, liveStates, sourceGroupId })` — pure: takes the data it needs explicitly rather than reading module-level `_cachedGroups`/`_cachedItems`/`_cachedLiveStates`; returns the same row-shape array as today. `applyGroupPickerFilter(rows, query) → { visibleCount, visibility: boolean[] }` — pure: takes the row list + query, returns filter decisions; DOM mutation (`row.hidden = !match`), highlight reset, and `aria-activedescendant` clearing stay in sidepanel (they touch live DOM). Sidepanel refactors: `_buildGroupPickerRows(sourceGroupId)` becomes a one-line adapter over the module caches; `_applyGroupPickerFilter()` reads `groupPickerFilterEl.value`, calls the shared function, applies the visibility map to DOM rows, then runs existing highlight logic. In-test reproductions at `tests/b029-group-picker.test.js:132-172` and `:211-223` deleted; tests import the shared fns and feed them the same fixture ctx.<br>**AC3 — B-027 test deferral.** `tests/b027-group-header-menu.test.js:138-322` reproduces `openGroupContextMenu` as a ~185-line DOM-construction mirror of sidepanel's `_openGroupContextMenu` (L3691+). Extracting a pure `buildGroupMenuModel(group, items, liveStates) → MenuActionModel[]` descriptor would require refactoring `_openGroupContextMenu` to consume a model — out of scope for a Fast Track S item. After refactor the suite MUST stay 721/721 — no test added, removed, or skipped. Permitted diffs in the three test files: (a) new `import` at the top, (b) deletion of the reproduced function body, (c) at most one alias line per file so existing test bodies continue to resolve the same identifier.<br>**AC6 — Behavior preservation.** No user-visible change. No perf change (extraction is call-site rewiring — no new allocations, no new passes over items). Restructure so the outer try only guards `_sendMessage` calls; the inner catch for file-read remains. Eliminates confusing nesting; behavior identical. S30 R4 MEDIUM carry-forward. | B-096 | As a maintainer, I want `background/import/json-validator.js validatePreferences` synced with the canonical 13-slug `_VALID_THEME_SLUGS_WRITE` enum from `background/storage/preferences.js`, so that JSON backups containing the new B-037 theme slugs (e.g., `'dracula'`, `'nord'`) are accepted on import instead of silently dropped via the pre-existing 3-slug allow-list. | P2 | XS | done | 32 | B-037 ✅ — Sprint 31 R4 [security-reviewer] MEDIUM finding (pre-existing fail-closed defect; B-037 widens the gap). | **Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — validator widening; no destructive write behavior change. The import "Replace all?" preview gate (B-070 §AC4) remains the destructive-confirmation surface.<br>**R1 LOCKED DECISIONS**<br>**D-1 — Shared constant location: `shared/theme-slugs.js`.** Extract the slug lists to a new `shared/theme-slugs.js` exporting two named constants: `VALID_THEME_SLUGS_WRITE` (array of 13 slugs: `'system'`, `'github-light'`, `'tomorrow'`, `'atom-one-light'`, `'solarized-light'`, `'github-dark'`, `'tomorrow-night'`, `'atom-one-dark'`, `'solarized-dark'`, `'dracula'`, `'nord'`, `'one-dark'`, `'monokai'`) and `VALID_THEME_SLUGS_READ` (Set = 13 above + `'light'` + `'dark'`). All three callers import from this module: `background/storage/preferences.js` (`validatePrefsPatch` — write-side, uses `VALID_THEME_SLUGS_WRITE`), `background/storage/shapes.js` (`isPreferences` — read-side, uses `VALID_THEME_SLUGS_READ`), `background/import/json-validator.js` (`validatePreferences` — import-side, uses `VALID_THEME_SLUGS_READ`, see D-2). This eliminates future enum drift across all three callers.<br>**D-2 — Import validator accepts 15 (read-side, `VALID_THEME_SLUGS_READ`).** `validatePreferences` in `json-validator.js` MUST accept the full 15-value set (13 new slugs + `'light'` + `'dark'`), matching `isPreferences` in `shapes.js`. Rationale: a backup exported before B-037 shipped legitimately contains `theme: 'light'` or `theme: 'dark'`. Rejecting those backups at import would silently destroy the user's stored theme preference. After the import write completes, `getPreferences()` runs its B-037 D-2 migration which normalises `'light'` → `'atom-one-light'` and `'dark'` → `'one-dark'` before any caller sees the value. The import validator's role is structural gating (reject unknown/garbage values), not migration — migration is the storage layer's responsibility.<br>**D-3 — Test strategy: parameterize over the slug list.** New tests import `VALID_THEME_SLUGS_WRITE` and `VALID_THEME_SLUGS_READ` from `shared/theme-slugs.js` and iterate over them. This means future slug additions are auto-covered without test edits.<br>**AC1 — Extract shared slug constants.** Create `shared/theme-slugs.js` exporting `VALID_THEME_SLUGS_WRITE` (Array, 13 slugs) and `VALID_THEME_SLUGS_READ` (Set, 15 values). Update `background/storage/preferences.js` to import `VALID_THEME_SLUGS_WRITE` (drop the private `_VALID_THEME_SLUGS_WRITE` constant). Update `background/storage/shapes.js` to import `VALID_THEME_SLUGS_READ` (drop the private `_VALID_THEME_SLUGS_READ` constant). PASS = three callers import from `shared/theme-slugs.js`; no private slug constants remain in any file; FAIL = any private slug array/set survives.<br>**AC2 — Fix `validatePreferences` in `json-validator.js`.** Replace the hard-coded `p.theme !== 'light' && p.theme !== 'dark' && p.theme !== 'system'` guard (3-value inline check, circa line 147) with `!VALID_THEME_SLUGS_READ.has(p.theme)` (imported from `shared/theme-slugs.js`). PASS = the guard uses the shared constant; FAIL = inline string comparisons remain.<br>**AC3 — Import smoke regression (new slugs).** A backup JSON with `theme: 'dracula'` (or any of the 12 other new B-037 slugs) passes `validatePreferences` and imports without `preferencesSkipped`. PASS = `res.preferences.theme === 'dracula'`; FAIL = `preferencesSkipped: true` or validation rejection.<br>**AC4 — Legacy slug acceptance (read-side compat).** A backup JSON with `theme: 'light'` or `theme: 'dark'` passes `validatePreferences` (D-2 rationale). PASS = `res.preferences.theme === 'light'` (import layer passes it through; storage migration is separate); FAIL = `preferencesSkipped: true` or exception thrown.<br>**AC5 — Unknown slug rejection.** A backup JSON with `theme: 'neon-pink'` (or any other non-enum value) is rejected by `validatePreferences` → `preferencesSkipped: true`, no crash. PASS = `res.preferencesSkipped === true`; FAIL = unknown slug accepted or exception propagates.<br>**AC6 — No new permissions, no manifest changes.** Zero `manifest.json` changes. PASS = `git diff manifest.json` empty; FAIL = any permission or entry added.<br>**AC7 — Test coverage (≥ 4 net new tests, parameterized).** New tests in `tests/b045-json-validator.test.js`. Tests MUST import `VALID_THEME_SLUGS_WRITE` and `VALID_THEME_SLUGS_READ` from `shared/theme-slugs.js` and iterate: (a) all 13 write-side slugs accepted individually; (b) both legacy slugs (`'light'`, `'dark'`) accepted individually; (c) unknown slug rejected with `preferencesSkipped`; (d) `theme` field absent → existing behaviour preserved (no regression). Minimum ≥ 4 named test blocks; parameterized loops count as one block each. PASS = ≥ 4 new tests green + zero regressions; FAIL = fewer tests or any regression.<br>**AC8 — Out of scope.** (a) Storage schema changes. (b) Any user-visible UI changes. (c) Re-running comprehensive UAT sweep. (d) Any change to the B-037 D-2 migration logic in `getPreferences()`. |
| B-100 | As a user, I want the Delete (X) button on a live bookmark to CLOSE THE TAB by default rather than DELETE THE BOOKMARK, with bookmark removal made into a more deliberate action (e.g., context menu "Delete bookmark" with confirmation), so that I don't accidentally destroy bookmarks when I just want to close the tab. | P2 | M | done | 35 | B-026 ✅ (item context menu host), B-099 ✅ (claim semantics finalized). **UX redesign filed from S33 B-099 UAT-14 user feedback.** | **R1 LOCKED DECISIONS (2026-04-26)**<br><br>**Q1 — Default Delete-button click action when item is LIVE: LOCKED → close the tab, preserve the bookmark.** The X button on a live item row now dispatches `MSG_CLOSE_TABS { tabIds: [liveState.tabId] }` (the same message used by the context-menu "Close tab" entry), NOT `MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM`. The bookmark survives; the tab closes. Rationale: the default action should match the most common user intent (close the page they are done with) and the most-reversible action (close tab → can re-open from bookmark; delete bookmark → permanently lost saved item). `MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM` is now only reachable via the explicit context-menu "Delete bookmark" path (Q2) when the item is live — where it semantically means "remove bookmark AND release claim" as a single deliberate act.<br><br>**Q2 — "Delete bookmark" location: LOCKED → context menu only ("Delete bookmark" entry, red text, on ALL items live + non-live).** The context-menu "Delete" entry is renamed to "Delete bookmark" and appears on every item row regardless of live state. On live items it replaces the old demote-on-click (which was context-menu "Delete" → `MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM`) with an explicit label. On non-live items it mirrors the existing X-button delete path. Rationale: discoverability — the context menu is the established host for destructive actions (B-026 precedent); Shift+Click is invisible and not self-documenting. The existing context-menu "Close tab" entry (live-only, non-destructive) is RETAINED — users can close the tab alone without touching the bookmark via the context menu.<br><br>**Q3 — Confirmation pattern for "Delete bookmark" context menu entry: LOCKED → inline toast with Undo (5–8 s).** Clicking "Delete bookmark" in the context menu: (a) immediately fires `MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM` if item is live (releases claim + deletes bookmark + closes tab is NOT part of this action — close is via Q1 X-button; demote is bookmark removal with tab preserved) OR `MSG_DELETE_ITEM` if item is non-live; (b) shows an `Undo` toast for 5 s; (c) if Undo is clicked within the window, dispatches `MSG_CREATE_ITEM` with the full pre-delete `Item` snapshot to restore the bookmark. On live items, `MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM` preserves the live tab — Undo then calls `MSG_CREATE_ITEM` (not re-claim, because the tab identity is still live and claim will be re-established on next reconcile). On non-live items, Undo calls `MSG_CREATE_ITEM` with the captured snapshot. Rationale: toast+Undo avoids flow interruption (no modal block); B-070 toast precedent; captures the full Item object pre-delete so restoration is complete. NOTE: The X-button close-tab action on a live item (Q1) does NOT show a toast — closing a tab is a browser-native action, not a storage-destructive action (bookmark survives), so no confirmation or Undo is needed.<br><br>**Q4 — Non-live item X-button (Delete button) behavior: LOCKED → unchanged (deletes the bookmark, same as current behavior).** The X button on a non-live row continues to dispatch `MSG_DELETE_ITEM` after an `openConfirmDialog` confirmation (the existing modal). Rationale: there is no tab to close on a non-live row — the only sensible X-button action is bookmark deletion. The modal confirmation on non-live X is RETAINED (existing behavior; it is the primary destructive-action guard for non-live items). Non-live items also gain "Delete bookmark" in the context menu (Q2) as an alternative path with toast+Undo instead of modal — two paths, two UX patterns; R2 confirms whether to unify or keep them distinct.<br><br>**Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: RETAINED for "Delete bookmark" context-menu path on both live and non-live items — the toast+Undo IS the confirmation mechanism (reversible for 5 s). RETAINED (modal) for the non-live X-button delete path (existing `openConfirmDialog` behavior — unchanged by this item). EXPLICITLY WAIVED for the new live-X-closes-tab path (Q1): closing a tab is not a storage-destructive action — the bookmark survives and the tab can be re-opened from it; no confirmation is warranted. Rationale for waiver: the waived action is fully reversible by the user (re-open from bookmark), leaves no persistent data loss, and matches browser-native "close tab" UX expectations.<br><br>**Selector audit (rehome items)**: N/A — the Delete button (`.item-action-delete`, `data-action="delete"`) remains in the same DOM location (`.item-actions` strip on each `.item-row`). Only its click-handler logic changes. No DOM element is moved between surfaces. No existing test file selectors are invalidated by this item.<br><br>**ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA — 8 ACs (R1 LOCKED 2026-04-26)**<br><br>**AC1 — Live-item X button closes tab, preserves bookmark.** Clicking `.item-action-delete` (data-action="delete") on a row where `row.dataset.live === "true"` dispatches `MSG_CLOSE_TABS { tabIds: [liveState.tabId] }` (re-read `_cachedLiveStates[itemId]` at click time per B-026 H-1 pattern). The bookmark remains in storage; `_ensureItemRow` subsequently marks the row non-live when the SW broadcasts the tab-closed event. No toast, no confirmation dialog. PASS = tab closes + bookmark survives; FAIL = bookmark deleted OR no tab close.<br><br>**AC2 — Non-live item X button deletes bookmark (unchanged behavior).** Clicking `.item-action-delete` on a row where `row.dataset.live !== "true"` continues to call `openConfirmDialog(item, cb)` followed by `MSG_DELETE_ITEM { id: itemId }` on confirm. The modal confirmation is RETAINED for this path. PASS = behavior identical to pre-B-100 non-live delete; FAIL = modal skipped or wrong message dispatched.<br><br>**AC3 — Context menu gains "Delete bookmark" entry on ALL items (live + non-live), styled red (destructive class).** `openContextMenu()` in `sidepanel/sidepanel.js` always appends a "Delete bookmark" button with `class="context-menu-item context-menu-item--destructive"`, regardless of `isLive`. The old "Delete" label is replaced with "Delete bookmark". On live items the entry is visible below the existing "Close tab" separator; on non-live items it is visible in the same position the old "Delete" entry occupied. PASS = "Delete bookmark" appears in context menu for both live and non-live rows; FAIL = absent on either type OR old "Delete" label still present.<br><br>**AC4 — "Delete bookmark" context-menu click immediately dispatches the correct storage message and shows a 5 s Undo toast.** On live items: fires `MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM { itemId }` (preserves tab, deletes bookmark, releases claim — existing B-099 semantics). On non-live items: fires `MSG_DELETE_ITEM { id: itemId }`. In both cases, a `showToast("Bookmark deleted", { action: "Undo", duration: 5000 })` (or equivalent) is shown immediately after the message is dispatched (fire-and-forget pattern per C-11 popup-lifecycle precedent, although sidepanel context does not tear down; fire-and-forget is used for consistency). No `openConfirmDialog` is called for the context-menu "Delete bookmark" path. PASS = message dispatched + toast appears + no modal shown; FAIL = modal shown OR toast absent OR wrong message.<br><br>**AC5 — Undo within the toast window restores the bookmark via MSG_CREATE_ITEM.** Before dispatching the delete message in AC4, the full `Item` object is fetched via `MSG_GET_ITEM { id: itemId }` and captured in closure. If the user clicks "Undo" within 5 s, `MSG_CREATE_ITEM` is dispatched with the captured Item payload (same fields: `id`, `url`, `title`, `groupId`, `sortOrder`, `tags`, `pinned` — all fields required by `createItem` in `storage-handlers.js`). The toast dismisses. PASS = bookmark reappears in sidepanel with original title/URL/group after Undo; FAIL = no restore OR partial restore (wrong group, lost title). If `MSG_GET_ITEM` fails before delete, the "Delete bookmark" action is aborted with `showToast("Couldn't delete bookmark — try again")` (no data to capture for Undo).<br><br>**AC6 — Bulk action bar "Remove" operation is UNCHANGED.** The multi-select bulk action bar (`#bulk-action-bar`) "Remove" button continues to: (a) call `MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM` for live selected items; (b) call `MSG_DELETE_ITEM` for non-live selected items; (c) show the existing bulk confirmation dialog before firing. No changes to `sidepanel.js` lines handling the bulk Remove path (~5190–5280). PASS = bulk Remove behavior identical to pre-B-100; FAIL = any deviation in message type, confirmation dialog, or selection logic.<br><br>**AC7 — Keyboard Delete key on focused item row mirrors X button behavior.** If a `.item-row` has focus (or a child element is focused) and the user presses the Delete key, the handler fires the same action as clicking the X button: `MSG_CLOSE_TABS` if live, `openConfirmDialog` + `MSG_DELETE_ITEM` if non-live. R2 confirms whether a `keydown` handler already exists on `.item-row` or whether this requires a new `document.addEventListener("keydown")` delegation branch. PASS = Delete key on live row closes tab + preserves bookmark; Delete key on non-live row opens confirm dialog; FAIL = key press does nothing OR triggers wrong action.<br><br>**AC8 — Test coverage: ≥ 6 tests in `tests/b100-delete-on-live.test.js`.** Required cases: (T1) X-button on live row dispatches `MSG_CLOSE_TABS` (not `MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM`); (T2) X-button on non-live row opens confirm dialog then dispatches `MSG_DELETE_ITEM`; (T3) context-menu "Delete bookmark" on live row dispatches `MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM` + triggers toast; (T4) context-menu "Delete bookmark" on non-live row dispatches `MSG_DELETE_ITEM` + triggers toast; (T5) Undo toast click dispatches `MSG_CREATE_ITEM` with captured pre-delete payload; (T6) bulk Remove path unchanged (regression guard — `MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM` still dispatched for live items in bulk). All tests use `chrome-mock.js`.<br><br>**Out of scope**: newtab item row Delete behavior (separate surface, future item); popup surfaces (no item rows); `MSG_CLOSE_TABS` error handling beyond existing toast pattern; Undo persistence across sidepanel close/reopen (toast-window only); re-claiming the tab after Undo on live items (claim reconciler handles this on next SW state refresh). |
| B-101 | As a user, I want the drift indicator to be more subtle and integrated with the existing live-state visual treatment, communicated via a dotted vertical bar stacked beside the active row's solid green left-border (instead of a 16 px warning triangle in the indicators strip), so that drift is conveyed in the same gutter as the active state without crowding the indicators strip with another icon. | P3 | S | done | 34 | B-099 ✅ (drift behavior + indicator infrastructure shipped). **UX refinement filed from S33 B-099 UAT-9/10 user feedback; design locked in pre-S34 brainstorm with product-owner.** | **R1 LOCKED DECISIONS (2026-04-26 pre-S34 brainstorm)**<br><br>**Q1 — Visual treatment: LOCKED → second vertical bar in the row's left-edge gutter, stacked parallel to the existing 3 px solid `var(--active-border)` bar.** Bar style: `border-left: 3px dotted var(--drifted-color)` (or equivalent box-shadow / pseudo-element implementation). Rendered AT ALL TIMES the row is drifted (not only when active) — drift is independent of active state. When the row is BOTH active AND drifted, the two bars sit side-by-side in a ~6 px left gutter (3 px solid green + 3 px dotted amber). R2 picks the implementation (pseudo-element CANNOT carry `title`, so a thin sibling `<span>` is the likely path).<br><br>**Q4 — Coverage scope: LOCKED → sidepanel + standalone surfaces only (this item).** Newtab `.newtab-indicator-drifted` 12 px dot stays as-is for now (separate row layout doesn't have an "active green line" gutter to stack alongside). PASS = `grep -n "item-drifted-icon\|_createDriftedIcon" sidepanel/sidepanel.js` returns zero hits; FAIL = any residual.<br><br>**AC3 — Drift bar coexists with active-row green bar.** When a row is both `data-active="true"` AND drifted, the active 3 px solid green border AND the 3 px dotted amber bar render side-by-side. | B-105 | As a user on the `solarized-light` theme, I want body text against the secondary background to clear the WCAG AA 4.5:1 contrast threshold, so that group headers, panels, and any tinted surface remain accessible without forcing other themes to ship at zero tint. | P2 | S | done | 35 | B-037 ✅ (theme system). **Pre-existing theme defect surfaced (NOT caused) by S34 B-104 R4 contrast computation: `--text-primary` (`#586e75`) vs `--bg-secondary` (`#eee8d5`) measures 4.392:1 — sub-AA before any tint is applied.** B-104 worked around by shipping `--group-header-tint-amount: 0%` on solarized-light; B-105 tracks the underlying palette fix so future tinted-surface features can apply non-zero tints on this theme. | **R1 LOCKED DECISIONS (2026-04-25)**<br><br>**Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — visual/palette refinement only. No items, groups, bookmarks, or tabs are created, modified, or deleted. This item changes a single CSS token value and one tint-amount override in `shared/themes.css`. No destructive-action confirmation dialog is required or applicable.<br><br>**Selector audit (rehome items)**: N/A — not a rehome. No DOM elements move between surfaces. Existing test selectors are not invalidated by a token hex-value change.<br><br>**Q1 — Which palette token to adjust: LOCKED → darken `--text-primary` from `#586e75` to `#546a71`.**<br><br>Computed using the WCAG 2.1 relative luminance formula (sRGB → linear per-channel, then `L = 0.2126R + 0.7152G + 0.0722B`; contrast = `(L_lighter + 0.05) / (L_darker + 0.05)`):<br><br>| Candidate | R | G | B | Luminance | Contrast vs `#eee8d5` | AA? |<br>|-----------|---|---|---|-----------|----------------------|-----|<br>| `#586e75` (current) | 88 | 110 | 117 | 0.14511 | 4.392:1 | ✗ FAIL |<br>| `#566c73` (delta −2) | 86 | 108 | 115 | 0.13956 | 4.524:1 | ✅ PASS (tight) |<br>| `#556b72` (delta −3) | 85 | 107 | 114 | 0.13681 | 4.592:1 | ✅ PASS |<br>| **`#546a71` (delta −4)** | **84** | **106** | **113** | **0.13408** | **4.661:1** | **✅ PASS — CHOSEN** |<br>| `#536970` (delta −5) | 83 | 105 | 112 | 0.13138 | 4.731:1 | ✅ PASS |<br><br>**Chosen value: `#546a71` — contrast 4.661:1 against `#eee8d5`.** Target was ≥4.6:1 with a small safety margin; `#546a71` hits 4.661:1, providing 0.161 headroom above the 4.5:1 floor and 0.061 above the 4.6:1 target. Delta from canonical Solarized base01 (`#586e75`): −4 on each RGB channel. This is a ~3.4% luminance reduction — imperceptible in normal viewing at arm's length; well within the "defensible Solarized variation" range. The shift moves the text color toward (but not all the way to) Solarized base00 (`#657b83` — note: base00 is actually *lighter*, not darker, than base01 in Solarized's unusual lightness inversion; base01 `#586e75` IS the darker text color). `#546a71` is simply a slightly deeper cyan-grey that retains the warm neutral character of the Solarized light palette. No noticeable visual shift expected for most users.<br><br>**Rejected alternatives:** (b) Lighten `--bg-secondary` from `#eee8d5` → `#f5f1e3` — would require cascading changes to `--bg-hover`, `--bg-active`, `--border-primary`, `--border-subtle`, `--skeleton-base`, `--group-count-bg` (all currently match or derive from `--eee8d5`); high change surface for a 1-token-AA fix. (c) Split the gap (darken text + lighten bg) — same cascading bg problem; unnecessary for a tiny luminance gap.<br><br>**Q2 — Solarized canonical fidelity tradeoff: LOCKED → deviate from Solarized canonical to gain AA accessibility.**<br><br>Rationale: the delta from `#586e75` to `#546a71` is 4 units per RGB channel (luminance reduction of ~3.4%). In practice this is invisible to ~99% of users on typical consumer displays. WCAG AA accessibility is a stronger product value than palette purity for the overwhelming majority of Tab Junkie users — no user has ever filed an issue about text color precision in the Solarized Light theme; accessibility failures are a real barrier to use for low-vision users. A "Solarized Light (high contrast)" variant theme is rejected: it doubles the surface to maintain (14 themes → 15 themes), adds another R1 definition cycle, and delivers zero benefit to any current user who hasn't already complained about the text contrast. If a user ever needs the exact canonical palette, they can inspect and override the single CSS variable in developer tools. Decision: ship the single-token deviation; document the exact reason in `docs/design/45-b-037-themes.md`.<br><br>**Q3 — Surface regression check scope: LOCKED → R5 must verify the 6 surfaces below.**<br><br>The following surfaces are checked at WCAG AA for `--text-primary` (and where applicable `--text-secondary`) after `#546a71` lands. Pre-computed values confirm which pass and which are pre-existing sub-AA (not introduced by B-105):<br><br>| # | Surface | Text token | Text hex | BG token | BG hex | Contrast | AA? | Notes |<br>|---|---------|-----------|---------|---------|-------|----------|-----|-------|<br>| S1 | Main content area | `--text-primary` | `#546a71` | `--bg-primary` | `#fdf6e3` | 5.295:1 | ✅ | B-105 fixes baseline |<br>| S2 | Secondary panels / group headers (untinted) | `--text-primary` | `#546a71` | `--bg-secondary` | `#eee8d5` | 4.661:1 | ✅ | B-105 fixes baseline |<br>| S3 | Open Tabs section / hover states | `--text-primary` | `#546a71` | `--bg-hover` | `#e4dcc4` | 4.170:1 | ✗ | **Pre-existing** sub-AA on hover bg (slightly darker than `--bg-secondary`); not introduced by B-105. R5 flags but does NOT block — this surface uses slightly larger UI text (item row font-size ~14px) and/or icon color vs. nav bg which may qualify as large text under WCAG. R2 [solution-architect] assesses. |<br>| S4 | Active-row state | `--text-primary` | `#546a71` | `--active-bg` | `#e3eef7` | 4.852:1 | ✅ | Passes; active row uses blue-tinted bg |<br>| S5 | Drift indicator | `--drifted-color` | `#b58900` | `--bg-secondary` | `#eee8d5` | 2.619:1 | ✗ | **Pre-existing** sub-AA on drifted-color vs bg-secondary; not a text-level contrast issue (indicator icon, not body text); not introduced by B-105. Out of scope for this item. |<br>| S6 | Group count badge | `--group-count-text` | `#657b83` | `--group-count-bg` | `#eee8d5` | 3.636:1 | ✗ | **Pre-existing** sub-AA on `--text-secondary` (`#657b83`) vs `--bg-secondary`; this is a structural palette limitation of Solarized Light (the canonical palette has low contrast between its secondary text and base2). Not introduced by B-105 and not fixable without changing `--text-secondary` (separate concern). Out of scope for this item. |<br><br>R5 [test-engineer] runs spot-checks on all 6 surfaces. S1, S2, S4 must PASS. S3, S5, S6 are flagged as pre-existing sub-AA with scope notes; the R5 UAT report documents them explicitly so they are not silently lost.<br><br>**Q4 — `--group-header-tint-amount` cleanup: LOCKED → REPLACE `0%` override with `3%` (do NOT remove entirely).**<br><br>**Critical finding from R1 math:** Even after darkening `--text-primary` to `#546a71`, computing `color-mix(in srgb, <gc-slot> 12%, #eee8d5)` for all 9 solarized-light group-color slots shows every slot fails WCAG AA at 12% tint (worst: `red` at 4.135:1, `indigo` at 4.134:1). The maximum safe tint for `#546a71` against all 9 solarized-light `--gc-*` slots is **3%** (worst slot = teal at 4.609:1 at 3%). At 4%, the worst slot (indigo) drops to 4.473:1 — FAIL.<br><br>Tint safety boundary analysis (all 9 gc slots × new `#546a71` text):<br>- 1% tint: worst slot = teal, 4.609:1 ✅<br>- 2% tint: worst slot = red, 4.565:1 ✅<br>- 3% tint: worst slot = purple/teal, 4.524:1 ✅ ← **safe ceiling**<br>- 4% tint: worst slot = indigo, 4.473:1 ✗ FAIL<br><br>**Consequence:** The `--group-header-tint-amount: 0%` override on `[data-theme="solarized-light"]` CANNOT be fully removed. B-105 changes it from `0%` → `3%`. This gives solarized-light a subtle but non-zero tint (visible group color identity), while keeping all 9 slots above 4.5:1 contrast with the new `#546a71` text. At 3%, the minimum slot contrast is 4.524:1 (purple, worst case).<br><br>**B-106 implication (flagged for [scrum-master] and R2):** B-106 bumps the `:root` default from 12% → 18%. Solarized-light's per-theme `3%` override still wins the CSS cascade — it will not change when B-106 lands. B-106's spot-check matrix does NOT need to re-verify solarized-light beyond confirming the 3% override is present. R3 for B-106 must NOT remove the solarized-light override block.<br><br>**ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA — 6 ACs (R1 LOCKED 2026-04-25)**<br><br>**AC1 — `--text-primary` in `[data-theme="solarized-light"]` updated to `#546a71`.** `shared/themes.css` line currently reading `--text-primary: #586e75;` in the solarized-light block is changed to `--text-primary: #546a71;`. No other token in the block is modified. PASS = `grep "solarized-light" -A 60 shared/themes.css` shows `--text-primary: #546a71`; FAIL = old hex `#586e75` still present or wrong hex used.<br><br>**AC2 — `--group-header-tint-amount` override on solarized-light changed from `0%` to `3%`.** The line `--group-header-tint-amount: 0%;` in `[data-theme="solarized-light"]` is updated to `--group-header-tint-amount: 3%;`. The override block and its explanatory comment are RETAINED and UPDATED (comment must be revised to reference B-105 and explain the 3% ceiling). PASS = solarized-light block contains `--group-header-tint-amount: 3%` with an updated B-104/B-105 reference comment; FAIL = override removed entirely, or value unchanged at 0%, or no comment update.<br><br>**AC3 — WCAG AA contrast for `--text-primary` vs `--bg-secondary` ≥ 4.5:1 (computed).** Precise computed value: `#546a71` vs `#eee8d5` = **4.661:1** per WCAG 2.1 relative luminance formula. R5 [test-engineer] asserts this in the automated test suite using a JS luminance calculation function (see AC5). PASS = computed ratio ≥ 4.5:1 (expected 4.661:1); FAIL = ratio < 4.5:1 or computed with wrong formula.<br><br>**AC4 — WCAG AA surface regression spot-checks (6 surfaces per Q3): S1, S2, S4 pass; S3/S5/S6 documented as pre-existing sub-AA.** R5 [test-engineer] verifies: (S1) `#546a71` vs `#fdf6e3` ≥ 4.5:1 (expected 5.295:1 ✅); (S2) `#546a71` vs `#eee8d5` ≥ 4.5:1 (expected 4.661:1 ✅); (S3) `#546a71` vs `#e4dcc4` — documents 4.170:1, flags as pre-existing, confirms scope exclusion; (S4) `#546a71` vs `#e3eef7` ≥ 4.5:1 (expected 4.852:1 ✅); (S5) `#b58900` vs `#eee8d5` — documents 2.619:1, flags as pre-existing indicator (not body text), confirms scope exclusion; (S6) `--group-count-text` `#657b83` vs `--group-count-bg` `#eee8d5` — documents 3.636:1, flags as pre-existing `--text-secondary` palette limitation, confirms scope exclusion. UAT report lists all 6 with PASS/PRE-EXISTING status. PASS = S1/S2/S4 above 4.5:1; S3/S5/S6 documented without blocking; FAIL = S1/S2/S4 below 4.5:1 or pre-existing surfaces undocumented.<br><br>**AC5 — Test coverage: ≥ 4 tests in `tests/b105-solarized-light-contrast.test.js`.** Required test cases:<br>(T1) Computed WCAG AA contrast assertion — reads `--text-primary: #546a71` and `--bg-secondary: #eee8d5` from themes.css and asserts `contrast('#546a71', '#eee8d5') >= 4.5` (and expected value is within ±0.01 of 4.661). Test file includes the `toLinear()` / `luminance()` / `contrast()` helper functions inline (pure math, no DOM dependency).<br>(T2) Override regression guard — reads `shared/themes.css` raw text and asserts the `[data-theme="solarized-light"]` block contains `--group-header-tint-amount: 3%` (not `0%` and not absent). Prevents accidental reversion to 0% in future edits.<br>(T3) Cross-surface contrast spot-checks — asserts (a) `contrast('#546a71', '#fdf6e3') >= 4.5`, (b) `contrast('#546a71', '#eee8d5') >= 4.5`, (c) `contrast('#546a71', '#e3eef7') >= 4.5` (S1, S2, S4 from Q3 table).<br>(T4) Full theme catalog regression — reads `shared/themes.css` raw text and asserts: (a) all 13 other themes' `--text-primary` values are unchanged (spot-check 3 themes: `github-light`, `dracula`, `atom-one-light`); (b) no other theme gained a `--group-header-tint-amount` override in this changeset (regex: count of `--group-header-tint-amount` declarations in `[data-theme=` blocks should remain 1 after B-105 lands, before B-106 ships). PASS = ≥ 4 tests green + full test suite zero regressions; FAIL = fewer tests, any assertion failure, or any regression.<br><br>**AC6 — `docs/design/45-b-037-themes.md` updated with palette correction note.** A one-line note is added to the solarized-light theme entry in the §45 theme catalog (or in a "Known Palette Corrections" subsection if the R6 [solution-architect] prefers a new §45.N sub-section): *"B-105 (Sprint 35): `--text-primary` corrected from `#586e75` (Solarized canonical base01, 4.392:1 AA FAIL) to `#546a71` (4.661:1 AA PASS); `--group-header-tint-amount` raised from 0% to 3% (3% is the safe ceiling for all 9 group-color slots with the corrected text token)."* R6 [solution-architect] decides exact placement and whether a new sub-section is warranted. PASS = the corrected values and reason appear in the doc; FAIL = no update to §45 documentation. |

No confirmation dialog is required or applicable.<br><br>**Selector audit (rehome items)**: N/A — this item changes only the value of a CSS custom property in `shared/themes.css` `:root`. No DOM elements are moved between surfaces. No existing test file selectors are invalidated.<br><br>**Q1 — Default tint percentage: LOCKED → 18%.**<br><br>Computed WCAG AA contrast ratios at 18% using `color-mix(in srgb, slotColor 18%, --bg-secondary 82%)` (linear sRGB interpolation per CSS Color 4):<br><br>| # | Theme + Slot | Slot hex | `--bg-secondary` | Tinted bg @18% | `--text-primary` | Contrast @18% | Pass? |<br>|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|<br>| S1 | `dracula` + `red` (`#ff5555`) | `#ff5555` | `#1e1f29` | `#472931` | `#f8f8f2` | 12.09:1 | ✅ PASS |<br>| S2 | `github-light` + `pink` (`#bf3989`) | `#bf3989` | `#f6f8fa` | `#ecd6e6` | `#1f2328` | 11.53:1 | ✅ PASS |<br>| S3 | `one-dark` + `slate` (`#5c6370`) | `#5c6370` | `#21252b` | `#2c3037` | `#abb2bf` | 6.22:1 | ✅ PASS |<br>| S4 | `system-default` + `blue` (`#0969da`) | `#0969da` | `#f5f6f8` | `#cbddf3` | `#1a1d23` | 12.20:1 | ✅ PASS |<br>| S5 | `atom-one-dark` + `yellow` (`#e5c07b`) | `#e5c07b` | `#21252b` | `#444139` | `#abb2bf` | **4.78:1** | ✅ PASS |<br>| S6 | `solarized-light` (post-B-105) + `yellow` (`#d5a643`) | `#d5a643` | `#eee8d5` | `#eadcbb` | `#475158` (B-105 proposed fix) | 5.98:1 | ✅ PASS |<br><br>**Worst case: `atom-one-dark` + `yellow` at 4.78:1** — 0.28 above the 4.5:1 floor, comfortable safety margin.<br><br>**Why not 20%?** At 20%, `atom-one-dark` + `yellow` falls to **4.55:1** — only 0.05 above the floor, dangerously thin. At 22% it drops to 4.33:1 (FAIL). 18% is the safe ceiling before the yellow-on-dark-bg cliff drops us into borderline territory.<br><br>**Why not 16%?** The visual improvement from 12% → 16% is modest. 12% was already described as "a bit too dark." 18% provides noticeably brighter group identity without approaching the AA cliff (4.78:1 worst case vs 4.50:1 floor = 6% headroom). 16% gives less visual payoff for identical implementation cost.<br><br>**Note on solarized-light (S6):** This spot-check uses B-105's proposed `--text-primary` fix of `#475158` (darker than the current `#586e75`). If B-105 instead lightens `--bg-secondary` to `#f5f1e3`, the resulting contrast at 18% will be even higher (lighter bg + same dark text = more contrast headroom). Either B-105 fix approach leaves solarized-light well above 4.5:1 at 18% tint. R3 must re-verify with B-105's final locked hex values once B-105 R1 is committed.<br><br>**No per-theme overrides triggered.** All 20 rows of the B-104 §47.7 matrix plus solarized-light (post-B-105) pass at 18%. No theme requires a new `--group-header-tint-amount` override block in B-106 (except the existing solarized-light override, which B-105 removes).<br><br>**Q2 — Variable name: LOCKED → keep `--group-header-tint-amount` as-is.** The variable was introduced in v1.28.0 (S34 B-104). Renaming to `--group-tint-strength` or similar would (a) create churn in `sidepanel/sidepanel.css` (2 call-sites), `newtab/newtab.css` (2 call-sites), `shared/themes.css` (solarized-light override block), and `tests/b104-group-colors.test.js` (6 regex references); (b) break the muscle memory of any user who read the B-104 CHANGELOG entry; (c) provide no functional benefit. The existing name precisely describes the property's role — keep it.<br><br>**Wave 1 dependency:** B-106 R3 MUST NOT launch until B-105 R3 has merged. Reason: `shared/themes.css` currently declares `--group-header-tint-amount: 0%` on `[data-theme="solarized-light"]`. Until B-105 removes that override (because its palette fix now makes solarized-light safe at non-zero tint), bumping the `:root` default from 12% → 18% has zero visual effect on solarized-light — the per-theme 0% override wins the CSS cascade. The existing CSS fallback `var(--group-header-tint-amount, 12%)` in surface CSS is left as-is (it becomes a dead fallback; the `:root` value now wins). PASS = `grep ":root" shared/themes.css` shows a block containing `--group-header-tint-amount: 18%`; FAIL = `:root` block absent, value != 18%, or value set only in surface CSS rather than in the shared token file.<br><br>**AC2 — WCAG AA spot-check matrix re-verified at 18% default; all rows ≥ 4.5:1.** R3 [frontend-engineer] re-runs the 6-sample spot-check (S1–S6 above) at 18% and confirms all ratios ≥ 4.5:1. Additionally, R4 [qa-reviewer] spot-checks the full 20-row matrix from B-104 §47.7 at 18% using the same `color-mix` linear formula. No combination from that matrix may fall below 4.5:1 body-text contrast at the new default. Based on the R1 spot-check computation, no new overrides are expected at 18%. PASS = no new overrides needed (expected path); OR all overrides that are needed are present and re-verified; FAIL = a sub-AA combination exists with no override.<br><br>**AC4 — `docs/design/47-b-104-themed-group-colors.md` §47.7 spot-check matrix updated to reflect 18% default.** The table header in §47.7 currently reads "Tinted header bg (12% color-mix approx)".
