Full suite must remain green after T10 is added. |
| B-107 | As an assistive-tech user, I want the X button on a live bookmark row to announce "Close tab" instead of "Delete bookmark", so that the spoken label matches the action that fires (close tab when live, delete bookmark when not). | P3 | XS | done | 36 | B-100 ✅ (live X-button now closes tab; aria-label not yet updated). **Filed from S35 B-100 R4 qa-reviewer MEDIUM M-4 — WCAG 2.1 SC 4.1.2 name-role-value mismatch.** | **R1 LOCKED (2026-04-25)**<br><br>**Q1 — Aria-label values: LOCKED.**<br>- Live state: `"Close tab"` — short, imperative, matches the actual action dispatched by B-100 (`MSG_CLOSE_TABS`).<br>- Non-live state: `"Delete bookmark"` — current value, unchanged.<br><br>**Q2 — Implementation approach: LOCKED → JS-set on live-state change.**<br>CSS attribute selectors cannot mutate `aria-label` (it is a content attribute, not a CSS-controlled property). The JS path is the only feasible approach. Insertion point: the live-state patch loop in `sidepanel.js` (around line 3066) already sets `row.dataset.live = 'true'/'false'` on every `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` response. After that dataset mutation, add a one-liner targeting `row.querySelector('.item-action-delete')` — if `live?.live` is true set `aria-label` to `"Close tab"`, else set it to `"Delete bookmark"`. Second insertion point: `buildItemRow` (~line 2475) at the static creation site — initial value should be `"Delete bookmark"` (default non-live); the live-state patch loop handles the reactive flip. No separate `_ensureIndicators` change needed.<br><br>**Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — this item changes an accessibility label only; no destructive UI pathway is introduced or altered. The X-button click handler itself is unchanged (B-100 contract is verbatim).<br><br>**Selector audit (rehome items)**: N/A — no DOM element is moved between surfaces.<br><br>**Acceptance Criteria**<br>**AC1 — Live-row X-button label**: When a saved-item row has `data-live="true"` (the item is live), its `.item-action-delete` button MUST have `aria-label === "Close tab"`. PASS = screen reader announces "Close tab"; FAIL = announces "Delete bookmark" or any other string.<br>**AC2 — Non-live-row X-button label**: When a saved-item row does NOT have `data-live="true"` (the item is not live), its `.item-action-delete` button MUST have `aria-label === "Delete bookmark"`. PASS = screen reader announces "Delete bookmark" for non-live items; FAIL = any other value.<br>**AC3 — Reactive update on live-state change**: When a row transitions live→non-live or non-live→live (driven by a `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` response with updated `liveStates`), the `.item-action-delete` `aria-label` MUST update in the same synchronous patch pass — no page reload required. PASS = after the patch, querying `row.querySelector('.item-action-delete').getAttribute('aria-label')` returns the correct value for the new live state; FAIL = stale label survives after state flip.<br>**AC4 — Test coverage**: `tests/b107-live-x-aria.test.js` MUST contain ≥ 3 tests: T1 — live row X-button `aria-label === "Close tab"`; T2 — non-live row X-button `aria-label === "Delete bookmark"`; T3 — reactive update: build a non-live row, simulate a live-state patch, assert label flips to `"Close tab"` then patch back to non-live and assert it flips to `"Delete bookmark"`.<br><br>**Out of scope**: No changes to any other `aria-label` in the item row (edit button, drag handle, row-level label, window badge). No change to the X-button click handler behavior. No visual change. |
| B-108 | As a solarized-light user, I want secondary text (`--text-secondary`, including `--group-count-text`) to clear WCAG AA contrast (≥ 4.5:1) against `--bg-secondary`, so that group counts, helper text, and other secondary content remain accessible. | P3 | S | done | 36 | B-105 ✅ (primary text contrast fix). **Filed from S35 B-105 R4 qa-reviewer MEDIUM — `#657b83` text on `#eee8d5` measures 3.636:1.** | **R1 LOCKED (2026-04-25)**<br><br>**Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — CSS token value change only; no destructive user action is involved.<br><br>**Selector audit (rehome items)**: N/A — not a rehome; no DOM elements are moved between surfaces.<br><br>**Q1 — Token to adjust (LOCKED → darken `--text-secondary`)**<br>Current: `--text-secondary: #657b83` vs `--bg-secondary: #eee8d5` = **3.636:1** (FAIL).<br>Chosen hex: **`#546a72`** (delta −17 per channel from canonical; sRGB channels: R=84, G=106, B=114).<br>Computed contrast `#546a72` vs `#eee8d5` = **4.655:1** — 0.155 above the 4.5:1 AA floor. This mirrors the B-105 precedent margin (B-105 landed `--text-primary` at 4.661:1, margin 0.161). Approach (b) — lighten `--bg-secondary` — is explicitly REJECTED: `#eee8d5` is shared by `--border-subtle`, `--skeleton-base`, and the B-105-established `--text-primary` 4.661:1 baseline; any lightening risks cascading failures.<br><br>**Q2 — Solarized canonical fidelity (LOCKED)**<br>B-105 already deviated from canonical Solarized for `--text-primary` (`#586e75` → `#546a71`). B-108 continues the same precedent: a single-token deviation for WCAG AA accessibility takes priority over canonical-palette purity, per §52.3 D-1. This is the second and final deviation in the solarized-light block; both are documented in-CSS and in the design chapter. The tradeoff is well-understood and proportionate: delta −17 per channel (~3.6% luminance reduction) is imperceptible at arm's length on consumer displays.<br><br>**Q3 — Surface regression scope (LOCKED)**<br>`var(--text-secondary)` is consumed across **4 CSS files**: `sidepanel/sidepanel.css` (23 references), `newtab/newtab.css` (3 references), `popup/group-jump-popup.css` (1 reference — aliased to `--color-fg-muted`), `settings/settings.css` (5 references). Total: **32 references across 4 surfaces**. R5 spot-checks each surface in solarized-light theme against the new `#546a72` value — confirming every instance of `--text-secondary` on `--bg-secondary` or `--bg-primary` remains at or above 4.5:1. `--group-count-text` in `shared/themes.css` line 334 directly aliases `--text-secondary` hex; updating `--text-secondary` automatically fixes `--group-count-text` — no separate token edit required. `--group-count-bg` (`#eee8d5`) is unchanged — contrast of new `#546a72` vs `#eee8d5` is confirmed at **4.655:1**.<br><br>**Acceptance Criteria**<br>AC1: `shared/themes.css` `[data-theme="solarized-light"]` `--text-secondary` updated from `#657b83` to `#546a72`; inline comment added citing B-108, the contrast ratio (4.655:1), and the delta (−17 per channel).<br>AC2: Computed WCAG 2.1 contrast `#546a72` vs `--bg-secondary` `#eee8d5` ≥ 4.5:1, asserted as a passing automated test in `tests/b108-solarized-secondary-contrast.test.js`.<br>AC3: `--group-count-text` in solarized-light (`#657b83`, aliasing `--text-secondary`) inherits the fix automatically; test T2 guards that the `--group-count-text` declaration in the solarized-light block equals `#657b83` (i.e., the alias pattern is NOT separately hardcoded to the old value, proving inheritance).<br>AC4: Regression spot-check — no other theme's `--text-secondary` value changes (13 themes unchanged); automated test T4 asserts each of the 13 other themes' `--text-secondary` tokens is unchanged from pre-B-108 values.<br>AC5: Test file `tests/b108-solarized-secondary-contrast.test.js` contains ≥ 4 tests: T1 — computed WCAG AA contrast assertion (`#546a72` vs `#eee8d5` ≥ 4.5:1); T2 — token value regression guard (solarized-light `--text-secondary` == `#546a72` post-update; `--group-count-text` alias pattern intact); T3 — cross-surface completeness check (solarized-light `--text-secondary` surfaces spot-checked: at minimum sidepanel + settings confirm ≥ 4.5:1 on `--bg-secondary` and ≥ 4.5:1 on `--bg-primary` `#fdf6e3`); T4 — catalog regression (all 13 other themes' `--text-secondary` values unchanged). UAT ≥ 3 cases: UAT-1 group count badges legible in solarized-light; UAT-2 helper/secondary text legible across sidepanel + settings surfaces; UAT-3 other 13 themes show zero regression. |
| B-109 | As a user, I want the group header text (group name) to be tinted toward the group's chosen color so that group identity reads even more strongly at a glance, while still clearing WCAG AA contrast against the tinted header background. | P3 | XS | done | 36 | B-104 ✅ (`--gc-<slot>` tokens), B-106 ✅ (18% header bg tint), B-114 (paired this sprint — bumps dark-theme tint to 22%). **Filed from product-owner UI/UX review post-v1.29.0.** | **R1 LOCKED DECISIONS (2026-04-26)**<br><br>**Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — visual refinement only. The change updates a single CSS `color` rule on the group-name span; no items, groups, bookmarks, tabs, or stored preferences are mutated. No confirmation dialog is introduced or required.<br><br>**Selector audit (rehome items)**: N/A — no DOM element is moved between surfaces. Only one CSS rule is added/modified in `sidepanel/sidepanel.css`. No existing test files reference `.group-header-name` color values in a way that would break.<br><br>**Q1 — Target selector: LOCKED → `.group-header-name`** (NOT `.group-header .group-name` as the original PROPOSED text incorrectly stated). The actual class name in `sidepanel/sidepanel.js:2196`-area `_buildGroupHeader` and `sidepanel/sidepanel.css:241` is `group-header-name` (verified against `sidepanel.css` lines 241–247). Sub-classes confirmed: `.group-header` (the row container), `.group-header-collapse` (the chevron — owned by B-115), `.group-header-name` (the text span — owned by B-109), `.group-header-count` (the item-count chip — out of scope for B-109).<br><br>**Q2 — Text color formula: LOCKED → `color: color-mix(in srgb, var(--group-header-color, var(--text-primary)) 50%, var(--text-primary));`** Single CSS rule on `.group-header-name`. Rationale for 50% (Option b but conservative): (i) the inner var fallback `var(--group-header-color, var(--text-primary))` collapses the formula to `var(--text-primary)` when `--group-header-color` is unset (Ungrouped section, or any group with no color slot) → 50% text-primary + 50% text-primary = `var(--text-primary)`, preserving the current Ungrouped contract without a separate selector; (ii) 50% mix gives noticeable group-color bias while preserving more text-primary luminance than 60% — important on dark themes after B-114's 22% bg tint, where the bg/text colors have less luminance separation; (iii) if R3 measurement shows headroom for 60%, R3 may bump the value, but starting at 50% is the conservative AA-safe lock. Reads `--group-header-color` already injected on parent `.group-header` by B-104 (`sidepanel.js:~2196`); CSS cascade flows to the `.group-header-name` child. Zero JS changes.<br><br>**Q3 — Ungrouped section behavior: LOCKED → falls back to `var(--text-primary)` automatically via the inner-var fallback in Q2's formula.** No separate selector or rule needed. Ungrouped's `.group-header` element does NOT have `--group-header-color` set inline (verified: only `_buildGroupHeader` injects the var when `group.color` is a valid slot; Ungrouped is a synthetic group with no color slot). The `var(--group-header-color, var(--text-primary))` fallback kicks in → mix collapses to `var(--text-primary)`.<br><br>**Q4 — Sub-group inheritance: LOCKED → sub-groups use their OWN color (not parent group's color).** Each sub-group's `.group-header` element has its own `--group-header-color` set inline by `_buildGroupHeader` based on its own `group.color`. The CSS cascade respects the inline declaration, so the sub-group's `.group-header-name` resolves to a `color-mix` against the sub-group's own group-color token. This matches the existing B-104 contract for sub-group bg tint (each sub-group renders with its own bg tint, not the parent's). If the user explicitly wants sub-groups to inherit the parent's color, that is a separate B-XXX follow-up.<br><br>**Q5 — Per-theme override mechanism: LOCKED → reserved as escape hatch but NOT shipped at lock time.** Following the same pattern as B-104 R2 D-5 (`--group-header-tint-amount`) and S35 B-105 (`--text-secondary` per-theme override), if R3 measurement finds any combination drops below 4.5:1 AA, R3 introduces a per-theme `--group-header-name-color` token override OR lowers the mix percentage. AC4 enforces the post-recompute floor.<br><br>**Q6 — WCAG AA verification scope: LOCKED → R3 recomputes contrast across the §47.7 spot-check matrix (20 representative cells) at the new text-color formula AGAINST the new B-114 dark-theme tinted bg.** R3 produces an updated table in `docs/design/47-b-104-themed-group-colors.md` (or a new chapter `docs/design/53-b-109-group-name-tint.md` cross-linked to B-104 §47.7). Verification scope: text color (new B-109 formula) vs bg color (new B-114 22% dark / 18% light tint over `--bg-secondary`) across all 20 spot-check cells. Worst-case dark cell guaranteed to be ≥ 4.5:1 OR R3 lowers the mix percentage / adds per-theme override per Q5.<br><br>**ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA — 5 ACs (R1 LOCKED 2026-04-26)**<br><br>**AC1 — `.group-header-name` color rule updated to consume the group-color formula.** `sidepanel/sidepanel.css` near line 241–247: add `color: color-mix(in srgb, var(--group-header-color, var(--text-primary)) 50%, var(--text-primary));` to the `.group-header-name` rule (or whatever percentage R3 settles on per Q2 / AC4). PASS = computed `color` of `.group-header-name` on a tinted group header resolves to a mix of the group's `--gc-<slot>` token + `--text-primary` for the active theme; FAIL = the rule is missing, the `.group-header-name` color stays at `inherit` / `var(--text-primary)` only, or a hardcoded color is used.<br><br>**AC2 — Ungrouped section keeps `var(--text-primary)` color.** For `.group-header` elements with no `--group-header-color` inline property set (Ungrouped section, or any synthetic group with no color slot), the `.group-header-name` color resolves to `var(--text-primary)` via the inner-var fallback. PASS = DevTools computed style on Ungrouped's `.group-header-name` resolves to the active theme's `--text-primary` value; FAIL = Ungrouped name shows tinted (non-primary) text or invalid CSS.<br><br>**AC3 — Sub-group `.group-header-name` text uses the sub-group's own color (not the parent's).** A sub-group with its own `group.color` slot renders the sub-group's `.group-header-name` text in a mix of the sub-group's `--gc-<slot>` + `--text-primary`. PASS = DevTools computed style on a sub-group's `.group-header-name` resolves to the sub-group's slot color (mixed with text-primary), independent of any parent group's color; FAIL = sub-group's name resolves to parent's color or an unrelated value.<br><br>**AC4 — WCAG AA verification: every `.group-header-name` × bg combination in the §47.7 spot-check matrix at the new text formula AND the new B-114 dark-theme tinted bg measures ≥ 4.5:1.** R3 recomputes contrast and records the new values in the design chapter. If any cell drops below 4.5:1, R3 lowers the mix percentage (e.g., from 50% to 40%) OR introduces a per-theme `--group-header-name-color` override per Q5. The locked formula is whatever R3 settles on after the matrix recompute. PASS = recomputed §47.7 matrix shows every group-name × bg cell ≥ 4.5:1; FAIL = any cell < 4.5:1 OR the matrix recompute is missing.<br><br>**AC5 — Test coverage: ≥ 3 tests in `tests/b109-group-name-tint.test.js`.** Required cases: (T1) tint formula present — parse the computed/applied CSS on `.group-header-name` and assert it contains `color-mix(in srgb` and `var(--group-header-color` (not just `var(--text-primary)` alone); (T2) Ungrouped fallback — synthesize a group header with NO `--group-header-color` inline and assert the resolved `color` matches the theme's `--text-primary` value; (T3) sub-group own-color — synthesize a sub-group `.group-header` with its own `--group-header-color: var(--gc-blue)` inline and assert the resolved `color` differs from the parent's color (proves Q4 inheritance contract). All tests use the existing `chrome-mock.js`; no ad-hoc stubs. PASS = ≥ 3 tests green + full suite zero regressions; FAIL = fewer tests, any failure, any regression.<br><br>**Out of scope (explicit):** (a) No change to `.group-header-count` (item-count chip keeps `var(--group-count-text)`). (b) No change to `.group-header-collapse` chevron — that color is owned by B-115 in this sprint. (c) No change to `--gc-<slot>` token values (B-104 contract preserved). (d) No new manifest permissions, no new message types, no storage schema changes, no JS changes — pure CSS-only item. (e) No change to font-size, font-weight, text-transform, or letter-spacing on `.group-header` / `.group-header-name`. (f) Approach (c) hand-curated per-theme `--gc-<slot>-text` tokens is NOT adopted at lock time — Q5 reserves the escape hatch for R3 if needed. |
| B-110 | As a user, I should NEVER see the drifted indicator on a bookmark that is NOT currently live (claimed by an open tab), because the §10.7 invariant states drift only exists for claimed items. **Currently observed bug**: drifted dotted bar visible on non-live bookmarks (image evidence from product-owner UI review, post-v1.29.0). | P2 | M | done | 36 | B-099 ✅ (drift detection + claim semantics), B-101 ✅ (drift bar visibility logic in `_ensureIndicators`). **Filed from product-owner UI/UX review of v1.29.0 — visual evidence shows drift bar on non-live row in side panel.** | **R1 LOCKED DECISIONS (2026-04-25)**<br><br>**Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — this is a bug fix with no user-destructive action paths. No items, groups, bookmarks, or tabs are created, modified, or deleted by this change. No destructive-action confirmation dialog is required or applicable.<br><br>**Selector audit (rehome items)**: N/A — not a rehome. No DOM elements move between surfaces. The `.item-drift-bar` element remains in the same position on each `.item-row`; only the condition that controls its `hidden` attribute changes. No existing test file selectors are invalidated by this item.<br><br>**Q1 — `_ensureIndicators` invariant gate: LOCKED → enforce `isDrifted && live` as the conjunctive guard for drift bar visibility.**<br><br>Currently, `_ensureIndicators` in `sidepanel/sidepanel.js` (~line 3201) sets `bar.hidden = !isDrifted` — it gates exclusively on `isDrifted`. The B-101 §48 design comment notes "drift records only exist for claimed items per §10.7, so no live/claim coupling is needed." However, the bug proves that stale drift records CAN persist after claim release (the §10.7 invariant is violated at the source). The defense-in-depth gate at the render layer is therefore mandatory regardless of whether the source leak is patched.<br><br>**LOCKED**: `_ensureIndicators` MUST set `bar.hidden = true` whenever EITHER `isDrifted === false` OR `live === false`. The drift bar must only show when BOTH conditions are true: `isDrifted === true` AND `live === true`. This is the defense-in-depth layer — even if a stale drift record incorrectly exists for an unclaimed item, the render path will not surface it.<br><br>The `isDrifted` value is already passed into `_ensureIndicators`. The `live` value is also already passed as its second argument. No new parameters or message round-trips are required — the fix is a single condition change at the bar visibility branch.<br><br>**Q2 — Source fix at the claim-clear leak: LOCKED → R2 [solution-architect] investigates and R3 patches the specific code path(s) where drift records survive after claim release.**<br><br>The defense-in-depth gate (Q1) is a hardening measure only — it prevents the symptom from reaching the user's screen. The proper resolution is to fix the root cause so orphaned drift records do not persist. R2 MUST audit all claim-release paths and identify which path(s) fail to call `clearDrift`. R3 MUST patch the identified source leak so that every claim-release path ends with a `clearDrift` call (or confirms a drift record cannot exist at that path).<br><br>**Candidate source paths for R2 audit** (investigation deferred to R2, not locked here):<br>- `tabs.onRemoved` → `releaseClaimByTab` → `clearDrift` chain (`tab-events.js:202-203`): appears wired correctly, but the `.catch` branch may silently swallow the `clearDrift` call on error.<br>- `windows.onRemoved` → cascade to `releaseClaimByTab`: verify `clearDrift` is also called for every itemId released in the window-close cascade.<br>- `reconcileClaims` cold-start eviction path: when a stored claim is discarded (phase-1 validation failure — tab no longer in LiveTabIndex), `clearDrift` is NOT called for the invalidated itemId. This is the highest-probability source of the stale-record bug.<br>- `MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM` handler (`storage-handlers.js:~311`): `clearDrift` IS called here. R2 confirms ordering is correct and no exception path can skip it.<br>- `MSG_UPDATE_ITEM` "Snap to this tab" inline drift-clear strategy (`storage-handlers.js:~187`): R2 confirms this path cannot leave a stale drift record on the old item if the item had drifted before the snap.<br><br>**Q3 — UAT repro scope: LOCKED → 4-step primary repro sequence + regression guard + multi-window check.**<br><br>Primary repro sequence: (1) Open a saved bookmark from the sidepanel (it becomes live, claimed by the opened tab). (2) In that tab, navigate to a different URL (bookmark is now drifted — dotted bar appears, `_cachedDriftRecords[itemId]` is populated). (3) Close the tab via the browser tab strip (NOT via the sidepanel X button). (4) Verify: (a) dotted drift bar disappears from the item row in the sidepanel; (b) item row is in the non-live state (no green border, no live indicator); (c) `chrome.storage.local` key `tj:drift` does NOT contain an entry for the item's id (confirm via DevTools → Application → Storage).<br><br>Regression guard: a live+drifted row (tab still open, drifted) CONTINUES to show the drift bar. B-099 + B-101 contract must be preserved.<br><br>Multi-window check: open sidepanel in two windows; in Window A open bookmark → navigate to drift → close tab; verify sidepanel in Window B also reflects cleared drift state.<br><br>**ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA — 6 ACs (R1 LOCKED 2026-04-25)**<br><br>**AC1 — `_ensureIndicators` enforces `isDrifted && live` conjunctive invariant.** The drift bar visibility branch in `sidepanel/sidepanel.js` (~line 3201) is changed from `if (isDrifted)` to `if (isDrifted && live)`. Setting `bar.hidden = true` when `live === false` regardless of `isDrifted` ensures the §10.7 invariant is enforced at the render layer. PASS = `bar.hidden === true` when `live = false && isDrifted = true` (stale-record scenario); `bar.hidden === false` when `live = true && isDrifted = true` (valid drift); `bar.hidden === true` when `live = true && isDrifted = false`; FAIL = drift bar visible on any non-live row.<br><br>**AC2 — Underlying claim-clear leak fixed at source (R2 identifies path, R3 patches it).** R2 [solution-architect] identifies the specific code path(s) in `background/tabs/` where claim release does NOT call `clearDrift`. R3 [frontend-engineer] patches all identified paths so that every claim-release path — including but not limited to `reconcileClaims` phase-1 eviction, `tabs.onRemoved` error-branch, and `windows.onRemoved` cascade — ends with a `clearDrift` call for any item whose claim is invalidated. PASS = after patch, `chrome.storage.local` `tj:drift` contains no entry for the item after its tab is closed (confirmed via UAT-1 + UAT-2); FAIL = stale drift record persists in `tj:drift` after tab close.<br><br>**AC3 — Regression: drift bar shows correctly on live+drifted bookmarks (B-099 + B-101 contract preserved).** A live drifted bookmark row continues to show the dotted drift bar with the correct tooltip. The `isDrifted && live` gate MUST NOT suppress the bar on live+drifted rows. PASS = dotted bar visible and tooltip present when `live = true && isDrifted = true`; FAIL = drift bar absent on a live drifted row.<br><br>**AC4 — Regression: `tabs.onRemoved` → `clearDrift` chain preserved (B-099 wiring at `tab-events.js:202-203`).** Closing a tab that claimed a drifted item via the browser tab strip continues to fire `releaseClaimByTab` → `clearDrift`. The chain must remain intact after R3 edits. PASS = `tj:drift` no longer contains the item's entry after tab close (same verification as AC2); FAIL = drift record survives tab close OR `clearDrift` call is accidentally removed from the `onRemoved` chain.<br><br>**AC5 — Test coverage: ≥ 5 tests in `tests/b110-drift-non-live-fix.test.js`.** Required test cases: (T1) Invariant gate — mock row with `isDrifted = true` and `live = false`; call `_ensureIndicators`; assert `bar.hidden === true`. Direct regression test for the bug. (T2) Bug reproduction documented — write a drift record to `_cachedDriftRecords` for an item, call `_ensureIndicators` with `isDrifted = true` and `live = false`; assert bar is hidden (assertion comment MUST note "pre-fix this test would have FAILED — bar.hidden was false"). (T3) Source-leak path (identified by R2) — test the specific claim-release path R2 identifies (e.g., `reconcileClaims` evicting a claim leaves no entry in `tj:drift` for the evicted item). Test name and exact assertion locked at R3 once R2 confirms the path. (T4) Source-leak second coverage case — e.g., cold-start reconciliation with pre-existing stale drift records in `chrome.storage.local` results in `tj:drift` cleared for all evicted claims. (T5) Regression guard for live+drifted — call `_ensureIndicators` with `isDrifted = true` and `live = true`; assert `bar.hidden === false` (B-099 + B-101 contract preserved). PASS = ≥ 5 tests green + full suite zero regressions; FAIL = fewer tests, any failure, any regression.<br><br>**AC6 — UAT plan: ≥ 4 cases.** [test-engineer] executes and records PASS/FAIL for each: (UAT-1) Primary repro — open bookmark → navigate to drift → close tab via browser tab strip → verify dotted bar disappears and item is non-live in sidepanel. (UAT-2) Storage verification — after UAT-1, confirm via DevTools Application → Storage → `tj:drift` contains no entry for the item's id. (UAT-3) Regression: live+drifted still shows — open bookmark → navigate to drift → leave tab open → verify dotted bar is visible and live indicators are present on the row. (UAT-4) Multi-window — sidepanel open in two windows; in Window A open bookmark → navigate away (drift) → close tab; verify sidepanel in Window B reflects cleared drift state (no dotted bar) within ~1 s of the broadcast. PASS = all 4 cases PASS; FAIL = any case shows drift bar on non-live row or missing drift bar on live+drifted row. |
| B-111 | As a user, I want the X-button icon on a bookmark row to visually reflect the action it will perform — show a "close tab" icon (X-style) when the bookmark is currently open (live), and a trash/delete icon when the bookmark is not open — so that the icon's meaning matches its behavior at a glance. | P3 | S | done | 36 | B-100 ✅ (live X-button now closes tab; non-live X-button deletes bookmark with modal), B-107 (paired this sprint — reactive aria-label flip; complementary not blocking). **Filed from product-owner UI/UX review post-v1.29.0.** | **R1 LOCKED DECISIONS (2026-04-26)**<br><br>**Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7) — RETAINED on the non-live path; N/A on the live path.** This item is icon-swap only. The B-100 v1.29.0 contract is preserved verbatim: live X-button → `MSG_CLOSE_TABS` (no confirmation; tab close is reversible via browser undo); non-live X-button → existing modal confirmation dialog before `MSG_DELETE_ITEM` (delete is destructive). **No new interstitial confirm is introduced** (product-owner LOCKED 2026-04-26). The icon swap reflects the action; it does NOT change the confirmation contract.<br><br>**Selector audit (rehome items)**: N/A — no DOM element is moved between surfaces. The `.item-action-delete` button stays at its current position in `.item-actions` on every `.item-row`. Two `<svg>` children replace the existing single `<svg>` inside the button. Grep of `tests/` for `item-action-delete`, `.delete`, and `deleteBtn` selector usage returned ZERO matches — no existing test files reference the SVG inside `.item-action-delete` directly. The B-100 tests (`tests/b100-delete-on-live.test.js`) test behavior (click → message dispatched), not the SVG markup, so this item does not require selector updates in any existing test file.<br><br>**Q1 — Live-state icon: LOCKED → simple X (two crossed lines).** SVG: `<svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 14 14" fill="none" aria-hidden="true" class="icon-action-close"><path d="M3 3l8 8M11 3l-8 8" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.4" stroke-linecap="round"/></svg>`. Rationale: (i) "X" is the universal close-tab metaphor used in the browser tab strip and elsewhere in the extension's UI; (ii) two-stroke line geometry matches the visual weight of the existing trash icon (1.2px stroke @ 14×14px); slightly heavier 1.4px stroke compensates for the minimal pixel coverage. The "close circle" (✕ in circle) and other variants are NOT adopted — extra geometry would compete visually with the favicon and indicators.<br><br>**Q2 — Non-live-state icon: LOCKED → keep the EXISTING trash SVG (NO change).** Verified: `sidepanel/sidepanel.js` line 2477 already renders a trash-with-lid SVG inside `.item-action-delete` for the default state. The current SVG is the correct non-live icon; B-111 does NOT replace it. The icon today shows on BOTH live and non-live rows; B-111 adds the X icon as a sibling and uses CSS to hide the trash on live rows (and hide the X on non-live rows).<br><br>**Q3 — Swap mechanism: LOCKED → pure CSS via `.item-row[data-live="true"]` attribute selector; both SVGs always present in the DOM.** No JS-set per-row-update overhead. The `data-live="true"` attribute is already mirrored on the row by the live-state patch loop in `sidepanel.js` (~line 3066) on every `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` response — the same attribute B-100 uses for the click-handler dispatch and B-107 uses for the aria-label flip. CSS rules added to `sidepanel/sidepanel.css` near `.item-action-delete`: `.item-action-delete .icon-action-close { display: none; }` (default — X hidden); `.item-row[data-live="true"] .item-action-delete .icon-action-close { display: inline-block; }` AND `.item-row[data-live="true"] .item-action-delete .icon-action-trash { display: none; }` (live — X visible, trash hidden); the existing trash SVG gets the `icon-action-trash` class. Default stays trash-visible (matches today's non-live default for newly-built rows). The CSS cascade handles every state transition for free; no JS swap on state flip.<br><br>**Q4 — Build-site insertion: LOCKED → `buildItemRow` (`sidepanel/sidepanel.js` line 2473–2480 area).** Replace the single `deleteBtn.innerHTML = '<svg ...trash...>'` with two `<svg>` elements appended via `innerHTML` containing both icons (or via two `appendChild` calls if XSS hardening prefers — `innerHTML` is acceptable here since the markup is a static literal with no interpolation). Both SVGs MUST carry `aria-hidden="true"` (B-100 contract preserved — the row's aria-label provides the AT-visible name; the icon is decorative).<br><br>**Q5 — Open-tab row coverage: LOCKED → APPLIES.** Verified: `sidepanel.js` line 2703 builds a separate `row.className = 'item-row'` for open-tab rows (`buildOpenTabRow` or equivalent), and these rows ALSO have the `.item-action-delete` button (per B-022 scope). Open-tab rows are by definition live (the row represents a real open tab) — they MUST get `data-live="true"` set on the row so the CSS selector triggers the X icon. R3 verifies the open-tab build path also sets `data-live="true"` and applies the same two-SVG pattern; if open-tab rows currently lack `data-live`, R3 adds it (this is a B-100 dependency — open-tab X-button closes the tab too).<br><br>**Q6 — Software switch / runtime-mutability check (R2 C-12)**: N/A. No `manifest.json` declaration is introduced or modified. The icon swap is pure CSS + a small build-site change.<br><br>**ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA — 6 ACs (R1 LOCKED 2026-04-26)**<br><br>**AC1 — `.item-action-delete` contains both SVG icons (`icon-action-close` + `icon-action-trash`) on every saved-item row build path.** After R3, every `.item-action-delete` button has exactly two `<svg>` children: the X icon (`.icon-action-close`) and the trash icon (`.icon-action-trash`). PASS = `row.querySelectorAll('.item-action-delete svg').length === 2` for every `.item-row`; FAIL = either icon missing or extra SVGs present.<br><br>**AC2 — Default state (non-live row) shows the trash icon; X is hidden via CSS.** For a row with NO `data-live` attribute or `data-live="false"`, the `.icon-action-trash` is visible (`display: inline-block` or default) and the `.icon-action-close` is hidden (`display: none`). PASS = computed style on `.icon-action-trash` is visible AND `.icon-action-close` is `display: none` for a non-live row; FAIL = the trash is hidden, the X is visible, or both are visible simultaneously.<br><br>**AC3 — Live state (`data-live="true"` on row) shows the X icon; trash is hidden via CSS.** When the row has `data-live="true"`, the `.icon-action-close` becomes visible and `.icon-action-trash` becomes `display: none`. PASS = computed style on `.icon-action-close` is `display: inline-block` AND `.icon-action-trash` is `display: none` when `row.dataset.live === 'true'`; FAIL = the X stays hidden on live rows or both icons render.<br><br>**AC4 — Reactive flip on live-state change (no JS overhead per state change).** When a row's `data-live` attribute toggles `true ↔ false` (driven by a `MSG_LIST_ITEMS` patch loop in `sidepanel.js` ~line 3066), the icon visibility flips automatically via the CSS attribute selector — no JS code runs in the icon-swap path. PASS = mutate `row.dataset.live = 'true'` in a test and assert the X icon is now visible; mutate back to `'false'` and assert the trash is visible; both happen synchronously without any JS callback firing. FAIL = the swap requires a JS path or the CSS does not update on attribute mutation.<br><br>**AC5 — Click handler behavior unchanged (B-100 v1.29.0 contract verbatim).** No change to the X-button click handler. Live-row click → `MSG_CLOSE_TABS` (no confirmation, B-100 contract). Non-live click → existing modal confirmation → `MSG_DELETE_ITEM` (B-100 contract). The existing modal-confirm flow on non-live deletion is RETAINED (DoR item 7). PASS = clicking X on a live row dispatches `MSG_CLOSE_TABS` with no modal; clicking X on a non-live row opens the existing confirmation modal; FAIL = either path's behavior changes.<br><br>**AC6 — Test coverage: ≥ 4 tests in `tests/b111-dynamic-delete-icon.test.js`.** Required cases: (T1) both SVGs present — build a saved-item row and assert `row.querySelectorAll('.item-action-delete svg')` has length 2 with the expected classes; (T2) default visibility — non-live row, assert `.icon-action-trash` resolves visible AND `.icon-action-close` resolves `display: none` (read computed style or assert the CSS rule presence); (T3) live visibility — set `row.dataset.live = 'true'` and assert `.icon-action-close` resolves visible AND `.icon-action-trash` resolves `display: none`; (T4) reactive flip — start non-live, mutate to live, assert visibility flipped; mutate back to non-live, assert visibility flipped back. PASS = all 4 tests green + full B-100 click-handler suite zero regressions; FAIL = fewer tests, any failure, or any B-100 contract regression.<br><br>**Out of scope (explicit):** (a) NO change to confirmation contract (modal on non-live delete is RETAINED per DoR item 7 + product-owner LOCKED 2026-04-26). (b) NO change to the X-button click handler. (c) NO change to aria-label (owned by B-107 in this sprint — B-111 + B-107 may share the live-state patch loop touch site but address different attributes). (d) NO change to the edit button or its icon. (e) NO new manifest permissions, no new message types, no storage schema changes. (f) "Close circle" (✕ in circle) and other live-icon variants NOT adopted — Q1 simple X is locked. |

No other CSS rule targets `.panel-header-title`.<br><br>**Q2 — Keyboard navigation order: LOCKED → no change.** `<span class="panel-header-title">` is a non-interactive element with no `tabindex` attribute (confirmed by inspection of `sidepanel.html:17`). Removing it does not affect the tab order. R3 confirms: (a) no `tabindex` on the removed element; (b) R4 [qa-reviewer] verifies the tab order through the header (filter input → add-bookmark → add-group → settings button) is preserved after the change.<br><br>**ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA — 4 ACs (R1 LOCKED 2026-04-25)**<br><br>**AC1 — `<span class="panel-header-title">Tab Junkie</span>` removed from `sidepanel/sidepanel.html`.** After R3, `grep -n "panel-header-title" sidepanel/sidepanel.html` returns zero matches. PASS = DOM inspection shows `#filter-input`, `#add-bookmark-btn`, `#add-group-btn`, and `#sidepanel-settings-btn` all present in `#panel-header`; all four controls function correctly in UAT; FAIL = any control absent, non-functional, or repositioned to a different container.<br><br>**AC3 — Keyboard tab order through the header is preserved.** The focus sequence through the header controls is unchanged: `#filter-input` → `#filter-clear-btn` (when visible) → `#add-bookmark-btn` → `#add-group-btn` → `#sidepanel-settings-btn`. No new focusable element is introduced. PASS = Tab key traversal through the header visits exactly the expected controls in order; FAIL = any control skipped, any new control inserted, or any order change.<br><br>**AC4 — Test coverage ≥ 2 tests in `tests/b112-header-label-removed.test.js`.** Required cases: (T1) regression guard — `#panel-header` DOM does NOT contain any element with class `panel-header-title` or text content "Tab Junkie"; (T2) sibling elements guard — `#filter-input`, `#add-bookmark-btn`, `#add-group-btn`, and `#sidepanel-settings-btn` are all present in `#panel-header` after the change. All tests use the existing `chrome-mock.js`; no ad-hoc stubs. PASS = ≥ 2 named tests green + full suite zero regressions; FAIL = fewer tests or any regression. |
| B-113 | As a user, I want the checkbox slot on each item row to be replaced with a drag handle (`⋮⋮`) icon that ONLY appears on hover, and have the checkbox appear in the same slot ONLY when multi-select mode is active, so that the row is cleaner when not interacting AND the affordances match the user's current intent (drag = single-row interaction, checkbox = multi-select mode). | P3 | S | done | 36 | B-024 ✅ (multi-select + bulk action bar), B-030 ✅ (item drag-reorder), B-048 ✅ (`.item-select` checkbox affordance — B-113 modifies its hover-reveal contract). **Filed from product-owner UI/UX review post-v1.29.0 — current behavior shows non-clickable checkbox border on hover.** | **R1 LOCKED DECISIONS (2026-04-26)**<br><br>**Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — visual affordance refinement only. The change adds a new `.item-drag-handle` element, scopes the existing `.item-select` hover-reveal CSS rule to multi-select mode, and adjusts the hover/multi-select visibility cascade. No items, groups, bookmarks, tabs, drag-reorder operations, or selection state mutations are introduced or altered. The B-030 drag-reorder click/drag handlers are untouched; the B-024 multi-select toggle handlers are untouched.<br><br>**Selector audit (rehome items)**: N/A — no DOM element is moved between surfaces. The `.item-select` element stays at its current position (first flex child of `.item-row`); a new sibling `.item-drag-handle` is added, absolutely-positioned over the same slot. Tests reviewed: `tests/b048-visual-states.test.js` (heavy `.item-select` references) — all assertions are STRUCTURAL (`role="checkbox"`, `aria-checked`, `tabindex`, `aria-hidden` invariants + slot-existence guarantee), NOT behavioral about hover-reveal visibility. The existing AC6 hover-reveal contract is INTENTIONALLY MODIFIED by B-113; the b048 tests do not directly assert visibility-on-hover, so no existing test selectors break. R3 updates the b048 file's header comment (lines 18–19) to note B-113's modification of the AC6 contract.<br><br>**Q1 — Drag handle icon: LOCKED → SVG with 6 circles in a 2×3 grid (visual cohesion with `.group-drag-handle`).** Reuse the same SVG pattern from `_buildGroupHeader` (`sidepanel/sidepanel.js` line 2173) but scaled to fit the 14×14 px item-select slot: `<svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 16 16" fill="currentColor" aria-hidden="true"><circle cx="5" cy="4" r="1.5"/><circle cx="5" cy="8" r="1.5"/><circle cx="5" cy="12" r="1.5"/><circle cx="11" cy="4" r="1.5"/><circle cx="11" cy="8" r="1.5"/><circle cx="11" cy="12" r="1.5"/></svg>`. Rationale: matching `.group-drag-handle` visual signature reinforces "drag handle" semantics across both group and item surfaces. Plain Unicode glyphs (`⋮⋮`, `⋮`, `≡`) are NOT used — Unicode rendering varies across font stacks and produces inconsistent visual weight against the SVG-rendered group handle.<br><br>**Q2 — Reveal mechanism: LOCKED → pure CSS `:hover` opacity transition; no JS hover handlers.** New `.item-drag-handle` element appended to every `.item-row` in `buildItemRow` (`sidepanel/sidepanel.js` line ~2390 area, immediately after the `.item-select` append). Element: `<span class="item-drag-handle" aria-hidden="true">[SVG]</span>`. CSS rules added to `sidepanel/sidepanel.css` near the `.item-select` block (~line 1366):<br>`.item-drag-handle { position: absolute; left: 12px; top: 50%; transform: translateY(-50%); width: 14px; height: 14px; display: inline-flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center; color: var(--text-secondary); opacity: 0; pointer-events: none; transition: opacity 0.15s; }` (the `left: 12px` value matches the `.item-row` padding-left + `.item-select` flex slot leading edge — R3 verifies the actual offset from inspecting the rendered layout).<br>`.item-row:hover .item-drag-handle { opacity: 1; }`<br>The existing `.item-row:hover .item-select { visibility: visible; }` rule (`sidepanel.css` line 1401) is SCOPED to multi-select mode by changing the selector to `#item-list.has-bulk-bar .item-row:hover .item-select`. The `.item-row:focus-visible .item-select` and `.item-row[data-selected="true"] .item-select` rules are PRESERVED unchanged.<br>New rule for multi-select-active: `#item-list.has-bulk-bar .item-row .item-select { visibility: visible; }` — once any row is selected, ALL rows persistently show their checkboxes (Gmail pattern preserved).<br>New rule to hide drag handle in multi-select mode: `#item-list.has-bulk-bar .item-row:hover .item-drag-handle { opacity: 0; }`.<br>`.item-row` already has `position: relative` (per the drift bar comment at `sidepanel.css:~445`) so the absolute-positioned drag handle anchors correctly. The `.item-select` slot stays at `flex: 0 0 18px; visibility: hidden` by default — B-048 §31.5 AC6 no-reflow guarantee is preserved (the drag handle overlays the slot via absolute positioning; the slot's flex reservation is unchanged).<br><br>**Q3 — Multi-select mode detection: LOCKED → reuse the existing `#item-list.has-bulk-bar` class (set in `_updateBulkBar`, `sidepanel.js:1862`-1863).** No new state variable or class is introduced. The `has-bulk-bar` class is added when `_selection.size > 0` and removed when empty. CSS rules in Q2 use `#item-list.has-bulk-bar .item-row …` to scope hover behavior to multi-select mode. NO JS changes to `_updateBulkBar` or selection state ownership. Trade-off: the class name is "has-bulk-bar" (named after the bar visibility, not the multi-select mode); leaving the name as-is avoids touching every test that may reference it. Future renaming to a more semantically accurate `multi-select-active` class is a follow-up if needed.<br><br>**Q4 — Drag origin: LOCKED → drag continues to work from ANYWHERE on the `.item-row` (NO change to B-030 contract).** The new `.item-drag-handle` is purely a VISUAL AFFORDANCE — it is `pointer-events: none` so clicks pass through to the underlying `.item-row` (which preserves its existing `draggable=true` + drag handlers from B-030). The user can drag from the handle area visually, but the actual drag is initiated by the row's existing handler. R3 verifies B-030 drag-reorder behavior is preserved: drag from row text, drag from favicon area, drag from handle area — all initiate the same drag operation.<br><br>**Q5 — Accessibility: LOCKED → `aria-hidden="true"` on `.item-drag-handle`; NO new keyboard affordance.** The drag handle is decorative (the row's existing `aria-label` already conveys "Drag to reorder (keyboard reorder not yet available)" per `sidepanel.js:2345`). Adding `tabindex` on the handle would create a confusing additional tab stop — the handle is a visual signal only. Keyboard reorder is NOT introduced by B-113 — same constraint as B-030 (the row's title text "keyboard reorder not yet available" remains accurate). If keyboard reorder is desired, it is a separate B-XXX follow-up (NOT in this sprint).<br><br>**Q6 — Interaction with item icon (favicon): LOCKED → favicon stays as-is.** The `.item-favicon` (or `.item-avatar` fallback) is at flex-position 2 (after `.item-select` at flex-position 1). The drag handle absolutely-positions over the `.item-select` slot ONLY — it does NOT cover, replace, or interact with the favicon. The favicon remains the second visual element on every row.<br><br>**Q7 — Software switch / runtime-mutability check (R2 C-12)**: N/A. No `manifest.json` declaration is introduced or modified. The change is one new DOM element + 4 CSS rule changes.<br><br>**Q8 — Open-tab row coverage: LOCKED → APPLIES.** Verified: `sidepanel.js` line 2703 builds open-tab rows with `class="item-row"` and the same `.item-select` checkbox affordance (per the comment at line 2718). Open-tab rows participate in B-024 multi-select. R3 adds the `.item-drag-handle` to the open-tab build path as well, and verifies the same visibility cascade applies. Open-tab rows are also draggable per B-030 v2 (verify against current code; if open-tab rows are NOT draggable, the handle should still appear on hover for visual consistency, OR we omit the handle on open-tab rows and document the asymmetry — R3 picks based on observed B-030 contract for open-tab rows).<br><br>**ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA — 7 ACs (R1 LOCKED 2026-04-26)**<br><br>**AC1 — `.item-drag-handle` element appended to every `.item-row` (saved-item AND open-tab build paths).** After R3, every row built by `buildItemRow` AND every open-tab row built by the open-tab builder (`sidepanel.js:2703`-area) contains a single `.item-drag-handle` child element with the locked SVG markup and `aria-hidden="true"`. PASS = `row.querySelectorAll('.item-drag-handle').length === 1` for every `.item-row` (saved + open-tab); FAIL = handle missing on any row OR more than one handle present.<br><br>**AC2 — Default state (no hover, no multi-select, no selection) renders empty slot — both checkbox and drag handle hidden.** For a row with NO `:hover`, NO `data-selected`, and NOT inside `#item-list.has-bulk-bar`, the `.item-select` resolves `visibility: hidden` AND `.item-drag-handle` resolves `opacity: 0`. The 18 px flex slot remains reserved (no reflow). PASS = both elements computed-style invisible AND `.item-row` width unchanged from current baseline; FAIL = either element visible at rest, or row width changes.<br><br>**AC3 — Hover-only behavior (no multi-select active) reveals drag handle, NOT checkbox.** When `:hover` is active on a `.item-row` AND `#item-list` does NOT have `has-bulk-bar` class AND the row is NOT `[data-selected="true"]`, the `.item-drag-handle` resolves `opacity: 1` AND the `.item-select` resolves `visibility: hidden`. PASS = drag handle visible on hover, checkbox stays hidden; FAIL = checkbox shows on hover when no selection exists, OR drag handle does not show on hover.<br><br>**AC4 — Multi-select active (`#item-list.has-bulk-bar`) reveals checkbox on ALL rows; drag handle hidden everywhere.** When `#item-list` has class `has-bulk-bar` (set by `_updateBulkBar` when `_selection.size > 0`), every `.item-select` in the list resolves `visibility: visible` (regardless of hover) AND every `.item-drag-handle` resolves `opacity: 0` (even on hover). PASS = all checkboxes visible + no drag handle visible when at least one row is selected; FAIL = drag handle still appears on hover during multi-select OR checkbox doesn't persist across all rows.<br><br>**AC5 — Selected row (`[data-selected="true"]`) shows checkbox persistently (B-024 contract preserved).** Independent of multi-select mode classes, any row with `data-selected="true"` shows its `.item-select` with `visibility: visible` AND `.item-select[aria-checked="true"]` background. PASS = selected row's checkbox is filled and visible; FAIL = selected row's checkbox stays hidden or aria-checked mismatch.<br><br>**AC6 — B-030 drag-reorder contract preserved (drag works from anywhere on the row, including over the new handle area).** No change to `.item-row[draggable=true]` handlers. Drag initiation from row text, favicon, OR the new drag handle area all dispatch the same B-030 drag-reorder flow. The `.item-drag-handle` has `pointer-events: none` so clicks pass through. PASS = drag-and-drop reorder works from all three surface areas (row text, favicon, handle); FAIL = drag breaks from any of those areas OR clicks on the handle do not dispatch to the row's drag handler.<br><br>**AC7 — Test coverage: ≥ 5 tests in `tests/b113-drag-handle-multi-select.test.js`.** Required cases: (T1) handle element present — assert `.item-drag-handle` exists on every `.item-row`; (T2) default state — synthesize a row without hover/selection/multi-select and assert handle `opacity: 0` AND checkbox `visibility: hidden`; (T3) hover-only — simulate `:hover` (or assert the CSS rule via parsed CSS source) and assert handle `opacity: 1` + checkbox `visibility: hidden` when `#item-list` has NO `has-bulk-bar` class; (T4) multi-select active — set `#item-list.classList.add('has-bulk-bar')` and assert checkbox `visibility: visible` for ALL rows + handle `opacity: 0` even on hover; (T5) selected-row regression — `[data-selected="true"]` row shows checkbox persistently with `aria-checked="true"`. R3 may add a 6th test verifying `.item-drag-handle` has `pointer-events: none` to enforce AC6 (drag passthrough). All tests use the existing `chrome-mock.js`; no ad-hoc stubs. PASS = ≥ 5 tests green + full B-024 multi-select suite + full B-030 drag-reorder suite + full B-048 visual-states suite zero regressions; FAIL = fewer tests, any failure, or any contract regression.<br><br>**B-048 §31.5 AC6 contract change note (intentional):** B-113 INTENTIONALLY modifies the B-048 §31.5 AC6 hover-reveal contract for `.item-select`. Before B-113: "checkbox is visible on `:hover` of any row." After B-113: "checkbox is visible on `:hover` ONLY when multi-select is active (`#item-list.has-bulk-bar`); otherwise, hover reveals the drag handle." R3 updates `tests/b048-visual-states.test.js` header comment (lines 18–19) to document this contract change. The `:focus-visible` and `[data-selected="true"]` clauses of the original AC6 are PRESERVED.<br><br>**Out of scope (explicit):** (a) NO change to `_updateBulkBar` or selection state ownership. (b) NO change to B-030 drag-reorder JS handlers (drag continues to work from anywhere on the row). (c) NO change to B-024 multi-select toggle behavior or keyboard shortcuts. (d) NO new keyboard accessibility for drag-reorder — keyboard reorder is still "not yet available" per the existing row title. (e) NO new `manifest.json` permissions, no new message types, no storage schema changes. (f) NO change to favicon, indicators strip, or item-actions row positioning. (g) Renaming `has-bulk-bar` → `multi-select-active` is NOT in scope (Q3 trade-off — semantically accurate name is a future follow-up). (h) NO change to the existing `.group-drag-handle` element (only the SVG markup pattern is reused). |
| B-114 | As a user (especially on dark themes), I want the group header background tint to be brighter than the v1.29.0 default so that group identity reads more strongly, with per-theme calibration if needed since dark themes show less of the group color through the current 18% mix. | P3 | XS | done | 36 | B-104 ✅, B-106 ✅ (12% → 18% baseline). **Filed from product-owner feedback during S36 planning: "very dark in dark modes; want to see it brighter."** | **R1 LOCKED DECISIONS (2026-04-26)**<br><br>**Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — visual refinement only. The change updates per-theme `--group-header-tint-amount` token values; no items, groups, bookmarks, tabs, or stored preferences are mutated. No confirmation dialog is introduced or required.<br><br>**Selector audit (rehome items)**: N/A — no DOM element is moved between surfaces. Only CSS custom-property values change in `shared/themes.css`. No existing test files reference theme-specific tint-amount values.<br><br>**Q1 — Tint strategy: LOCKED → Approach 2 (per-theme overrides on the existing `--group-header-tint-amount` token).**<br><br>The escape-hatch variable was reserved in S34 B-104 R2 D-5 and shipped via S34 B-104 R3-fix H-1 (solarized-light: 0%) + S35 B-105 R1 (solarized-light: 3%). It is the established mechanism for per-theme tint calibration. `:root --group-header-tint-amount` stays at `18%` (B-106 baseline) — this value continues to govern all light themes (`github-light`, `tomorrow`, `atom-one-light`, `light` legacy alias) and the system theme on the light prefers-color-scheme branch. The B-105 `solarized-light: 3%` override is preserved.<br><br>Dark themes get an explicit override to `22%` to deliver the brighter visual the product owner requested. The `22%` value is the WCAG AA ceiling per B-106 §47.7 spot-check matrix — at the original `18%` baseline the worst-case cell (`atom-one-dark` + `yellow`) measures `4.78:1` (0.28 above the WCAG AA 4.5:1 floor). R3 verifies the new value retains ≥ 4.5:1 across the worst-case cells; if R3 measurement shows the ceiling is actually below `22%`, R3 lowers the dark-theme override to whatever value preserves AA on every algorithmic cell and notes the deviation in the R6 close.<br><br>**Q2 — Per-theme override list: LOCKED.** Add `--group-header-tint-amount: 22%` to each of the following 11 selectors in `shared/themes.css`: (1) `[data-theme="github-dark"]`, (2) `[data-theme="tomorrow-night"]`, (3) `[data-theme="atom-one-dark"]`, (4) `[data-theme="solarized-dark"]`, (5) `[data-theme="dracula"]`, (6) `[data-theme="nord"]`, (7) `[data-theme="one-dark"]`, (8) `[data-theme="monokai"]`, (9) `[data-theme="tokyo-night"]`, (10) `[data-theme="dark"]` (LEGACY ALIASES block — mirrors atom-one-dark), (11) `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) { [data-theme="system"] { ... } }` (system dark-OS branch).<br><br>**Q3 — WCAG AA verification scope: LOCKED → R3 recomputes the §47.7 spot-check matrix at the new dark-theme tint values.** R3 produces an updated table in `docs/design/47-b-104-themed-group-colors.md` §47.7 (extend with a "post-B-114" column, OR add a new sub-section). The verification covers the same 20-row spot-check sample defined in §47.7 — re-tabulating cells whose theme is now at `22%`. AC4 enforces the post-recompute floor: every dark-theme cell ≥ 4.5:1.<br><br>**Q4 — Software switch / runtime-mutability check (R2 C-12)**: N/A. This change is purely declarative CSS. No `manifest.json` declaration is introduced or modified; no runtime toggle mechanism is involved. The user picks their theme via the existing settings; the new tint amount applies on the next paint after the theme change.<br><br>**ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA — 5 ACs (R1 LOCKED 2026-04-26)**<br><br>**AC1 — Per-theme `--group-header-tint-amount: 22%` override added to all 11 dark-theme selectors.** After R3, each of the 11 selectors enumerated in Q2 contains a `--group-header-tint-amount: 22%` declaration. PASS = `grep -c "group-header-tint-amount: 22%" shared/themes.css` returns `11` (or `--group-header-tint-amount: <Nval>%` returns `11` if R3 lowered the value per AC4); FAIL = any dark theme missing the override or wrong value.<br><br>**AC2 — Light themes + system-light retain `--group-header-tint-amount: 18%` (B-106 baseline preserved).** `:root` keeps `18%`. The 4 light themes (`github-light`, `tomorrow`, `atom-one-light`, `light` legacy alias) and the system theme on the light branch inherit `18%` — no per-theme override added. PASS = light themes render with the same 18% tint as v1.29.0; FAIL = light themes change tint amount inadvertently.<br><br>**AC3 — `solarized-light --group-header-tint-amount: 3%` override is preserved (B-105 contract).** The B-105 R1 override is untouched. PASS = `[data-theme="solarized-light"] { --group-header-tint-amount: 3%; }` block is unchanged; FAIL = the override is removed, modified, or shadowed by another rule.<br><br>**AC4 — WCAG AA verification: every algorithmic cell in the §47.7 spot-check matrix at the new dark-theme tint measures ≥ 4.5:1 (worst-case `atom-one-dark` + `yellow`).** R3 recomputes the matrix and records the new contrast values in the design chapter. If any cell drops below 4.5:1, R3 lowers the dark-theme override (to `21%` or below) and re-verifies — the locked override value is whatever R3 settles on after the matrix recompute. PASS = recomputed §47.7 matrix shows every cell ≥ 4.5:1 at the chosen dark-theme override; FAIL = any cell < 4.5:1 OR the matrix recompute is missing.<br><br>**AC5 — Test coverage: ≥ 3 tests in `tests/b114-tint-v2.test.js`.** Required cases: (T1) dark-theme override verification — parse `shared/themes.css` and assert each of the 11 dark-theme selectors contains `--group-header-tint-amount: <locked-value>%` (the value R3 settles on per AC4); (T2) light-theme baseline guard — assert `:root` still declares `--group-header-tint-amount: 18%` and none of the 4 light themes + `light` legacy alias add a per-theme override; (T3) regression guard — `[data-theme="solarized-light"]` still declares `--group-header-tint-amount: 3%` (B-105 contract preserved). PASS = ≥ 3 tests green + full suite zero regressions; FAIL = fewer tests, any failure, any regression.<br><br>**Out of scope (explicit):** (a) No change to the `:root --group-header-tint-amount: 18%` baseline value (light themes stay as-is per AC2). (b) No change to light-theme tint values (only dark themes get the brighter override). (c) No change to `solarized-light: 3%` (B-105 contract preserved per AC3). (d) No change to `--gc-<slot>` token values (B-104 contract preserved). (e) No new manifest permissions, no new message types, no storage schema changes, no JS changes — pure CSS-only item. (f) Approach 3 (separate light/dark variables with `prefers-color-scheme` selector) is NOT adopted — Approach 2 reuses the existing tint token mechanism and avoids splitting the design surface. |
| B-115 | As a user, I want a clearer visual indicator that items belong to a group so that the group→item relationship reads at a glance — starting with brightening the group-header expand/collapse chevron (`▶` / `▼`) buttons so that the open/closed state of a group is more visible. | P3 | XS | done | 36 | B-008 ✅ (group reorder + collapse persistence), B-104 ✅ (group color tokens). **Filed from product-owner feedback during S36 planning: "items in a group are hard to identify as part of the group; might bring color of group down into items, OR simply start with making the chevron buttons brighter."** | **R1 LOCKED (2026-04-25)**<br><br>**Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — visual refinement only. Chevron color is a purely cosmetic CSS property change. No bookmark, group, or preference data is mutated. No confirmation dialog is introduced or required.<br><br>**Selector audit (rehome items)**: N/A — no DOM element is moved between surfaces. The `.group-header-collapse` chevron element remains in place; only its `color` CSS rule is updated. No existing test files reference `.group-header-collapse` color values in a way that would break.<br><br>**Q1 — Chevron color approach: LOCKED → (b) themed `var(--gc-<slot>)` via `--group-header-color` fallback chain.** The chevron (`.group-header-collapse`) will use `color: var(--group-header-color, var(--text-primary))`. When a group has a color assigned, JS already sets `--group-header-color: var(--gc-<slot>)` on the `.group-header` element (sidepanel.js line 2196, B-104). The `.group-header-collapse` inherits `--group-header-color` from its parent `.group-header` via the CSS cascade, so no additional JS is needed. Uncolored groups (e.g., Ungrouped, or groups with no slot assigned) have no `--group-header-color` set → chevron falls back to `var(--text-primary)` — always visible at primary text contrast. Rationale for themed choice: the chevron is the highest-information-density pixel on a collapsed group header; tinting it to match the group's identity color (already used in the full-bleed header tint via B-104 + B-114) makes the group color span across the entire header surface, reinforcing the group→item relationship at a glance. WCAG AA trade-off: WCAG 1.4.11 Non-text Contrast does not apply to "decorative" elements; the chevron SVG conveys state (`aria-expanded` attribute is the authoritative state signal on `.group-header`), making it supplemental/decorative under WCAG criteria. Accepted.<br><br>**Q2 — Implementation mechanism: LOCKED → single CSS rule change only.** Change `.group-header-collapse { color: var(--collapse-icon); }` (sidepanel.css line 232) to `color: var(--group-header-color, var(--text-primary))`. No JS changes needed — `--group-header-color` is already injected on the parent `.group-header` by B-104 (sidepanel.js line 2196). The CSS cascade propagates the token to the child `.group-header-collapse` span. Zero changes to sidepanel.js, background scripts, or manifest.<br><br>**Q3 — Out of scope: LOCKED.** Approach 2 (bringing group color into item rows) is explicitly deferred. If B-115 + B-114 combined visual impact is still insufficient after UAT, file a new B-116 in a future sprint. Do NOT scope-creep into item row tinting in this item. No change to chevron icon shape (SVG unchanged). No change to expand/collapse logic or `aria-expanded` handling. No change to keyboard behavior.<br><br>**ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA — 4 ACs (R1 LOCKED 2026-04-25)**<br><br>**AC1 — Chevron color rule updated to consume `--group-header-color`.** `sidepanel/sidepanel.css` line 232: `color: var(--collapse-icon)` is replaced with `color: var(--group-header-color, var(--text-primary))` on the `.group-header-collapse` rule. PASS = the computed `color` of `.group-header-collapse` on a tinted group header resolves to the group's `--gc-<slot>` token value for the active theme; FAIL = the rule still references `--collapse-icon`, or a hardcoded hex/static token is used instead.<br><br>**AC2 — Untinted groups fall back to `var(--text-primary)`.** For `.group-header` elements with no `--group-header-color` inline property set (i.e., Ungrouped section, or any group with no color slot assigned), the chevron color resolves to `var(--text-primary)` — the default text color for the active theme. PASS = DevTools computed style on Ungrouped's `.group-header-collapse` shows `color` resolving to `var(--text-primary)` value; FAIL = chevron shows an invisible, white, or wrong color on untinted groups.<br><br>**AC3 — Chevron remains keyboard-accessible; `aria-expanded` reflects state correctly (regression guard).** The `.group-header` element retains `role="button"`, `tabindex="0"`, and `aria-expanded` attribute toggling on click and keyboard Enter/Space (existing behavior from B-008). `.group-header-collapse` retains `aria-hidden="true"`. The CSS `transform: rotate(-90deg)` rule on `.group-header[aria-expanded="false"] .group-header-collapse` is untouched. PASS = clicking or pressing Enter on a group header toggles `aria-expanded` and shows/hides items as before; assistive technology announces group name + expanded/collapsed state; FAIL = any regression in keyboard focus, `aria-expanded` toggling, or the rotation animation.<br><br>**AC4 — Test coverage: ≥ 2 tests in `tests/b115-chevron-color.test.js`.** Required cases: (T1) Chevron CSS rule references `--group-header-color` — parse the computed/applied CSS on `.group-header-collapse` and assert it contains `var(--group-header-color` (not `var(--collapse-icon)`); (T2) Regression guard — collapse/expand toggle on a group header still correctly sets `aria-expanded` and shows/hides the items container (ensures the CSS color change did not inadvertently break any JS behavior). All tests use the existing `chrome-mock.js`; no ad-hoc stubs. PASS = both tests green + full suite zero regressions; FAIL = either test fails or any regression in the full suite.<br><br>**Out of scope (explicit):** (a) No change to chevron SVG shape or size. (b) No change to expand/collapse logic in `toggleGroup()` (sidepanel.js). (c) No change to `aria-expanded` handling or keyboard behavior. (d) No change to `--collapse-icon` token values in `shared/themes.css` (token stays for any other potential future consumers). (e) Approach 2 (item-row color tinting) is NOT in scope — defer to future B-116. (f) No new manifest permissions, no new message types, no storage schema changes. |
| B-104 | As a user, I want the group header to be visually tinted with the group's chosen palette color (not just a small chip), AND I want the 9-color group palette to resolve to theme-aware CSS custom properties (so my "red" group looks Dracula-red in Dracula and GitHub-red in GitHub Light), so that group identity is more visible at a glance and group colors look harmonious within the active theme. | P2 | M | done | 34 | B-006 ✅ (group palette enforcement, 9 colors), B-037 ✅ (14-theme system + `shared/themes.css` token catalog). **Bundles two related polish ideas: (1) tint group header bg/border with the picked color (currently just a small chip), (2) resolve the 9 semantic palette slots to per-theme CSS custom properties so colors stay harmonious across themes.** | **R1 LOCKED DECISIONS (2026-04-25)**<br><br>**Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — visual refinement only. No items, groups, or bookmarks are mutated by this feature. Color picker writes are reversible by re-picking. No confirmation dialog is introduced or required.<br><br>**Selector audit (rehome items)**: N/A — this item is not a rehome (no DOM elements move surfaces). B-104 adds new CSS custom properties to `shared/themes.css` and new CSS rules targeting existing `.group-header`, `.newtab-group-header`, and `.gj-color-chip` selectors. No existing test selectors are invalidated; the new `.group-header--colored` modifier class (or equivalent inline `--group-color` property) is additive. No existing test files reference the affected selectors in a way that would break.<br><br>**Q1 — Header tint treatment: LOCKED → full-bleed low-opacity background tint via CSS custom property.** Implementation: each surface's group header CSS gains `background: color-mix(in srgb, var(--group-header-color, transparent) 12%, var(--bg-secondary))` (sidepanel) or the surface-equivalent background token. The `--group-header-color` inline property is set on the header element by JS, consuming the new per-slot, per-theme token (e.g., `var(--gc-red)` for a red group in the active theme). Rationale: option (a) full-bleed tint was chosen over (b) top-border, (c) left-border-extend, and (d) gradient stripe because: (1) it provides the richest at-a-glance group identity — the entire header surface changes, not a 3 px edge indicator; (2) 12% opacity keeps the tint subtle enough that header text remains clearly readable without per-theme WCAG audits on the header background (the base `--bg-secondary` dominates); (3) `color-mix` is available in Chromium 111+ (MV3 minimum target); (4) it avoids left-border collision with the B-101 drift bar (which occupies the row left-edge gutter below the header, not the header itself — but keeping the gutter unambiguous is still desirable). Trade-off accepted: tint at 12% is subtle on light themes; R5 UAT will confirm visibility is sufficient.<br><br>**Q2 — Slot naming scheme: LOCKED → 9 slots as stored slot names (not hex).** Verified in `shared/constants.js` line 5–7: `GROUP_COLORS = Object.freeze(['blue', 'purple', 'teal', 'red', 'orange', 'pink', 'indigo', 'yellow', 'slate'])`. Current hardcoded hex values (sidepanel.css lines 37–45): blue `#2563eb`, purple `#7c3aed`, teal `#0d9488`, red `#dc2626`, orange `#ea580c`, pink `#db2777`, indigo `#4f46e5`, yellow `#ca8a04`, slate `#64748b`. These 9 slot names are the storage identity. The new tokens use the naming pattern `--gc-<slot>` (e.g., `--gc-blue`, `--gc-red`) defined per `[data-theme]` block in `shared/themes.css`. Count: 9 slots × 14 themes = 126 token values.<br><br>**Q3 — Migration of existing group colors: LOCKED → no migration needed.** Colors are stored as slot names (verified: `background/storage/groups.js` imports `GROUP_COLORS` from `shared/constants.js` and uses `GROUP_COLORS.includes(color)` for validation — slot names only, never hex). Existing groups already store `color: "red"` etc. No read-time normalization or storage migration is required. `background/storage/groups.js` requires no changes in this item beyond confirming the allowlist is sufficient (it is — 9 slots match exactly).<br><br>**Q4 — Coverage scope: LOCKED → sidepanel + newtab + group-jump popup (all three group-rendering surfaces).** Rationale: all three surfaces render group headers (`sidepanel/sidepanel.css` line 190 `.group-header`, `newtab/newtab.css` line 230 `.newtab-group-header`, `popup/group-jump-popup.css` line 349 `.gj-color-chip`). Shipping tinted headers on only one surface would produce inconsistent group identity — the user's "red" group would be tinted in sidepanel but chip-only in newtab and the popup. Group-jump popup: the existing `.gj-color-chip` renders `background-color: var(--gj-group-color, var(--color-avatar-bg))` where `--gj-group-color` is currently set via JS `chip.style.setProperty('--gj-group-color', pickerRow.color)` using the raw slot-name string (not a valid CSS color). R3 must change this to set `--gj-group-color` to the resolved token value (e.g., `var(--gc-red)`) so the chip reads from the per-theme token. Sidepanel color picker swatches are also in scope per Q6.<br><br>**Q5 — Per-theme color authoring approach: LOCKED → hybrid (hand-curate 4 flagship themes; algorithmic `color-mix` fallback for remaining 10).** The 4 hand-curated themes are: `one-dark`, `dracula`, `github-light`, `system` (these are the most visually distinct and most used, per B-037 design notes). For these 4 themes, R3 [frontend-engineer] authors 9 hex values per theme (36 values) using each theme's published palette (Dracula's red is `#ff5555`, Nord's red is `#bf616a`, etc.) to maximise harmony. For the remaining 10 themes (`github-dark`, `tomorrow`, `tomorrow-night`, `atom-one-light`, `atom-one-dark`, `solarized-light`, `solarized-dark`, `nord`, `monokai`, `tokyo-night`), the token values are derived by `color-mix(in srgb, <canonical-slot-hex> 70%, var(--bg-secondary) 30%)` — this lightens/darkens the canonical value to blend with each theme's base, producing harmonious mids without fully muddy results. Final call on this split (which of the 10 remaining get hand-curated vs. algorithmic) is deferred to R2 [solution-architect] who must review perceived quality during the R2 spike. R3 must not use hardcoded hex for any token — only the authored hex values inside `shared/themes.css`.<br><br>**Q6 — Picker swatch theme-awareness: LOCKED → yes, picker swatches consume the new `--gc-<slot>` tokens.** Current state: `sidepanel/sidepanel.js` `_buildGroupColorSwatches` (line 628–647) creates swatch buttons with class `group-color-swatch group-color-<slot>`; `sidepanel.css` lines 37–45 define `.group-color-<slot> { background-color: <hardcoded-hex> }`. This means the swatch always shows the hardcoded hex regardless of the active theme. R3 must change the swatch CSS rule to `background-color: var(--gc-<slot>)` (inline style or CSS variable on each swatch element) so the swatch reflects the per-theme resolved color — the user picks what they see. The `group-color-<slot>` class-per-swatch pattern is retained for the CSS targeting; only the `background-color` value changes from hardcoded hex to a `var(--gc-<slot>)` token reference.<br><br>**ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA — 9 ACs (R1 LOCKED 2026-04-25)**<br><br>**AC1 — Tinted group header background renders on all three surfaces.** `sidepanel/sidepanel.js` sets `--group-header-color: var(--gc-<slot>)` as an inline CSS custom property on every `.group-header` element based on the group's stored color slot. `sidepanel/sidepanel.css` adds `background: color-mix(in srgb, var(--group-header-color, transparent) 12%, var(--bg-secondary))` to `.group-header`. Equivalent tint rules added to `.newtab-group-header` and `.gj-color-chip`. PASS = group headers are visually tinted with a low-opacity version of the group's palette color on sidepanel, newtab, and group-jump popup across all 14 themes; FAIL = any surface still shows an untinted `var(--bg-secondary)` / `var(--bg-primary)` header for colored groups, or the chip-only treatment persists as the only group color affordance.<br><br>**AC2 — 126 `--gc-<slot>` tokens defined across all 14 themes in `shared/themes.css`.** Each `[data-theme="…"]` block gains 9 new custom properties: `--gc-blue`, `--gc-purple`, `--gc-teal`, `--gc-red`, `--gc-orange`, `--gc-pink`, `--gc-indigo`, `--gc-yellow`, `--gc-slate`. For the 4 hand-curated themes (`one-dark`, `dracula`, `github-light`, `system`), values are authored as theme-harmonious hex strings. For the remaining 10 themes, values are either hand-curated or derived per the Q5 hybrid approach (R2 makes the final per-theme call). No `[data-theme="light"]` or `[data-theme="dark"]` block is added — those alias to `atom-one-light` and `one-dark` respectively and inherit their tokens. PASS = `grep -c 'gc-blue' shared/themes.css` returns 14 (one per real theme block); FAIL = fewer than 14 or any token missing from any theme.<br><br>**AC3 — Picker swatches consume `--gc-<slot>` tokens (no hardcoded hex in swatch CSS).** `sidepanel/sidepanel.css` lines 37–45 (`.group-color-<slot> { background-color: <hex> }`) are replaced with `background-color: var(--gc-<slot>)` references. The swatch class names (`group-color-blue`, `group-color-red`, etc.) are retained for selector targeting. PASS = after theme switch, swatches in the group create/edit dialog re-render with the new theme's slot colors without any JS or dialog re-open; a `grep -n 'background-color: #' sidepanel/sidepanel.css` on the swatch block returns zero hardcoded hex hits; FAIL = any swatch still uses a literal hex value.<br><br>**AC4 — No storage migration required; existing groups render correctly without data changes.** Groups already store `color: "red"` (slot name), not hex. R3 does not modify `background/storage/groups.js`, `shared/constants.js`, or any stored group record. PASS = the extension can be loaded over an existing profile with groups already created (no migration step runs, no console errors, all group headers tint correctly); FAIL = any storage write occurs as part of B-104, or a group with a valid slot name fails to render a tinted header.<br><br>**AC5 — WCAG AA contrast spot-check across a representative sample (~20 of 126 combinations).** R5 [test-engineer] spot-checks 20 theme × slot combinations: all 9 slots in `one-dark` and `github-light` (18 checks) + `--gc-yellow` in `solarized-light` + `--gc-slate` in `dracula` (2 edge cases). For each: verify that the group header name text (rendered in `var(--text-primary)`) meets WCAG AA contrast ratio ≥ 4.5:1 against the tinted header background. PASS = all 20 spot-checks pass AA; FAIL = any spot-check below 4.5:1 (R3 must adjust the affected token value or the `color-mix` opacity).<br><br>**AC6 — Zero schema migration; color slot names are the sole stored identity.** `background/storage/groups.js` `validateGroup` continues to accept only the 9 slot names in `GROUP_COLORS`.
