| B-102 | As a user with multiple windows open, I want demote / delete-bookmark actions to broadcast correctly to ALL windows so that the bookmark's appearance is consistent across surfaces, instead of vanishing entirely from non-originating windows while showing as Open-Tabs-only on the originating window. | P2 | M | done | 35 | B-014 ✅ (multi-window awareness), B-050 ✅ (state broadcast). **Cross-window broadcast bug filed from S33 B-099 UAT-13 user feedback.** Bug: after demote, originating window correctly shows item moved to Open Tabs; other windows show item COMPLETELY GONE from both groups + Open Tabs. | **Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — B-102 is a bug fix to the broadcast propagation layer. The destructive-action confirmation UX for demote (existing modal/X-button flow) is NOT modified by this item. B-100 owns any UX changes to the demote confirmation pattern. B-102 only repairs cross-window state convergence after the action has already been confirmed and dispatched.<br><br>**Selector audit (rehome items)**: N/A — no DOM elements are moved between surfaces in this item. The fix is entirely in the broadcast/receiver layer (`background/broadcast.js`, `background/messages/storage-handlers.js`, and/or `sidepanel/sidepanel.js` broadcast receiver). No existing test file selectors are invalidated.<br><br>**R1 LOCKED DECISIONS (2026-04-26)**<br><br>**Q1 — Expected post-demote state across ALL windows: LOCKED.** After `MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM` completes in the service worker, every open sidepanel window — originating AND non-originating — MUST reach the same end-state within ~500 ms of the broadcast: (a) the demoted item is ABSENT from its group section in the items list; (b) the tab formerly claimed by that item IS visible in the Open Tabs section (assuming the tab is still alive in that window's browser session); (c) no stale item row persists in any group, and no tab row is silently omitted from the Open Tabs section. This is the authoritative post-demote convergence contract. R2 root-causes which code path breaks this contract on non-originating windows — the fix must make all windows converge to this same state.<br><br>**Q2 — Multi-window UAT requirement: LOCKED.** R5 [test-engineer] MUST verify the bug fix against a real browser session with at least 2 sidepanel windows open simultaneously. `chrome-mock` can simulate broadcast delivery and refetch, but the observed symptom (item GONE from both group AND Open Tabs on non-originating windows) may have a DOM-execution-order or receiver-side cache-miss manifestation that only surfaces in a real multi-window browser session. UAT must include: (1) open 2+ sidepanel windows side by side; (2) demote a live bookmark in Window A; (3) verify within ~500 ms that Window B (non-originating) shows the item gone from its group AND the tab visible in Open Tabs; (4) verify Window A (originating) shows the same end-state. The multi-window UAT case is a MANDATORY gate — the item cannot be marked done on automated tests alone.<br><br>**Q3 — Scope boundary: LOCKED.** In scope: `MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM` cross-window broadcast propagation — specifically ensuring non-originating windows refetch BOTH the `items` scope AND the `openTabs` data after a demote broadcast, and that the receiver correctly updates the Open Tabs DOM (not just the item list DOM). Also in scope: the equivalent "delete bookmark on live item" path if B-100's R3 wires its new action through `MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM` or a new message type that also requires cross-window broadcast repair (R2 confirms). Out of scope: `MSG_DELETE_ITEM` for non-live items (assumed working; any fix belongs in a separate item); `MSG_UPDATE_ITEM` broadcast propagation (B-099); `MSG_PROMOTE_TAB` duplicate-row bug (B-103 — separate item). If R2 discovers that `MSG_DELETE_ITEM` for non-live items ALSO fails to converge across windows, [scrum-master] creates a new backlog item — do NOT expand B-102 scope mid-sprint.<br><br>**R2 INVESTIGATION HINTS (for [solution-architect])**<br><br>**Hint 1 — `diffAndPatch` 'patch' branch does NOT call `patchOpenTabsSection`.** In `sidepanel/sidepanel.js` lines ~5082-5123, the `delta.deltaType === 'patch'` branch calls `_patchSingleRow` for each changed item and then `_setCachedOpenTabs(itemsResp.openTabs)` (cache updated), but it does NOT call `patchOpenTabsSection(_cachedOpenTabs)`. When `MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM` produces a `delta.kind === 'removed'` delta, the item row is removed from the group DOM by `_patchSingleRow`, but the Open Tabs DOM is NEVER refreshed. The `renderAll` fallback (line 5128) DOES call `patchOpenTabsSection` indirectly, but only if `patched === false`. If the delta is processed cleanly as a 'patch' (likely on non-originating windows where cache is clean and no sortOrder change exists), `patched === true` and the Open Tabs DOM is silently stale. Fix candidate: add `patchOpenTabsSection(_cachedOpenTabs)` after the patch loop when `scope === 'items'` and `patched === true`.<br><br>**Hint 2 — Originating window may fall through to `renderAll` for a different reason.** The originating window dispatches `MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM` then receives the broadcast. If its `_cachedItems` was mutated locally (e.g., a drag state left `_cachedItemsGen` stale, or the sortOrder check at line 5089 triggers), it may fall through to `renderAll` — which rebuilds everything including Open Tabs via `renderAll(…, itemsResp.openTabs)`. Non-originating windows have clean cache and cleanly enter the 'patch' branch, skipping `renderAll` and thus skipping the openTabs DOM update. R2 must confirm whether this is the divergence point.<br><br>**Hint 3 — Broadcast delivery scope is NOT the issue.** `background/broadcast.js` uses `chrome.runtime.sendMessage` which delivers to ALL `runtime.onMessage` listeners (all open sidepanel contexts). The `sender.id !== chrome.runtime.id` guard in `sidepanel.js:4993` is correct — all messages from the SW pass this check. The `scope === 'items'` branch fires for `MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM` (per `MUTATION_BROADCASTS` table in `storage-handlers.js` line 118). Delivery is not the issue; receiver-side DOM update is.<br><br>**ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA — 6 ACs (R1 LOCKED 2026-04-26)**<br><br>**AC1 — Demote broadcast reaches non-originating windows within ~500 ms.** After `sendMessage(MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM, { itemId })` resolves in the originating window's sidepanel, all other open sidepanel windows MUST have received and processed the `MSG_STATE_CHANGED { scope: 'items' }` broadcast and completed their DOM update within ~500 ms. PASS = non-originating window DOM reflects updated state within 500 ms of action; FAIL = state update absent, delayed > 500 ms, or requires manual sidepanel refresh. (This AC guards broadcast delivery — if R2 discovers broadcast is NOT reaching non-originating windows, the fix is in `background/broadcast.js`; if it IS reaching them, proceed to AC2.)<br><br>**AC2 — Non-originating windows refetch BOTH items AND openTabs scopes on broadcast.** When a non-originating window receives `MSG_STATE_CHANGED { scope: 'items' }`, its broadcast handler MUST: (a) call `sendMessage(MSG_LIST_ITEMS)` to retrieve fresh item list AND the enriched `itemsResp.openTabs` field; (b) update `_cachedOpenTabs` via `_setCachedOpenTabs(itemsResp.openTabs)`. No code path on the receiver side may silently skip the openTabs refetch when processing an items-scope broadcast. PASS = `_cachedOpenTabs` on the non-originating window is updated within the same broadcast-handler promise chain as the items update; FAIL = `openTabs` field silently ignored or cache not updated.<br><br>**AC3 — Demoted item's tab appears in the Open Tabs DOM section in ALL windows (assuming tab is alive).** After the demote action + broadcast cycle: (a) the `patchOpenTabsSection(_cachedOpenTabs)` function (or equivalent `renderAll` path) is called on every sidepanel receiver — including non-originating windows — so the Open Tabs DOM reflects the newly-unclaimed tab; (b) the tab row for the formerly-demoted item's tab appears in the Open Tabs section with the correct `data-tab-id` and title; (c) this holds in BOTH the `diffAndPatch` 'patch' branch AND the `renderAll` fallback branch — neither branch may skip the openTabs DOM update. PASS = Open Tabs section shows the demoted tab on all windows; FAIL = tab absent from Open Tabs on any non-originating window OR Open Tabs section is visually stale (showing pre-demote state).<br><br>**AC4 — Demoted item is REMOVED from its group section in ALL windows.** After the demote action + broadcast cycle: (a) the item row with `data-item-id="<demotedItemId>"` is removed from the `.group-items` container in every open sidepanel; (b) no ghost row persists for the demoted item in any group on any window; (c) if the group becomes empty after the demote, the empty-state for that group renders correctly on all windows. PASS = `querySelectorAll('.item-row[data-item-id="<id>"]')` returns empty NodeList on all windows after demote; FAIL = row persists on any non-originating window OR empty-state does not render for a now-empty group.<br><br>**AC5 — Regression: originating-window behavior unchanged.** After the B-102 fix, the originating window's demote behavior MUST be identical to its pre-fix behavior: item removed from group, tab visible in Open Tabs (assuming tab alive). The fix must not break the originating window's current (correct) behavior by changing code paths it relies on. PASS = originating window shows same end-state as pre-fix baseline AND passes existing `b099-drift-fix.test.js` T8 (explicit demote regression test) unchanged; FAIL = originating window regresses to showing item in group OR Open Tabs section is broken post-fix on the originating window.<br><br>**AC6 — Test coverage ≥ 5 tests in `tests/b102-cross-window-demote.test.js`.** Required tests (using existing `chrome-mock.js`; no ad-hoc stubs):<br>(T1) `MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM` handler → `broadcast(SCOPE.ITEMS)` fires with correct scope (regression guard — broadcast scope unchanged).<br>(T2) Broadcast receiver: after items-scope broadcast with a 'removed' delta, `_cachedOpenTabs` is updated AND `patchOpenTabsSection` is called (or `renderAll` is called) — neither is skipped when `patched === true`.<br>(T3) Simulated non-originating window: receive `MSG_STATE_CHANGED { scope: 'items' }` with demoted-item as removed delta → item row absent from DOM AND Open Tabs row present for the formerly-claimed tab (multi-context simulation via chrome-mock).<br>(T4) `patchOpenTabsSection` correctness: given a fresh `openTabs` array that now includes a formerly-claimed tab, the function adds the tab row to the Open Tabs DOM and updates the count badge.<br>(T5) Full pipeline regression: `MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM` → broadcast → items-scope receiver → `renderAll` fallback path → Open Tabs section contains the demoted tab. PASS = ≥ 5 named tests green + full suite zero regressions; FAIL = fewer tests, any test red, or any regression in the existing suite.<br><br>**Out of scope (explicit):** (a) Any change to the demote confirmation UX (button label, modal, toast) — that is B-100. (b) `MSG_DELETE_ITEM` cross-window behavior — assumed working; any issue is a new backlog item. (c) `MSG_PROMOTE_TAB` duplicate row bug — B-103. (d) Any change to `MUTATION_BROADCASTS` scope mappings (the `SCOPE.ITEMS` mapping for `MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM` is correct). (e) Popup surfaces — popup does not render item rows and is not affected by this bug. |

| B-099 | As a user, I want my saved bookmark to keep its association with its claimed tab even when the tab navigates to a different URL (drift), so that the bookmark visibly indicates "drifted" instead of disappearing into the Open Tabs section as an orphaned live tab. Plus, when a bookmark is drifted, I want a one-click action to update the bookmark's saved URL to the current tab URL, so that I can easily reset the bookmark to the page I'm now looking at. | P1 | M | in-progress | 33 | B-011 ✅ (drift detection), B-026 ✅ (item context menu — host for the new action). **Bug fix + UX polish** — drift behavior has been broken since Sprint 1 (B-001d): `reevaluateTab` releases the claim on URL mismatch BEFORE `detectDriftForTab` runs, which causes the original item to lose its drift indicator AND the tab to appear as orphaned in Open Tabs. **Option B (never release on URL change)** is the locked behavior per product-owner direction. Pre-S33 conversation captures the full options analysis (A/B/C). | **Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: **RETAINED** — the "Snap to this tab" action permanently overwrites `item.url` in persistent storage. An undo-capable inline toast (5-10 s window) is the confirmation affordance. Pattern follows B-070 plain-language toast precedent. Full rationale under Q2 below.<br><br>**R1 LOCKED DECISIONS (2026-04-25)**<br><br>**Q1 — Action label: LOCKED → "Snap to this tab"**. Rationale: "Save current page" implies creating something new (confusing for an existing bookmark). "Update bookmark URL" is accurate but 4-word technical; user mental model is "snap the bookmark to where I am now." "Snap to this tab" is concise (4 words), directional (it's the tab that the bookmark is being snapped to), and consistent with Tab Junkie's tab-centric language. "Reset bookmark to current tab" is wordy and "reset" implies rollback. LOCKED: "Snap to this tab".<br><br>**Q2 — Confirmation pattern: LOCKED → inline toast with "Undo" button (5-10 s)**. Rationale: A modal requires an extra click and breaks flow; the action is low-blast-radius (URL is overwritten but no item is deleted, and undo reverses it). Toast pattern matches B-070 precedent. The undo action dispatches `MSG_UPDATE_ITEM { id, patch: { url: originalUrl } }` and also re-writes the drift record (SW side: because the URL reverts, `detectDriftForTab` will re-detect drift on the next tab-update event naturally, OR the undo handler explicitly re-writes the drift record). LOCKED: toast + Undo.<br><br>**Q3 — Drifted-to URL surface: LOCKED → title attribute tooltip on the drift indicator icon (`.item-drifted-icon` in sidepanel; `newtab-indicator-drifted` in newtab)**. Rationale: Inline text ("drifted to: github.com/foo") is prohibited in dense layout (B-092 line-height budget). Context-menu-only hides the URL from users who don't right-click. Hover tooltip on the drift indicator is zero layout impact, works in all 14 themes, and is already the pattern for the audible icon's aria-label. Implementation: `title="Drifted to: <hostname>"` on the drift indicator element (hostname only — not full URL — to keep the tooltip concise). Full `driftedToUrl` also visible in the context menu entry's secondary line (AC5). LOCKED: tooltip (hostname) on drift indicator icon.<br><br>**Q4 — Drift indicator visual treatment: LOCKED → warning-triangle SVG icon (existing `.item-drifted-icon`) + CSS `color: var(--color-warning)` (orange/amber on all 14 themes) + `title` tooltip (Q3) + `font-size` bumped from current 14 px to 16 px in sidepanel, kept 12 px in newtab (dense indicator row)**. Rationale: The current warning-triangle SVG already exists in `_createDriftedIcon` and `_ensureIndicators` at 14 px. Increasing to 16 px gives the icon more presence alongside the green live-dot and the window badge without breaking any layout (indicators container is flex-row, width is not capped). The `--color-warning` CSS variable already exists across all 14 themes for B-092 dense-row warnings; reusing it guarantees WCAG AA contrast without per-theme audits. No new color token is introduced. LOCKED: 16 px triangle + `--color-warning` + tooltip.<br><br>**Q5 — Indicator priority when live + active + audible + drifted simultaneously: LOCKED → additive, drift indicator displayed last in the indicators strip (after window badge, audible icon), with NO suppression of other indicators**. Rationale: Drift is a state alert on the saved item; the live/active/audible indicators describe the current live-tab state — all four coexist. The user needs to know the tab is active (green dot, or window badge), playing audio (audible icon), AND drifted (warning triangle) simultaneously. Priority order in DOM left-to-right: window badge → audible icon → drift icon. This matches the existing `buildItemRow` DOM order in `sidepanel.js` (window badge prepended, audible appended, drift appended last). PASS also = no drift clear occurs when `patch` does not include `url` (non-regression guard); FAIL = spurious drift clears on title-only or order-only patches.<br><br>**AC8 — Confirmation toast with Undo affordance.** After "Snap to this tab" click: (1) the action dispatches immediately (no pre-action modal); (2) `showToast('Bookmark snapped to current tab', { undoLabel: 'Undo', onUndo: () => sendMessage(MSG_UPDATE_ITEM, { id: itemId, patch: { url: originalUrl } }) })` is called within the same click handler; (3) the undo lambda captures `originalUrl` (the pre-snap item URL, read from `_itemById` before dispatch); (4) the toast is visible for 5-8 s then auto-dismisses. Undo reversal: dispatches `MSG_UPDATE_ITEM { id, patch: { url: originalUrl } }`; this does NOT re-write the drift record (the item URL returns to its saved value, which matches the original saved URL, so `clearDrift` in AC7 does not trigger again — drift will be re-detected on the next tab URL event if the tab is still at the drifted URL). PASS = toast with "Undo" appears immediately after snap; Undo click reverts URL; FAIL = modal shown, or toast absent, or Undo not reverting URL.<br><br>**Part 3 — Drift indicator visual prominence**<br><br>**AC9 — Drift indicator sizing and color across sidepanel and newtab.** `sidepanel/sidepanel.css` (or inline in `_createDriftedIcon`): `.item-drifted-icon svg` width/height 16 px (bumped from 14 px). `color: var(--color-warning)` applied via CSS class `.item-drifted-icon` (inherits from the element's color). `newtab/newtab.css` `.newtab-indicator-drifted` retains 12 px dot size (newtab dense row is constrained). Spot-check across all 14 themes: `--color-warning` is defined in `shared/themes.css` for every theme; no new token needed. PASS = drift icon renders at 16 px in sidepanel, `color` resolves to the theme's warning amber/orange; FAIL = drift icon same size as audible (14 px) or using a hard-coded hex color.<br><br>**AC10 — Drifted-to URL visible in tooltip on the drift indicator.** `_createDriftedIcon` in `sidepanel/sidepanel.js` and `_ensureIndicators` are updated to accept and set `title="Drifted to: <hostname>"` on the drift icon element. Hostname is extracted from `driftedToUrl` via `new URL(driftedToUrl).hostname` (with try/catch fallback to the raw URL if parsing fails). Same hostname-only pattern applied to the newtab `.newtab-indicator-drifted` dot via `aria-label` and `title`. PASS = hovering the drift indicator shows a browser-native tooltip with "Drifted to: github.com" (hostname of the drifted URL); FAIL = tooltip absent or showing full raw URL including path/query.<br><br>**Part 4 — Tests + UAT**<br><br>**AC11 — Automated tests: ≥ 8 new tests in `tests/b099-drift-fix.test.js`.** Required test cases:<br>(T1) URL-change on claimed tab → claim preserved, drift record written (`reevaluateTab` + `detectDriftForTab` integration).<br>(T2) Return-to-saved-URL on drifted tab → drift record cleared (`detectDriftForTab` AC2/AC4 path).<br>(T3) Tab close on claimed+drifted tab → claim released + drift record cleared (`tabs.onRemoved` path).<br>(T4) `MSG_UPDATE_ITEM` with `patch.url` equal to `driftedToUrl` → `updateItem` called + drift record cleared (AC7).<br>(T5) `MSG_UPDATE_ITEM` with `patch` NOT containing `url` → drift record NOT cleared (regression guard, AC7).<br>(T6) `buildOpenTabs()` excludes a tab that is claimed by a drifted item (AC4 regression guard).<br>(T7) Re-claim contention: claimed tab navigates to another item's URL → original claim preserved, new item unclaimed (AC2).<br>(T8) Explicit demote (`MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM`) on drifted item → drift record cleared, claim released (existing path, regression guard). All tests use the existing `chrome-mock.js`; no ad-hoc stubs. PASS = ≥ 8 named tests green + zero regressions in full suite; FAIL = fewer tests or any regression.<br><br>**AC12 — UAT plan `docs/UAT_B-099.md` with ≥ 8 cases.** Required UAT scenarios:<br>(UAT-1) Drift + reload: bookmark claimed, tab navigates to different URL → bookmark shows drift indicator, NOT orphaned in Open Tabs.<br>(UAT-2) Drift + navigate back: drifted tab navigates back to saved URL → drift indicator clears automatically.<br>(UAT-3) Drift + "Snap to this tab": drifted bookmark → right-click/ellipsis → "Snap to this tab" → toast appears, drift indicator clears, saved URL updated in edit dialog.<br>(UAT-4) Undo snap: "Snap to this tab" → Undo → URL reverted to original saved URL, drift indicator reappears.<br>(UAT-5) Re-claim contention: two bookmarks for the same URL → claimed bookmark drifts → second bookmark does NOT gain claim on the tab.<br>(UAT-6) Demote drifted item: "Close tab & unsave" on a drifted item → drift cleared, tab closes or is released.<br>(UAT-7) Drift across windows: claimed tab moved to a second window → drift still tracked on original bookmark.<br>(UAT-8) Indicator spot-check: drifted + audible simultaneously → both warning triangle and audible icon visible; drifted + active → live green dot AND warning triangle visible. PASS = all 8 UAT cases PASS; FAIL = any case FAIL.<br><br>**Part 5 — Out of scope**<br><br>**AC13 — The following are explicitly OUT OF SCOPE for B-099:** (a) A push notification or browser badge when drift is detected. (b) A "Restore tab to bookmark URL" action (navigating the live tab back to the saved URL). (c) "Save as new bookmark" from the drift indicator. (d) Drift record auto-expiry after N hours/days. (e) An aggregate "N items drifted" count badge anywhere in the UI. (f) A dedicated "Drifted Items" section or filter in the sidepanel. All of (a)-(f) are deferred to the icebox and must not be included in the B-099 R3 build. |

| B-091 | As a user, I want a redesigned full-page Settings surface that hosts theme selection, display-mode preference, layout density, sub-group auto-collapse, and import/export controls in one cohesive view, so that I have a single discoverable home for all configuration. | P2 | L | done | 30 | B-089 (modal deprecated/repurposed); B-038, B-040 (existing prefs to migrate). **Spike-First — R0 spike resolves D-1 surface strategy.** Wave 0 anchor; Wave 1 consumers: B-092 dense layout, B-093 import/export rehome (S30); B-037 themes (S31). | **Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — B-091 is read/write on the existing prefs partition plus tab dispatcher; no items, groups, or bookmark data are mutated, and pref changes are reversible. The Data section (import/export) preserves the existing B-070 §AC4 "Replace all data?" preview-and-confirm dialog without modification; that confirmation flow is owned and enforced by B-093 — B-091 only hosts the section container.<br><br>**AC1 — Surface (D-1 locked: Candidate B).** Settings opens via `chrome.tabs.create({ url: chrome.runtime.getURL('settings/settings.html') })`. This is the R0 spike §44.2 binding decision. No deviation permitted without a new R0 spike. PASS = clicking the gear button opens a full Edge/Chrome tab at `chrome-extension://<id>/settings/settings.html`; FAIL = any other surface (modal, panel takeover, popup window).<br><br>**AC2 — Tab dispatcher: focus existing else create new.** The gear button click handler fires `chrome.tabs.query({ url: chrome.runtime.getURL('settings/settings.html') })`; if a matching tab is found, it calls `chrome.tabs.update(tabId, { active: true })` + `chrome.windows.update(windowId, { focused: true })` (focus-existing path); if no matching tab is found, it calls `chrome.tabs.create({ url: chrome.runtime.getURL('settings/settings.html') })` (create-new path). Dispatcher failure (e.g., `chrome.tabs.create` rejects) surfaces a toast via the sidepanel's existing toast infrastructure — the gear button must not appear broken. Pattern mirrors §41 B-035 D-3 option (c) precedent. PASS = second gear click focuses the existing Settings tab rather than opening a duplicate; FAIL = multiple Settings tabs open simultaneously, or a dispatcher failure is silently swallowed.<br><br>**AC3 — Page structure.** Three new files are created: `settings/settings.html`, `settings/settings.js`, `settings/settings.css`. One additional file: `settings/theme-init.js` (verbatim copy of `sidepanel/theme-init.js`, per §42 newtab precedent — FOUC prevention). HTML structure: `<head>` loads `settings/theme-init.js` before stylesheet (FOUC guard); `<body>` contains a `<main role="main">` landmark with an `<h1>` page title ("Settings") and exactly five `<fieldset>` section blocks, each with a `<legend>` heading. PASS = all four files present; `<main>`, `<h1>`, and five `<fieldset>` elements present in DOM on load; FAIL = missing file, missing landmark, wrong element count.<br><br>**AC4 — Section ordering (locked top-to-bottom).** Sections render in this fixed order, each as a `<fieldset class="settings-section">` with `<legend class="settings-section-legend">`: (1) **Display** — hosts B-038 `displayMode` select; (2) **Layout** — hosts B-092 `denseLayout` toggle (placeholder row rendered by B-092 Wave 1); (3) **Groups** — hosts B-040 `autoCollapseSubGroups` toggle; (4) **Theme** — placeholder only in Sprint 30 (see AC9); (5) **Data** — hosts B-093 import/export controls (placeholder container rendered by B-093 Wave 1). PASS = sections present in this exact DOM order with these exact legend text values; FAIL = wrong order, missing section, or different heading copy.<br><br>**AC5 — Existing prefs migrated into the Settings page (Wave 0).** B-038's `displayMode` select row (label: "Open Tab Junkie on click", options: "Side panel" / "Standalone window", default: "sidepanel") and B-040's `autoCollapseSubGroups` toggle row (label: "Auto-collapse sub-groups", default: false) are registered in `settings/settings.js` by calling `renderSelect` and `renderToggle` from `settings/settings-fields.js`. These are the only two pref controls registered by B-091 itself — B-092 (denseLayout) and B-093 (Data section) add their own rows as Wave 1 consumers. PASS = both rows render in the correct sections with correct labels, defaults, and keyboard interaction; FAIL = either row absent, wrong section, or pref not reflected from storage.<br><br>**AC6 — Forked field helpers module.** A new module `settings/settings-fields.js` is created. It exports `renderToggle(spec)` and `renderSelect(spec)` with signatures byte-for-byte compatible with the B-089 originals: `renderToggle({ key, label, defaultValue, section })` and `renderSelect({ key, label, options, defaultValue, section })`. PASS = `grep -r 'settings-dialog' sidepanel/` returns zero matches; `#settings-dialog` absent from DOM; FAIL = any remnant of the B-089 modal survives in sidepanel files.<br><br>**AC9 — Theme section placeholder.** The "Theme" `<fieldset>` (section 4) renders a visible but inactive placeholder row: a `<p class="settings-section-placeholder">` element with text "Theme selection coming in a future update." The section is visible to users — it is NOT hidden or omitted — so the scaffolding is present when B-037 ships in S31. No toggle or select rows are registered in this section by B-091. PASS = "Theme" `<fieldset>` visible in DOM with placeholder text; no interactive controls inside it; FAIL = section hidden, missing, or broken-looking empty fieldset.<br><br>**AC10 — Error and empty states (C-9 compliance).** (a) Pref load failure (`MSG_GET_PREFERENCES` rejects): a top-of-page error banner appears with text "Could not load settings — " and a "Reload" button that calls `location.reload()`; all controls render disabled until the reload. (b) Pref save failure (`MSG_SET_PREFERENCES` rejects): an inline per-row error message ("Could not save. Try again.") appears adjacent to the changed control; the control reverts to its last-known-good value (optimistic-revert pattern from B-089). (c) Page opened with no prefs yet stored (fresh install): all controls render at their `defaultValue` (no error state). (d) Broadcast received (`MSG_STATE_CHANGED`, scope: `prefs`): controls update in-place to reflect the new values without a page reload. (e) "Theme" section (S30): placeholder message renders; no empty-fieldset visual artifact. PASS = all five states render correctly without console errors; FAIL = any state crashes, shows blank, or throws.<br><br>**AC11 — Performance.** (a) Settings tab first paint (time from `chrome.tabs.create` to `DOMContentLoaded` paint) < 300 ms on a desktop Edge instance (extension-page budget, not subject to the 200 ms sidepanel constraint). (b) `MSG_GET_PREFERENCES` round-trip + control paint < 200 ms. (c) Pref save (`MSG_SET_PREFERENCES`) + broadcast delivery round-trip < 500 ms. These are R5 UAT-measured targets. PASS = all three within budget during UAT; FAIL = any budget exceeded consistently (≥ 3 consecutive measurements).<br><br>**AC12 — Accessibility.** (a) ARIA landmarks: `<main role="main">` wraps all content; `<h1>` page title is the first heading. (b) Section headings: each `<fieldset>` uses `<legend class="settings-section-legend">` (native `<fieldset>`/`<legend>` semantics — no additional `role` needed). (c) Control association: every toggle `<input type="checkbox">` and every `<select>` has an associated `<label for="settings-ctl-{key}">` (pattern from B-089 `_buildFieldDom`). (d) Focus management: on `DOMContentLoaded`, focus moves to the first focusable control once prefs load; during the async prefs fetch, a skeleton or disabled state is shown (not a blank). (e) Focus ring visible in both light and dark themes. (f) WCAG AA color contrast on all text and controls in both themes. (g) Keyboard navigation: Tab cycles through controls in DOM order; Enter/Space activates toggles; the page has no keyboard traps. PASS = axe-core zero critical violations; all focus and label checks pass; FAIL = any WCAG A/AA violation or missing label association.<br><br>**AC13 — Broadcast subscription.** `settings/settings.js` subscribes to `chrome.runtime.onMessage` on module load (via the forked module's `init()` call). The listener validates sender (`sender.id === chrome.runtime.id`) and scope (`scope === SCOPE.PREFERENCES`) before acting — pattern identical to B-089. When a `MSG_STATE_CHANGED` (scope: `prefs`) broadcast arrives, the listener calls the field registry's `_refreshFromBroadcast()` equivalent to re-fetch prefs and repaint controls in-place without a page reload. If a second Settings tab is open simultaneously, both tabs reflect the same pref state after any save. Subscription cleanup is automatic: tab close GC's the JS context and its listeners (no explicit `removeEventListener` needed — per R0 spike R-3 analysis). PASS = toggling a pref in one Settings tab while a second is open causes the second tab to update within broadcast latency; FAIL = second tab is stale or requires reload.<br><br>**AC14 — Manifest zero new.** Zero new `manifest.json` entries of any kind: no new `permissions`, no `web_accessible_resources`, no `chrome_url_overrides` changes, no `commands`, no CSP relaxation. `chrome.tabs.create` operates under the existing `tabs` permission. `settings/settings.html` is extension-origin and reachable without a WAR declaration. `git diff manifest.json` must be empty (version bump in `manifest.json` is applied only at sprint close, not per-item). PASS = `git diff manifest.json` empty; FAIL = any manifest change.<br><br>**AC15 — Test coverage.** New file `tests/b091-settings-page.test.js` covers (target ≥ 15 net new passing tests): (1) tab dispatcher — `chrome.tabs.query` finds existing tab → `tabs.update` + `windows.update` called, NOT `tabs.create`; (2) tab dispatcher — `chrome.tabs.query` returns empty → `tabs.create` called; (3) tab dispatcher failure → toast displayed, no unhandled rejection; (4) page render — `settings/settings.html` DOM has `<main>`, `<h1>`, and 5 `<fieldset>` elements; (5) section order — legend texts in DOM order match [Display, Layout, Groups, Theme, Data]; (6) pref load success — controls painted from `MSG_GET_PREFERENCES` response; (7) pref load failure — top banner visible, Reload button present; (8) pref save success — `MSG_SET_PREFERENCES` dispatched with correct patch; (9) pref save failure — control reverts to previous value, inline row error visible; (10) broadcast sync — `MSG_STATE_CHANGED` (scope: `prefs`) triggers control repaint; (11) `renderToggle` API parity — same spec object accepted by `settings-fields.js` and `settings-dialog.js` (B-089 ref); (12) `renderSelect` API parity — same check; (13) gear button → tab dispatcher invoked (not `openSettingsDialog`); (14) B-089 modal absent — `#settings-dialog` absent from sidepanel DOM; (15) ARIA — `<main>` present, first `<fieldset>` has associated `<legend>`, first toggle has `<label for>`. PASS = all ≥ 15 tests green + zero regressions in existing suite; FAIL = any regression or fewer than 15 net new tests.<br><br>**Out of scope (explicit).** (a) Theme picker UI — B-037, S31. (b) Import/Export controls UI — B-093, Wave 1. (c) Dense layout toggle UI — B-092, Wave 1. (d) Storage schema changes — none; `tj:prefs` shape is unchanged. (e) New keyboard shortcut for opening Settings — deferred to S31+ polish (would require a new `commands` manifest entry). (f) "Back to bookmarks" navigation button — omitted by design (closing the tab is the natural exit; a small footer note "Close this tab to return to your bookmarks" satisfies discoverability). (g) Toolbar popup (B-082) Settings entry — out of scope; flagged as a future polish candidate. |
| B-092 | As a user, I want an opt-in "compact / dense" layout toggle in Settings that shrinks bookmark rows from two-line (title + URL) to single-line with smaller fonts, so that I can fit more bookmarks on screen when working with large collections. | P3 | XS | done | 30 | B-091 (Wave 1). | **Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — visual toggle is reversible; preference write is undoable by toggling back. No items, groups, or bookmarks are mutated.<br>**AC1 — Toggle in Settings page "Layout" section.** B-091's Settings surface exposes a `renderToggle({ key: 'denseLayout', label: 'Compact layout', defaultValue: false })` row in the "Layout" section. Default state: OFF. PASS = toggle renders, is keyboard-reachable, and its label reads "Compact layout"; FAIL = missing, unlabelled, or unreachable.<br>**AC2 — Persistence key `denseLayout`.** Canonical pref key is `denseLayout` (camelCase, matching convention of `displayMode`, `autoCollapseSubGroups`, `importSkipDuplicates`). Two changes required in `background/storage/shapes.js`: (a) `DEFAULT_PREFERENCES` gains `denseLayout: false`; (b) `isPreferences` validator gains an optional boolean check `if ('denseLayout' in v && !isBool(v.denseLayout)) return false;` (OPTIONAL pattern matching `importSkipDuplicates` precedent — pre-B-092 stored prefs lack the key and `getPreferences()` merges DEFAULT_PREFERENCES, so the key is always populated at runtime). PASS = `chrome.storage.local` `tj:prefs` contains `{ denseLayout: false }` on first run and the new value after toggle; FAIL = key absent, wrong type, or validator rejects valid objects.<br>**AC3 — When ON: `.tj-dense` on `<body>`, all three surfaces.** The `.tj-dense` CSS class is added to `<body>` in sidepanel, newtab, and standalone window surfaces (all three, since they share rendering paths). Class is applied via JS at pref-load time (startup hydration) and updated on MSG_STATE_CHANGED (scope: `prefs`). No per-row JS — all visual changes are pure CSS descendant selectors scoped to `.tj-dense`. CSS deltas (R3 may tune values within ±1px/1pt; these are design targets): **Sidepanel** — `.tj-dense .item-row`: `padding` reduces from `6px 12px` → `3px 12px`; `min-height` reduces from `44px` → `28px`. `.tj-dense .item-title`: `font-size` reduces from `13px` → `12px`. `.tj-dense .item-url`: `display: none` (URL hidden; was `font-size: 11px, color: var(--text-secondary)`). **Newtab** — `.tj-dense .newtab-item-row`: `padding` reduces from `8px 14px` → `4px 14px`. `.tj-dense .newtab-item-title`: `font-size` reduces from `14px` → `12px`. `.tj-dense .newtab-item-url`: `display: none`. `.tj-dense .newtab-item-text`: `flex-direction: row` (or `gap: 0`) so title fills the single line. PASS = dense rows are visibly single-line (title only) in all three surfaces; FAIL = URL still visible or row height unchanged.<br>**AC4 — When OFF: existing two-row rendering preserved exactly.** Removing `.tj-dense` from `<body>` (or absence at startup) restores full padding, font-sizes, and URL visibility. PASS = `git diff` on CSS shows only additive `.tj-dense` selectors; zero edits to baseline rules; FAIL = baseline rules altered.<br>**AC5 — Live broadcast propagates without reload.** Toggling dense layout in Settings fires `MSG_SET_PREFERENCES` → SW persists + broadcasts `MSG_STATE_CHANGED` (scope: `prefs`) → all open surfaces receive the broadcast and flip `.tj-dense` on `<body>` immediately (no page reload). PASS = two simultaneously open sidepanel surfaces both update within broadcast latency; FAIL = stale surface or reload required.<br>**AC6 — Accessibility.** (a) WCAG AA contrast: `.item-title` at `12px` uses `var(--text-primary)` which must meet AA (≥ 4.5:1 at small sizes) — confirm token values pass in both light and dark themes. URL is hidden (`display: none`) so its `var(--text-secondary)` contrast is not a concern. (b) The toggle control itself meets existing dialog accessibility standards (label, focus ring, keyboard toggle). (c) `min-height` reduction to 28px still meets a 24px minimum touch/click target for desktop-first use (CLAUDE.md: desktop-first, no mobile layout required). PASS = axe-core zero critical on dense-mode sidepanel; FAIL = any WCAG A/AA violation introduced by dense selectors.<br>**AC7 — Validator and schema integrity.** `isPreferences` validator updated to treat `denseLayout` as OPTIONAL boolean (same pattern as `importSkipDuplicates`, line 135 in `shapes.js`): if key present, must be boolean; if absent, valid (pre-upgrade stored prefs). `getPreferences()` merge of `DEFAULT_PREFERENCES` guarantees runtime value is always populated. PASS = validator accepts objects with or without `denseLayout`; rejects objects where `denseLayout` is present but non-boolean; FAIL = regression on existing prefs or false rejection of valid objects.<br>**AC8 — Test coverage `tests/b092-dense-layout.test.js`.** Net new ≥ 5 passing tests: (1) pref save: `MSG_SET_PREFERENCES { denseLayout: true }` persists to `tj:prefs`; (2) body class ON: after pref load with `denseLayout: true`, `document.body.classList.contains('tj-dense')` → true; (3) body class OFF: after pref load with `denseLayout: false`, class absent; (4) broadcast sync: `MSG_STATE_CHANGED` (scope: `prefs`, `denseLayout: true`) causes listener to add `.tj-dense`; (5) validator optional: `isPreferences` accepts valid object lacking `denseLayout` key; rejects `{ denseLayout: 'yes' }`. PASS = all 5 tests green + zero regressions in existing suite; FAIL = any regression or fewer than 5 net new tests.<br>**Out of scope**: granular density tuning (B-086 territory), per-surface variation (all three surfaces share identical CSS class), animation/transition on toggle, group-header density changes (headers retain existing size), drag-ghost density override. |

| B-087 | As [solution-architect], I want a new C-11 "Popup-lifecycle message ordering" check in the R2 Correctness Checklist in `CLAUDE.md`, so that future R2 designs for popup extension surfaces (B-023 group-jump, B-035 standalone window, B-036 new-tab page) avoid the post-focus-shift message race that B-022 UAT-4 D-UAT-3 surfaced. | P1 | XS | done | 27 | — Sprint 26 retro HIGH action item. Pattern follows B-085 (C-10 addition) + B-077 (DoR Gate 7 subsection). Small CLAUDE.md edit; no code. | **Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — documentation edit only.<br>**AC1 — C-11 added to R2 Correctness Checklist table.** `CLAUDE.md` §Round 2 table gains a new row immediately after C-10: `C-11 \| Popup-lifecycle message ordering \| For extension-popup surfaces that trigger focus changes (tab activation via chrome.tabs.update active, window focus via chrome.windows.update focused, or any other focus-shifting API), all storage-write messages (MSG_*_ADD, MSG_*_UPDATE, MSG_RECENCY_ADD, MSG_BULK_*) MUST be queued via chrome.runtime.sendMessage BEFORE the focus-shifting API call. An await after the shift is a race — the popup's JS context tears down when focus leaves the browser window, and any post-shift await sendMessage is dropped without SW-side delivery. The Sprint 26 B-022 UAT-4 D-UAT-3 failure mode — MSG_RECENCY_ADD awaited after a chrome.tabs.update({active:true}) call, popup closed mid-await, recency never persisted in tj:recency — is the blocking precedent. Fire-and-forget via chrome.runtime.sendMessage().catch(() => {}) BEFORE the navigate await ensures native runtime delivery survives popup teardown.`<br>**AC2 — Only the R2 Correctness Checklist table edited.** No other CLAUDE.md sections altered. PASS = `git diff` limited to the R2 table; FAIL = any drift.<br>**AC3 — Existing test suite green.** `npm test` stays at 1119/1119 baseline (documentation edit, no runtime code).<br>**AC4 — Build clean.** `./build.sh` produces a clean package (CLAUDE.md is included in the zip).<br>**AC5 — Out of scope.** (a) No changes to other correctness checklist items. (b) No new ACs on other CLAUDE.md subsections. (c) No new tests — this is a documentation-only edit. |

If `_groupDragState.validReorderTargetIds` already returns `null` for the parent header (since the parent is not a sibling), the PROMOTE intercept naturally fires. R2 must confirm this chain is unambiguous or propose a tiebreaker (e.g., explicit `if (targetGroupId === draggedGroup.parentId && proposedMode === 'REORDER_ABOVE') → PROMOTE`).<br>2. **`_computeGroupDropTarget` null-return augmentation**: whether the function can carry promotion metadata or whether a separate `_computeGroupPromoteTarget` helper is needed alongside it.<br>3. **`computeGroupReorder` PROMOTE mode**: whether adding `PROMOTE` to the existing pure helper is clean or whether a new `computeGroupPromote(groups, draggedId, insertAfterGroupId)` is preferable.<br>4. **`.group-reorder-indicator` positioning reusability**: confirm `translateY` strategy works for between-top-level-group positions (the indicator's `containerRect` anchor is `itemListEl`; `sectionBottoms` cache has bottom edges of all `.group-section`s — sufficient to compute inter-group positions).<br><br>**Selector audit (rehome items)**: N/A — this item adds a new drop-zone state to `_computeGroupDropTarget` but does NOT move any existing DOM element from one surface to another. The `.group-reorder-indicator` element already lives in `itemListEl` (appended at `sidepanel/sidepanel.js:2166`) and remains unchanged. No existing test files reference a "promote-to-root" selector because this path is new. No existing test files require selector updates.<br><br>**Performance acceptance criteria (DoR item 6)**: N/A — drag-state mutation is in-memory only. The storage write (`MSG_UPDATE_GROUP`) is a single-record update, same shape and write path as B-031's `MSG_UPDATE_GROUP { collapsed: false }` fire-and-forget (§38.4.1). Sort-order recalculation reuses `computeGroupReorder`/`normaliseGroupSortOrders`. The drag-reorder performance ceiling (B-031) already governs; B-122 reuses the same write path.<br><br>**ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA — 9 ACs (R1 LOCKED 2026-04-29)**<br><br>**AC1 — Drag a sub-group outside all `.group-section` containers → promote-to-root drop zone activates.** When `_groupDragState.isSubGroupDrag` is `true` (`sidepanel/sidepanel.js:4245`) and the pointer is not over any `.group-section > .group-header[data-group-id]` (i.e., `_computeGroupDropTarget` at `sidepanel/sidepanel.js:5214` returns `null` or returns a parent-header REORDER_ABOVE that maps to PROMOTE per Q4 tiebreaker), the drag-tick activates a "promote to root" state: `.group-reorder-indicator` shows at the computed insertion position (between the two nearest top-level `.group-section` siblings, or above the first, or below the last). Drop fires `MSG_UPDATE_GROUP { id: draggedGroupId, patch: { parentId: null, sortOrder: <computed> } }` (`shared/messages.js:22`). PASS = sub-group becomes a top-level group with `parentId: null`; PASS = `MSG_UPDATE_GROUP` payload contains `parentId: null`; FAIL = sub-group stays nested or message not fired.<br><br>**AC2 — Visual indicator parity with drag-reorder (no new visual primitive).** The "promote to root" insertion cue uses the same `.group-reorder-indicator` DOM element (`sidepanel/sidepanel.js:431`) and the same CSS class as B-031's group drag-reorder line. No new DOM element, no new CSS class, no new visual treatment is introduced. PASS = same indicator element and class appear during promote-to-root drag; FAIL = any new `<div>`, class, or CSS rule is added solely for the promotion visual.<br><br>**AC3 — Mid-list ordering: drop between two top-level groups inserts at that ordinal.** When a sub-group is dropped between top-level group A and top-level group B, the promoted group is inserted at that ordinal with a recalculated `sortOrder`. Recalculation reuses the `computeGroupReorder` `PROMOTE` mode (or equivalent new helper). PASS = promoted group appears between A and B in the rendered list; PASS = `sortOrder` values for all affected top-level groups are consecutive integers post-normalisation; FAIL = ordinal off-by-one or unstable ordering after subsequent reloads.<br><br>**AC4 — Above-own-parent edge → promote (Q4 tiebreaker). R2-VERIFY required.** Dragging a sub-group so that the pointer is in the REORDER_ABOVE zone (top 25%) of its own parent's `.group-header` (`sidepanel/sidepanel.js:5232–5246`) is treated as PROMOTE. PASS = `.group-reorder-indicator` appears above the parent's `.group-section` at the insertion position; PASS = drop fires `MSG_UPDATE_GROUP { parentId: null }`; FAIL = gesture silently ignored, fires a sibling-bucket REORDER, or shows REJECT indicator. Drift bar is therefore NOT a candidate for a spacer fix (it is already out-of-flow).<br>- `sidepanel/sidepanel.js:2436-2448` — `.item-drift-bar` `<span>` is injected as the FIRST child of every `.item-row` built by `buildItemRow`; always present in DOM; toggled by `hidden` attribute. `buildOpenTabRow` (`sidepanel.js:2788`) does NOT inject a drift bar (open-tab rows cannot drift).<br>- `newtab/newtab.css:398-429` — newtab uses dot indicators inside `.newtab-item-indicators` (right-side, flex-end) — no left-side border indicator; any alignment issue on newtab is structurally different. **R2-VERIFY** whether the newtab surface actually has a left-side alignment problem at all.<br>- `newtab/newtab.js:1092-1093` — `_buildIndicators(itemId)` returns `null` when no live/active/audible/drifted state applies; indicator wrap is NOT appended to the row in the no-indicator case — right-side only, does not affect left content origin. **R2-VERIFY** newtab alignment impact.<br>- `popup/popup.css:314-334` — popup uses `.qs-favicon-overlay` dot at `bottom: -2px; right: -2px` over the favicon; no left-side border indicator; left-side content origin is uniform across all popup rows. **R2-VERIFY** popup alignment impact (likely N/A).<br><br>**ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA — 7 ACs (R1 LOCKED 2026-04-29)**<br><br>**AC1 — Sidepanel: content origin parity across indicator variants**. After the fix, the computed left position of the first visible flex content child (favicon or `.item-select` checkbox, measured via `getBoundingClientRect().left`) is identical — within ±0.5 px rounding — across all four sidepanel row variants: (a) plain row (no indicator), (b) `data-live="true"` row, (c) `data-active="true"` row, (d) drifted row (drift bar is absolutely positioned and out-of-flow — `sidepanel.css:583-591` — and must not shift content). The fix approach (CSS-only transparent left border, padding adjustment, or dedicated spacer span) is decided at R2; this AC is outcome-based, not implementation-prescribed. PASS = `getBoundingClientRect().left` of the first visible flex child is identical across all four variants in the same group; FAIL = any variant deviates.<br>- **Sub-point 1**: The `.item-drift-bar` span (`sidepanel.css:583-591`) is already absolutely positioned and out-of-flow. It does NOT contribute to flex layout offset and requires no spacer treatment. The scope of AC1 is confined to the `border-left` indicator offset only.<br>- **Sub-point 2**: Dense-layout variant (`.tj-dense .item-row` at `sidepanel.css:544-547`, `padding: 3px 12px`) must also be verified — spacer compensation must apply equally in dense mode. **R2-VERIFY** whether the dense-mode padding differs from normal mode and whether a separate CSS rule is needed.<br><br>**AC2 — Sidepanel: drift bar does not require a spacer (confirmed out-of-flow)**. If the positions match, no additional spacer work is required for the drift bar. Whatever structural approach is introduced: (a) no visible border, background-color, or color property; (b) no `aria-*` attribute, no `role`, no `title`; (c) if a DOM node, carries `aria-hidden="true"`; (d) does not appear in the accessibility tree or perturb screen-reader navigation order. **R2-VERIFY**: confirm the chosen approach (CSS vs DOM node) does not add an unexpected node to the AT tree. PASS = screen-reader DOM inspection shows no new announced element; computed style shows zero visual properties on any added element; FAIL = any visual bleed or AT-announced node introduced.<br><br>**AC4 — Surface coverage (R2-scoped)**. No confirmation dialog is required for promotion — it is reversible via `MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM`. No selector rehome. No other test files are known to assert floating-row selectors. R2 must verify and enumerate if additional files exist.<br><br>**Performance acceptance criteria (DoR item 6)**: N/A — this is a CSS and DOM-attribute change only. No new search, render, or startup paths are introduced. Render path is the existing B-121 synthetic row injection (`sidepanel/sidepanel.js:2869` — `buildFloatingTabRow`; `newtab/newtab.js` R2-VERIFY). No performance ceiling impact expected.<br><br>**AC1 — Dotted green vertical bar renders on floating-tab rows across all render surfaces.**<br>Floating-tab rows (`[data-floating="true"]`) display a 3 px dotted vertical bar in the row's left gutter, styled via a new `.item-floating-bar` child element. The bar mechanism mirrors the B-101 dotted drift bar approach (`sidepanel/sidepanel.css:583-589` — `.item-drift-bar { border-left: 3px dotted var(--drifted-color) }`). The floating bar uses `border-left: 3px dotted var(--floating-bar-color)` (see AC2 for token). The bar renders on all three surfaces: sidepanel (`sidepanel/sidepanel.js:2869` — `buildFloatingTabRow`), newtab (`newtab/newtab.js` R2-VERIFY exact function), and standalone (same file as sidepanel — `background/service-worker.js:78-92` confirms standalone is `sidepanel/sidepanel.html` in a popup window; one fix covers both per `docs/design/60-b-121-floating-tab-render.md §60.2.4`).<br><br>**AC2 — Bar color parameterized via new CSS token `--floating-bar-color`; default is green; documented for future swap.**<br>A new CSS custom property `--floating-bar-color` is added to `shared/themes.css`. Its default value (light/base theme) equals the existing `--live-indicator` value for that theme (R2-VERIFY: `shared/themes.css:78` shows `--live-indicator: #16a34a` for the base light theme — R2 must confirm the exact value and whether a direct reference `var(--live-indicator)` is the correct default or a literal hex is required per the B-108 D-2 precedent). The token is defined in all 14 theme blocks. A code comment on the token declaration documents: "Default is green (matches live indicator). To swap to yellow for future yellow-bar design: change this token value only — no other CSS changes required." R2 to own the 14-theme enumeration in the WCAG AA audit table (see AC6).<br><br>**AC3 — Floating-tab rows have a distinct ARIA label that differs from saved-bookmark live-tab rows.**<br>The `aria-label` on a floating-tab row reads `"floating tab — <title>"` (plus active/audible/selected suffixes as applicable). The existing `buildItemRowAriaLabel` function (`shared/aria-label.js:22-35`) currently appends `"live tab"` when `live?.live === true` (`shared/aria-label.js:30`). Floating-tab rows must NOT use the generic `buildItemRowAriaLabel` path that produces `"live tab"`. Instead, `buildFloatingTabRow` (`sidepanel/sidepanel.js:2869`) sets an explicit `aria-label` of the form `"floating tab — <title>"` (with active/audible/selected suffixes appended where applicable, in the same order as `buildItemRowAriaLabel`). The patch path (`patchFloatingMembersSections`, `sidepanel/sidepanel.js:2890`) must also update the `aria-label` when a floating row is patched in-place. The newtab floating-row builder (R2-VERIFY) must apply the same label. No changes are made to `shared/aria-label.js` itself — the distinct label is applied directly in the floating-row builders.<br><br>**AC4 — Hover CTA "Save as bookmark" appears on floating-tab row hover; clicking it dispatches `MSG_PROMOTE_TAB`.**<br>On pointer hover over a `[data-floating="true"]` row, a "Save as bookmark" button/affordance is revealed (CSS `visibility` or `opacity` toggle on `:hover`, matching the pattern used for existing row action buttons). Clicking the CTA dispatches `MSG_PROMOTE_TAB` with `{ tabId: <number>, groupId: <string|null> }` (`shared/messages.js:35` — `export const MSG_PROMOTE_TAB = 'tj/promoteTab'`; `background/messages/storage-handlers.js:297-304` — handler accepts `tabId: number`, `groupId: string|null`). The `tabId` is sourced from the row's `data-tab-id` attribute (set by `buildOpenTabRow` which `buildFloatingTabRow` delegates to — `sidepanel/sidepanel.js:2869`). The `groupId` passed is the parent group's ID (sourced from `member.parentItemId` R2-VERIFY — R2 must confirm whether `groupId` should be the floating member's `parentItemId`'s group or `null`; if the existing `MSG_PROMOTE_TAB` handler at `storage-handlers.js:297` accepts a synthetic floating-member shape, no new message type is needed). Error handling mirrors the existing Open Tabs save CTA pattern (`sidepanel/sidepanel.js:6233-6244` — `ERR_SAFE_MODE`, `ERR_DUPLICATE_URL`, `ERR_VALIDATION` toasts). R2-VERIFY: confirm `MSG_PROMOTE_TAB` payload accepts a `tabId` sourced from a floating-tab row (no saved `itemId` backing it) — the handler at `storage-handlers.js:297` calls `chrome.tabs.get(p.tabId)` which is tab-ID-only and does not require a saved item, so this should work, but R2 must confirm.<br><br>**AC5 — Floating-tab rows are visually distinct from saved-bookmark live-tab rows by at least one non-color cue.**<br>The dotted bar (AC1) is the primary non-color cue — saved-bookmark live-tab rows have a solid 3 px green left border (`sidepanel/sidepanel.css:468` — `border-left: 3px solid var(--live-indicator)`); floating-tab rows have a dotted 3 px green left bar (`.item-floating-bar`). Dottedness is the structural non-color differentiator. Color may also differ across themes where `--floating-bar-color` diverges from `--live-indicator` (future yellow swap), but the dotted-vs-solid distinction is the mandated non-color cue that must hold even when the two token values are identical (i.e., in the default green configuration).<br><br>**AC6 — WCAG AA contrast preserved across all 14 themes (B-117 matrix-audit pattern).**<br>R1 enumerates the audit scope; R2 ([solution-architect]) owns the full 14-theme matrix table (per `docs/design/57-b-117-gc-matrix-audit.md` pattern). Audit scope: (a) `--floating-bar-color` dotted bar color vs row background color across all 14 themes; (b) hover CTA button text/icon color vs CTA background color across all 14 themes; (c) floating-tab row text color contrast vs row background across all 14 themes (only if B-124 introduces any text styling changes — if text is unchanged from the existing open-tab row style, this sub-scope is N/A and R2 must state so explicitly). All three dimensions must pass WCAG AA (4.5:1 for normal text, 3:1 for large text and UI components). The 14 themes are enumerated in `shared/themes.css` (R2-VERIFY exact theme selector list).<br><br>**AC7 — Automated tests in `tests/b124-floating-visual.test.js` cover: dotted-bar presence, ARIA label, hover CTA dispatch, and the drift-skip invariant.**<br>(a) Dotted-bar presence: a floating-tab row rendered via `buildFloatingTabRow` contains a child element with class `.item-floating-bar` (or equivalent class name — R2 to finalize). (b) ARIA label: the row's `aria-label` starts with `"floating tab — "` and does NOT contain `"live tab"`. (c) Hover CTA dispatch: simulating a click on the save CTA within a `[data-floating="true"]` row dispatches `MSG_PROMOTE_TAB` with the correct `tabId`. (d) Drift-skip invariant: `detectDriftForTab` (`background/tabs/drift.js:29`) must return early (no `tj:drift` write) for a tab whose `tabId` is NOT present in `claimsMirror` (i.e., a floating tab). This is already guaranteed by `drift.js:31-34` — `getItemIdForTab(tabId)` returns `null` for unclaimed tabs and the function returns immediately at the AC6 guard. The test must assert: given a `tabId` that exists in `LiveTabIndex` but is NOT in `claimsMirror`, calling `detectDriftForTab(tabId, someUrl, items)` produces zero writes to `tj:drift`. This invariant is the Q2 confirmed contract encoded as a regression test. (b) Drift behavior for promoted items — B-099. (c) Tab-claim invariants and `claimsMirror` contract changes — B-121. | B-125 | As a user, when a tab claimed by saved bookmark A navigates to a URL matching saved bookmark B (or to no bookmark at all), the original tab should either (a) remain claimed by bookmark A and enter a drifted state per the B-099 Option B contract, OR (b) be released cleanly with the original bookmark un-claimed — but in NO case should the tab spawn a duplicate live-row, jump claim ownership to a different bookmark mid-navigation, or create a new tab where none was opened. | P0 | M | in-progress | 38 | B-001c (TabClaims), B-001d (drift), B-010 (live indicator), B-099 ✅ (drift fix Option B — claim survives URL change), B-100 ✅ (delete-on-live UX), B-102 ✅ (cross-window demote broadcast), B-103 ✅ (promote-tab duplicate). **Filed from S37 close 2026-04-28 product-owner regression report with screenshots.** Repro: open `https://xcelenergy.sharepoint.com/` (claimed by bookmark "The Source") → click in-page link to a Workday URL → observe two duplicate "Home - Workday" rows in sidepanel + a new tab opens each time. Suspected root cause: B-099 Option B contract violated — the tab's claim is being released and a new claim opened on a different bookmark instead of the original entering a drifted state. Could also be opener-chain or floating-group append racing with claim-reassignment. | **R1 LOCKED (2026-04-28)**<br><br>**Destructive-action confirmation (DoR item 7)**: N/A — the fix is a read-only behavior gate added to `reevaluateTab`'s auto-claim branch. No destructive action is introduced or removed. A misclaimed tab is recoverable by closing the spawned tab or invoking MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM, both of which carry their own confirmations where required. No confirmation dialog is added or removed by this item.<br><br>**Source-citation gate (B-118 self-applied)**:<br>- `background/tabs/tab-claims.js:193-219` — `reevaluateTab` auto-claim branch (verified via Read: lines 193-219 contain the `alreadyClaimed` short-circuit and the `candidates` filter; zero calls to `releaseClaimByTab` present).<br>- `background/tabs/tab-events.js:140-171` — opener-chain inheritance path: `recordOpener`, async `walkOpenerChain` block, `appendFloatingGroup` call, and `broadcast(SCOPE.LIVE_STATE, 'tab/opener-inherited')` (verified via Read: lines 140-171).<br>- `background/tabs/opener-chain.js` — `openerMap` + `walkOpenerChain` (referenced by §58.3(e); file verified to exist; line-level citations deferred to R2-VERIFY per R0 spike §58.2 which read the file but cited it as 85 lines without per-function line offsets).<br>- `docs/design/58-b-125-b-121-r0-spike.md §58.3(e)` — auto-claim of opener-chain-spawned tab identified as HIGH (75%) root cause (verified via Read: lines 123-170).<br>- `docs/design/46-b-099-drift-fix.md` — B-099 Option B design chapter; confirms release-path inventory and D-1/D-3 contracts. R2-VERIFY: read the chapter to confirm the four sanctioned `releaseClaimByTab` call sites match §58.5 X3 table before R2 is finalized.<br><br>**R0 revised diagnosis**: The user's original framing ("B-099 Option B regression — original tab's claim is being released") is INCORRECT per R0 static analysis. The actual root cause is `reevaluateTab` (`tab-claims.js:193-219`) firing for the opener-chain-spawned NEW tab and auto-claiming it to a coincidentally URL-matching saved bookmark ("Home - Workday"), because no gate exists to detect that the new tab is already "spoken for" by opener-chain inheritance. The ORIGINAL tab's claim is intact (B-099 holds). The "duplicate Home - Workday" rows are: row 1 = the saved bookmark row ("Home - Workday") now claimed by the new tab (live indicator present); row 2 = a second new tab (from a subsequent click) landing in Open Tabs because "Home - Workday" is already claimed. Confidence: HIGH (75%) per R0 §58.6.<br><br>**Q1 — Mechanism (LOCKED)**: R3 distinguishes "opener-chain-spawned new tab" (should NOT auto-claim) from "user-initiated new tab" (SHOULD auto-claim) via an ephemeral SW-memory `inheritedTabs: Set<number>`. When the `tab-events.js:140-171` opener-chain async block succeeds — i.e., `walkOpenerChain` returns a non-null `result` AND `appendFloatingGroup` resolves without throwing — `tab.id` is added to `inheritedTabs`. The `reevaluateTab` function checks `inheritedTabs.has(tabId)` before the `!alreadyClaimed` auto-claim block; if the tab is in the set, the auto-claim is skipped entirely. The set is pruned when `chrome.tabs.onRemoved` fires. Pattern mirrors `openerMap` in `background/tabs/opener-chain.js`. No storage schema change; the set is ephemeral and lost on SW restart (cold-start re-association via `tj:floatingGroups` is the recovery path, per §58.8 C-3 analysis).<br><br>**Q2 — B-099 release-path regression guard (LOCKED)**: ACs must include an explicit assertion that the four sanctioned `releaseClaimByTab` call sites enumerated in §58.5 X3 are the ONLY call sites in the post-fix codebase. No new call site may be introduced by the B-125 fix. The existing `tests/b099-drift-fix.test.js` T1 + T7 + `tests/tab-url-change.test.js` D-1/D-3 tests remain the primary regression guards and must pass unchanged after R3.<br><br>**Q3 — User-initiated tab regression guard (LOCKED)**: A new tab that does NOT pass through the opener-chain inheritance path (`tab.openerTabId` is `undefined` or `walkOpenerChain` returns `null`) and whose URL matches an unclaimed saved bookmark STILL auto-claims via `reevaluateTab` as before. The B-125 fix narrows the auto-claim gate; it does not widen the skip condition. AC2 + T-125-B are the regression guards.<br><br>**Q4 — Out of scope (LOCKED)**: (a) B-099 Option B contract revisions — no changes to D-1/D-3 behavior; (b) new storage schema or storage partition changes — none; (c) new message types in `shared/messages.js` — none; (d) multi-window broadcast path changes — none; (e) demote/promote flow changes — none; (f) any changes to `tab-claims.js` outside the `reevaluateTab` function body and the new `inheritedTabs` set declaration; (g) sidepanel UI changes — none; (h) B-121 floating-group render pipeline — separate item, separate R3.<br><br>**Performance acceptance criteria (DoR item 6)**: `chrome.tabs.onCreated` handler latency ≤ 500ms (existing B-013 contract). The `inheritedTabs.has(tabId)` check inside `reevaluateTab` is O(1) — overhead is negligible and well within the existing contract ceiling.<br><br>**C-9 empty-state design**:<br>(i) **Opener-chain returns no parent** (`walkOpenerChain` returns `null`): `tab.id` is NOT added to `inheritedTabs`; the tab proceeds normally with `reevaluateTab` auto-claim eligible. No behavior change from current code.<br>(ii) **Opener-chain returns a parent but `appendFloatingGroup` throws**: SAFE-FALLBACK — `tab.id` is NOT added to `inheritedTabs` (the `appendFloatingGroup` call is the gate event; if it fails, the floating-group write did not succeed, so the user expects the tab to behave like an ungrouped opener-tab; auto-claim is therefore permitted). `inheritedTabs.add(tab.id)` MUST be placed strictly after the `await appendFloatingGroup(...)` call resolves successfully, not before.<br>(iii) **`appendFloatingGroup` succeeds but the parent bookmark is deleted before `reevaluateTab` runs**: the tab stays in `inheritedTabs` until `chrome.tabs.onRemoved` prunes it. Subsequent `reevaluateTab` invocations skip auto-claim because the marker is still present. The tab is now ungrouped-but-inheritance-marked; it will live in Open Tabs (or float without a visible parent group) until it is closed. This is acceptable — the alternative (claiming a different bookmark because the parent is gone) is the bug we are fixing. Documented as known edge case in AC7.<br><br>**B-119 fix-scope test-assertion enumeration (pre-existing assertions to update)**:<br>The following test files assert `reevaluateTab` behavior and must be reviewed at R3 to confirm they still pass after the `inheritedTabs` gate is added. All calls in these files pass tabs that are NOT in `inheritedTabs` (the set starts empty in each `beforeEach` reset), so the gate condition is false and existing assertions remain valid — NO assertion text changes are required. Enumerated for R3 completeness:<br>- `tests/b099-drift-fix.test.js:125` — T1: `reevaluateTab(10, ...)` on a claimed tab; `alreadyClaimed` short-circuit fires before the `inheritedTabs` gate is reached. No update needed.<br>- `tests/b099-drift-fix.test.js:296` — T6: `reevaluateTab(15, ...)` on a drifted-but-claimed tab. Same short-circuit. No update needed.<br>- `tests/b099-drift-fix.test.js:332` — T7: `reevaluateTab(17, ...)` re-claim contention. Tab 17 is claimed; `alreadyClaimed` fires. No update needed.<br>- `tests/b099-drift-fix.test.js:397,407` — T9: double-drift `reevaluateTab(19, ...)`. Tab 19 is claimed. No update needed.<br>- `tests/tab-url-change.test.js:46` — D-1: `reevaluateTab(50, ...)` on a claimed tab. No update needed.<br>- `tests/tab-url-change.test.js:74` — D-1: `reevaluateTab(60, ...)` on a claimed tab. No update needed.<br>R3 MUST verify that `__resetTabClaims` (called in `beforeEach`) also resets `inheritedTabs` (or that `inheritedTabs` is exported from `tab-claims.js` alongside `__resetTabClaims` so tests can reset it). If the set is declared in `tab-events.js` instead, R3 must ensure the test setup imports and resets it there. R2-VERIFY which module owns `inheritedTabs`.<br><br>**ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA — 7 ACs (R1 LOCKED 2026-04-28)**<br><br>**AC1 — Opener-chain-spawned tab does NOT auto-claim a URL-matching saved bookmark.** When the `tab-events.js:140-171` opener-chain async block fires successfully (`walkOpenerChain` returns a non-null result AND `appendFloatingGroup` resolves without throwing), the new tab's `id` is added to `inheritedTabs: Set<number>`. When `reevaluateTab` is subsequently invoked for this tab (via the `onUpdated` URL-change path) and its URL matches an unclaimed saved bookmark, the auto-claim block (`tab-claims.js:204-213`) is skipped because `inheritedTabs.has(tabId)` is `true`. The original tab's claim is unaffected (B-099 D-1 holds). The "Home - Workday" bookmark is NOT claimed by the spawned tab. PASS = the opener-chain-spawned tab remains unclaimed by the matching bookmark; the bookmark row shows no live indicator for the new tab; the new tab appears in Open Tabs (pre-B-121 behavior) rather than as a spurious claim on the bookmark. FAIL = any claim is written to `claimsMirror` for the opener-chain-spawned tab pointing at the matching bookmark.<br><br>**AC2 — User-initiated tab DOES auto-claim (regression guard).** A new tab that (a) has no `openerTabId` OR (b) has an `openerTabId` but `walkOpenerChain` returns `null` (no parent found in the claimed item set) AND whose URL matches an unclaimed saved bookmark URL still auto-claims via `reevaluateTab` as before B-125. `inheritedTabs` does NOT contain this tab's `id`. PASS = claim is written to `claimsMirror` for the unclaimed bookmark → new tab pairing; bookmark row shows live indicator. FAIL = no claim established for a user-initiated tab that matches an unclaimed bookmark URL.<br><br>**AC3 — `inheritedTabs` set pruned on tab close.** When `chrome.tabs.onRemoved` fires for a tab whose `id` is in `inheritedTabs`, that `id` is removed from the set. PASS = `inheritedTabs.size` decreases by 1 after tab removal; subsequent `reevaluateTab` calls for the same `id` (impossible in practice after removal, but verifiable in tests) would not be skipped. FAIL = `id` remains in `inheritedTabs` after removal (memory leak).<br><br>**AC4 — B-099 Option B regression guard.** Existing B-099 D-1 drift-on-URL-change behavior is completely unchanged. Specifically: (a) the original tab's claim (`T_orig` → bookmark A) survives URL navigation to a different-bookmark URL (user's exact repro: open `https://xcelenergy.sharepoint.com/` claimed by "The Source" → navigate to Workday URL → original tab still claimed by "The Source"; drift record written; NO claim transfer to "Home - Workday"); (b) the four sanctioned `releaseClaimByTab` call sites in §58.5 X3 (`tab-events.js:202` onRemoved, `tab-events.js:280` windows.onRemoved cascade, `storage-handlers.js:331` MSG_DEMOTE_ITEM, `storage-handlers.js:396` MSG_NAVIGATE_TO_ITEM AC3) remain the ONLY call sites in the post-fix codebase — no new call site introduced by B-125. R2-VERIFY: confirm exact line numbers for the four call sites before R3 begins. PASS = `tests/b099-drift-fix.test.js` T1/T6/T7/T9 + `tests/tab-url-change.test.js` D-1 (both cases) all pass unchanged after B-125 R3; grep for `releaseClaimByTab` call sites post-fix returns exactly four. FAIL = any of the above tests fail; any new `releaseClaimByTab` call site exists.<br><br>**AC5 — Test file `tests/b125-claim-inheritance-gate.test.js`.** A new test file is created with at minimum the following cases:<br>- **T-125-A** — Opener-chain-spawned tab does NOT auto-claim a URL-matching unclaimed bookmark. Seeds: item A (`https://a.example`) claimed by tab 100; item B (`https://b.example`) unclaimed; simulates `chrome.tabs.onCreated` with `openerTabId: 100` and URL `https://b.example`; manually adds tab 101 to `inheritedTabs`; calls `reevaluateTab(101, 'https://b.example', items)`; asserts item B has no claim in `claimsMirror`.<br>- **T-125-B** — User-initiated tab (no opener, not in `inheritedTabs`) with matching URL DOES auto-claim. Seeds: item B (`https://b.example`) unclaimed; tab 102 NOT in `inheritedTabs`; calls `reevaluateTab(102, 'https://b.example', items)`; asserts item B is now claimed by tab 102.<br>- **T-125-C** — Repeated opener-chain clicks (tabs 101, 103, 104, all from opener tab 100) all stay floating; none claim item B. Seeds: item A claimed by tab 100; item B unclaimed; tabs 101, 103, 104 all added to `inheritedTabs`; `reevaluateTab` called for each; asserts item B unclaimed after all three calls.<br>- **T-125-D** — `inheritedTabs` set cleared on tab close. Seeds: tab 101 in `inheritedTabs`; simulates onRemoved; asserts tab 101 no longer in `inheritedTabs`.<br>- **T-125-E** — B-099 regression guard: original tab T_orig (claimed by item A) navigates to item B's URL; item B stays unclaimed; item A retains its claim. (Mirrors T7 in `b099-drift-fix.test.js`; presence here pins the B-125-specific regression surface.)<br>All helpers import from existing test infrastructure; no `chrome-mock.js` changes; no new mock API calls beyond what the existing tab-claims + opener-chain test suite uses. PASS = all five cases green; full suite baseline ≥ 1,641 (R2-VERIFY: confirm count at R3 start). FAIL = any case fails or introduces a regression in the existing suite.<br><br>**AC6 — No regressions.** Full test suite (post-S37 baseline: 1,641 passing; R2-VERIFY: recount at R3 start as B-126 and B-120 may adjust the total) passes with zero failures after all B-125 changes. `./build.sh` produces a clean package. PASS = all constraints verified. FAIL = any test failure or build error.<br><br>**AC7 — Out of scope (explicit).** Per Q4 above: (a) no changes to `tab-claims.js` outside the `reevaluateTab` function body and the `inheritedTabs` set declaration/export; (b) no changes to `shared/messages.js`; (c) no `manifest.json` changes; (d) no storage schema changes; (e) no UI changes to sidepanel, newtab, popup, or standalone surfaces; (f) B-121 floating-group render pipeline — separate item; (g) the `appendFloatingGroup` parent-itemId latent bug (§58.4(i)) — deferred; the known edge case from C-9(iii) (tab marked inherited after parent bookmark deleted stays in Open Tabs) is documented here and acceptable. |

| B-131 | As a user, when I open a new floating tab within a group (e.g., middle-click a link), I want the row to render with the new tab's actual title from the start — not display the title of a different existing item in the list which then "corrects itself" only after I activate the page. | P1 | TBD | superseded-by-B-137 | 40 | B-013, B-018, B-121, B-124. **Filed 2026-04-30 from product-owner observation post-v1.33.0 ship.** Repro: open floating tab via opener-chain inheritance (e.g., middle-click a link inside a group) → newly-rendered floating row briefly shows the title of an unrelated sibling item; on tab activation the row "corrects" to the right title. Suspected race in `buildFloatingTabRow` / `patchFloatingMembersSections` rendering — the floating-member descriptor may be sourced/looked-up against a stale or off-by-one index, OR the `chrome.tabs.onCreated` event delivers `tab.title === ''` initially and the rendered row displaces another item's title via incorrect key/lookup. **Tier**: TBD — likely M Full or Spike-First (touches the same B-121 floating-render pipeline that needed an R0 spike in S38). **R1 / R2 to investigate** the descriptor → row mapping, the sequence of `chrome.tabs.onCreated → opener-chain walk → appendFloatingGroup → patch broadcast`, and whether row-key reuse / dedup is causing title-bleed across rows. **2026-04-30 product-owner update**: bug may no longer be reproducible after the v1.33.1 ship — Sprint 40 R1 must FIRST verify reproducibility on the latest `release/v2` build before sinking effort into R2. If R1 cannot reproduce, downgrade to a static-analysis review of the suspected descriptor/dedup paths to confirm no latent race remains, then close as `wontfix-not-repro` if review is clean. |
| B-132 | As a user, after reloading the extension I want floating tabs (opened via opener-chain inheritance, e.g., middle-click a link inside a group) to continue appearing inside the group they originated in — not silently revert to landing in the Open Tabs section as if the inheritance link was lost. | P1 | M | done | 40 | B-013, B-018, B-121, B-125. **Filed 2026-04-30 from product-owner observation post-v1.33.0 ship.** Repro: open extension fresh → floating tabs appear in group correctly (per B-121 contract). Reload extension via Edge `Reload` button → newly-spawned floating tabs now go to Open Tabs section instead of the originating group. Suspected: SW restart loses the in-memory `inheritedTabs: Set<number>` (B-125) AND/OR the `tj:floatingGroups` cold-start re-association path is failing to re-bind opener-chain inheritance for tabs that existed before the reload. Could also be that the opener-chain itself (`openerMap` in `background/tabs/opener-chain.js`) is purely in-memory and SW restart drops it, so post-reload `chrome.tabs.onCreated` events have no opener-chain context to walk. **Tier**: TBD — likely M Full or Spike-First (this is core B-121 + B-125 subsystem). Distinguish: (a) does the bug ONLY affect tabs spawned post-reload (SW lost opener context), or (b) does it also affect tabs that were already floating PRE-reload (cold-start re-association regression)? **R1 / R2 to investigate** the opener-chain memory persistence story and the cold-start floating-group re-bind path. |
| B-133 | As a user, I want items rendered in the Open Tabs section to use the **same dotted-green indicator** as floating tabs (instead of the current solid-green live indicator), so that the visual treatment for ephemeral items is consolidated — both Open Tabs section rows and floating-group rows represent state that will be lost when the tab is closed (or when the browser closes). Solid-green stays reserved for persistent (saved-bookmark, currently-live) rows. | P3 | XS | done | 40 | B-124 ✅ (introduced `--floating-bar-color` token + dotted-green visual cue), B-130 ✅ (consolidated dotted-green to override existing live indicator border). **Filed 2026-04-30 from product-owner UX-consolidation request after v1.33.1 ship.** Rationale: today the Open Tabs section uses the SOLID green live-indicator (`var(--live-indicator)`), identical to a saved-bookmark row whose claimed tab is currently open. But Open Tabs rows are EPHEMERAL — when the user closes the tab, the row is gone; when the browser closes, the row is gone. They share the ephemeral nature of floating-group rows (also live tabs that aren't saved). Visual taxonomy proposal: **solid-green = persistent (saved bookmark, currently live)** · **dotted-green = ephemeral (floating tab, or Open Tabs section row)**. **Tier**: TBD — likely Fast Track XS (CSS rule extension to cover the Open Tabs row selector with `border-left-style: dotted` + `border-left-color: var(--floating-bar-color)`, mirroring B-130's pattern). **R1 / R2 to investigate** the Open Tabs row builder + selector (sidepanel `buildOpenTabRow`, newtab equivalent if applicable, popup quick-search if applicable), confirm no left-side-indicator collision, and decide whether to apply across all surfaces (sidepanel + newtab + popup) or sidepanel-only. **Out of scope** (R2 to confirm): no change to `--live-indicator` token, no change to `--floating-bar-color` token, no change to drift bar (B-101), no change to active state (`[data-active="true"]`), no change to ARIA labels (Open Tabs rows do not get the "floating tab — " ARIA prefix per B-124 §61.8 — they remain "open tab — " or whatever the existing convention is). |

| B-149 | As a user, when a saved bookmark's tab has navigated to a different URL (drifted state) and I leave the page sitting idle for a few minutes, I want the tab to remain claimed by the saved bookmark (in drifted state) — NOT silently lose its tracking and migrate to the Open Tabs section. The B-099 Option B contract says claims survive URL change; the user's smoke-test post-v1.35.0 shows drifted tabs losing their claim association after a few minutes idle on the page (no extension reload, just SW idle-timeout-driven cold-start). | P0 | TBD | backlog | TBD | B-099 ✅ (drift-fix Option B contract — claim survives URL change), B-125 ✅ (claimsMirror + inheritedTabs + reconcileClaims architecture), B-132 ✅ (cold-start preMarkInheritedFromFloatingGroups + reconcileClaims Phase 2 gate). **Filed 2026-04-30 from product-owner observation during v1.35.0 pre-merge testing.** Repro: saved bookmark with claimed tab → user navigates tab to different URL → tab enters drifted state correctly → user leaves page idle for ~few minutes → tab migrates to Open Tabs section (claim is lost). **No explicit extension reload required**, distinguishing this from the dev-environment reload-clears-session-storage pattern. Suspected mechanisms (R0 spike to bisect): (a) `chrome.storage.session.tj:tabClaims` write is failing silently — claims aren't actually persisted across SW idle-timeout-driven restarts; (b) `chrome.storage.session` is being cleared more aggressively than expected (Edge-specific behavior?); (c) `reconcileClaims` Phase 1 (evict claims pointing at no-longer-live tabIds) is mistakenly evicting LIVE drifted claims via a URL-match step that shouldn't be there; (d) some other code path explicitly calls `releaseClaimByTab` on drifted tabs (regression of B-099 Option B contract). **Tier**: TBD — likely M Full (tab-claims subsystem; B-099/B-125/B-132 architecture); possibly **Spike-First** if (b) Edge-specific behavior turns out to be the root cause and we need a different persistence layer (e.g., `chrome.storage.local` instead of `chrome.storage.session`). **Distinguishing test**: SW console inspection of `chrome.storage.session.tj:tabClaims` BEFORE and AFTER the few-minute idle-timeout. If the partition is empty or stale post-idle, that's mechanism (a) or (b). If the partition has correct data but the in-memory claimsMirror is empty, that's a SW-startup re-hydration bug. |
